# Changelog

All notable changes to Coffee Pub Chameleon. Format follows Keep a Changelog, and versions follow SemVer.

## [Unreleased]

### Documentation catch-up, batch 8 (2026-11-14): Documents, the Whiteboard's boards, the guest door and two small ones

Documentation only; no code changed. Written from the code on main (`origin/main` at 0cb42cf8: the module manifests and sources, `server/index.js`, `server/guest-door.js`, `public/guest.js`, `public/admin.js`, the commits and their titles). **Read as code, not verified:** nothing here was run in a browser, with two people, on a phone or in a real call, and the module tests and the other checks were not rerun. **Checked by tool:** `npm run check:docs`, and `node tools/check-names.mjs` (ends at 2 problems, as it should).

- **Documents 0.11.12 to 0.11.31.** [userguide-documents](documentation/userguides/userguide-documents.md) and [architecture-documents](documentation/architecture/architecture-documents.md) now cover: writing together (#665; 0.11.21 to 0.11.27, behind the module setting **Let several people type in a document at once**, which stays off) with its channel, update gate, elected writer, stored state, shared title and page settings, the document **Lock**, the editors list, one edit window for each person and the notes a person reads; the title-bar document dropdown (0.11.30, with **New document**, **Rename** and **Delete** in it); **Print**, **Save as Markdown file** and **Open Markdown file** (0.11.18); the 12 px docked gutter (0.11.28, #777); and the vendored Yjs bundle (0.11.13). Cursors and selections of other people are not built. TODO.md's Documents entry and known-issues.md are brought to 0.11.31. The `host.header` select and its `header` event were already in [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md).
- **Whiteboard 0.1.60 to 0.1.64.** The boards are chosen from a dropdown in the title bar, with **Manage boards…** (New, Rename, Delete, Deleted boards) in it for people who can change the board; the toolbar tabs and the title bar's **Boards…** entry are gone. The Eraser shows its icon beside its ring (0.1.61); Escape from the toolbar or the title bar returns to Select (0.1.62); the dropdown's menu is the host's (0.1.63); a guest's pointer label shows the guest mark once (0.1.64). [userguide-whiteboard](documentation/userguides/userguide-whiteboard.md), [architecture-whiteboard](documentation/architecture/architecture-whiteboard.md). The Whiteboard section of known-issues.md, written for 0.1.49, was read again against 0.1.64 and now carries the open issues' limits (#445 and the open feature issues).
- **The guest door and the Guests section** (#742, #743, #741, #759). The owner's switch is gone: the host's switch (`ENABLE_GUEST_DOOR`, or the host console's checkbox) makes the door available and each space opens itself with **Visiting guests**. With no space open the guest lands on a Home that says no spaces are open. A guest's name is stored and shown as "<typed> (Guest)", once, everywhere; a member's display name may not end in it. The landing has **Continue** and a small **Leave**, and reads again every 12 seconds. The environment's **Users** tab has its own **Guests** section. [userguide-accounts](documentation/userguides/userguide-accounts.md), [userguide-environment-settings](documentation/userguides/userguide-environment-settings.md), [userguide-settings-reference](documentation/userguides/userguide-settings-reference.md), [architecture-access](documentation/architecture/architecture-access.md), [architecture-navigation](documentation/architecture/architecture-navigation.md). The previous text described the owner's switch and the "(guest)" label; known-issues.md no longer lists the member-name gap.
- **Two small ones.** A bell notice that opens something (Manage, or a space's Set up) shows a pointer and a highlight on hover and focus (#761, [userguide-spaces](documentation/userguides/userguide-spaces.md)). A moderator's `GET /api/spaces/:id` answer gains the optional `templateName` (#818, [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md), "A space's own template").

### What merged on 2026-11-12 to 2026-11-14, Workspaces, space templates and tooling (documented afterwards)

Written from the code on main (`origin/main` at de4f2f31), the plans and the pull request titles, not from running anything. **Checked by tool:** `npm run check:docs` and `node tools/check-names.mjs`. **Not verified:** nothing here was run in a browser, on a phone, with a second environment or in a real call; the other checks were not rerun. Where the plan and the code differ, the code is what is written.

- **Workspaces: two kinds, six layouts** (#785, #796, #791, #799, #805, #809; [plan-workspaces](documentation/plans/plan-workspaces.md)). A **Customizable Workspace** (the Workspace and saved layouts) is arranged by the person; a **Fixed Workspace** (**Calendar**, **Map**, **Conference**, **Whiteboards**, **Documents**) is not. The tabs follow the owner's order and hide, set in Set up under **Layouts** (**Show in the bar**, **Move up**, **Move down**, **Opens first**; owners, the admin and moderators). On a Fixed Workspace the **Modules** tab shows only its own switches (today Calendar's **Show in the Calendar**, the To-do module's `inCalendar`) or "There is nothing to configure here." [userguide-spaces](documentation/userguides/userguide-spaces.md), [userguide-call](documentation/userguides/userguide-call.md), [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md) ("Saved layouts"), [architecture-canvas](documentation/architecture/architecture-canvas.md).
- **A member opens on the layout they last used** (#799, #809). The server keeps each signed-in person's last layout for each space (`PUT /api/spaces/:id/last-layout`; `last` in `GET /api/spaces/:id/layouts`). Entering opens, in order: a request to open one object, that layout, the owner's **Opens first**, the Workspace. A guest keeps none and follows the owner's choice, with the Workspace when it is a fixed layout; nothing is written on a restore.
- **Call position: seven beside the work, five on the Conference layout** (#767, #775, #791, #806). The call's title bar is always shown, with **Start call** and its own **⋮** menu (Chat's menu has only **Save the chat** and **Delete the chat**); a large **Start call** shows only where the call has an area of its own, on the Conference layout and **In the workspace**. Beside the work: **Top of the column**, **Bottom of the column**, **Left of the chat**, **Right of the chat**, **Floating window**, **In the workspace**, **Its own window**. On the Conference layout: **Beside the chat**, **Left of the chat**, **Above the chat**, **Below the chat**, **Call only**. They are kept in the browser for each space (`app.column.<space id>`: `callPosition`, `callFloat`, `conferencePosition`, `conferenceCallOnly`). The collapsed rail has a call button. At 640 pixels and below there is no column, no layout tabs and no Conference layout.
- **Who can enter is four switches, and a space can be closed for set up** (#772, #788). **Members of <space>** (off closes it: only owners, the admin and moderators enter), **All <environment> members**, **Invited guests**, **Visiting guests**. [userguide-spaces](documentation/userguides/userguide-spaces.md), [architecture-access](documentation/architecture/architecture-access.md).
- **A space can read its own template** (#810, #813, #816; [plan-space-templates](documentation/plans/plan-space-templates.md), issue #750). A space record may carry `template`; inside that space the words for space, aside, canvas, module, object, moderator, member, guest and the layout verb, the phases and the module names and icons are that template's, and the owner's own words still win. Environment, owner, home, host, admin and the entering verb stay the environment's. Owners and the admin choose it in Set up on the **Space** tab and in the **Add** dialog; a moderator sees it named and cannot change it (it shows the template's id until #815). Manage's **Template** tab has **Let a space use its own template** (off keeps each space's choice and ignores it). `PATCH /api/spaces/:id { template }` and `POST /api/spaces`, `GET /api/spaces/:id/look` (a 404 identical to an unknown id for anyone who may not enter, no template id in any body), `look` in the guest-link read, the module context's per-space words, phases, name and icon. A template a space names cannot be deleted (409); the console's **Used by** adds "N spaces". Phase dates for a phase the new template lacks stay on the space. [userguide-templates](documentation/userguides/userguide-templates.md), [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md) ("A space's own template"), [architecture-environments](documentation/architecture/architecture-environments.md).
- **Documents 0.11.31: no blank first page** (#802, #804). A page break at the very start of the text no longer leaves an empty first page in Paper (rich text). Documented from the code: the rich editor reads only the clipboard's plain text, as Markdown (clipboard HTML is never read as formatting; with no plain text its words alone are taken), and lines indented by four spaces or a tab are one code block. [userguide-documents](documentation/userguides/userguide-documents.md), [architecture-documents](documentation/architecture/architecture-documents.md).
- **Tools: no orphaned Chrome after a killed check** (#807). `tools/browser-launch.mjs` starts every check's headless Chrome in its own process group and ends it on exit, signal or (through `tools/browser-watchdog.mjs`) SIGKILL; `node tools/reap-browsers.mjs [--min-age=<seconds>] [--dry-run]` kills what a dead check left; `tools/check-browser-orphans.mjs` is in `npm run check`. [architecture-demo-site](documentation/architecture/architecture-demo-site.md).
- **For module authors** (#783, #784, #720, #726). A `secret` setting type: write-only, sealed in `modules/secrets.json`, never read back, left out of an owner's copy. `needsModule` on a setting and `showWhenSetting` on a destination panel. `hint` on the rows of a `host.header` select. [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md), [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md).
- **About lists the open-source software** (#779, #782). The **About** tab of Manage lists what the product uses, with its licence, a **Website** link and **Licence text**, and our own background pictures. [userguide-environment-settings](documentation/userguides/userguide-environment-settings.md).

### What merged on 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12, #629 to #715 (documented afterwards)

Written from the code on main (`origin/main` at 800ec427), the plans and the pull request titles, not from running anything. **Checked by tool:** `npm run check:docs` and `node tools/check-names.mjs` (the same two read-only-folder cases fail as root, which is not a defect); `npm run docs:build`. **Not verified:** nothing here was run in a browser, on a phone, on Safari or an iPad, on a server with several environments or in a real call by this entry's author, and the checks the pull requests report were not rerun. `check-module-guards` fails on main (#686). Where a detail was not certain it says "read from code".

- **Terms and a beta NDA before entering** (#636, #653, #645). The host can name a terms file and an NDA file (`TERMS_FILE`, `NDA_FILE`). Everyone types their name and agrees once for each version of a text; a changed text asks everyone again. A file that is missing, empty or too big, or a blank setting, stops the server from starting. A restored copy of an environment forgets who agreed unless it is restored as a host backup (`?backup=1`, and a checkbox in the host console). Also in this change: a sign-in redirect that could leave the site is fixed. [architecture-access](documentation/architecture/architecture-access.md), [userguide-accounts](documentation/userguides/userguide-accounts.md).
- **Who can enter a space, and Join** (#664). Each space has **Who can enter**: **Invited people only**, **Anyone with an account** or **Anyone, including guests**. A space open to accounts shows **Join** on Home. A closed space answers a stranger as if it did not exist (404). [userguide-spaces](documentation/userguides/userguide-spaces.md), "Who can enter".
- **The guest door** (#702, #704, #708). **Continue as guest** on the sign-in page: a name and the agreement, no account, 24 hours by default. Off until the host turns it on (`ENABLE_GUEST_DOOR`, or the host console's checkbox for an environment) and the owner turns it on in Manage. A guest sees a landing page with the spaces open to guests and what a guest cannot do, a banner inside a space with **Leave**, and the label "(guest)" on every chat message. A name cannot mix alphabets, look like a member's name, or pass 40 characters. Limits: `GUEST_SESSION_HOURS`, `GUEST_DOOR_PER_ADDRESS`, `GUESTS_PER_SPACE`, `GUESTS_PER_ENVIRONMENT`. [userguide-settings-reference](documentation/userguides/userguide-settings-reference.md).
- **Request access** (#710, #711, #712). A space can let people who cannot enter ask: they see its picture, name and description with **Request access**, then **Requested** with **Withdraw**, or **Declined** for 24 hours after a denial. Owners get a bell notice that opens Set up, **Members**, where **Waiting to come in** has **Approve** and **Deny**. Limits `ACCESS_REQUESTS_PER_DAY` (5), `ACCESS_REQUESTS_PER_ADDRESS` (20), `ACCESS_REQUESTS_PENDING_PER_SPACE` (50), `ACCESS_REQUESTS_PENDING_GUESTS_PER_SPACE` (20) and `ACCESS_REQUEST_DAYS` (7). `access.json` is left out of an owner's copy and kept in the host's backup. The bell labels these notices "Modules" and the Trips step (leaving) is not built; both are in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- **Layouts: New layout, Save as new layout, Modified and Update** (#637). Any layout can be current with a temporary tab. A **Modified** row appears under the layout shown once the Workspace changes, with **Update** where you may change it. Guests see shared layouts and may only load them. [userguide-call](documentation/userguides/userguide-call.md), "Saved layouts".
- **Documents 0.8 to 0.11.12: a rich editor and Paper** (#611 to #675). A **Markdown | Rich text** switch (the rich editor is ProseMirror, vendored and licensed in `THIRD-PARTY.md`); **Link** through the shared prompt; **Layout** **Fluid** or **Paper** (Letter or A4, a 1 inch margin, real pages in reading and while typing in the rich editor, page numbers, **Page break**); one **Insert** menu (**Grid**, **Formula**, **Page break**); grids in the rich editor (**Tab** adds a row, at most 200 rows and 20 columns); Paper thumbnails; zoom in the title bar while editing and **Fit width** up to 200 percent; the editing sheet grows with its text. [userguide-documents](documentation/userguides/userguide-documents.md), [architecture-documents](documentation/architecture/architecture-documents.md). Not verified: Safari, an iPad and a phone; the Markdown box is not paged; page counts can differ for a very large grid.
- **Whiteboard 0.1.59: several boards in a space** (#555, 0.1.54 to 0.1.59). Up to ten boards, as tabs first in the toolbar and a **Boards…** menu in the title bar: **New board…**, **Rename board…**, **Delete board…** (a 7-day trash, restorable from **Deleted boards…**). Each board has its own blocks, background, timer, Present state and pointers. A narrow board shows the current tab and lists the others in **Boards…**. **Save as image** names the file after the board. Unsent changes now survive a reload or a closed tab, and a network error or a server error keeps them and tries again. [userguide-whiteboard](documentation/userguides/userguide-whiteboard.md), "Boards"; [architecture-whiteboard](documentation/architecture/architecture-whiteboard.md). The limits (the 10 boards and the name rules are the page's own) are in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- **Whiteboard 0.1.50 to 0.1.58: Eraser and insert tools** (#662, #673, #674). **Eraser** (key E) removes drawings and highlights a drag passes over, at most 100 a sweep ("Erased 100. Lift and sweep again."), one **Undo** step. With a tool that puts a block on the board, a click on a block that takes words edits it and a click on another block picks it, instead of placing on top; the active tool's icon follows the pointer.
- **Vault 0.4.7: Properties** (#628). **Info** and **Where it is used** are one **Properties** pane, and the file's name can be changed there. [userguide-vault](documentation/userguides/userguide-vault.md).
- **For module authors** (#617, #620, #675). `host.ui.prompt` asks a one-line question in the shared dialog. `host.menu.show` takes `onClose`. A toolbar button with no `on` is not a toggle. Zoom icons are `host.header` items. [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md).
- **Design** (#650, #661, #691). Placeholder pictures (initials and the guest plate) follow the environment's theme. Shared menus show a fade along the bottom edge while more entries are below. On a phone, switches, the paste button on an image slot and the status strip's chips get a 44 pixel tap area; `tools/check-phone-targets.mjs` measures it. [design-theme](documentation/designsystem/design-theme.md).
- **Home row in Manage** (#643). Home's row wears the product's mark and has no **Edit** button.
- **Studio card settings.** `STUDIO_NAME`, the two logo files and the four links are in the [settings reference](documentation/userguides/userguide-settings-reference.md).

### What merged on 2026-11-10 and 2026-11-11, #521 to #594 (documented afterwards)

Written from the code on main (`origin/main` at 0c5fa780), the plans and the pull request titles, not from running anything. **Checked by tool:** `npm run check:docs`. **Not verified:** nothing here was run in a browser, on a phone or in a real call; the module checks were not rerun. Where a detail was not certain it says "read from code".

- **Documents 0.6.1: catch up after a dropped connection** (#594). When the connection returns, the list and the open document are read again and merged. Documents 0.6.0 (#593): tags on documents (a picker, chips and a filter in the toolbar); the tag ids sit in a record of their own so tagging never clashes with a text save. 0.5.0 (#588): **List** and **Thumbnails** views. 0.4.0 (#587): **Fit width** and zoom, with less gutter. 0.3.0 (#584): a page colour for each document, white by default. 0.2.2 (#583): all chrome from the shared toolbar, title bar and menus. 0.2.1 (#579): one box while editing, and a page look. 0.2.0 (#576): writing with the lock. 0.1.1 and 0.1.0 (#570, #571): the list, reading and the trash. New: [userguide-documents](documentation/userguides/userguide-documents.md) and [architecture-documents](documentation/architecture/architecture-documents.md); [plan-documents](documentation/plans/plan-documents.md) has the licence and no-outside-service rules (#568) and the progress note. `modules/documents/THIRD-PARTY.md` lists the vendored library (marked 18.2.0, MIT). Not built: links, pictures, export, live co-editing. Read from code; the lock and trash paths are listed in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- **Modules hear "reconnected"** (#591). `host.on('reconnected')` fires once each time the event stream dropped and came back, so a module can read again what it shows. **Whiteboard 0.1.49** (#592) uses it; 0.1.47 (#586) did the same on a timer as a stopgap; 0.1.48 (#589) stops an error when the module's content is gone. [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md), "Live changes".
- **Shared tags** (#582, #585; [plan-tags](documentation/plans/plan-tags.md)). One list of tags for each space, shared by the modules that say `"tags": true`. Server: `server/tags.js` (one set of rules: a name of 1 to 24 characters in lower case with letters, digits and dashes; eight tones; 8 tags on an object; 200 in a list) and the routes under `/api/spaces/:id/tags`. A new right, **Rename, recolour and delete tags** (`manageTags`), is on for moderators and owners by default and never for guests. SDK: `host.tags` (`list`, `add`, `chip`, `picker`, `filterOptions`) and the `tags` event. Set up, **Settings** tab, has a **Tags** section to rename, recolour and delete. Documented in [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md), [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md) and [userguide-spaces](documentation/userguides/userguide-spaces.md). Known gaps (an element is needed for the picker, no per-row control, list search does not match tag names, no divider for action buttons) are in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- **Toasts at the top centre of the canvas, with kinds** (#557, #558). A toast now shows under the top bar at the centre of the canvas, not in a corner, with a subtle border in the kind's colour. `host.ui.toast(text, { kind })` takes `info`, `success`, `warning` or `danger`; they stay 4.5, 4.5, 6 and 7 seconds. [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md), [design-theme](documentation/designsystem/design-theme.md), "Toasts", and [userguide-spaces](documentation/userguides/userguide-spaces.md).
- **The space page never scrolls; tablets use plain full height** (#577, #578). The page is pinned to the visible screen so an iPad no longer pushes the top bar away when a field takes focus. `--app-vh` (the visible height, from `visualViewport`) is now kept at phone width only (640 pixels and narrower); [plan-phone-experience](documentation/plans/plan-phone-experience.md) is corrected. Console fixes (#590): the Vault's resize observer is null-safe (Vault 0.4.5), `mobile-web-app-capable` is in the page heads, and no inline handlers. Read from code; not tried on a real iPad.
- **The demo space can be added to any environment from the host console** (#552, #553). It runs as a stand-in user called `host-demo` with no password, and a stalled request ends after 180 seconds (`DEMO_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`). [architecture-demo-site](documentation/architecture/architecture-demo-site.md).
- **Every environment gets Strong Coffee once** (#532). Built-in themes are now seeded by id with a list of those already given (`builtinThemesSeeded` is a list, not a flag), so a built-in added later reaches an environment seeded before it, and a theme an admin deleted does not come back. Data saved with the old flag counts as having been given all but Strong Coffee (a one-time migration Thomas allowed on 2026-11-09, to be removed at the next reset of test data).
- **Whiteboard 0.1.23 to 0.1.45** (#528 to #571), in [userguide-whiteboard](documentation/userguides/userguide-whiteboard.md) and [architecture-whiteboard](documentation/architecture/architecture-whiteboard.md):
  - *Pointers* (0.1.23): other people's pointers, in their colour, with their name, over live messages.
  - *Colours and backgrounds* (0.1.24 to 0.1.26, 0.1.34): fixed colours and a floating colour strip; the board background in **Default**, **Light**, **Dark** and **Tinted** groups, with a question before a change; the same groups for a block's border, background and words.
  - *Toolbar* (0.1.25 to 0.1.32): a new tool order; **Pen**, **Shape** and **Flowchart** as families with their kinds on a strip; one **Tools** switch and a context strip for every tool that stays on until **Escape**; **Find** and zoom in the title bar; **Select** has its own strip; **Flowchart** auto-connect and **Table** properties; the **Timer** is one floating clock for the board.
  - *Grid* (0.1.33, 0.1.35): a **Grid** button (style, size, colour, opacity); **Overview** moves beside it; the panel no longer closes when you click inside it.
  - *Frames and Present* (0.1.36 to 0.1.45): a spotlight that dims the rest of the board (to full black, following the frame's rounded corners); **Immediate**, **Fade** and **Pan** between frames; **Present to everyone** (shared presenting, one presenter, a 35-second timeout); **Restart**, **Jump to Frame** and a play icon; speaker notes with a notes mark on the frame and a movable notes box; the padlock shows on hover; the notes box breakpoint is 640 pixels (0.1.46, #581).
  - Checked by tool as the pull requests report (the Whiteboard's `tools/check-whiteboard-*.mjs`), not rerun here. Not tried: several real people, a real dropped connection, the notes box's drag. See [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- **`host.live` and `host.chat.attach`** (#523, #524, #526). Live messages for modules: the server half (`POST /api/modules/:id/live`, join and leave, events on the module stream, limits `MODULE_LIVE_*`, access rechecked) and the SDK (`publish`, `subscribe`, `on('people')`, `state`, `tint`), also for guests with the guest pass; none of it uses the call. `host.chat.attach({ label, text })` or `({ label, ref })` stages words or an object in Chat's input and sends nothing until the person presses **Send**. [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md), "Live messages", [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md), [plan-module-live](documentation/plans/plan-module-live.md) and [plan-ai-pictures](documentation/plans/plan-ai-pictures.md), which have the progress notes. Pictures are not attachable yet.
- **Plans, TODO and known issues.** [TODO](documentation/TODO.md) has the current queue (Documents' next steps, an access bundle plan, Google Drive #566, a completeness audit, a marketing agent, Whiteboard #554 and #555). [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md) gains the Whiteboard colour swatch notes, the Documents and tags gaps and the no-divider note. The Vault's share-link note was already there.

### What merged on 2026-11-09, #514 to #520 (documented afterwards)

Written from the code on main (`origin/main` at a778045) and the build notes, not from running it. **Checked by tool:** the documentation checks listed at the end of this section. **Not verified:** nothing here was run in a browser by this entry's author, and nothing was tried on a real phone or in a real call (there is no LiveKit server in the checks).

- **A Vault share link holds only the files its maker can still see** (#514, #520; Vault 0.4.4; `plan-vault-privacy.md`, decision 8). Each time someone opens a share link, the server checks again which files the person who made it can see. A file they cannot see drops out of the link, and a hidden file answers exactly as a missing one. If no file is left, the link reads "This link has ended." Nothing is stored, so if the file is shown to its maker again it comes back to the link; an owner or the admin who made a link keeps every file. This replaces the earlier entries' statement that a link kept working after its file was narrowed.
  - **Changing a link.** A change may not add a file the maker cannot see: 400 "The person who made this link can't see that file." when the person changing it can see the file, otherwise 404 "There is no such file."; files already on the link are kept.
  - **Words.** The share sheet and the question before narrowing say a link "stops showing the file" (not "ends"). The people picker uses the shared action bar: **Done** is on once the choice differs, and **Cancel**, **Close** and Escape ask "Discard changes?" after an edit.
  - **For Thomas.** Decision 8 of the plan reads as if a link ends for good; it is kept as built (a file can return) and listed in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md). Documented in [userguide-vault](documentation/userguides/userguide-vault.md), [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md) and [architecture-modules](documentation/architecture/architecture-modules.md). Gaps that remain are in known-issues: the server sends `visibility` only to the adder and owners; no dry run of who loses sight; "File removed" after deleting a restricted file; no role words in the picker; the list of links still counts a file its maker lost sight of; the choice resets to Everyone when **Add files** is reopened mid-upload.
- **A module's toolbar items can go at the right, and a switch folds its last choices** (#516, #519). `host.ui.toolbarButton`, `host.ui.viewSwitch` and the items of `host.toolbar.set` take `zone: 'end'` (exactly `'start'` or `'end'`; the default is the left). Right-zone items sit at the far right, after the search and **Filters**. A module's first two icon-only right-zone buttons are held in place and never go into **More**; every other right-zone item folds into **More**, and a right-zone switch folds its own choices first. A module cannot set `pin`, and in a destination page a module's `pin` and `zone` are dropped. A switch holds up to 12 options (it was 8). A switch too wide for the row folds only its last choices into **More**; the chosen one always stays, and the whole switch moves only when the chosen one alone does not fit.
  - **The switch's name** is **Tools** (it was **View**) to a screen reader and as the heading in **More**. `host.ui.viewSwitch({ label })` (and a `tabs` item's `label`) names it: a plain string, spaces collapsed, at most 30 characters, ignored when empty, not a string or containing `<` or `>`; direction and zero-width characters are removed. The bundled modules name theirs: Research **Whose** and **Layout**, Places **Whose**, Polls and To-do **Show**, Calendar, the Vault and Budget **View**; Travel and the Whiteboard keep **Tools**. Versions: Budget 0.1.25, Calendar 1.24.39, Places 0.12.22, Polls 1.15.29, Research 0.4.33, To-do 1.15.27, Vault 0.4.4.
  - **Known limits.** At 110 pixels with three or more right-zone icon buttons the row overflows by 12 pixels; only the switch's name is cleaned of direction characters. Documented in [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md), [architecture-module-window](documentation/architecture/architecture-module-window.md) and [design-theme](documentation/designsystem/design-theme.md).
- **Whiteboard 0.1.20 to 0.1.22: an overview map, Find, and one tool switch** (#515, #517, #518). *0.1.20, Overview:* **Show overview** (and **Hide overview**) in the title bar's menu puts a small map of the board at the bottom right, with blocks as boxes, frames as outlines and the view as an accent rectangle; click or tap it to centre the view there at the same zoom, or drag the rectangle. Your choice stays in your browser (`whiteboard-overview`), off at first, never in the board's data; the map is hidden while presenting, on an empty board and in a module smaller than 360 by 320. *0.1.21, Find:* **Find** in the title bar's menu, or the **/** key when the board has the keyboard and nothing is being edited, opens a bar at the top right that searches the words in notes, text, shapes, frame titles, table cells, connector labels and picture descriptions, ignoring case and accents. It says "2 of 7" or "No matches"; **Enter** and **Shift+Enter** go to the next and previous match, wrapping; matches are outlined, the view glides to one out of sight without zooming in, and what is picked stays picked. *0.1.22:* one switch named **Tools** (Select, Hand, Pen, Note, Text, Rectangle, Ellipse, Line, Arrow, and Select many on touch screens only); **H** toggles the Hand and back; **Snap to grid** is the button at the far right; on a narrow board the last choices and other buttons fold into **More** while the tool in use stays; the board's help is shorter. Documented in [userguide-whiteboard](documentation/userguides/userguide-whiteboard.md) and [architecture-whiteboard](documentation/architecture/architecture-whiteboard.md) (new libraries `lib-n-overview` and `lib-o-find`, tests `check-whiteboard-minimap.mjs` and `check-whiteboard-find.mjs`).
  - **Not verified:** Find with the formatting bar's link field or a frame title being edited; a real phone; a real call.
  - **Checked by tool:** `npm run check:docs`, `check-facts`, `check-links`, `check-setup-links`, `check-brand-hosts`, `check-product-name` and `check-names`. The module tests were not rerun here.

### What merged on 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-09 (documented afterwards)

These entries were written after the work reached main, from the code on main (`origin/main` at 56f6ac5) and the pull requests' own descriptions. **Checked** for all of them: the documentation checks listed at the end of this section. **Not verified** for all of them: nothing here was run by eye in a browser by the author, and there is no LiveKit server here, so no call was tried. "Read as code" below means exactly that. The Whiteboard's entries are separate.

- **Set up is one compact flyout, and the Layout button is gone** (#454, #455, #459, #460, #474, #475; try builds, for Thomas's review). The tabs are compact and the ones that do not fit go into a **...** (**More tabs**) menu. The former **Show** menu is now the first tab, **Modules**: the module switches with their descriptions, **Arrange**, **Layouts**, and, for owners and the admin, the note "Modules are turned on for the whole environment in Manage." The order is **Modules**, the space's own tab, **Members**, **Phases**, **Settings**. **First visit** is a section inside **Settings**, with **Turn AI off in this space**, **What AI may see** and each module's settings; the read-only module list is gone. The panel is 460 pixels wide with a stronger shadow, and its title and the button's tooltip read "<space> Configuration" (an aside uses the aside's word; a panel that has only the Modules tab is titled **Modules**). **Delete this <space>** is the last section of the space's tab. The top bar's **Layout** button is gone: **Set up** opens on **Modules**, and **Set up** is now there for every member of a space (a member or a guest gets the **Modules** tab alone). **Manage** is a gear button in the top bar beside **Set up** (owners and the admin), no longer in the profile menu; in the narrow layout it stays in the menu. The undo note sits one layer under the panel while it is open. The switches under **What AI may see** line up at the right.
  - **For developers.** Internal tab ids are kept: `show` is the Modules tab and `modules` is Settings (saved links use `#modules`). `canvas.mountMenu()` and `mountHostMenu()` draw the shared menu into the panel; `configurationLabel()` in `public/space-setup.js` makes the name. The **Button for the canvas layout** word in Manage is kept but no control reads it now. `tools/check-setup-tabs.mjs` is new. Documented in [Spaces](documentation/userguides/userguide-spaces.md) ("Set up"), [The call](documentation/userguides/userguide-call.md) ("The Modules tab"), [architecture-navigation](documentation/architecture/architecture-navigation.md) and [architecture-canvas](documentation/architecture/architecture-canvas.md), and every guide that said "Layout menu".

- **Chat: icons instead of the words public, private and shared; an Assistant question sends at once** (#461; try build). A message's header shows one person for "Only you", three people for "Everyone" and, on your own shared command or answer, "Shared with everyone" (tooltips and labels; no lock). An `/ai` question appears in the Assistant tab as soon as it is sent, with "Waiting for the answer...", and the box is free, so several can be out together and each answer lands under its own question. A failed ask stays in place as `Failed to send "<message>"`, with the reason and a **Send again** button. The limit of six asks a minute per person is unchanged: the seventh is refused with "You have asked the AI too many times in a minute. Try again in a moment." Documented in [Chat](documentation/userguides/userguide-chat.md). **Verified:** read as code (`public/chat-input.js`, `server/ai-policy.js`).

Every link out is built from the origin of `PRODUCT_SITE_URL`; with it unset those parts are hidden. A module's own checks live in `modules/<id>/test/`, run by `tools/check-module-tests.mjs` from `npm run check`; `test/` is not in the module's zip nor its version fingerprint. `public/design-system/sources.js` holds nothing from `modules/`; the Design System pages read module stylesheets and icons at run time from `GET /design-system/module-sources.js`. ### What merged on 2026-11-09, documented later

Written from the code on main (`origin/main` at b52694e), not from the pull requests' descriptions, which could not be read from here. **Checked:** the documentation checks listed at the end of this section. **Not verified:** none of this was run by eye in a browser by this entry's author; "checked by tool" below is what the pull requests' commits say their own checks cover, not rerun here. No real call, Firefox, Safari, touch screen or screen reader was tried.

- **Stack docked modules in one column** (#511, GitHub #387; plan-panel-stacking built). A docked module's title-bar menu has **Stack with...** (**Above** or **Below** a module in each other column), **Move up**, **Move down**, **Move out of the stack**, **Collapse** or **Expand**, and **Equal heights**. Dragging a title bar over another docked module stacks it (the upper half above, the lower half below; the outer edge of a column puts it in its own column). A stack is one column of the docked limit, holds up to three modules, and has a divider moved by pointer or keys. **Unstack all** is in **Arrange** on Set up's **Modules** tab. **Window Mode** puts stacks away and **Panel Mode** brings them back. The call, Chat and composites such as the Calendar and the Map cannot be stacked. A phone keeps a remembered stack's width untouched.
  - **For developers.** A saved layout's docked entry may carry `stack` (up to 32 of `[a-z0-9]`), `at` (0 to 2), `h` (0.1 to 0.9, together 1) and `folded`. The server cleans a bad stack and never refuses the layout: kept members of a stack are placed together, entries cut from a stack stay as ordinary columns (the count in equals the count out), and `h` is kept only when the raw values add up to 1. The remembered layout also holds `ownW` and, while modules float, `__stacks`. See [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md), "Saved layouts", and [architecture-canvas](documentation/architecture/architecture-canvas.md), "Stacks".
  - **Known limit:** without `moveBefore()` (non-Chromium browsers) the tab order of stacked sections can differ from what is drawn ([known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md)).
  - **Checked by tool, as the pull request reports:** `tools/check-stacking.mjs` (headless Chromium, light and dark), `check-canvas`, `check-layouts` and `check-nav`. Not rerun here.
- **The host console is redesigned** (#509, GitHub #213; plan-host-console built through slice 6). A left navigation (**Environments**, **Templates**, **Modules**, **Plans**, **Server**), addresses `#environments/<slug>/<tab>` and `#templates/<id>`. **Environments** is a list with a needs-attention strip whose counts are filters, search, a status filter, a sort, a **Comfortable** or **Compact** switch and a **More** menu on each row. **New environment** is its own view. Each environment has its own page with the tabs **Overview**, **Plan and modules**, **Template** (with the offer and **Review**), **Owners and two-step**, **Backups** and **Danger zone**; **Domain** is drawn as "later". **Templates** is a list with **Used by** links, and its editor is its own view. **Modules** and **Plans** are read only; plans can be changed only through the API (`PUT /api/host/plans`). Delete asks for the address typed out, and Suspend, Resume, Restore, removing a host admin and resetting an owner's second factor ask first. The console is dark only. Documented in [architecture-environments](documentation/architecture/architecture-environments.md), "The console", and the guides ([getting started](documentation/userguides/userguide-getting-started.md), [templates](documentation/userguides/userguide-templates.md)).
  - **Not built:** the Modules route (`GET /api/host/modules`; slice 7), light mode (needs a server change), and a list of owners on the Owners tab. The server still lets a host admin remove themselves ([TODO](documentation/TODO.md), [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md)).
  - **Fixed with it:** the console's Maps tab no longer runs past the right edge on a narrow screen. The pull request's check reports no sideways scroll at 390 px for each section; not rerun here.
  - **Checked by tool, as the pull request reports:** `tools/check-host-console.mjs` (Chromium, throwaway hosted servers with 30, 6 and no environments) and `check-first-password-console`. Not rerun here.
- **Whiteboard 0.1.17: the board's background colour** (#508, GitHub #502). One shared setting stored with the board (`_board:main:settings`), changed by anyone who can edit, in the right-click menu on empty space and in the title bar's menu. The block palette plus **Default**, as an 18 percent wash that follows light and dark mode. One undo step, with a "left alone" note if someone else changed it since. **Save as image** and **Print** use it; **Clear** keeps it. See [userguide-whiteboard](documentation/userguides/userguide-whiteboard.md), "Background colour".
- **Whiteboard 0.1.18: align, distribute, lock and group** (#510, GitHub #500). **Arrange** (toolbar and right-click menu) aligns two or more blocks and distributes three or more, one undo step each. Guides show while dragging with **Snap to grid** off (**Alt** turns them off for a drag; the grid wins when Snap is on). **Lock** (`locked: true`, Ctrl or Cmd L): a locked block cannot be moved, resized, deleted, recoloured, moved to another layer or edited, but can be picked, copied, duplicated and saved as an image; a padlock marks it. **Group** (a `group` id, Ctrl or Cmd G, with Shift to ungroup): picking one member picks the group; a copy is a new group. The toolbar's order is Delete, Arrange, Snap to grid. **Limits:** resizing a group as one selection is not built; Ctrl+L and Ctrl+G in a real browser are unverified. **Checked by tool:** `check-whiteboard-arrange.mjs` and `check-whiteboard-background.mjs`, as the pull requests report; not rerun here.
- **Whiteboard 0.1.19: frames and Present** (#512, GitHub #498). A frame is a titled area that holds blocks (the **Frame** tool, key **S**, in the **Flowchart** menu, or **Add a frame** in the **+**). Moving a frame moves the blocks inside it in one undo step; **Save frame as image…** saves exactly the frame. **Present** shows the frames one at a time with **Back**, a count, **Next** and **Exit**, and the arrow, Page, Home, End, Space and Escape keys; it is each person's own and works for people who can only look. A block's own colour now reads **Default** in the menus, not "Usual". For module authors: a block type may be a `container`, and the renderer has `setView` and `limits`. **Known limits:** a connector with a free end inside a frame does not follow it; the glide, reduced motion, Print with frames and a real long press were read as code only. **Checked by tool:** `check-whiteboard-frames.mjs` (15 cases), as the pull request reports; not rerun here.
- **Documented with it:** the Whiteboard follow-ups (#492, #498 to #503) are in [TODO](documentation/TODO.md). The Vault privacy entry earlier in this file no longer says "HELD, not merged": slice 1 is on main (#424).

### Changed
- **The product host's front page is now a start page, and the old product pages are gone** (`plan-product-site.md`, slices 4 and 5; a try build, for Thomas's review). `/` on the product host is a plain page with four parts: **Recent** (the environments this browser reached through the page, up to five, each removable; not drawn when empty), **Find your environment** (type the address, with the base domain after it; the name shows as you type, and **Go** opens only that environment's own sign-in), **Got a link?** (open the link itself) and **Get your own** (the sign-up form, open on the page; it asks for no password, because the owner chooses it on the new environment's own page). When `PRODUCT_SITE_URL` is set, an **About {short name}** link goes to it. There is no marketing, no screenshots and no demo. The old front page, `/travel`, `/games` and their screenshots are removed, with no redirect: those addresses now answer 404 on the product host.
  - **New setting, `PRODUCT_SITE_URL`.** The address of the product's page on your website: an absolute `http://` or `https://` address, for example `https://www.example.com/collaborator`. Unset means no link. A value that is not one stops the start with one line. Added to the settings reference, the getting-started guide and a commented example in `docker-compose.yml`. Thomas sets his own value in his compose file.
  - **For developers.** `GET /api/product` no longer has `pages` and gains `productSiteUrl` (the address, or `null`); `GET /api/host/settings` gains it too, and the console's facts read **Start page** and **Product site**. The product host's `/theme.css` sends the bundled default theme (light and dark), since no environment is there. New design token `--brand-coral` (`#ff8264` dark, `#e0553c` light) for the product's name in the header, large text only ([design-theme](documentation/designsystem/design-theme.md)). Removed: `public/landing.*`, `travel.html`, `games.html`, `public/assets/images/brand/landing/`, `PRODUCT_PAGES` and `livePages()`, `tools/check-product-pages.mjs` and `tools/landing-preview.mjs`, and the `OWN_PALETTE` exception for the old page's palette. New `tools/check-start-page.mjs`. Documents updated: [architecture-environments](documentation/architecture/architecture-environments.md) ("The start page"), the settings reference, getting-started, environments and templates guides, and the status lines of [plan-product-site](documentation/plans/plan-product-site.md), [plan-product-pages](documentation/plans/plan-product-pages.md) (superseded) and [plan-template-games](documentation/plans/plan-template-games.md) (steps 7 to 9 superseded).
  - **Open for Thomas.** Manage's **Upgrade** link still points at `<product domain>/#plans`, and the start page has no Plans list yet, so it opens the start page. **Try the demo** is absent (slice 6 of the plan, the demo hook, is not built).
  - **Checked:** by tool, run by this entry's author on the documentation branch: `check:docs`, `check-brand-hosts`, `check-product-name`, `check-compose`, `check-facts`, `check-links`, `check-setup-links`, `check-prototype-words` and `check-start-page` (the files, and 8 live cases in headless Chromium) all pass; `check-names` passes except its 2 `--migration` cases that fail only because this container runs as root. Not verified: a real domain behind a proxy, and a call (no LiveKit server here). The documents were written from the code on main, not from a run of the start page by eye.
- **Sign-up asks for no password; the owner chooses it on the new environment's own page** (`plan-first-password.md`, steps 1 to 3; Security). The start page's form has no password field and says "Next you choose your password, on your new environment's own page." After sign-up the person is sent to the environment's own page by a link that works once, for 15 minutes, and there chooses a password (8 to 200 characters, enforced by the server). If the environment asks for a second step, the person is taken on to set it up; otherwise they are signed in. The password is never typed on the start page or sent through it.
  - **A new host console action, "New first-password link."** For an owner who has no password, a host admin makes a new link on the environment's card. It works once, for 24 hours, replaces older links, and is shown once with **Copy**. The host admin never sees the password.
  - **Closed doors while an owner waits.** The link's token is in the address's fragment only, kept as a hash on the account, never stored or logged, and left out of the owner's export. Every failure is the same sentence and counts against the environment's sign-in limit. Self sign-up (`/api/register`) answers "sign-up is turned off" while an owner waits.
  - **Sign-up fixes.** The owner's login is checked before anything is made, so a bad login no longer leaves an environment with no owner. A leftover folder for an address is answered as taken ("is already in use", 409) and never touched. A display name that is not text is refused.
  - **Fix-ups after the security reads.** The set step also sends the login the page was told, and the server refuses it unless it is the owner of that step, so a page naming an older link's owner cannot set a newer one's password. The same link opened twice in one tab keeps the form instead of showing "expired". A link opened in a tab that already shows the page is used, and removed from the address and history. The page's file answers only at `/login/first-password`; `/login-first-password.html` is a 404. Sign-up refuses a name that is not text (400). The used link carries a random claim id, so a step begun from an older use cannot set the password. The password field has no length limit of its own; the 8 to 200 rule comes from the page's script and the server. The browser's navigation timing entry holding the token, and a lookalike environment sending its own link, are accepted as low risks (architecture-environments, "The first password").
  - **For developers.** Routes, the `firstPassword` field, the window and the framing rule are in [architecture-environments](documentation/architecture/architecture-environments.md), "The first password". `tools/check-first-password.mjs`, `check-first-password-page.mjs` and `check-first-password-console.mjs` are in `npm run check`. The single-environment install is unchanged: the new routes do nothing without `BASE_DOMAIN`.
  - **Checked:** by tool, re-run for this entry on the branch (tip 432fc10): `check-first-password` (28 passed), `check-first-password-page` (16, headless Chromium) and `check-first-password-console` (4). The builders ran the full `npm run check` earlier: all passed except `check-names`'s two cases that need a non-root user. Two independent security reads, live on throwaway servers; a third short read of the last fix-up was under way, and the project manager will update this line. **Not verified:** that the link's fragment survives the move from the product host to an environment on separate domains, or a proxy redirect, in Chromium, Firefox and Safari; that the token is absent from the proxy's access log; that password managers offer to save the login. A real call is not involved. **Held for Thomas's merge:** the work is on the branch `pm/first-password-server`, not on main.

### Added
- **Vault 0.4.2: you choose who can see a file** (`plan-vault-privacy.md`, slices 2 and 3; #507, documented afterwards). When you add files you now choose who may see them: **Everyone in this space**, **Only me**, or **Chosen people**. The server's rule from slice 1 (#424, which is on main, though its entry below still says held) now has its screens. Under a file's name a mark says **Only me** or "N people" to the person who added it, and **Not shared with you** to an owner or the admin looking at a file that was not shared with them. Nobody else sees marks, and a member who may not see a file gets nothing of it in the list, grid, search, counts or links.
  - **Add files.** Three choices, then a people picker for **Chosen people**: names with a picture or initial, owners shown as "Always can", a search, a limit of 200 ("N of 200 chosen"). The choice is fixed when the files are picked and goes back to Everyone each time the panel opens. The choices stay off while files are queued, going up or waiting for **Try again**; a file keeps the choice it was picked with.
  - **The file window.** Info has **Who can see this**, for the person who added the file, owners and the admin. An owner or the admin looking at a file that was not shared with them is told so. When a change would take sight from people who have links to lose, the Vault asks first (**Change who can see this file?**, with **Make it only me** or **Remove those people**); widening, or narrowing with no links, asks nothing. The share sheet adds a sentence for a restricted file. The Roles tab has a **See every file** row (owners locked on, guests locked off).
  - **For developers.** `host.library.add(blob, { visibility, people })`: `visibility` is a short non-empty string, `people` a list of at most 201 strings, anything else is refused with a 400 before a request is sent (the server then checks the words and the members). New `host.library.pickPeople({ chosen })` opens the page's people picker and resolves the chosen keys or `null`; the adder is taken from the page's own sign-in, and the call goes through the library gate first. `host.ui.confirm` now passes only `title`, `message`, `action`, `cancel`, `kind` and `typed` (`cleanConfirm`); the page-only `list` and `solid` options cannot be asked for by a module. The `library` event gains `op: 'visibility'` (it was sent already). `public/vault-privacy.js` draws the picker, the Info control and the question. `modules/vault` 0.3.5 to 0.4.2. Documented in [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md), [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md) and [architecture-modules](documentation/architecture/architecture-modules.md).
  - **Known gaps** (in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md)): the server sends `visibility` only to the adder and owners, so the read-only line "Shared with you and others.", **Not shared with you** for a moderator who holds **See every file**, and the share sheet's sentence for a person on a chosen list never show for ordinary people; there is no server dry run, so the question's numbers are the page's estimate; **File removed** is not returned after a restricted file is deleted; the picker shows no role words; reopening **Add files** during an upload resets the choice to Everyone and locks it until the queue ends. (The share link wording in this entry was corrected in Vault 0.4.4; see the entry above it.)
  - **Verified:** an independent security read in a real browser, three rounds, and the module lane (`npm run check:module -- vault`), as reported in the pull request; this entry's author reran neither. **Not run:** the full `npm run check`, a real sandboxed frame calling `library.add`, a hosted install with `BASE_DOMAIN`, a LiveKit call. The guide, API and architecture text was written from the code on main (109f161) and checked by the documentation checks listed under "Checked by tool".
- **Whiteboard 0.1.11: connectors and flowchart shapes** (#487; documented afterwards, no code changed here). The **Flowchart** menu in the toolbar holds the **Connector** (C) and the **Rectangle**, **Rounded box**, **Diamond**, **Pill** and **Parallelogram**; a connector joins two blocks, follows them, takes a label, and goes when one of its blocks is deleted (one undo brings both back). The **Line style and arrowheads** button sets **Elbow** or **Straight** and **None**, **End**, **Start** or **Both**. **Connect the 2 picked blocks** is in the **+**. Snap to grid also puts a connector's free end on a dot.
- **Whiteboard 0.1.12: Fill, Marker, Brush and Timer** (#488; documented afterwards). The **Paint** menu holds **Fill** (click a block to fill it; **Alt** or **Option** takes a colour), the **Pen**, the **Marker** and the **Brush** (wider where a pen or finger presses harder). The **Timer** is a countdown block, five minutes at first, with **Start**, **Pause**, **Reset**, a **Length** menu, a soft tone and **Mute the sound**; every page counts from the server's time.
- **Whiteboard 0.1.13: formatting bar, text size and alignment** (#489; documented afterwards). Words in notes, text, shapes and table cells take **Bold**, **Italic**, **Code**, lists, **Heading** and **Link** from a small bar over the block (also **Ctrl+B**, **I**, **E**, **K**). A link opens with **Ctrl+click** or **Cmd+click**, or **Open link** in the right-click menu. **Text size and alignment** sets **Small** to **Huge** and **Left**, **Centre** or **Right**.
- **Whiteboard 0.1.14: note pointer and Highlighter** (#490; documented afterwards). **Add pointer** gives a note a tail whose tip you drag to point at something. The **Highlighter** (U) lays a see-through band over words and shapes; **Shift** makes it straight.
- **Whiteboard 0.1.15: Table** (#491; documented afterwards). **Table** (G) in the Flowchart menu, or **Add a table** in the **+**: cells with the same formatting bar, **Tab** and **Enter** between cells, rows and columns added and deleted, a header row, lines dragged to size, and rows pasted from a spreadsheet become a table. At most 20 columns, 50 rows and 500 characters in a cell.
- **Whiteboard 0.1.16: Save as image and Print** (#493; documented afterwards). **Save as image…** (title bar menu, and the right-click menus) saves a PNG of everything, what is selected or what is in sight, on the board colour or transparent, at 1x or 2x. **Print** in the title bar menu prints the whole board fitted to the paper, in the light colours.
  - **For developers.** [architecture-whiteboard](documentation/architecture/architecture-whiteboard.md) gains the toolbar, connectors, paint, markup, pointer, timer, tables and export sections; [userguide-whiteboard](documentation/userguides/userguide-whiteboard.md) gains Paint, Flowchart and connectors, Timer, Tables, Write words (formatting, text size), Note pointer and Save as image and print; the limits are in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
  - **Checked by tool:** `npm run check:module -- whiteboard` on the documentation branch: the module's 11 tests passed (connector 14 cases, export 29, history 51, live 17 in Chromium, markup 41, pictures 40, pointer 3, right-drag 21, sync 69, table 13, timer 40). The core lint and the other checks in that run passed except `check-names`, whose two `--migration` cases fail as root (known; not a defect).
  - **Not verified:** the documents were written from the code on main (read as code; the pull requests' own descriptions could not be read from here, so what each author checked by eye is not repeated). Not run: the real print dialog, Print in Safari or Firefox, a pen or touch screen that senses force, pasting rows from a real spreadsheet clipboard, the timer's tone on a real device and a real LiveKit call (there is no LiveKit server here). Escape in a table cell puts back only that unfinished cell's words, by design.
- **Whiteboard: documented** (module 0.1.4 to 0.1.10, on main; a documentation entry, no code changed). A shared board for a space: notes, text, rectangles, ellipses, lines, arrows, drawings and pictures, each colour set on its own (Border, Background, Words) with a strip that shows them, Snap to grid, layers, select several, copy and paste, your own undo and redo, moving the board (Space, middle button, wheel, Hand tool, right-drag) and zoom. Everyone in the space sees one board; each block is saved whole when you let go of it. Two permissions on the Roles tab: **See the whiteboard** and **Draw on the whiteboard, add pictures and change what is on it**.
  - **Two limits, not one.** Pictures: **5 MB for each picture file** (the module's upload rule), at most 500 picture files for a space, 30 pictures a minute for a person, and a picture of a very large pixel size is refused. Stored board data: **5 MB in all** for every space's board together (`LIMITS.moduleBytes` in `server/module-data.js`); pictures do not count toward it. One block's value is at most 60 KB.
  - **New documents.** [userguide-whiteboard](documentation/userguides/userguide-whiteboard.md) and [architecture-whiteboard](documentation/architecture/architecture-whiteboard.md); the Whiteboard is added to the module list in [userguide-modules](documentation/userguides/userguide-modules.md) and to `home.md`. Known limits are in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
  - **For developers.** [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md): `host.ui.toolbarButton` takes `disabled` and its `set` changes it; the `change` event carries `updatedAt` (the server's time of the write, since #479); `host.uploads.inspect` is optional. [architecture-modules](documentation/architecture/architecture-modules.md): a bundled module's own checks in `modules/<id>/test/`, run by `npm run check:module -- <id>` and by `npm run check`.
  - **Checked:** the documents were written from the code on main at the Whiteboard 0.1.10 (read as code; the limits were read in `module.json`, `server/module-data.js` and `lib-a-core`), and `npm run check:docs` and `npm run docs:build` were run on them. **Not verified live:** the behaviour the guide describes was not run by eye, and nothing was tried on a real Mac, on Windows or with a screen reader. The later stages (0.1.11 to 0.1.16) are documented in the entries just above this one.
- **Vault: the server decides which files a person may see** (`plan-vault-privacy.md`, slice 1; on main since #424, with the screens that set it following in #507; the first version of this entry said "HELD, not merged", which was out of date). Each file can be for **everyone** (as now), **only me**, or **chosen people** (at most 200 members of the space). The adder, owners and the admin always see a file. A new Vault permission, **See every file**, is off for every role and lets a moderator see them all.
  - **A hidden file is a missing file.** On every library route a file the person cannot see answers exactly as one that is not there (404 "There is no such file.", same body and headers). Links from other tools read as hidden, never "File removed", and never carry the name. Search, citations, the AI's pool and the live streams leave a hidden file out. Linking a hidden file links nothing; unlinking one the person cannot see does nothing and says nothing different. Share links: making or changing one with a hidden file is refused whole; the list shows each person only the files they can see; a maker can always turn their own link off.
  - **Strict requests.** A visibility sent on add or change must be an exact word with a valid list of members; an empty, repeated, unknown or odd value answers 400 and is never read as public. A file that is not for everyone writes no activity line.
  - **Accepted limits** are in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md): the storage total and the "space is full" refusal can show that hidden files take space; share links of a file kept working after it was narrowed until #514 (see the entry for the share links above); an empty share shows "0 files" to its maker; an object holding a hidden link still tells its viewers that it changed.
  - **For developers.** `server/index.js` (`fileVisible`, `fileManageable`, the one door `libraryFile`), `server/vault-store.js` (`setVisibility`), `server/vault-share.js`. `modules/vault` 0.3.4 to 0.3.5 (the `see_all` permission). `tools/check-vault-visibility.mjs` is in `npm run check` after `check-vault-share`; `check-vault-links`, `check-vault-share` and `check-vault` have added cases.
  - **Checked:** several independent security reads, live on throwaway servers, found issues and re-found them after fixes; all were fixed. `check-vault-visibility` ran 4359 cases, as reported by the build; this entry's author did not rerun the checks. **Not verified:** a real LiveKit call, a hosted install, a browser pass of the new rules, and the Vault screens (slice 3, not built). Slices 2 to 4 (screens, links, export, erase and leavers) are still to do.
- **A generic demo loader, and a demo data file with dates as offsets** (`plan-demo-space.md`, slice 1; GitHub #416; nothing a person sees changes, except the `by` below). `server/demo-loader.js` fills one marked demo space and its marked demo people (`mia`, `lee`, `sam`) from `templates/demo/<template id>.json`, through requests to the server's own address, each as a signed-in demo person, so every module's real rules run. `npm run seed:demo` is now a thin command over it.
  - **What it never does.** It changes no environment-wide setting: no module installed or turned on, no calendar or map option, no place search, no template switch. A module that is off is skipped, and the report says so in plain words. It refuses a space or a person that has the demo's name or login but not the marker, before anything is changed. A rerun resets only the demo space and the demo people. The demo people's passwords are random on every run and returned to the caller; the command prints them once and never prints the admin password. Its messages use the environment's own words (the `words` option, else the target's `/api/branding`, else the plain defaults: a space reads "trip" on a travel environment). A template with no demo file reports "no demo" and creates nothing. A guest link that fails is listed in `refused`.
  - **The data file.** `templates/demo/travel.json` holds the Portugal trip. Every date is an offset: `today+N`, `start+N` (start is today plus the file's `anchor`, 45 days), `T09:30` for a time, a trailing `Z` for a timestamp, `ms:today+2T10:00` and `ms:now` for numbers; `@login`, `{space}` and `{base}` stand for a person, the space id and the server address. An unknown base refuses the whole file before anything is made. Module ids must match `^[a-z0-9-]+$`. The folder is `templates/demo/` because the template loader reads only `*.json` directly in `templates/`.
  - **What the seeder keeps.** Its environment-wide setup for throwaway environments (template switch if none, install and enable every bundled module, calendar and map options, place search, map file), then the loader. Its output and counts are unchanged (Planner 76, Places 37, Calendar 77, To-do 16, Polls 3, Budget 18, Research 4, Chat 25).
  - **One difference.** The server records who wrote an object, so the `by` on the objects, and on 61 Calendar copies, is now the demo person (`mia`, or the voter) and not `admin`.
  - **For developers.** `tools/check-demo-loader.mjs` (14 groups) is in `npm run check` after `check-seed-demo`. `tools/check-compose.mjs` (72 checks) also requires that the `Dockerfile` copies the loader and the data. The `roomType` allow-list line for `tools/seed-demo.mjs` in `tools/check-names-allow.json` is removed, since the key moved into the data file. See `documentation/architecture/architecture-demo-site.md`.
  - **Checked:** an independent QA read, as reported: read as code, and live on throwaway servers (the guards, the output identical to the old seeder, mutations of the checks). The checks were not rerun by this entry's author. **Not verified:** a real LiveKit call, a hosted install with a real domain, the map file (offline), the Docker image build. **Known limits**, in `documentation/known-issues.md`: a real account with a demo person's login and display name is adopted and its password reset; the space marker is a public string; two runs at once are not safe; the retry after a 429 has no overall limit; `base` is not validated. Slice 4 is to deal with them.
- **The demo seeder is in the published image** (`Dockerfile`, `tools/check-compose.mjs`; GitHub #416 is the planned feature that replaces the environment-wide part). You can now run `docker exec -it collaborator-app npm run seed:demo` in a running container and get one space, **Demo: Portugal**, with data in every module (Planner 76 objects, Places 37, Calendar 77 events, To-do 16, Polls 3, Budget 18, Research 4, Chat 25) and three invented demo people. See [Getting Started](documentation/userguides/userguide-getting-started.md), "Try it with demo data".
  - **What it does to an environment.** It installs and turns on every bundled module, turns on the calendar and map options, sets the place search to the public Photon service (`SEED_SEARCH_URL` swaps it) and switches an environment with no template to travel. Use it on a throwaway, test or demo environment, not one with real data. It prints the three demo people's one-time passwords at the end. Re-running resets only the demo space and those three people. `SEED_NO_AI=1` skips the two `/ai` exchanges and `SEED_MAP=0` skips the map file, which needs the network.
  - **Hosted.** A hosted install is refused unless `DEMO_ALLOW_HOSTED=1` and `DEMO_ENVIRONMENT` are set, and it needs an owner's login in `ADMIN_LOGIN` and `ADMIN_PASSWORD`.
  - **For developers.** The `Dockerfile` copies `tools/seed-demo.mjs` and `tools/seed-demo-map.mjs`; the seeder also reads `modules/maps/src/maps-lib-b-pmtiles.js`, already in the image. `tools/check-compose.mjs` now reads the `Dockerfile` COPY lines and fails if the seeder, or anything it imports or reads from the repository, is not copied.
  - **Checked:** by tools, as reported by the builder, not rerun by this entry's author: `tools/check-compose.mjs` (70 checks pass; it fails when the COPY line is removed). Run by hand, as reported: the seeder from an image-layout folder (only the files the `Dockerfile` copies, production dependencies only) against a throwaway server finished with the counts above, reset on a rerun, and the hosted guard refused correctly. **Not done:** a real Docker image build, a run inside a real container, and a hosted run.
- **A check that neighbouring surfaces differ enough in every theme** (GitHub #371, item 5; a tool only, nothing a person sees changes). `tools/check-surface-steps.mjs` opens the Prototype page in headless Chromium on a throwaway server for each theme file in `public/design-system/themes/` (Strong Coffee, Burnt Orange, Calming Teal), dark and light, and reads each `--mf-surface-*` colour through a probe element, so the browser resolves the real `color-mix()` and each theme's overrides. It asserts a step of at least CIE deltaE 2000 of 2.0 (`MIN_STEP`) between neighbours: title bar and toolbar, toolbar and content, content and row, row and status bar; the title bar and the status bar must be equal by design. `--table` prints every value. It is in `npm run check` after `check-module-chrome`, takes about 11 to 15 seconds with one Chromium, and says SKIPPED and exits 0 with no Chromium (it fails if `CI` is set), like its neighbours. The smallest step per theme (dark / light) is 3.48 / 2.06 for Strong Coffee, 4.42 / 2.93 for Burnt Orange and 4.41 / 2.91 for Calming Teal; the minimum sits just under the smallest approved core step (Strong Coffee light, content and row, 2.06), the one to watch. A `KNOWN` list names themes allowed below the minimum; it is empty, and the check fails if it names a core or missing theme. The surface rule is in [design-theme](documentation/designsystem/design-theme.md). **Not covered:** only those six theme and mode views; a per-environment `/theme.css` is not measured. **Checked:** as reported by the builder, not rerun by this entry's author: it passes, and raising the toolbar mix from 93% to 99% panel made every theme and mode fail with a line naming the theme, mode and pair. The full `npm run check` was not run.
- **Print a picture or a PDF from the Vault** (Vault 0.3.4; GitHub #399). **Print** is in the file window's toolbar and in a file's **More** menu, for people who may download files. It opens the browser's print dialog, so you can also save as PDF. It is hidden on a phone, as **Download** is, and is not on the public share page. For module authors, `host.library.print(id)` does the same for a picture or a PDF and needs the same right as `host.library.download`; other kinds are refused ([api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md)). Covered by `check-module-guards` and `check-access-key` as the builder reported (not rerun by this entry's author); read as code only otherwise. Printing a PDF has not been seen in a real browser, and two problems found in review are in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- **The AI can look things up in Chat, and an answer says what it looked up** (`plan-ai-integration.md`, slice 2, steps 8 to 10; `ask@2`). When you ask `/ai` in a space, the AI may now search that space's objects and read the ones it needs before it answers, as you, so it can answer "what does the ferry note say?" without you attaching the note. It sees only what you could open yourself, only in this space, and it cannot change anything. Under the answer, **AI used:** gains **looked up N objects** with links to the first few it read; while it works the note under the box changes from "Asking the AI..." to "Looking things up..." after a few seconds. Where it is set: **Let AI look things up in this space** is a new switch in **What AI may see**, on by default, on **Manage**, **AI service** and in a space's **Set up**. A space can only turn it off: when the environment has it off, the space's switch is shown off, greyed, with "Off for the whole <environment>.".
  - **Limits.** One question is at most 4 rounds, 8 look-ups and 120 seconds. It is still one turn of the six a minute and one call against the host's monthly calls (counted only when the service billed a round); every round's tokens count against the allowance. A question that does not finish says "The AI didn't finish its answer." or "The AI service took too long to answer. Try again in a moment.".
  - **Shared answers.** When an answer is shared, a reader other than the asker gets only the objects they can open: `GET /api/spaces/:id/chat` filters the pointers per reader, so their **looked up N objects** counts what they can see. The live `chat-visibility` event carries no pointers; the page reads its copy from the list.
  - **For developers.** Three versioned tools in `server/prompts/tools/`, golden-checked in `tools/golden/tools/`: `search_objects@1` (`words`, `kind`, `from`, `to`, `has: "place"`; up to 15 summaries without text, best first, `more`), `read_object@1` (`n`; the text up to 8,000 characters and up to 5 linked objects) and `answer@1` (the final answer as a tool). The model is given numbers `n`, never pointers; a number the server did not give out, a deleted object and one the person can no longer read are all "Not found.". Every result goes through the policy's `admit` with part `lookup`, so What AI may see, the space pinning, the person's rights and redaction hold. `ask@2` is `ask@1` plus a look-up paragraph and renders as `ask@1` when no tool is offered; pinning `AI_PROMPT_VERSIONS=ask@1` turns look-ups off. A module's ask is offered no tools. A compatible service that refuses tools is asked again once without them, remembered for that setting. The stored answer keeps `sent.lookup` (`{ searches, read }`, the read objects as pointers, which join `used`). The call's record gains `tools`, `rounds`, `toolCalls`, `looked` and `stop`, never a tool's input or result. `What AI may see` gains the key `lookup`. Documented in `architecture-ai.md`, `api-modules.md`, `userguide-chat.md`, `userguide-modules.md`, `userguide-spaces.md` and `userguide-assistant.md`.
  - **Checked:** by tools, as reported by the builders and read from the checks' headers, not rerun by this entry's author: `tools/check-ai-tools.mjs` (the tools, the loop, the limits, the policy on every result, the errors a model is sent, a service refusing tools, shared answers), `check-prompts.mjs` (golden files for `ask@2`), `check-ai-page.mjs` (the line, the switch, the locked switch, a shared answer). All ran against `tools/fake-model.mjs` only. **Not verified:** any real provider. No real OpenAI, Anthropic or compatible service has answered a question with tools or without them, so how real models use the tools and stop, what a round costs in tokens, Anthropic's forced `answer` tool and OpenAI's required tool choice with strict schemas are unseen. The next step is the plan's 13-step test script (step 11); the owner supplies one provider key with a spending limit set on the provider's console (the plan suggests about US$10), an environment with a monthly allowance of 500,000 tokens that no real people use, and optionally a compatible service on the same machine.
- **Modules can say what their actions do, and the server lists the changes an AI could be asked to propose** (`plan-ai-integration.md`, slice 2, step 12; Budget 0.1.23, Calendar 1.24.36, Places 0.12.21, Research 0.4.32, To-do 1.15.26, Planner 0.16.5). Nothing a person sees changes yet, and nothing proposes or makes a change: this is the list, not the proposals (step 13, not built). An action in `module.json` may now carry `description` (one sentence, up to 200 characters), `effect` (`write`, the default, or `destructive`) and `ai: false`. The server makes a catalog from the enabled modules' manifests whenever it is asked, so it cannot drift from them. The six modules above gave their actions descriptions, each a patch version; four actions are tools today: `places__setPlacePoint`, `todo__linkTask`, `todo__setTaskDue` and `travel__addToDay`.
  - **For developers.** A module is refused at install, with a sentence beginning `module.json: action "<name>"`, for a `description` that is not text, is empty or is over 200 characters, an `effect` other than `write` or `destructive`, an `ai` that is not true or false, and, on a `local` action, an `effect` or `ai: true`. An action becomes a tool only when it acts on an object that already exists (a required pointer input and no whole-object input), is described, is `write`, is not `local`, not asker-only and not `ai: false`, and `<module>__<action>` is 1 to 64 letters, digits, `_` and `-`. A pointer input becomes a number in the tool's schema; the schema is the strict form both providers take (every field required, an optional one allowed to be `null`). `checkToolInput` answers one sentence for a bad input; an empty optional text counts as absent, a date must be a real day, a wrong pointer number names its kind. `GET /api/ai/catalog?space=` lists the tools for the person, narrowed to modules that are on, not kept out by What AI may see and that the person could change there. Documented in `architecture-ai.md` and `api-modules.md`.
  - **Checked:** by tools, as reported by the builders, not rerun by this entry's author: `tools/check-catalog.mjs` (the fields and their refusals, the schemas, `tools/golden/catalog.json`, the route through a throwaway server with the fake model) and `check-module-versions`. **Not verified:** that any real model sends valid input for these schemas.
- **The Vault (0.3.2): the keyboard stays where you were, taps are bigger on a phone, bulk adds wait as told, and a link can be turned off by whoever should.** *Keyboard.* After **Remove**, focus goes to the neighbouring file (or the empty list's button), not the top of the page. **Esc** closes **Add files** and puts the keyboard back on the **+** that opened it (`host.add.focus()`). The **Remove** question now ends "It is removed from the Vault for everyone in this space, and from any share link it is in. A link left with no file ends. This can't be undone.". *Phones.* The Vault's own small buttons are 44 pixels on a phone or a touch screen. *Bulk adds.* The Vault's adds used to share the 30 a minute that picture uploads have, so a drop of a few hundred files was slowed. The Vault now has its own budget: 400 files and 400 small pictures a minute for each person, 1,500 of the two together for one environment; reading a file or its small picture is never counted; a module's picture uploads keep their own 30 a minute, and the small picture of such an upload is never counted. When the server says to wait, the Vault waits that long (up to two minutes) and tries again, and a file that still fails shows the server's own sentence. *Turn off.* A share link is turned off by its maker, an owner or a moderator of the space (the admin counts as an owner); `GET /api/modules/:id/library/shares` carries `canTurnOff` for each link, and **Turn off** is offered only then.
  - **Every module's 429.** The sentence for a module that is going too fast now names the module and the wait: "<name> is busy. Wait N seconds and it will carry on." ("Wait a moment" when there is no number); it was "This module is doing that too often. Try again in a moment." for all of them.
