  - **For module authors.** `host.add.focus()` puts the keyboard on the module's **+** and resolves `true`, or `false` where there is none. A host call refused with 429 rejects with `err.retryAfter`, the seconds the server asked to wait. Documented in `api-module-sdk.md`, `api-modules.md` ("Limits" and the library routes), `architecture-modules.md` and `userguide-vault.md`.
  - **Checked:** by tools, as reported by the builders, not rerun by this entry's author: `tools/check-vault-module.mjs` (focus, Esc, the sentences, the sizes), `check-vault.mjs` (the budgets, the 429 and `Retry-After`) and `check-module-versions`. The share-link rule was documented earlier (`canTurnOff`, in the Vault's entries below). **Not verified:** a real phone's touch targets, and a bulk drop of several hundred real files.
- **The Prototype page draws map themes and Map options** (`plan-map-options.md`, step 0; design-process only, nothing ships). A new **Maps** section draws the map's own Light and Dark themes apart from the app theme, and the Maps module's **Map options** panel opened from the "..." menu, on a picture of a map made from the map tokens, in the app's dark and light themes side by side, with each map's contrast measured. Thomas's decisions of 2026-11-04 are drawn as decided; one question is still open with its recommendation. Steps 1 to 4 (the themes' tokens, the theme's default field in `/theme.css`, the Maps entry and panel) wait until he has seen it. Held menu over the pins, names and chips kept whole inside the map and a readable picture title were fixed on it. **Checked:** `tools/check-prototype-page.mjs` and `tools/check-prototype-words.mjs`, as reported by the builder, not rerun by this entry's author; not seen in a browser by this entry's author.
- **The AI is asked through a prompt layer, and an answer says what it used** (`plan-ai-integration.md`, slice 1). Under each answer in Chat a line reads **AI used:** and names the objects you attached (as links), then counts the related objects, linked objects, earlier questions, chat messages and people that went with the question; a second line may say **Not sent:** (objects from another space or kept out by the owner's setting), **Some objects were left out to fit**, or **The answer was cut short**. A citation such as [1] or [2, 3] in the words is a link that opens the object it names. A question may be left empty when an object is attached: Chat asks "What should I know about <its title>?". A question is 3 to 1,000 characters; a longer one is refused with its length, not cut. The question carries the asker's time zone, so "today" is theirs. An answer ending short no longer carries a note in its text; the page says it. The owner has two new sections on the AI configuration page: **What AI may see** (the objects a person picks, objects that fit the question, linked objects, earlier questions, the space's chat, who is in the space, the environment's objects outside any space, and which modules' objects), and **AI use this month** (tokens and calls by kind, person and space, refusals by reason, failures at the service, answers cut short and the last 50 calls, never any content). A space's Set up has its own **What AI may see**, following the environment's choice unless changed. See [userguide-chat](documentation/userguides/userguide-chat.md), [userguide-assistant](documentation/userguides/userguide-assistant.md) and [userguide-modules](documentation/userguides/userguide-modules.md).
  - **For developers.** Every word said to a model is now a versioned template (`ask@1`, `summarise@1`, `tags@1`, `paste@1`) in `server/prompts/`, frozen by golden files in `tools/golden/prompts/`; `AI_PROMPT_VERSIONS` pins a template to its earlier version and `AI_LOG_PROMPTS=1` writes full prompts to the log (ignored on a host). One policy (`server/ai-policy.js`) decides who may ask, which objects may be sent (the narrowest of the person's own rights, the current space and the owner's setting) and redacts secrets; the context builder (`server/ai-context.js`) sends the space, today, the phases, up to 15 relevant objects, linked objects and the asker's last 6 turns within a 12,000 token budget and records what it cut; the adapters (`server/ai-providers.js`) ask for structured output where the provider takes it. Calls are recorded without content in `ai-log.jsonl` (30 days) and used by template, person and space. New: `GET /api/ai/usage`, `sees` on `PUT /api/ai`, `aiSees` on `PATCH /api/spaces/:id`; an AI answer carries `prompt`, `sent`, `used`, `numbered`, `leftOut` and `cut`; all AI asks share one limit of six a minute; refusals have the reasons `member`, `chat` and `module` as well; the monthly cap is checked against each call's estimate and what asks on their way hold. `host.chat.ask({ question: '', refs })` asks for an object with no question; `host.ai.ask` sends the time zone. See [architecture-ai](documentation/architecture/architecture-ai.md), [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md) and [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md).
  - **Checked:** by tools, as reported by the builders: `tools/check-prompts.mjs` (golden prompts, parsing, the context, the policy, What AI may see, records and usage), `check-ai.mjs`, `check-ai-prompts.mjs` and `check-ai-page.mjs`, all against the fake model (`tools/fake-model.mjs`); not rerun by this entry's author. **Not verified:** a real OpenAI, Anthropic or compatible service answering the new prompts and structured output; the pages walked with two roles on a real server.
- **The Vault, a place for a space's files** (`plan-attachments-vault.md`, steps 1 to 3; Vault 0.2.2). A module for photos, audio, video, PDFs and Markdown notes, in the Travel template called Documents and in the Games template Handouts. It shows the files as a **List** or a **Grid**, with **Search files** and **Filters** (sort, type, added by, linked), **Add files** with progress and a choice, **Remove where it was taken** (off by default; remembered for you), **Rename** and **Remove** (with where each file is used), **Select** for several, and a status line with the space's room used. A file opens in a window of its own over the canvas: pictures zoom, audio and video play, Markdown is drawn as text, a PDF opens in the app's own sandboxed viewer with page buttons and search. Up to four windows at once ("Close a window to open another."), **Previous** and **Next**, **Info**, **Download**, **Full screen**, and **Close all** in the Layout menu under **Open files**. The server decides a file's type from its own bytes, refuses PDFs that hold scripts, programs to open, forms that send or files of their own (a PDF it could not check is kept for download only), and keeps the bytes outside the data folder (`VAULT_DIR`), under limits for a space, a person and the server. Guests never reach it. See [userguide-vault](documentation/userguides/userguide-vault.md). Linking a file from other modules and sharing a file outside the space came after this, in the entry that follows.
  - **For developers.** `"library": true` in `module.json`; the routes under `/api/modules/:id/library`; `GET /viewer/pdf` and the pinned pdf.js under `/sdk/pdfjs/`; `host.library` (`list`, `add`, `rename`, `remove`, `uses`, `thumb`, `url`, `blob`, `download`) and `host.files.open(pointer, { list, name })` with the `fileclosed` event; a module other than the Vault opens a Vault file only if its manifest consumes `vault:file` and an owner approved it. Variables: `VAULT_DIR`, `VAULT_MAX_GB`, `VAULT_SPACE_MAX_GB`, `VAULT_PERSON_MAX_GB`, `VAULT_MB_PER_MINUTE`, `VAULT_MIN_KB_PER_SECOND`, `VAULT_SLOW_CUTOFFS`, `VAULT_SLOW_MINUTES`, `VAULT_SCAN_SECONDS`; other routes' bodies must now arrive within `REQUEST_BODY_SECONDS` (300). See [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md), "Library routes", [architecture-modules](documentation/architecture/architecture-modules.md) and [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md).
  - **Checked:** by tools, as reported by the builders (`tools/check-vault.mjs`, `check-pdf-viewer.mjs`, `check-vault-viewer-cases.mjs`, `check-vault-module.mjs`, and live in headless Chromium for the file windows); not rerun by this entry's author. **Not verified:** a real phone; the module walked by two people at once; a PDF made by a real scanner or an office program; the sandboxed viewer in Firefox and Safari. **Known:** the page checks for new files every 15 seconds; see `known-issues.md`.
- **Other tools can link to a file, and a file can be shared outside the space** (`plan-attachments-vault.md`, steps 4 to 6; Vault 0.3.1, Planner 0.16.4). *Linking.* A stay or a journey in the Planner has an **Attachments** row: **Add file** picks files from the space's Vault, they show as chips (type icon, name, size) on the card and in the form, a click opens the file in its window with **Previous** and **Next** over that stay's files, and **Unlink** takes one off. A chip reads **File removed**, **File missing** or **Not available to you** where the file is gone, its content is gone, or you may not see it, with **Remove link** for people who can edit. The Vault's **Where it is used** and its Remove question come from the real links, and chips and open file windows change at once when someone renames or removes a file (the Vault's own list too; it still asks again every 15 seconds in case a message was missed). *Sharing.* People with the new permission **Make share links** (moderators; not members, never guests) choose **Share** on a file, on several selected files or in a file's window, name the link, choose when it **Ends** (1, 7 or 30 days), optionally set a password of 8 characters or more and optionally allow download. **Link made** shows the address once, with **Copy address**. **Share links** in the Vault's menu lists them with how many times each was opened, **Edit** and **Turn off**; a **Shared** filter and a small mark show which files are in a link. The address opens a public page, **Shared files**, with no sign-in: a grid, a viewer with **Back**, **Previous file** and **Next file**, **Download** only where the link allows it, and a password page where it has one. A link ends at its end, when turned off, when its last file is removed, when the Vault is turned off for the environment or uninstalled (for good), when the space is deleted, and when the person who made it leaves the space or loses **Make share links** (or **Download files**). A space holds at most 200 links and a link 500 files. *Keeping it tidy.* On start the server removes unfinished uploads, bytes with no record after a week, folders of deleted spaces and the records and links of deleted spaces (within `VAULT_SWEEP_SECONDS`, 10, and the rest on the next start), and counts records whose bytes are gone, which show as **File missing** until someone removes them. The Vault's files now have their own volume: the Dockerfile sets `VAULT_DIR=/app/vault` and `docker-compose.yml` lists the Vault's variables and mounts a second folder; back it up with the data folder. See [userguide-vault](documentation/userguides/userguide-vault.md) and [userguide-planner](documentation/userguides/userguide-planner.md).
  - **For developers.** `POST /api/objects/links` takes `kind` and `op` (`add` or `remove`, with a kind) so a module changes only its links to files; a `file` pointer resolves, searches and opens like any object (summary `icon`, `subtitle`, `group`, `size`, `type`, `viewable`, `missing`); a link to a removed file is kept and comes back as `{ title: "File removed", removed: true, state: "gone" }`. New in the SDK: `host.files.pick`, `link`, `unlink`, `resolve`, `host.ui.fileChips`, `host.library.share` and `shares` (events `sharechanged`, `sharechoose`), `host.objects.open(fileRef, { list })`, and the `library` event (`{ op, id, module, spaceId }`). The library list carries `missing` and `shared`; `GET /api/modules` carries `libraryFiles` and `libraryBytes`, and uninstall answers `filesRemoved` or `filesKept`. Share routes: `GET`, `POST`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /api/modules/:id/library/shares`, and without a session `GET /shared/<token>`, `GET /api/shared/<token>`, `POST /api/shared/<token>/unlock` (cookie `share_unlock`) and the file and thumbnail routes, every refusal an ended link where it could leak. The limits against guessing: 5 wrong passwords per link and address in 15 minutes, 30 per link, 20 per address, 100 lookups of links that do not open per address, password checks off the main thread (2 at once, 50 waiting), `SHARE_UNLOCKS_PER_MINUTE` (60) per environment. **Behind a reverse proxy, `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` must equal the number of proxies (0 when the container is exposed directly)**, because those limits count by the address the nearest proxy saw. New variables: `VAULT_SWEEP_SECONDS`, `SHARE_UNLOCKS_PER_MINUTE`. See [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md), "Share links" and "Library routes", [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md), "Linking files from your objects", and [architecture-modules](documentation/architecture/architecture-modules.md).
  - **Checked:** by tools, as reported by the builders and as read from the checks' own headers; not rerun by this entry's author except `tools/check-compose.mjs` (65 checks, passed): `tools/check-vault-links.mjs` (pointers, links, the event), `check-vault-share.mjs` (the share routes, limits and headers against throwaway servers), `check-vault-share-page.mjs` and `check-vault-link-live.mjs` (the public page, the sheet and the Planner's chips in headless Chromium, if one is installed), `check-vault.mjs` (the sweep) and `check-compose.mjs`. **Not verified:** a real proxy in front of a public address (the address limits under `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`); a real phone; the page in Firefox and Safari; two people using the same link at once; a backup restored without `VAULT_DIR` on a real install. **Not built:** the Modules page does not show the uninstall counts or mention files when you uninstall.
- **Clear can be undone for 30 seconds, and the server keeps what was cleared.** Clearing a module in a space now asks you to type its name, says "Cleared <name>." with **Undo**, and puts everything back as it was, with who added each thing and when, if you press **Undo** in time. (Corrected below: **Undo** works only from the page that cleared, and only until that page is closed or reloaded.) Until the time is over the files the things name, and their copies in other modules, stay. A second clear of the same module is refused while one can be undone ("<name> was just cleared. You can undo that for N more seconds..."), and a module can be cleared five times a minute in an environment. **Clear** is no longer offered where it would remove nothing (a module with no objects in the space), for the Vault (it holds files), or to a role without the right: the Planner has a new permission, **Clear the whole trip**, on for moderators and off for members. For developers: `DELETE /api/modules/:id/data` answers `restoreToken`, `undoSeconds` and `undoUntil`; `POST /api/modules/:id/data/restore` undoes it (409, 410 and 429 each with their sentence); the hold is `DATA_DIR/modules/<id>/trash/`; `CLEAR_UNDO_SECONDS` (1 to 600, default 30); keys that start with `_` are bookkeeping and uncounted; a big `changes` batch carries `reload: true`; `GET /api/modules/for-space` carries `clear` and `undoSeconds`. See [userguide-modules](documentation/userguides/userguide-modules.md), [architecture-modules](documentation/architecture/architecture-modules.md) and [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md). **Checked:** by `tools/check-clear-undo.mjs`, as reported by the builder; not rerun by this entry's author. **Known:** after a restart a refused Undo says the time is over whatever became of that token; see `known-issues.md`.
- **SDK: `host.ui.timeField`, `host.objects.pick` and a `kind` for searches.** `host.ui.timeField(input)` makes a time input follow the environment's 12 or 24 hour clock while its value stays `HH:MM` (the Calendar's and the Planner's times use it). `host.objects.pick({ modules, kinds, scope, space, title, multiple })` opens a window the host draws to choose another module's object, searching as the person, and resolves with a pointer, a list or null. `host.objects.search(text, { kind })` (and `GET /api/objects/search?kind=`) returns up to 1,000 objects of one kind. A small icon button (`.sdk-icon-btn`) is 44px by 44px on a touch screen. The design system pages document the time field and the object picker, and `--text-danger` and the Calendar's tokens. See [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md) and [design-theme](documentation/designsystem/design-theme.md). **Checked:** by `tools/check-sdk-pick.mjs`, as reported; not rerun here.
- **The Planner (0.16.1) links a stop to a Place and plans a car's whole stay** (Calendar 1.24.34, To-do 1.15.24). A stop has **Place**: **Pick from Places**, **Save as a place**, **Open in maps** and **Unlink**; a linked place shows as a chip that opens it. A rental car has a **Drop-off day** that agrees with **How long** and the end time, and its Calendar copy ends there. A trip has a **Time zone** ("Where the trip is"), or each person's own, and changing it moves the Calendar copies. **Decisions** lists polls to vote on, tasks due before the trip and anything else linked, soonest first, with the count in its tab. **Money** starts with the total of every cost and what is paid so far. An object's details show **Linked from**, and deleting a used object from its menu says "Used by N. Really delete?". **Clear** needs the new permission **Clear the whole trip**, with a typed name and an Undo. Times in the Planner and the Calendar follow the clock setting; the To-do's link search uses the environment's word for an object. See [userguide-planner](documentation/userguides/userguide-planner.md). **Checked:** by tools, as reported by the builders (`check-sdk-pick`, `check-clear-undo`); not rerun by this entry's author. The low-severity audit items PLN-13, PLN-14 and PLN-17 to PLN-25 are not touched; see `TODO.md`.
- **Modules can hold their data to finer rules on the server** (`guards` in `module.json`; Research 0.4.28, Polls 1.15.26, Budget 0.1.21, Planner 0.15.61). A module can now declare who may write which keys, who may change or remove what someone else added, which fields only the voter or the adder may set, when an object is closed, the shape of a stored value, and which fields a guest never receives, and the server enforces it on the data routes, not only the module's own page. A refusal is one plain sentence. Research keeps a photo to its adder (and to **Remove photos other people added**) and checks the photo's file; a poll's votes are the voter's alone and stop when the poll closes; costs, estimates, booking codes and who paid are kept from guests in Budget and the Itinerary. `access.personWrite` names the permission for writing a person's own data. The full contract and every refusal sentence are in [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md), "Data guards"; the reasons in [architecture-modules](documentation/architecture/architecture-modules.md).
  - **Guests and costs.** A space has a new switch in Set up, **Guests can see costs and booking codes**, off unless an owner turns it on (`guestsSeeHidden`, `PATCH /api/spaces/:id`, owners and the admin). While it is off, a guest does not receive the hidden fields from the data routes, the live stream, summaries or the published calendar, and `GET /api/objects/search?has=amount` answers a guest 403.
  - **Also in the API:** `GET /api/modules/for-space` carries `clear` for each module (whether this person may clear everything it holds there; the canvas shows **Clear** only then); a summary may carry `doneAt`, a time after which `done` is true (a poll closed by its time).
  - **Checked:** by tools, as reported by the builders (`tools/check-module-guards.mjs`, with the rules and the routes against throwaway servers, and the `check-modules`, `check-chat-page` and `check-link-preview` updates); not rerun by this entry's author. **Not verified:** the module pages drawn in a browser with a guest and a member side by side. **Known:** only Research's photos are held to their owner; see `known-issues.md`.
- **Link previews are limited and have a deadline** (Research 0.4.28). The server reads a link's page and picture only on the web's own ports (80 and 443), never from a private, loopback, link-local, reserved or its own address (including the special-use ranges), checks each redirect the same way, and gives up on one preview, redirects included, after 10 seconds ("that page took too long"). The picture route answers only for a picture this place already knows (one a saved link names, or one a preview offered in the last 30 minutes), and never for a guest. Documented in [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md). **Checked:** by `tools/check-link-preview.mjs`, as reported; not rerun here.
- **The Calendar shows the Itinerary's copies as the trip wrote them** (Calendar 1.24.32, Planner 0.15.61). A copy of an Itinerary object now carries its length (the check-out day and time, or the minutes), is marked with the object's icon and "from the Itinerary", and is shown in the trip's own clock; when that is not yours the tooltip says so ("Lisbon time"). Ticking **All day** on a copy no longer erases the original's time. **Hide** (it was Delete) takes a copy off the Calendar for good: the object stays on the plan and the copy is not made again, even when its day changes. For module authors: a twin's summary carries `copy`, `twinOf` and (on the original) `twins`; a wall-clock `dated` may name `endTime`, `minutes` and `zone`, an instant one `tz`; `host.storage.set(key, value, { tz })`. See [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md), "Dated twins". **Checked:** by tools, as reported (`tools/check-calendar-twins.mjs`, `check-travel`, `check-links`, `check-destinations`); not rerun here. **Not verified:** the `.ics` feeds do not treat copies specially; that was not found in the code and is not claimed.
- **The Calendar page's toolbar has two zones** (Calendar 1.24.32, To-do 1.15.23). On the left, the period's title, back, **Today** and forward, then **Month | Week | Day**; at the far right, never folded into **More**, **Agenda | To-do**, with the shown panel's own items before it. Other changes in the same pass: Undo for a ticked task or a deleted one inside the To-do's own box, an overdue task in a readable danger tone, the chosen day lighter in the light mode with today's ring in both, a keyboard path through the month, and the focus ring on the chosen day. This also settles the known issue that today and the chosen day looked alike. A host page can place a toolbar item in the `end` zone; the module frame marks it `mf-zone-end`. See [architecture-module-window](documentation/architecture/architecture-module-window.md). **Checked:** by `tools/check-calendar-todo.mjs` (in headless Chromium), `check-conformance` and `check-module-chrome`, as reported; not rerun here.
- **The map reads on every theme** (Maps 0.9.14, Places 0.12.18). The map's colours are now tokens in the SDK stylesheet (`--map-land`, `--map-water`, `--map-park`, `--map-wood`, three road classes, `--map-building`, `--map-label`, `--map-boundary`), and the Maps style moves any of them that a theme sets badly until it holds a minimum against the land (names 4.5 to 1, roads, water with a blue of its own, parks, boundaries). Pins have a thin ring. Other fixes from the health audit of Maps and Places (MP-02 to MP-22): **+** opens its dialog where it can be seen on a narrow screen, Places reads what points at its places in one request, the Itinerary tells the host what its cards point at so Places can say "Used by" and warn before a delete, and a role that may not edit places is not shown **+**. For module authors: `host.objects.linksToAll(refs)` and `POST /api/objects/links/batch`; the Maps destination fields `mapOn` and `changed`. The tokens are in [design-theme](documentation/designsystem/design-theme.md), "The map". **Checked:** by `tools/check-maps.mjs` (every shipped theme against the minimums) and `check-maps-live` (headless Chromium; SKIPPED without one), as reported; not rerun here.
- **A demo seeder.** `npm run seed:demo` fills a running server, through its own API, with one space, "Demo: Portugal", a ten-day trip with every module holding data, three demo people, a guest link, the place search chosen and a small map file of Lisbon, Sintra and Porto; running it again resets only the demo. It can fill one environment of a hosted install (`DEMO_ALLOW_HOSTED`, `DEMO_ENVIRONMENT`, that environment's own address, an owner's login). `npm run seed:demo-map` makes the map file alone. The settings, three recipes for a nightly reset (cron, Task Scheduler, GitHub Actions) and the checks added since 0.4 are in [architecture-demo-site](documentation/architecture/architecture-demo-site.md). **Checked:** `tools/check-seed-demo.mjs` (a throwaway server, seeded twice), as reported; not rerun here. The recipes were written from how the seeder behaves and have not been run.
- **Documentation:** [architecture-ai](documentation/architecture/architecture-ai.md) and [architecture-demo-site](documentation/architecture/architecture-demo-site.md) are new. [architecture-teams-and-budgets](documentation/architecture/architecture-teams-and-budgets.md) gains the usage file the project manager reads before launching agents, the quick-fix lane for small changes, and the weekly ceiling of 85% (Thomas, 2026-11-03). Several plans' status lines were brought up to date.
- **A touch check and day-head assertions.** `tools/check-touch.mjs` emulates a touch device and checks that every visible text field is at least 16px (about 40 seconds); `tools/check-prototype-parity.mjs` gained assertions for the Planner's day head. **Checked:** by those tools, as reported by the builder; not rerun by this entry's author. Not verified on a real iOS or Android device.
- **The Prototype runs the real Planner, with a parity check** (Planner 0.15.55). The Prototype page of the design system pages now has a Planner section that runs the Planner's own markup and styles on a sample week, with a jump list to each part and a **Light** switch that tries four candidate light modes for Strong Coffee (Paper, Cool mist, Cream and Contrast; none is shipped). `tools/check-prototype-parity.mjs`, in `npm run check`, measures it against the real Planner in headless Chromium (positions, heights, shared edges, 44px taps at 390px) and checks the copied markup. See `design-theme.md`. **Checked:** by that tool, as reported by the builder; not rerun by this entry's author. Chromium only.
- **The design check runs on GitHub** (`.github/workflows/design-check.yml`). One job for each of the four scenarios (1280 and 390 wide, light and dark) on every push to main and every pull request, and `npm run check:design` runs all four locally. The workflow has not yet run on GitHub.
- **Auto-scroll while you drag in the Planner.** Dragging a stop near the top or bottom edge of the list scrolls it, faster the nearer the edge, and more slowly if your system asks for reduced motion. It does not scroll elsewhere, and starting a drag no longer moves the list.
- **The product page can live on its own domain** (`plan-domains.md`, steps 1 to 6). A new setting, `PRODUCT_DOMAIN`, puts the product page, the use pages and sign-up on a host of their own, so the product's name no longer has to be in every environment's address. Unset, nothing changes: the product page stays on the bare `BASE_DOMAIN`, the console at `admin.<base domain>` and each environment at `<slug>.<base domain>`. The console stays at `admin.<base domain>` (there is no setting for it). When `PRODUCT_DOMAIN` is set, the bare base address sends visitors on to the product host (a 302 that keeps the path and query). A host in `PREVIOUS_BASE_DOMAINS` is now redirected after the product host is checked, so an old base that has become the product host is not caught. Both new settings are ignored, with a line in the log, without `BASE_DOMAIN`. Start checks, run before anything is written: `PRODUCT_DOMAIN` equal to `admin.<base domain>` stops the start, and so does a product host whose first label (directly under the base) is an existing environment's name. A value that is not a host name is ignored with a line. LiveKit is unchanged.
  - **Reserved names.** `livekit`, `turn`, `stun`, `console`, `signup` and `login` join the built-in list. A new setting, `RESERVED_SLUGS` (comma separated), adds names of the host's own, and the first label of `PRODUCT_DOMAIN` and of `LIVEKIT_HOST` is reserved when it sits directly under the base. A reserved name is refused only where an environment is made (the console's create form, sign-up and `MIGRATE_ENVIRONMENT_SLUG`) with "<name> is reserved. Pick another."; an environment that already has such a name keeps working, and the start logs a line once for each.
  - **Sign-up and sign in across the two sites.** The sign-up form checks the name as it is typed against a new public route, `GET /api/product/address?slug=`, and says "is reserved. Pick another." instead of "is free" for a reserved name. After sign-up the person lands on the new environment's sign-in with their login filled in (`/login?login=<login>`); no one-time sign-in link was added. The product page skips the "Sign in to ..." shortcut for the environment last used when it is on a different site from the base, since that is remembered for the base domain only. Manage's **Upgrade** and the console's facts ("Product pages") use the product host.
  - **API:** `GET /api/product/address`; `productDomain` in `GET /api/product` and `GET /api/host/settings`; sign-up's `url` ends in `/login?login=<login>`. Documented in `architecture-environments.md`; the deployment steps (a record, an ordinary certificate and a proxy host for the product host, the base domain's wildcard unchanged) are in `userguide-getting-started.md`; `docker-compose.yml` has both settings as commented examples. The getting-started example environment name is now neutral.
  - **Checked:** by tools: `tools/check-origin.mjs` and `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` (the door's order, the redirects, the reserved names, the address route, the start checks) and `tools/check-brand-hosts.mjs` (no brand domain in code). Only `check-brand-hosts` was run for this entry; the other two are named from the build commits and were not rerun here. The product page's new behaviour was read as code only here. **Not verified:** QA has not finished; sign-in from the product host into an environment on another site in Chromium, Firefox and Safari (with and without two-step sign-in); a real deployment on the new domains (Thomas's step 7); a real call (there is no LiveKit server here).
- **Budget pulls the Planner's priced bookings in; the Planner's own money steps aside** (`plan-budget-module.md`, step 3; Budget 0.1.4, Planner 0.15.22, `host.objects.priced()`, `host.budget.elsewhere()`). In **Costs**, Budget now also lists every booking that has a **Cost** in the Planner, marked **From Planner**, read-only, with **Open in Planner** on its row. Budget counts such a booking as paid, in the category **Other**, in the totals, the burndown, **Settle up** and the strip's figure. A person sees only the bookings of modules they may read; when some are left out, Budget says "Some costs are from a module you can't see." Nothing is copied or deleted: while Budget is on in a space, the Planner's **Money** view and its **Spent** chip on the strip are hidden, and they come back when Budget is turned off; the **Cost** and **Paid by** fields stay on each booking. Budget asks again every 30 seconds and when its window is shown again, because the SDK has no event that tells a module another module's objects changed.
  - **For module authors:** `host.objects.priced()` resolves to `{ summaries, withheld }`, the summaries that carry an `amount`, as the person may read them, and the number left out (never a title or a figure); `host.objects.search(text, { has: 'amount' })` is the same search. `host.budget.elsewhere()` is true when another module with `"budget": true` is on in the space and open to the person; the Planner uses it to step aside. On the server, a summary may now carry `amount`, `paidBy` and `sharedBy`, and `GET /api/objects/search` takes `has=amount` and answers `withheld`; any other `has` is 400 "has must be place or amount". The Planner's booking summary names its `cost`, payer and sharers (Planner 0.15.19). Documented in `api-module-sdk.md`, `api-modules.md` and `userguide-budget.md` ("Costs from other modules").
  - **Known, recorded in `known-issues.md`:** the Planner's last **Spent** report can stay on the strip for up to 7 days, beside Budget's, until someone who can change the Planner opens it; and Budget is up to 30 seconds behind a change in the Planner. Step 5 (the travel template turns Budget on) is still not built.
  - **Checked:** by tools, `tools/check-budget.mjs` and `tools/check-travel.mjs` (the amount reader, the pulled-in costs counted once and as paid, the withheld note, the Planner stepping aside), `tools/check-destinations.mjs` (the search filter and `withheld`) and `check-module-versions`, as reported by their builders; not rerun by this entry's author and not walked live against a real server with two roles.
- **Owners and the admin are told about new bundled modules and updates.** The bell shows one notice, from **Modules**, for each module that ships with the server and is new to the environment ("Budget is available") and for each installed module with a newer bundled version waiting ("Budget has an update (0.1.2)"). It comes once for each module and each version, and choosing it opens **Manage** on the Modules tab. Members and guests are not told, and a brand new environment is told nothing, since its owner is looking at the whole list. On the Modules tab the **Updates available** and **Available** chips and the **Manage** entry show a green count while it is above zero. The record is kept per environment in `modules/announced.json`. See `userguide-modules.md` ("Notices about new modules and updates"). Checked by a tool, `tools/check-module-notices.mjs`; the bell and the green counts were read as code only, not walked live.
- **The Planner words what has no day as "Unscheduled"** (Planner 0.15.21 and 0.15.22). "The line" is no longer a word on screen. The section that was **Not on a day yet** is **Unscheduled**, and so are its group in the lists of days and the form's button. **Back to the line** is **Move to unscheduled**. **Earlier** and **Later** are **Move up** and **Move down** for an object with no time (it swaps with its neighbour on the same day), and **30 minutes earlier** and **30 minutes later** for one with a time; either stays on its day. **Move to** is **Move to another day…**: a list grouped by phase, with an **Unscheduled** group ("Before the first day", "Between Oct 16 and Oct 17", "After the last day") and, last, **Pick a date…**. In the form, **When**, **Return day** and **Check out** are a date field that reaches every day of the space's phase timeline, with **Pick from the list** and a button for no day. Every phase has its own "starts" and "ends" marker on the itinerary; they are no longer merged. Only the days of the **Trip** phase are numbered "Day 3 of 9"; a day dated in another phase shows its date and its phase tag. The form's **When**, time and **How long** are one wrapping row, and a note and a stay have no **How long**. Stored data and the API are unchanged; the note an arriving object produces now says "Unscheduled". See `userguide-planner.md`. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs` as reported by the builder; not walked live.
- **Budget, a module for what a space spends** (`plan-budget-module.md`, steps 1, 2 and 4; Budget 0.1.0). Any space can keep its costs, not only a trip. Each cost has a title, a category, a planned and a paid amount, who paid, who shares it, a date and notes. Budget has three views. **Overview** shows the target, what is spent, what is expected and what is left, a bar of spent against the target, each category's planned against paid, and a burndown (the target left over time, with the phase bands behind it; it is the burndown #216 asked for). **Costs** lists every cost, grouped by day or category, filtered by category and by who paid. **Settle up** shows each person's paid, share and net, and the fewest payments that settle it; it appears only when a cost has a payer and the space has more than one person. A **+** in the titlebar adds a cost. Categories are a list the owner edits in Budget's settings (Travel, Lodging, Food, Supplies, Services, Fees, Other), with **Other** always there, as Places does. Budget gives other modules an `addCost` action, a `cost` kind to point at, and reports "Spent $X of $Y" to the status strip. Everyone who can see the module reads the budget; guests cannot. See `userguide-budget.md`.
  - **For module authors:** a module that says `"budget": true` in `module.json` may read the space's target and change it with `host.budget.can()` and `host.budget.set(target)`; `set()` needs the target or `null`, never nothing, and a currency sent alone replaces the whole target. `GET /api/modules/for-space` carries `budget` (true or false) on each module. Documented in `api-module-sdk.md` and `api-modules.md`.
  - **Not built yet:** the travel template does not turn Budget on (step 5). (The Planner's costs reaching Budget, step 3, is the next entry.) Where the build differs from the plan: **Set a target** opens an editor inside the module rather than Set up; the category filter is a row of buttons; the summary's `amount` field is declared but not carried until the server's list of summary fields gains it (step 3).
  - **Checked:** by tools, `tools/check-budget.mjs` (16 tests: totals, categories, settle-up, the burndown, the fact's words, the amount reader, the host's rules for the target) and `check-facts`, `check-nav`, `check-modules` (33 groups) and `check-module-versions`, all run again for this entry. **Not verified:** the module drawn in a browser (the documentation was written from the code), two people adding costs at once, a real phone.
- **Who sets the budget target.** The space's owners, the admin and the space's moderators set it, in Set up (on the **Phases** tab; a space without phases shows it on the tab named for the space, **Space** by default, and a moderator without that tab sees it on **First visit**). Members read it through Budget and guests not at all; it is still left out of the presence list, the space lists, the stream's status and every access key view. This replaces "owners and the admin only". A target may be a currency alone, so a space with no budget can still choose the currency its costs are worded in; a currency sent alone replaces the whole target. The server refuses an amount that is sent but is not a good number, so a bad entry never deletes a stored target. Amounts typed as `1.234,56` or `1,234.56` are read as 1234.56. Checked by tools: `check-facts` (245 checks), `check-nav`. Documented in `api-modules.md`.
- **Guests in Home's chat** (GitHub #289). A guest who comes in on the Lobby's guest link now reads Home's chat live and can post in it. The server limits them: a guest link may post `GUEST_CHAT_POSTS_PER_MINUTE` messages a minute (20), one address half of that; a guest link may hold `CHAT_STREAMS_PER_GUEST_LINK` live streams open (25), one address 40% of that. Too many answers 429 with the seconds to wait. The stream ends at once when the guest link is turned off or replaced. Guests still have no Assistant. Checked by tools: `tools/check-guest-chat.mjs` (79 checks). Documented in `api-modules.md` and `userguide-accounts.md`.
- **The time in the call** (GitHub #37). The call's titlebar and the call's own titlebar when it floats or has a window show how long you have been in the call, as `12:34` and `1:00:00` from the first hour. It counts from when you joined, not from when the call began, and it restarts correctly when a hidden page comes back. Checked by `check-canvas`.
- **In the narrow layout, floating windows dock** (GitHub #268). At 640 pixels and narrower every floating module that can dock moves into the narrow layout's tabs, and floats again at its remembered place when the window is wide. The narrow-layout real iPhone and tab bar always sit above the canvas. Checked by `check-canvas`; not verified on a real phone.
- **The Itinerary draws phase markers** (Planner 0.15.16). In a space with phases, the line between days shows **<Phase> starts** and **<Phase> ends** from the bar's dates (joined into one marker where one phase ends as the next begins, until Planner 0.15.21 gave each phase its own); they replace the four automatic markers.
- **Set up and the Layout menu show each module's one-line description**, two lines at most, with the whole text on hover. Checked by `check-nav`.
- **The one bar and a left column that is always there** (`plan-one-bar.md`, steps 1 to 8, and its fixes). In a space, the top bar now holds the space's name with the switcher, the layout tabs, **Who is here**, the bell and your picture; the space bar is gone. At the left of the canvas is a column that every layout shows: **Discussion | Assistant** in one box (Assistant 0.1.23), and the call above or beside it. Your commands and the Assistant's answers are private to you; **Share** makes one public. The column can be dragged from 45% of the window for the chat up to the whole width (it can be resized again after a fix), and in the narrow layout the tabs stay as they were.
  - **The call.** The call has its own titlebar and menu. **Call position** is **Top**, **Bottom**, **Left**, **Floating** or **Its own window** (it pops out again), and the video cannot squeeze the chat below 45% of the column. On a short window the call is a slim strip. Out of a call there is no call area, only **Join the call**; there is no "hide the video" any more. **Leave** is in the space switcher's menu.
  - **Set up** replaces **Edit** for owners, the admin and moderators.
  - **Checked:** by the tools the builders ran (`check-nav`, `check-canvas`, `check-layouts`) and live in headless Chromium at 1280 and 390 pixels with a stand-in LiveKit. **Not verified:** a real call (there is no LiveKit server here), a screen share lifting into the Workspace, Pop out with the column.
- **Phases, set per space** (`plan-one-bar.md` steps 1 to 3, `plan-planner-phases.md`). A template defines a space's phases; each space gets its own dates. Phases are one run of days with no gaps, and **any** phase's end date can be set or cleared, **Post-trip** included. Days are read as UTC days everywhere. The phase sheet (opened from the status strip) shows a bar and one row per phase, and closes after **Save**. The Planner reads its dates from the space.
  - **On the Calendar and the Itinerary** a phase shows as a soft fill on its days, with its name on the first day, never a coloured border (Calendar 1.24.8, Planner 0.15.10). The Calendar's Day view keeps the phase bar in sight as you scroll.
  - **The travel template** gives each phase an icon (a Font Awesome Free name; template version 6). A template phase may carry an optional `icon`; branding and the space read pass it on.
  - **API:** `PATCH /api/spaces/:id { phases }`; the contract is in the API documents.
- **The status strip tells the journey** (`plan-space-status-strip.md` steps 1 to 4, `plan-space-story-strip.md` steps 1 and 2). Under the top bar, a 44 px strip holds the phase chip, a bar of the phases drawn as bands filled with the phase colours (each with its icon and name where the band is wide enough), and one sentence such as "Planning, day 20 of 20". The chip no longer has a caret, as it opens the phase sheet, not a menu. On a short or narrow window it falls back to a slim line.
  - **Needs action.** The strip shows a count of what needs you and a list. Modules report with `host.attention.set` and `host.attention.clear`; the server keeps counts per space and per person (dropped after 7 days). Polls reports the open polls you have not voted in (Polls 1.15.4).
- **Set up: every setting of a space in one panel** (`plan-setup-panel.md`, steps 1 to 8). The panel has tabs: **Trip** (the old space tab: name, picture, description, links, **Delete**), **Members**, **Phases**, **First visit** and **Modules** (each module's settings in a section). One **Save** sits in a bar locked to the bottom, with **Cancel**; clicking outside does not close the panel; a dot shows unsaved changes. Invite links, guest links and file deletion stay instant actions. A moderator sees **Phases**, **First visit** and **Modules**, not **Members**. Open it by address with `/?setup=<space id>#<tab>`.
  - **Removed:** the full settings page and the module settings page. `/spaces/:id` and `/module-settings` redirect (302) to the panel, so saved links still work.
  - **First visit** is one choice: what a new visit opens (the Workspace, Calendar or Map layout). It is independent of **Opens with**.
- **Layouts for every view** (`plan-layout-views.md`, steps 1 to 6). Workspace, Calendar and Map are layouts, shown as tabs right after the space switcher, in one style. Calendar and Map appear while those modules are on; Workspace is the default and can be renamed, not deleted. **Rename** and **Change icon** work on every layout row, per person and per space. Favorites are tabs too, with no limit; tabs that do not fit go into a **...** menu, first. Only the Workspace remembers changes. Map and Calendar load as layouts, and the old view code is gone. The Layout menu and window tools show on every layout tab. **Global Calendar** and **Global Map** in the profile menu replace the environment Calendar and Map names.
  - **Calendar and Map on the Workspace.** The Calendar (events and tasks together) and the Map (Maps and Places together) are each one module in a space, with the space's data and a toggle per space at the environment level (`plan-calendar-todo.md` and `plan-map-places.md`, steps 1 to 3). `GET /api/destinations/:id?space=<id>` gives the parts for a space; stored keys and links are unchanged.
- **Home in three columns** (`plan-home-lobby.md`, steps 1 to 4). Home shows **Chat**, **Spaces** and **Modules** across the page width, with **Who is here** above the spaces. The Lobby is no longer a card on Home; its record is kept as Home's hidden backing, so the chat history stays. Home's chat is live without a call, has an unread badge, and is swapped for a space's own chat when you go Home from a space and stay in the call (Away set).
  - **API:** `GET /api/spaces/:id/chat/stream` streams a space's chat (used for Home's).
- **A "+" in every module's titlebar and a status line at its foot** (`plan-module-titlebar-add.md`, steps 1 to 6). The row of buttons along the bottom of module windows is gone. One **+** in the titlebar opens the module's adds; the big buttons (Add event, Add a place) moved there. The bottom is a slim status line (counts, the chosen day). Done in Research, the Planner, Calendar, To-do, Polls, Places and Maps, and for the Calendar and Map layouts, in the narrow layout and in a pop-out.
  - **For module authors:** `host.add.set(...)` and `host.status.set(...)`.
- **A picture map for an invented world** (`plan-map-image.md`, steps 1 to 4). In **Maps' settings**, beside **Map files**, an owner can upload one raster PMTiles file as the **Picture map**, and **Clear** it. While it is set it replaces the world map in every space of the environment, and the outside place search and the real-world actions (and the map credit) are off. **Map files** marks each file **Picture** or **Map data**. A place saved before the picture map keeps its position. The picture map stops zooming at the file's own highest zoom.
  - **For developers:** the upload is streamed, limited by `MAP_IMAGE_MAX_MB` (default 100); `environment.pictureMap` in a module's context tells it; a place search answers `configured: false, pictureMap: true`. Maps 0.8.7 and 0.8.8 draw it; Places follows a change live.
  - **Not verified:** the picture drawn with WebGL (headless Chromium here has none) and a very large upload on a real server.
- **One way to add a place** (`plan-map-places.md`). Maps' **+** and Places' editor open the same **Add a place**, with a target button beside **Position**, **Where** in every context, and the environment's name in the filter. Notes that were messages are toasts: `host.ui.toast(text, { ... })` for module authors (Places 0.10.7, Maps 0.8.9).
- **Place categories are a list** the owner edits in Places' settings. A place with an unknown category shows under **Other** and keeps its id (Places 0.10.8, Maps 0.8.10). Maps draws a place in its chosen category colour (Maps 0.8.11).
- **The Planner's day menu** has **Add** beside it, **Move all to...** and **Clear this day** (Planner 0.15.8). Calendar and To-do scroll as one in a stacked window (Calendar 1.24.7, To-do 1.15.6).
  - **Checked:** these module builds were checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs` and `tools/check-destinations.mjs`; not walked live by this entry's author.
- **A calendar address for each space, a published calendar, and one Calendars tab on your profile** (GitHub #179, `plan-space-calendars.md`, steps 3 to 5 of 8). Your profile has a new **Calendars** tab, between **Profile** and **Spaces**, with one line at the top, "Calendars coming in are only for you. Addresses going out let your calendar app show events from here.", and two sections: **Your calendars, coming in** (the former **Other calendars**, moved here, its line now "Their events show in your Calendar, only to you. In a space, you can share when you are busy, never what you are doing.") and **Addresses, going out** (the former **Calendar feed**, widened). Addresses are rows: **Everything** first (every event you can see, in one calendar), then one row per space you belong to, with the space's icon and name, so a trip can be its own calendar in Google, named after the space. Each row shows on or off, "Made <date>. Last read <time ago>." (or "Not read yet."), **Make an address**, **New address** and **Turn off**; the address is shown once, in its row, with **Copy** and the Google and Apple steps, which end "Google can take 8 to 24 hours to show a change." A space's row says "Your everything address already holds this <space>, so you only need one of them." when you have both. Whatever can't be used says why in place, never by hiding the tab: "Owners have not turned on private addresses in <environment>." (an owner reads "Turn it on in Calendar's configuration." after it, with **Calendar** as the link), "Calendar is off in <environment>.", "No module here has events to add to a calendar app.", a space row's "Calendar is off in this <space>.", the Everything row's "There are no events here you can add to a calendar app.", and, for an address kept while the switch is off, "Private addresses are off in <environment> for now, so this address does not work." with **Turn off** still offered. The tab shows when the Calendar is on and an owner has turned on a switch, to owners whenever the Calendar is on, and to anyone who still holds an address or an other calendar; `/profile#calendars&space=<id>` opens it on a space's row. An owner opening someone's profile sees each of their addresses on or off with "Made … Last read …" and **Turn off** on the same tab (no longer in the account section), never the address. Leaving a space, being taken off its member list, or the space being deleted ends your address for it. For owners, the Calendar's configuration page now shows the third switch, **Published calendar** ("One address with every space's events, for anyone you give it to."), with its address under the switches: "No address yet." and **Make an address**, then "Made <date>. Last read <time ago>." with **New address** and **Turn off**; the address once, with **Copy** and "Copy it now. It will not be shown again. Give it to whoever should have it; anyone with the address can read every event in it, without signing in. Google can take 8 to 24 hours to show a change." It holds the environment's events and every space's where the Calendar is on, except the Lobby and spaces an owner leaves out: a space's page has a new **Calendar** section with **In the published calendar**, on by default, shown only while publishing is on and never for the Lobby. The **Private addresses** line reads "Lets each person add this environment's events, or one space's, to their own calendar app with a private address." again. Busy times (steps 6 and 7) are not built: the tab has no third section yet, and the Calendar's menu in a space has no entry opening the tab.
  - **API:** `GET /api/me/calendars` answers the tab in one (`switches`, `reasons`, `module`, `everything`, `spaces[]` with each row's `address { allowed, reason, on, made, readAt }`, `external`); `POST /api/me/spaces/:id/feed` (201 `{ url, made }`, once; 403 with the row's reason; 404 "no such space" for the Lobby), `DELETE /api/me/spaces/:id/feed` (204), `DELETE /api/users/:key/spaces/:id/feed` (owners and the admin, 204); `GET /api/settings` gains `publishedCalendarFeed { on, made, readAt }`; `POST /api/settings/published-calendar` (201 `{ url, made }`; 403 "The published calendar is off in this environment." while the switch is off) and `DELETE` (204); `PATCH /api/spaces/:id { publishCalendar }` (400 "publishCalendar is true or false"); each entry of a user's `spaces` carries `calendarFeed { on, made, readAt }` for those who see the personal one. `GET /feed/<token>.ics` looks a hash up as an everything address, then a space address, then the published one, with the same 404, 429, 304 and 200 rules; a space address is named after the space (`X-WR-CALNAME`) with the space's name left out of each description and the same `UID`s as the everything address; the published one is named after the environment, each description naming its space, read with nobody's rights. **Stored:** `user.spaces[<id>].calendarFeed { hash, made, readAt }`, `settings.publishedCalendarFeed { hash, made, readAt }` and `space.publishCalendar: false` (absent means in); only hashes, never a token; `readAt` at most hourly; tokens dropped on leaving, on a `members` patch, on a space's or a person's removal; on a hosted server each token works only in its own environment. `GET /api/me/feed` stays. No module changed, so no module version bumps.
  - **Where the build differs from the plan** (the plan's "Progress" lists them for Thomas): the tab also shows to owners with both switches off; it reads two routes (`/api/me/calendars` and `/api/me/external-calendars`); the Google line sits at the end of a just-made address's steps; `everything.reason` and `module` were added to the answer; a `members` patch also drops tokens; `switches.busy` and `busy` are left to step 6; with the Calendar off the published address answers an empty calendar, not a 404.
  - Documented in `api-modules.md` ("Calendar feeds", rewritten for the three kinds of address), `architecture-modules.md`, `architecture-environments.md`, `userguide-accounts.md` (the new "The Calendars tab"), `userguide-calendar.md` (the new "The published calendar (owner)"), `userguide-spaces.md`, the plan's "Progress" and `TODO.md`. **Verified live** by the builders in Chromium at 1280 and 390 px as the admin, an owner, a member and a guest, and the server live with curl on a single-environment and a hosted install. **Checked by a tool:** `tools/check-feed.mjs` (34 groups: the tab's answer, a space address made once and kept as its hash holding that one space's events under its name, none for a guest, a non-member or a non-member owner, 404 with the module off in the space, with `read` lost, with the switch off, after **New address**, **Turn off**, an owner's **Turn off**, after leaving, removal, a `members` patch, the space's deletion and the person's; the published calendar made by owners and the admin only, 403 with its switch off, every space's events and the environment's with nobody's rights, a space left out, 404 with the switch off and after **Turn off**; the Configure page and the space's settings as code) and `tools/check-external-calendars.mjs` (31 checks), each run again for this entry. **Not tried:** a real Google or Apple subscription to a space address or the published calendar. Quality-assurance's result is to be added.
- **An uploaded module's form opens over the page too** (GitHub #192, `plan-editor-window.md`, step 11 of 12, its last build step). A module someone uploaded runs in a sandboxed frame, where a form window can only be as big as the frame. Now, when such a module opens its Add or Edit form through the SDK's editor window, its whole frame is lifted over the page for as long as the form is open: 560 px wide (880 px for a large form) by the window's height less 48 px, centered, with the page behind tinted and waiting, and a full-screen sheet at 640 px and below; the form fills the frame, so it looks and works as a bundled module's does. The frame goes back where it was when the form closes, when the module is moved between docked and floating (the form reopens there), or when the module is closed; on the module's own page it lifts over the header; in a module popped out into its own window nothing lifts, since the form fills that window already. No bundled module runs in a frame, so nothing changes for them.
  - **For module authors:** nothing to call; `host.ui.editor` does it. In a frame the SDK sends `editor.lift` `{ open, size }` over the bridge before `showModal()` and `{ open: false }` after the module's last open editor closes; the host answers `true` when it lifted the frame (the dialog then gets `sdk-editor-lifted` and fills the frame) and `false` in the page or in a window of its own. In the host: `liftFrame()` and `releaseLift()` in `mountModule()` (`public/module-host.js`, which takes `lift` and `onLift`), the classes `module-editor-lifted` and `module-editor-lifted-large`, `div.module-editor-backdrop`, the rest of the page made `inert` as a modal dialog does it, and the mount's `lift(false)`, which `public/canvas.js` calls before a move; `public/module.js` mounts a popped-out module with `lift: false`. Styles in `public/style.css` between `lift:start` and `lift:end` and in `/sdk/host.css`. **Where the build differs from the plan:** the frame is exactly the editor's size with the dialog filling it, not 32 px wider with the module showing behind. Documented in `api-module-sdk.md` ("An editor window"), `architecture-module-window.md`, `architecture-modules.md` ("The frame"), `userguide-modules.md` ("Add and edit forms") and the plan's "Built" note. No module changed.
  - **Checked by a tool:** `tools/check-module-host.mjs` (58 groups; the new one runs the lift on a stand-in page: the classes, one backdrop, exactly the right elements inert and un-inert again, what was inert before staying so, and `canvas.js` and `module.js` doing their parts) and `tools/check-module-window.mjs` (the SDK's asks around `showModal()` and `close`, `sdk-editor-lifted` filling the frame, and `style.css`'s block held to the editor's sizes, the backdrop, the narrow-layout sheet without `inset: 0` and the tokens), each run again for this entry. **Verified live** by the builder in Chromium at 1280 and 390 px with To-do switched to run in a frame by the admin: docked, floating, on its own page and popped out. **Not checked:** a real call, Firefox, Safari, a screen reader. Quality-assurance's result is to be added.
- **The Planner asks what a thing is in one field** (GitHub #193, `plan-editor-window.md`, steps 9 and 10 of 12). The Planner's Add and Edit form opens with one field at the top, **What is it**, showing the chosen kind's icon in its colour and its name, where there was a grid of about thirty tiles taking most of the form. Click it, press Down or start typing and the list opens: the kinds in their groups, **Getting there**, **Stay**, **Eat and drink**, **See and do**, **Markers** and **Other**, with **Recent** first once you have chosen something in this browser (up to four). Typing filters as you go: "fl" leaves Flight, and "plane" finds Flight too. Up and Down move, Enter or Tab chooses, Escape closes only the list; the list says "Nothing matches" when nothing does. In the narrow layout the list opens as a sheet over the lower half of the screen. A new object starts on the kind you chose last in this browser, else Sight, with the field focused so typing starts the search; an existing object opens on its own kind with focus on **Title**, as does one opened from a day's "..." or the + between days. Markers are one entry per type in the one **Markers** group (the **Time** and **Between days** groups are gone); **When** (the field the plan calls Where) decides whether the marker is in a day (with **Time** and **How long**) or between days (no time), and with a marker chosen **When** has no "Not on a day yet" (an existing marker saved with no day keeps it). When the list is filtered, kinds whose name starts with what you typed come first, then kinds matched by another word. The kind an object is saved with becomes recent, also when the bar, a day's "..." or a joint's + chose it. A kind that is no longer in the list (a marker type an owner removed) shows an empty field reading "Unknown kind", never another kind, and saving keeps the stored type unless another kind is chosen. What is stored is unchanged.
  - **For module authors:** `host.ui.kindPicker(el, { label, groups, value, start, recent, onChange })` draws the field into an element of your own and returns `{ value, set(id), setGroups(groups), remember(id), disabled, focus(), open(), close(), element, input, destroy() }`. `remember(id)` records a kind as recently used without choosing it; `setGroups()` that drops the chosen kind keeps it as the value, shows "Unknown kind" and calls `onChange(value)`; `recent.max` below 1 means 4; the chosen option's icon is `--on-accent` on its colour. Each option is `{ id, label, icon, color, words }`; `color` is any CSS colour, set as `--kind-color`. `recent: { key, max }` keeps the choices in this browser for your module (`localStorage` `app:kind:<moduleId>:<key>`) and `start: 'recent'` opens on the last one. Styles in `/sdk/host.css` between `kind:start` and `kind:end`. Documented in `api-module-sdk.md`, "Choosing a kind".
  - **Modules:** the Planner 0.15.1 (was 0.14.2). The Planner's `travel-lib.js` gains `TURNS`, `kindOf(tile)` and `tileAt(kind, where)`.
  - **Checked by a tool:** `tools/check-module-window.mjs` runs the picker's filtering, its recent list and the markup it builds (the combobox roles, the groups, the colours, "Nothing matches", the stored key) from a slice of `host.js`, and holds `host.css`'s block to the tokens; `tools/check-travel.mjs` checks the marker mapping (a day stores `block`, a joint `lane`, one kind for both), the one **Markers** group, the empty `#f-types` with no tiles left, and the focus rule. **Verified live** by the builder in Chromium at 1280 and 390 px on the Planner's own page; not on a space's canvas, not in a real call, not in Firefox, Safari or with a screen reader. **Quality-assurance:** the first round failed on a kind not in the list showing as Flight, a marker with no day losing its **When**, and the list not following a scroll; all three are fixed (the Planner 0.15.1), and the retest passed with notes.
- **Add and edit forms open in a window over the page** (GitHub #192, `plan-editor-window.md`, steps 1 to 8 of 12). In To-do, Calendar, Polls, Research, Places and the Planner, the form for adding or changing something no longer opens inside the module's own box, where a docked column or a small floating module squeezed it and made it scroll; it opens as a window over the whole page, 560 px wide (the Planner's object form 880 px, its plan form 560 px), and as a full-screen sheet at 640 px and below. A **Close** button sits in the form's top right corner, and **Save** and **Cancel** stay in view at the bottom while the form scrolls. Escape, **Close** and **Cancel** close at once when nothing changed; after typing they ask "Discard your changes?" with **Keep editing** and **Discard**. A click outside the form does nothing; the page behind waits, and the call's keys still work. Date pickers and menus opened from the form open inside it. When the form closes, focus goes back to what opened it, or to the row just saved. Something you may only read (a task, an event or a note from elsewhere) opens with no button row and takes no input at all: its fields, date pickers and type buttons are disabled, focus starts on the window itself, and Escape or the corner **Close** just closes it. In the Planner, after saving a new object, focus lands on it: its row's menu on a day, or its **Edit** button in a list. In Research and Places the row's button now reads **Cancel** (it read **Close**). In To-do, nothing can be dropped onto an open form any more (the page behind it waits); the link search stays. Moving a module between docked and floating keeps its form open. Documented in `userguide-modules.md` ("Add and edit forms") and each module's guide.
  - **For module authors:** `host.ui.editor(dialog, { size, isDirty, onClose })` on a `<dialog>` in the module's root returns `{ open({ focus, returnTo }), close(), cancel(), isOpen, element }`; mark the button row `sdk-editor-actions`. `cancel()` is what a module's own Cancel button calls, so it asks the same question as Escape and Close. `returnTo` is resolved a microtask after `onClose`, so a list redrawn after a save is the one searched. `host.menu.show`, `host.ui.datePicker` and `host.actions.pick` open inside an open editor window. `host.ui.datePicker` follows a disabled field: its button is disabled with the field and it won't open; call `refresh()` after toggling `disabled`. The new event `moved` (`{ mode }`) tells a module the host moved it between docked and floating; the SDK reopens its editor on it. The styles (`.sdk-editor`, `.sdk-editor-large`, `.sdk-editor-close`, `.sdk-editor-corner`, `.sdk-editor-actions`, `.sdk-editor-discard`, the `--sdk-editor-top` and `--sdk-editor-pad` properties) are in `/sdk/host.css`. A module in a sandboxed frame was unchanged by these steps: its dialog showed inside the frame until step 11 (the entry above). Documented in `api-module-sdk.md` ("An editor window"), `architecture-module-window.md` ("The editor window"), `architecture-modules.md` and `design-theme.md`.
  - **Modules:** To-do 1.15.2 (was 1.14.0), Calendar 1.24.2 (was 1.23.0), Polls 1.15.1 (was 1.14.0), Research 0.4.2 (was 0.3.0), Places 0.10.1 (was 0.9.1), the Planner 0.14.2 (was 0.13.2), Maps 0.8.2 (was 0.8.1).
  - **Fixed, found while building:** in Research, Places, the Planner and Maps, opening a form while its icons were still loading could freeze the page (each pending icon started another scan of the form, so a form with n pending icons scanned itself 2^n times). Pending icons are now waited for together, once.
  - **Checked by a tool:** `tools/check-module-window.mjs` (the six modules' `#editor` is a `<dialog class="sdk-editor">`, no overlay rule left, Cancel through `editor.cancel()`, icons waited for together, and the SDK's sizes, handle and thrown sentence run from a slice of `host.js`, with `host.css`'s widths and colours held to them) and `tools/check-travel.mjs` (the Planner's two sizes and its Cancel). **Verified live** by the builders in headless Chromium at 1280 px and 390 px, each module on its own page and on a space's canvas docked and floating: opening from every way in, save, delete, cancel, the question after typing and not before, a date picker and a menu inside, the narrow-layout sheet, and the form staying open across a move between docked and floating. QA's first round failed on a freeze and Cancel; the retest passed with notes, and those are fixed. **Not checked:** a real call with a form open (the keys and the inert call buttons), Firefox, Safari, a screen reader.
- **Look up a flight in the Planner** (GitHub #188, `plan-flight-lookup.md`). A flight's form in the Planner has a new row at the top, **Look up a flight**: enter the **Flight number** and the **Day it leaves**, choose **Find**, and if anyone on this server has saved that flight before, in any trip, the form is filled in: the departure day and time, the flight time, **Arrival on the ticket**, the airline, the number, both airports and the departure terminal. The title is filled only when empty, and the gate, seat, class, booking reference, cost, who paid, people and notes are never touched; nothing is saved until **Save**. Several matches give a "Which one?" list ("MDW 12:50 to SJC 15:25", "flies on this day of the week" or "last seen Oct 2026"). Nothing found says "No flight WN 2483 saved here yet. Fill it in, and it will be suggested next time." The **Return** of a round trip has its own row, **Look up the return flight**, and its own **Arrival on the ticket**, which the row fills. A second lookup replaces what the first filled (and empties a filled terminal when the new flight has none), but never what you typed. A flight number is saved cleaned ("wn2483" becomes "WN 2483"); one that can't be cleaned is saved as typed. Typing an airport code such as MDW and leaving the box fills an empty airport name ("Chicago Midway"), from a bundled list of 7,918 airports; a four-letter code such as KMDW fills the name and becomes MDW. Nothing outside the server is asked and there is no key to set: the server learns each flight's schedule when someone saves it, and a flight nobody has saved can't be looked up (fill it by hand, or bring in the booking through Chat's `/ai` or **Bring in research**). The Planner's new setting **Suggest flights from earlier trips** (on by default) turns the lookup off and keeps the environment's flights out; typed codes still fill names. It is one choice for the whole environment: with it off, no module there suggests or keeps flights. The admin sees how many flights the server has learned, and can forget one or **Clear all**, in a new **Flight schedule** section: on Manage's **Modules** tab on a single server, on the host console's **Host** tab on a server with environments.
  - **What is kept:** one schedule for the whole server, `flight-schedule.json` in the root `DATA_DIR` (beside `host.json` on a hosted server; never in an environment's folder, export or backup, and left at the root when a single install becomes the first environment). Per flight number, up to 5 schedules of the airline, the two airport codes, the local departure and arrival times, the days between, the flight time, the terminal, the weekdays seen and the last day seen; at most 20,000 numbers. A save is kept only if it departs no more than 400 days ahead and no more than 3 years back; an older save of a known schedule only adds its weekday and never overwrites a newer airline, terminal, days or minutes. Never who saved it, which environment or space, anyone's name, the seat, class, reference, cost, who paid, the people, the notes, the title or the gate. An answer gives the last day seen as a month only. A file that is not a schedule is moved aside as `flight-schedule.json.bad-<time>` (mode 600) and the schedule starts empty; a file over 40 MB is left as it is and nothing new is kept until **Clear all** replaces it.
  - **API:** `GET /api/modules/:id/flight-lookup` (`{ available }`; `?number=&date=` gives `{ flights }`, 400 `bad number` or `bad date`, 429 over the `search` limit; `?code=` gives `{ airport }` or 404 "There is no airport with that code."), `POST /api/modules/:id/flight-lookup/remember` (`{ object }`, always 204), for a module that declares `"lookups": ["flight"]` (404 "This module has no flight lookup." otherwise) and only with its `write` access, so never a guest. `GET` and `DELETE /api/host/flight-schedule` (host admins) and `/api/flight-schedule` (owners, single install), each 404 on the other kind of install; `DELETE ?number=` forgets one number (400 and 404 "No flight BA 117 is saved on this server."); `DELETE` with no number answers 500 "The flight schedule could not be cleared; the server log says why." when an oversized or unreadable file can't be replaced.
  - **For module authors:** the manifest's `lookups` and `host.lookup.flight(number, date)`, `host.lookup.airport(code)`, `host.lookup.available('flight')` and `host.lookup.remember('flight', object)`.
  - **Bundled data:** `server/airports.json`, built by the new `tools/build-airports.mjs` from mwgg/Airports at a pinned commit (MIT; the notice is `server/airports-LICENSE`).
  - **Modules:** the Planner 0.13.2 (was 0.12.0).
  - **Fixed, from quality-assurance's notes:** a round trip's **Return** now has its own **Arrival on the ticket**, with the same limit as the outbound's ("The return's arrival on the ticket can be at most 7 days after it leaves, and at most a day before."), so a return across time zones shows the right arrival; this closes the entry in `known-issues.md`. On the server: a save too far ahead or back is not kept, and when the file is read a last-seen day too far ahead is clamped and one too old is dropped; an older save no longer overwrites a newer schedule's fields; a corrupt file is moved aside instead of overwritten; an oversized file is read-only until cleared; the not-found sentence reads "No flight BA 117 is saved on this server."; and the setting is read across every module that declares the lookup. In the Planner: the saved number is the cleaned form; a second lookup replaces an earlier lookup's values and never typed ones; an ICAO code fills the name and shows as its IATA code; remembering a flight no longer sends typed airport names.
  - Documented in `api-module-sdk.md`, `api-modules.md`, `architecture-modules.md`, `architecture-environments.md`, `userguide-planner.md`, `userguide-modules.md`, `userguide-getting-started.md`, the README's credits and the plan's "Built" note. Verified live by the builders in Chromium, on a single-environment and a hosted install, the fixes included. Checked by the new `tools/check-flight-lookup.mjs` (37 checks, added to `npm run check`) and `tools/check-travel.mjs` (92 checks), run again for this entry. Quality-assurance: passed with notes; the notes are the fixes above. Not verified in Firefox or Safari, or with a real screen reader.
- **Favorite layouts** (GitHub #190, `plan-favorite-layouts.md`). Make a saved layout a favorite and it gets a button in the space bar, right of **Layout**, that loads it in one press. Every signed-in person now has a **⋮** on every layout row in **Layout ▾**, shared ones included; its first entry is **Favorite** (an outline heart) or **Unfavorite** (a filled heart), and the panel says "<name> is a favorite." or "<name> is no longer a favorite.". A favorite's row shows a small heart after its name. Favorites are each person's own, per space, kept on the server so they follow you to another computer, up to 3 per space. With 3, **Favorite** on another layout is greyed out with "You can have 3 favorite layouts. Unfavorite one first."; **Unfavorite** always works. Each button has a heart and the name, shown in full (cut at about 16 characters) whenever there is room, with the tooltip "Load <name>", in the order favorited. Only when the bar can't hold them all do the names shorten, evenly, down to about 6 characters, and only then does the last favorite leave the bar; at 1280 pixels and wider 3 full names show, and around 900 pixels they shorten. Favorites that leave the bar stay in **Layout ▾**, marked by their hearts, and there is no count of them. They give way before Online and never fold into the **…**. A favorite that leaves a module out says so under the favorites for 6 seconds, and to a screen reader. Not shown at 640 pixels and below, in an aside, on a narrow canvas, with no favorites, or to a guest; guests get no **⋮** and can't keep favorites. Deleting a layout takes it out of everyone's favorites, and removing a person drops theirs.
  - API: `GET /api/spaces/:id/layouts` adds `favorites`, the person's layout ids in the order favorited (`[]` for a guest). New `PUT` and `DELETE /api/spaces/:id/layouts/:layoutId/favorite`, both answering 200 `{ favorites }` and both a 200 that changes nothing when repeated; a 4th favorite in a space gets 409 "You can have 3 favorite layouts. Unfavorite one first." and changes nothing; 401 "Sign in first.", 404 for no such space or an aside, 403 "You are not in that space.", 403 for a guest ("Guests can load shared layouts but not keep favorites."), 404 "There is no such layout here.". Stored in `layouts.json` as `spaces[id].favorites = { "<user key>": [layout id] }`, written only when not empty; a file without it reads as before, and nothing is migrated. A list holds at most 3 ids. A stored list that is longer, or that names layouts the person can't see, reads as its first 3 visible ids and is trimmed at the next write; a space entry that holds only favorites naming nothing left is dropped.
  - For developers: the nav registry gains a `fitFirst` flag for a left-zone tool with `fit` (the favorites give way before Online) and the pure helper `labelsThatFit()` in `public/nav-bar.js`. Both are host internals; no SDK change, no module changed.
  - Documented in `api-modules.md` ("Saved layouts"), `architecture-navigation.md` (the new "Favorite layouts" and the fold), `userguide-call.md` (the space bar and the new "Favorite layouts") and the plan's "Built" note. Verified live by the builder in Chromium as a member, an owner and a guest, at widths from 1600 down to 390 pixels, with 0, 3 and 20 favorites; after Thomas's change of 2026-10-04 (decisions 12 and 13: the limit of 3, and names in full until the bar is out of room), the limit and the bar were verified live in Chromium by the builder again. Checked by `tools/check-layouts.mjs` (63 checks) and `tools/check-nav.mjs` (65 tests), run again for this entry. Quality-assurance: passed with notes; the notes (the server now prunes favorite ids the person can't see, and drops a space entry that holds only favorites) are fixed. Not verified in a real call, with a real screen reader, or in Firefox or Safari.
- **Calendar sharing is set up in the Calendar's own configuration** (GitHub #179, steps 0 to 2 of `plan-space-calendars.md`). The Calendar's **Module Configuration** button on Manage's Modules tab is no longer greyed out. Its page has a **Calendar sharing** section with two switches, each saved as it is flipped: **Private addresses** (the old **Calendar feeds**, same setting) and **Other calendars and busy times** (new; until an owner sets it, it follows Private addresses, so nothing changes in an existing environment). A third, **Published calendar**, is stored but hidden until its address is built in step 4. A **Top bar** section holds **Show in the top bar**, on the Calendar's page and on Maps'. Manage's Environment tab loses its **Top bar** and **Calendar apps** sections; one line, "Calendar sharing is set in Calendar's configuration.", links to the new place. A switch waiting on an update is off and greyed with "Approve Calendar's update in Manage > Modules first." and **Go to Modules**, for owners and the admin. A member who opens a module's Configure page sees "Only owners can change this." rather than being sent round a sign-in loop; only a signed-out person is sent to sign in. A new Travel environment starts with Private addresses and Other calendars and busy times on.
