  - **Fixed on Profile:** **Other calendars** reads its state again when you come back to the page, so an approval or switch made in Manage (in another tab, or before going back) shows the form without a reload; a read that fails says why ("Your other calendars could not be loaded (<reason>). Reload the page to try again."). Its reason now also covers a Calendar that is on but can't run: outdated (with the update to install, or needing its author), or something it requires outdated, off or missing. When the Calendar also has to be installed, updated or approved and the switch is off, the reason names that step first, then the switch. The Calendar feed line now reads "Google can take 8 to 24 hours to show a change."; with Private addresses off and an address kept, it reads "Private addresses are off in <environment> for now, so this address does not work.", and it too is read again when you come back to the page. A read again keeps each other calendar's row and the focus on it. A load that fails says "the server didn't answer" rather than the browser's wording, here and on the Configure page.
  - **API:** `GET` and `PATCH /api/settings` gain `otherCalendars` (answered as `calendarFeeds` until set) and `publishedCalendar` (default `false`); a value that is not a boolean gets 400 "<key> is true or false". `calendarFeeds` now governs only the addresses out; other calendars (`POST /api/me/external-calendars`, its refresh, `GET /api/modules/:id/external-events` and the stream's `external` event) follow `otherCalendars`, and the refusal is now 403 "Other calendars are off in this <environment>." (it was "Calendar feeds are off in this <environment>."). `GET /api/modules` gives each module `sharing` and `sharingReasons`. `sharingReasons`' reason is "Approve <module>'s update in Manage > Modules first.". `GET /api/me/external-calendars`' `why` has new sentences for the switch off and for a module that can't run, and names an install, update or approval before the switch's sentences. Templates may give `otherCalendars` and `publishedCalendar`; the Travel template is version 5. Nothing stored is rewritten; `#set-calendar-feeds` keeps its id on the new page. No module changed, so no module version bumps.
  - Documented in `api-modules.md`, `api-module-sdk.md`, `architecture-modules.md`, `architecture-environments.md`, `userguide-calendar.md` (the new "Calendar sharing (owner)"), `userguide-accounts.md`, `userguide-environment-settings.md`, `userguide-modules.md`, `userguide-templates.md`, `userguide-maps.md`, `userguide-places.md`, `userguide-todo.md`, `userguide-spaces.md` and the plan's "Progress", which lists where the build differs from the plan. The builders verified steps 0 to 2 live where their reports note it; the rest is checked by tool: `tools/check-feed.mjs` (22 groups), `tools/check-external-calendars.mjs` (29 checks, including Profile's section run as code against a stand-in page and server), `tools/check-templates.mjs` (34 groups) and `tools/check-nav.mjs` (63 tests), each run again for this entry and passing. QA passed with notes; its findings are fixed. The builders verified the fixes live in Chromium; a real outside calendar and a real approval-pending reason were not tried. No real calendar app or Google account was involved.
- **Tasks due and polls closing on the Calendar** (GitHub #167, `plan-calendar-markers.md`). The Calendar shows To-do's tasks on their due day and Polls' polls at their closing time as markers: small chips with the module's icon and colour that point back to the task or poll, never Calendar events and never copies. A task sits in the all-day row (Week, Day) or on its day (Month), and in the Agenda reads **Due**, or **Overdue** and dimmed once the day has passed; ticking it done takes it off. A poll reads "Closes 6:00 PM: Where for dinner?", and after its time stays, dimmed, as "Closed 6:00 PM: …" (a kind due at a time would read "Due 6:00 PM: …"); a poll closed by hand has no marker. Clicking a marker opens the task or poll in its own module (Ctrl or Cmd for a new tab); it can't be dragged, changed or deleted on the Calendar. A space's Calendar shows that space's; the environment's calendar and Calendar in the top bar (`/calendar`) show the environment's and every space the person is a member of, following the space switches, and only what the person can already see. Each kind has a switch, on by default, once the Calendar has shown one: **Tasks due** and **Polls closing** in the filter of Calendar in the top bar (remembered; while one is off the label adds "· Tasks due off"), in the row under the toolbar on the environment's calendar (not remembered), and in a menu under a **Show** button in a space's Calendar toolbar (remembered per space in this browser, `localStorage` `calendar-markers-off:<spaceId>`). The switch's words come from the kind. Guests in a space see the markers with their icons. Markers are not in calendar feeds or reminders. This changes `plan-calendar-destination.md` decision 15: tasks are now on the grid as well as in the To-do panel. Calendar 1.22.1, To-do 1.13.0, Polls 1.13.0 (Polls' summary now carries `done`, true for a poll closed by hand).
  - API: `"marker": true` beside `"dated"` on a `refs.produces[]` kind, refused with "marker must be true or false", "marker needs \"dated\"", "marker can't be used with \"mirror\"" and "... with \"feed\""; a `dated` instant's `start` and `end` may be milliseconds; `GET /api/modules/:id/markers?from=&to=[&scope=space&space=]` returns `{ markers: [{ ref, kind, title, start, allDay, due, past, module: { id, name, icon, color, svg } }] }` (`svg` the icon as inline SVG), at most 92 days counted to the end of a date-only `to`, days that don't exist (2026-02-30) refused, at most 500, done objects left out, read with the viewer's rights at each request through the asking module's approved `consumes`; nothing is written; the `markers` stream event `{ modules }`. `GET /api/modules/:id/external-events` now also refuses a `from` or `to` that doesn't exist. Every status and sentence is in `api-modules.md` ("Calendar markers").
  - **For module authors:** `host.objects.markers({ from, to })` and `host.on('markers', fn)`; the `toolbar` event gains `x` (a button's left edge, for a menu under it) and `host.ui.toolbarButton`'s `onClick` receives the event; the Calendar destination's state gains `markerKinds` (set by the `main` part) and `markersOff`.
  - Documented in `api-modules.md`, `api-module-sdk.md`, `architecture-modules.md`, `userguide-calendar.md`, `userguide-todo.md` and `userguide-polls.md`. Checked by the new `tools/check-markers.mjs` (14 groups: the manifest refusals, a number `start`, done objects left out, space A and B and a guest, an unapproved consumer, the 92-day limit, nothing written and no twin, the 500 cap) and new cases in `check-modules` and `check-module-host`, with `check-module-versions` and `check-feed`, each run again for this entry. Walked by quality-assurance in headless Chromium (month, week, day and agenda; a space popout, `/calendar` and the environment page; narrow width; a guest), and QA's fixes (colour, the Show menu's place, the per-space memory, guests' icons, the words, the date checks) checked again in a browser. No LiveKit call is involved.
- **Groundwork: objects can carry their details** (GitHub #182, step 1 of `plan-object-handoff.md`). Nothing changes on screen yet. The objects format, which an AI or a paste uses to hand things to Collaborator, can now say what a thing is in fields as well as words, so a later step can fill a Planner flight's airline, airports and times instead of putting the whole text in Notes.
  - The format has 18 kinds: the 13 travel kinds plus `event`, `task`, `poll`, `note` and `link`. `image` is kept only in a handoff between pages, never from an import or an AI's answer.
  - Each kind may carry an optional `details` object with its own fields (a flight's `airline`, `number`, `from` and `to`, `departs` and `arrives`, `terminal`, `gate`, `seat`, `reference`, and so on; the full list is in `api-modules.md`). Dates and times are local, `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM`, `YYYY-MM-DD` or `HH:MM`, with no zone: the reader drops a zone and seconds and keeps the clock time.
  - The reading is loose: a bad field is dropped on its own, a whole number in a text field is kept as text, text fields lose their HTML and Markdown, and `content` may be left out when the details keep at least one field.
  - The format stays version 1, and old files and answers read exactly as before. `GET /api/objects/format/schema` gains a `details-<kind>` definition for each kind, and an object now requires only `title` (with `content`, or `kind` and `details`).
  - Not yet: the instructions and `/ai`'s rule still name only the travel kinds (step 3); Chat's **Keep** still sends every non-travel kind to Research's note keeper (`keeperFor`), until **Send to...** (step 9); and no module fills its fields from `details` yet (steps 4 to 7). No module changed, so no version bumps.
  - Documented in `api-modules.md` ("The objects format") and `architecture-modules.md`. Checked by `tools/check-object-format.mjs` (11 new cases, 34 in all: every kind's fields, each bad value dropped, the three `when` shapes with zones dropped, `point` from a string and an object, details without content, `image` only in a handoff, Thomas's Southwest flight as a block and a file, the schema against the catalogue, and old files and answers read as before) and `tools/check-chat-page.mjs` (24 tests, with the new `keeperFor` case), each run again for this entry; not tried in a browser or with an outside AI.
- **A better prompt for bringing in plans from another AI, and modules that say what they take** (GitHub #182, steps 2 and 3 of `plan-object-handoff.md`). Partly groundwork. What changes for a person is the prompt: **Copy instructions for another AI** in Chat, and the rule `/ai` gives its own AI, now ask for a whole itinerary as one object per flight, train, stay, meal, visit and event, each with its date and times; tell the AI to copy dates and never guess them, and to ask for a missing one (`/ai`, which can't ask, leaves it out and says which dates it needs); ask for local 24-hour times with no time zone; and give one line of details per kind (a flight's airline, number, airports, times, seat and booking reference; `hotel` covers any stay). They list only the kinds the modules on in the environment can take. With the Planner on, those are the 15 kinds it takes (see the next entry, steps 4 and 5).
  - **Which kinds the prompt lists:** the kinds enabled modules name in their actions' `takes`, in the catalogue's order, plus the 13 travel kinds while any enabled action is the older typed keeper (`acceptSuggestion` without `takes`). With every bundled module on today, that is the 15 kinds the Planner 0.12.0 declares: the 13 travel kinds, `event` and `note`, each with its details line, about 3,500 characters copied. The travel fallback no longer applies with the bundled Planner on; it covers an older Planner, or another module still offering `acceptSuggestion` without `takes`. With no kind listed (no Planner and no module declaring `takes`), the instructions name no kinds. With every kind listed the copied instructions are about 3,600 characters. The last line now reads "Leave out tags, place and links when you have nothing for them, and content when "details" says it all." `GET /api/objects/format`'s `schema` is still the whole catalogue.
  - **For module authors:** an action in `module.json` may declare `takes`, 1 to 10 entries of `{ kinds, except?, as, permission? }`: catalogue kinds, `"*"` or `"text"`; `except` only beside `"*"`; `"*"` never covers `image`; `as` 1 to 40 characters ("Add to <module> as …"); `permission` one of the module's own. An action with `takes` needs exactly one input of the new type `object`, and an `object` input needs `takes`; install refuses anything else with a sentence naming the action. The bus cleans an `object` input with the format's checker (`cleanHandoff`) and refuses with 400 "object must be an object", "object needs a title, and content or details", "<module> cannot take a flight", "object is a picture, so its details.upload must be the id of a file uploaded to the module", or 403 "you may not add a poll to <module> here". `GET /api/spaces/:id/actions` and `GET /api/bus/actions` return `takes` with `may` on each entry, and an action's `may` is true when some entry's is; `GET /api/bus/actions` leaves out an action whose every entry is refused. `except` settles the plan's open question on the Calendar's kinds.
  - No module changed in steps 2 and 3. The first bundled module to declare `takes` is the Planner, 0.12.0, in the next entry.
  - Documented in `api-modules.md` (the manifest's `takes`, the bus and Chat routes, "The instructions" and the new "Handing an object to a module"), `api-module-sdk.md`, `architecture-modules.md`, `userguide-chat.md` and the plan's progress notes. Checked by the new `tools/check-object-handoff.mjs` (10 checks: which entry takes which kind, the bus's object, the prompt's kinds for different sets of modules, and, on a throwaway server with stand-in modules, `takes` and `may` on the actions list, every refusal above through `POST /api/spaces/:id/action` and `POST /api/bus/actions/request`, the 413, the copied instructions following the enabled modules, and a request queued before `takes` still running), and new cases in `check-modules` (29 groups), `check-ai` (14 groups) and `check-object-format` (35 checks), each run again for this entry. Not tried in a browser or with an outside AI.
- **Bookings kept from Chat fill the Planner's own fields** (GitHub #182, steps 4 and 5 of `plan-object-handoff.md`). A flight, train, bus, ferry, car, stay, meal, visit or event kept from Chat (an `/ai` answer, or research brought in from another AI) now arrives in the Planner with its fields filled instead of everything in Notes: the airline or operator, the number, the airports or stations, the departure and arrival times, terminal, gate, seat, class and booking reference; a car's company, pick-up and drop-off; a stay's check-in and check-out, room type and guests; a meal's party size and booking name; a visit's tickets; a length from a start and an end. Anything without a field of its own goes into Notes as a "Label: value" line after the text ("Cabin: 4B"). Titles and details lose their Markdown; Notes keep it, since the Planner draws them. Planner 0.12.0.
  - **Arrival on the ticket.** Flights, trains, ferries and buses have a new editor field, **Arrival on the ticket** (a day and a time). When it is set, the plan shows that arrival, with "the day before" when it lands on an earlier day across the date line, and the flight time stays as given rather than being used to work the arrival out; the form's worked-out "Arrives" line is hidden. Without it, the arrival is worked out from the departure and the length as before. It can be at most 7 days after the departure and at most a day before; the editor refuses anything else with "The arrival on the ticket can be at most 7 days after it leaves, and at most a day before.", and one handed in or saved outside that becomes a notes line, "Arrives: 2026-11-14 15:25". Moving the journey to another day (dragging it, **Move to**, changing its day in the editor, or dropping it on a day) moves a dated ticket arrival by the same number of days. A journey with no day (under **Not on a day yet** or on the line) keeps its arrival relative to the day it leaves, stored as a time with an offset of -1 to +7 days (`"06:30+1"`), and the arrival is dated again on whatever day the journey goes back on; in the editor, picking a day fills **Arrival on the ticket**. Stored as `arrives` (a local day and time, or a time alone; not for a car); plans saved before read unchanged.
  - **Seat** now shows in the editor for buses and ferries (up to 30 characters), and **Class** for trains, so a kept seat or class is no longer lost when the object is edited.
  - **Dates far off.** An object handed to the Planner (kept from Chat, or asked for by another module) dated more than 60 days from the rest of the plan goes under **Not on a day yet**, with "Dated <day>, outside the plan" at the top of its notes, and the Planner says "<title> is dated <day>, outside the plan's 60 days, so it is under Not on a day yet." There, its row reads "Dated <day>, outside the plan", and the editor's **When** keeps the day as a choice of its own ("<day>, outside the plan") so saving doesn't lose it. A day within the 60 still stretches the plan, as adding by hand does. A stay's check-out, or a journey's dated arrival, that would take the plan past 60 days, with or without the trip's dates set, is kept in its notes instead ("Check out: 2027-01-30 10:00", or "Arrives: …"), the object stays on its own day, and the Planner says "<title>: its check-out, <day>, is outside the plan's 60 days, so it is in its notes." ("its arrival" for a journey).
  - **For module authors:** `host.util.plain(text, { line })` turns Markdown and HTML into plain text, and `host.util.localWhen(when, day)` reads a `when` as `{ date, time }`; both are also on `window.hostText`. The Planner's `acceptSuggestion` gains an optional `object` input and declares `takes`: flights, trains, buses, ferries and cars as "{kind}", hotels as "a stay", restaurants, cafes, bars, sights, museums, tours, shows and events as "{kind}", and notes as "a note". Older requests with the flat fields run as before. Chat's **Keep** sends the object itself, details and all, to a keeper whose `takes` covers its kind, and the older flat fields, details as "Label: value" lines, to one that doesn't. Which keeper Keep picks hasn't changed: a travel kind goes to the Planner, and every other kind, `event` and `note` included, to Research's note keeper, until **Send to...** (step 9). With the Planner on, the copied instructions and `/ai` now list its 15 kinds.
  - Documented in `api-module-sdk.md` (`host.util.plain`, `host.util.localWhen`), `api-modules.md` ("The Planner's `acceptSuggestion`"), `userguide-planner.md`, `userguide-chat.md`, `architecture-modules.md` and the plan's progress notes. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs` (81 checks in all: `plain` on Markdown and HTML and in linear time on 100 KB crafted texts, `localWhen`'s formats and refusals, Thomas's Southwest flight, each kind, the drop's day over the object's, a 10-object itinerary over 4 days landing on its days, an object 90 days out and an older flat request going under Not on a day yet with the note said, a day within 60 stretching the plan, a check-out or arrival past 60 days kept as a notes line, the arrival's 7-days-after and 1-day-before limit, a dated arrival moving with its journey and keeping its offset with no day, an unclosed `<script>` in `plain`, and the page's new fields as text) and `tools/check-chat-page.mjs` (26 tests; the new one checks Keep's object input against the Planner's and Research's manifests and the server's `takersOf`), with `check-module-versions`, `check-object-handoff`, `check-ai`, `check-object-format`, `check-modules` and `check-syntax`, each run again for this entry. `check-syntax` now also parses each module page with its library spliced in, as the build does, so a name declared twice across them fails it. Verified live by the builder: the Planner's page loading, `acceptSuggestion` adding objects, and the editor opened on an undated object. Everything else (the other editor paths, the arrival moving or keeping its offset, Seat and Class, the **When** choice, the Planner's note) was checked by tool only; not tried with an outside AI.
- **Research, To-do, the Calendar and Polls take whole objects** (GitHub #182, steps 6 and 7 of `plan-object-handoff.md`). What changes for a person today is Chat's **Keep**: an event, task, poll, note or link kept from an `/ai` answer, or from research brought in from another AI, becomes a Research note that keeps the object's tags, its day and its place on the map, with an icon to match its kind, and its details as lines after the text ("Starts: 2026-11-14 19:00"); a note kept from `/ai` still carries the question as an "Asked:" line after its text. To-do, the Calendar and Polls can now be handed an object by any module, and will be offered by **Send to...** (step 9); nothing in Chat sends to them yet. The copied instructions and `/ai` now list all 18 kinds with every bundled module on (`task` with To-do, `poll` with Polls, `link` with Research), about 3,600 characters. Research 0.3.0, To-do 1.14.0, Calendar 1.23.0, Polls 1.14.0.
  - **Research:** `saveNote` takes any kind and a message's words, as "a note"; `saveLink` takes a link, as "a link", with `url` still required; the new `savePhoto` takes a picture already uploaded to Research's own uploads in this space and saves a photo captioned with the file's name ("IMG_2041.jpg" is "IMG 2041"), with a thumbnail. A thumbnail the picture already has is reused; otherwise only the uploader's, an owner's or an admin's Research page makes one, and on anyone else's the photo is saved without one and its card shows the whole picture.
  - **To-do:** `createTask` takes `due` and an object, as "a task". A task is due on its own day, and a time on `due` becomes the notes line "Due at 3:00 PM"; any other kind becomes a task named after it with no due date. A flat `due` wins over the object's day. To-do's rules honour an action's `needs`, so the Calendar's **Add it to the calendar** is offered only for a result with a date.
  - **Calendar:** `createEvent` takes an object, as "an event", but not the travel kinds, which reach the Calendar through the Planner. An event with a start time becomes a timed event, ending at its end time or after its length, read in the time zone of the browser whose Calendar carries the request out; anything else is all day on its day. Its `date` is now optional, with `needs: ["date"]`, and the object's day wins over it; with no day at all it is refused: "that needs a day to go on the calendar".
  - **Polls:** the new **Draft a poll from it** (`draftPoll`, local) takes a poll or a message's words, with the **create** permission, so only people who may start polls have it. It opens the New poll form filled in (the question, the options from the poll's options or from the message's short lines, the closing time, **More than one answer**) and saves nothing until **Start poll**. The object's other text is not used.
  - **For module authors:** `host.util.detailLines(details, { kind, skip })` returns details as "Label: value" lines in the Planner's words (also on `window.hostText`). The `takes` of every bundled module, each action's inputs, mapping and refusals are in `api-modules.md`, "What the bundled modules take".
  - Documented in `api-modules.md`, `api-module-sdk.md` (`detailLines`, the `markdown` fix), `architecture-modules.md`, `userguide-research.md`, `userguide-todo.md`, `userguide-calendar.md`, `userguide-polls.md`, `userguide-chat.md` and the plan's progress notes. Verified live in Chromium by the builder: Research, To-do, the Calendar and the Polls form. Everything else was checked by tool: `tools/check-object-handoff.mjs` (14 checks: the four modules' `takes` installing, and To-do's, the Calendar's and Polls' mapping), `tools/check-research.mjs` (7 checks: `objectNote`, `objectLink`, and `saveNote`, `saveLink` and `savePhoto` with an object), `tools/check-travel.mjs` (83 checks, with `detailLines` and the `markdown` fix), `tools/check-chat-page.mjs` (26 tests, Keep's "Asked:" line), `check-modules` (29 groups), `check-module-versions` and `check-syntax` (120 files, 9 module pages with their library), each run again for this entry. The photo's thumbnail on another person's page, and the Calendar's time zone across browsers, were not tried.
- **Choose where each object is kept, and give it a day** (GitHub #182, step 8 of `plan-object-handoff.md`). In Chat's **Brought in** preview, each object now shows its title as plain text, a line of its details ("Southwest 1234 · MDW 12:50 → SJC 15:25 · ABC123") and **Keep** followed by where it goes, "in Planner". When more than one module can keep it, that is a menu of **Add to <module> as <a kind>** ("Add to To-do as a task"); the choice is remembered for that kind of object in this browser and marked **Last used**. A flight, train, bus, ferry, car, stay, meal, visit, event or task with no day shows **No day** and a date field (only where something here could keep it with a day; clicking the words focuses the field); with two or more, **Same day for all** fills every empty one and never changes a day you picked. **Keep ticked** keeps each object in the place its row shows, and the confirm names the counts and places ("Keep 3 flights and 2 stays in Planner, 1 task in To-do?"), then what it leaves out: a poll, which opens a form ("2 polls open a form each in Polls: use each one's own Keep."), one whose module is closed ("2 polls need Polls open."), and one nothing here can keep ("N can't be kept here."). An AI answer's objects get the same Keep and menu; there, choosing from the menu keeps the object at once. Keep runs one at a time; the button then reads **Kept**, or **Waiting**, and stays off, while for Polls it reads **Opened in Polls** and can be pressed again. A kept or waiting row shows its place as plain words, its day field is off and **Same day for all** skips it; **Keep ticked (n)** counts only the rows it would send; and the preview uses an up-to-date list of modules.
  - **Keep follows what modules take.** An object goes to any module whose `takes` covers its kind and that the person may use, with `except`, required inputs (a title, and `url` from the object's first link) and `needs` honoured, so the Calendar is left out for an object with no day. The default is the last choice for that kind, then the module that takes the kind by name and names the fewest kinds, whatever the install order (a note to Research, an event with a day to the Calendar, a flight to the Planner), then one that takes any object, the note keeper first, so an object with no kind and a link with no address go to Research; a form (Polls' draft) is never the default for a kind it doesn't name. Thomas may still change this rule. Each row keeps the place it shows: "Last used" applies only when the preview is first drawn, and a row moves only when its place stops being allowed, when Keep says "<old> can't take this now. It goes in <new>: press Keep again." and Keep ticked's confirm starts "N have a new place: the one before is no longer allowed." A module without `takes` keeps the old rule. A closed local module (Polls) gives "Polls isn't open." and its menu entry says "Open Polls first".
  - **Server:** `GET /api/spaces/:id/actions` returns `needs` when an action declares it.
  - Documented in `userguide-chat.md` (the new "Where an object is kept"), `userguide-planner.md`, `userguide-research.md`, `userguide-todo.md`, `userguide-calendar.md`, `userguide-polls.md`, `api-modules.md` ("Chat's Keep", the space actions list) and the plan's progress notes. Verified live in Chromium by the builder, including the rework (the specialist default, each row keeping its place, what Keep ticked leaves out, Keep's states and the day field); the focus after a pick in an AI answer's menu is read as code only. Checked by `tools/check-chat-page.mjs` (41 tests: the older rule, the specialist order, each row keeping its place, what Keep ticked leaves out, Keep by `takes` with Research off, alone and with the Planner, what each place is sent, the confirm's words, the details line and the day asked for) and `tools/check-object-handoff.mjs` (16 checks: `needs` on the actions list), each run again for this entry.
- **Send a chat message to a module** (GitHub #181 and #182, step 9 of `plan-object-handoff.md`, its last build step). A message's **⋮** menu has **Send to...** when something on in the space can take it. It lists places such as **Research as an image**, **To-do as a task**, **Research as a link**, **Polls as a poll**, and, when the message names a day, **Calendar as an event** and **Planner as an event**; sent to the To-do, the day becomes the task's due date. Chat finds a day only in date-like words, and not in quoted lines: "on Friday", "next Friday", "Nov 14", "on 12/10", "12/10 at 7pm", "2026-11-14", "tomorrow"; it ignores "today" and "tonight", and a short weekday ("sat") unless it follows on, next or this. An AI answer with several objects shows one entry per module, such as **Planner (3)**, with a hint like "2 flights, 1 stay; 1 left out". The place last used for each kind of message (a picture, a link, words, objects) comes first, marked **Last used**, in this browser. On a private message, each entry's hint says "Everyone in this space will see it". A form module that is closed shows "Open Polls first" and sends nothing. For several polls, Polls' entry opens only the first ("Opens the first of 3"), and the note adds "The other 2: use each one's own Keep." ("The other one: use its own Keep." for one). When a form opens, Polls is brought forward, and in the narrow layout it replaces Chat in view. Choosing a place sends at once, and Chat's note line, a polite live region so a screen reader can read it out, says how it went: "Added to X as Y." with the module's own note, "X is adding it." while it is under way, "Waiting for X to open it." while a form is on its way, then "Opened in X." (and X is brought forward) or "X isn't open." (Chat keeps following a form for up to 70 seconds), "X couldn't add that: …", "Waiting: it is added when X is next open." or "Sent to X."; **Keep** and **Keep ticked** use the same words, and a Keep row reads **Adding** while it is under way. A picture is uploaded into the module's own uploads first, as the sender, and a GIF or any type but JPEG, PNG or WebP is sent as a JPEG of its first frame. One that can't be sent reads "That picture can't be sent to X.", and when the module refuses it, the upload is deleted again unless the picture is already a photo there or in use.
  - **Server:** `GET /api/spaces/:id/action/:requestId` returns `{ id, status }` (`pending`, `claimed`, `done` or `expired`), plus `ok`, `error` and `note` when done, each one line of at most 300 characters, for 10 minutes after it finished. It needs `chatRead`; only the person who asked may read it (anyone else gets 404 "no such request"), guests get 403 "Guests can't see how a request went", and a bad id gets 400 "a request id is a whole number". A `local` action asked through `POST /api/spaces/:id/action` expires after 60 seconds if no page claims it and never opens later; `/command` still waits, on purpose. The bus's statuses gain `expired`, and its stored requests gain `expiresAt` and `doneAt`. `POST /api/spaces/:id/chat/:messageId/keep` returns the request's `id`. `POST /api/spaces/:id/action`, `/command` and the chat keep route together allow 60 requests a minute per person, across every module (a space's guests share one count; a refused request doesn't count): over it, 429 "too many requests in a minute, slow down". `POST /api/bus/actions/complete` answers `{ ok: false }` for an expired request unless it was claimed in time. **Upload sweep:** when an image request is dropped without ever running (seven days unclaimed, the bus's cap, or expired), its picture is removed, only when it is in the request's own place, was uploaded by the person who asked and isn't named in the module's data; the removal shows in the activity list.
  - **For page authors:** the canvas's `show(id)` opens a module or brings it forward; Keep's places carry `as`. Chat's day reader is its own (`chatDay` in `public/chat-input.js`); `host.util.parseWhen` is unchanged and isn't on `window.hostText`.
  - **Where the build differs from the plan:** entries read "<module> as <what>" rather than "Add to <module> as <what>"; words with a day go to the Planner as an event, since the Planner's `takes` has no `"text"`; the message-to-object cases are in `check-chat-page`, not `check-one-input`. A local request can only be claimed within 60 seconds, so Chat's 70 seconds of following covers it.
  - Documented in `userguide-chat.md` (the new "Send a message to a module"), `api-modules.md` (the new route, the 60-second limit, `expired`, the sweep, the keep route's `id`, the 60-a-minute limit), `architecture-modules.md` and the plan's progress notes. Verified live in Chromium by the builder at 1280 and 390 pixels wide, including these fixes after QA: the day reader, the To-do due date, late forms, several polls, the private hint and the live region's markup. Checked by tool or read as code only, not seen live: deleting a picture's upload when the module refuses it, "That picture can't be sent to X.", "X is adding it." and **Adding**, the 60-a-minute limit and `complete`'s refusal of an expired request. Guests, and a picture received from someone else over a real call, are read as code only; there is no LiveKit server here. No screen reader was used: the live region is there, but nothing was heard. Checked by `tools/check-chat-page.mjs` (48 tests: the message-to-object rules and `chatDay`, the entries and their hints, the outcome sentences, the due date) and `tools/check-object-handoff.mjs` (22 checks: the minute's expiry, `complete` after it, a request dropped after seven days, the route's reader rules, the 60-a-minute limit, and on a throwaway server a picture never added being swept), with `check-chat-links` (17), each run again for this entry.
- **Where Keep and Send to... put things by default, confirmed** (GitHub #182). Thomas confirmed the default-place rule on 2026-10-04: when several modules take a kind, the most specific wins, so a note goes to Research, an event to the Calendar and a flight to the Planner. His exception: an event that reads as travel goes to the Planner first, with the Calendar still in the menu. It reads as travel only by its title, since the server keeps only an event's starts, ends, allDay and address. The title must have, as a whole word in any case (Unicode-aware, so "Détours", "Trainée", "Training" and "Tourism" don't count), tour, excursion, shuttle, flight, train, ferry, cruise, bus, taxi, boat trip, day trip, road trip, rental car, car rental, car hire, or airport pickup, pick-up or drop-off; "coach" counts only before to, from, trip or ride, and "transfer" only as an airport, hotel, station or port transfer or "transfer to/from", so "Bank transfer due" and "Wire transfer" stay with the Calendar (`eventLooksLikeTravel` and `placeOrder` in `public/chat-input.js`). Known edges: "Flight of stairs", "Train the trainer", "Transfer to savings" and "Bus driver training" still count as travel; "cab" is left out on purpose. The prompt, when it lists both `event` and travel kinds, now adds: "For a booking or a stop on a trip, use its own kind (…) rather than "event", even when it happens at a set time; use "event" only for anything else on a day, such as an appointment or a party." The copied instructions with every kind listed are now about 3,900 characters, and the check's size budget was raised to match. Documented in `userguide-chat.md`, `api-modules.md` ("Chat's Keep", "The instructions"), `TODO.md` and the plan's step 8 note. Checked by tool: `tools/check-chat-page.mjs` (49 tests, which run the travel rule on the real `cleanObject` output) and `tools/check-object-format.mjs` (36 checks), each run again for this entry; the builder also ran the real `keepTargets` and `sendChoices` against a live server's actions list in Node. The menus and the import preview were not looked at in a browser.
- **Your other calendars in the Calendar** (GitHub #42 Part 2, `plan-google-calendar.md`; this completes #42). On the profile, under **Calendar feed**, **Other calendars** takes a **Name** and an **Address** ("In Google Calendar, open Settings, pick the calendar, choose Integrate calendar, and copy the Secret address in iCal format."; `webcal://` works), up to five. Adding reads the address first (up to 15 seconds) and refuses one that can't be read, with the reason. Rows show the name, the host and "Read <time ago>" or the error, with **Refresh** (once a minute) and **Remove** (confirmed); the address is never shown again. It shows while **Calendar feeds** is on and an enabled module has the approved `external` hook; with the switch off it shows only to people who still have calendars, with **Remove** only. Members and owners have it; guests don't. In the Calendar (its own page and `/calendar`) the person's own other-calendar events show muted and italic, with a coloured edge and a tag naming the calendar (hollow dots in the narrow layout's month); opening one says "From your <name> calendar. Only you see this." and it is read only. Each calendar has a filter entry, marked "(only you)". They never show inside a space (its canvas, a window popped out of it, a keyed page, or any page opened over a call, `/calendar` included) or in the dashboard card, and refresh every 30 minutes. The filter's label counts them apart: "<Spaces> (x of y) · Other calendars (a of b)", the second part only while one is off. A calendar too complex to read (more than 200,000 repeat periods, or, as a backstop, 1,500 ms) is refused: "The calendar is too complex to read." Manage > Modules lists the Calendar's new approval as "See each person's own other calendars — only to them". Calendar 1.21.0.
  - API: `/api/me/external-calendars` (`GET`, `POST`, `POST /:id/refresh`, `DELETE /:id`) and `GET /api/modules/:id/external-events?from=&to=`, with every status and sentence in `api-modules.md` ("Other calendars"); the `external` stream event. Addresses are stored as `externalCalendars` on the user, sealed with the secrets key; events are in memory only. Limits: https only, 5 MB, 15 seconds, 30 days back to 180 ahead, 2,000 events per calendar.
  - **For module authors:** the `hooks.external` hook (an owner approves it); `host.external.events({ from, to })` returns `{ calendars, events }`; `host.on('external', fn)`; the Calendar destination's state gains `externalOff`.
  - Documented in `api-modules.md`, `api-module-sdk.md`, `architecture-modules.md`, `userguide-accounts.md`, `userguide-calendar.md` and `userguide-environment-settings.md`. Checked by the new `tools/check-external-calendars.mjs` (24 checks) and new cases in `check-secrets`, `check-modules` and `check-module-host`, each run again for this entry; not tried with a real Google or Apple calendar.
- **Calendar feeds: your events in your own calendar app** (GitHub #42 Part 1, `plan-google-calendar.md`). An owner turns on **Calendar feeds** in Manage's new **Calendar apps** section (off by default: "Lets people add this environment's events to their own calendar app with a private address."). Members and owners then get **Calendar feed** on their profile, after Call Settings, when there are offered events they can read (or while they still have an address): **Make an address** shows the address once, with **Copy** and the steps for Google Calendar and Apple Calendar; **New address** and **Turn off** confirm first; afterwards it reads "On, made <date>. Last read <time ago>." (lagging up to an hour). The feed holds the environment's events and those of the spaces the person is a member of, by their permissions at each read, leaving out events more than 90 days past; repeats repeat, a Planner object appears once as its Calendar twin, and each event links back to the Calendar. Owners see a "calendar feed" mark and **Turn off feed** on Manage > Users, and a "Calendar feed" row on a person's profile, never the address. Calendar 1.20.0 offers its events.
  - API: `settings.calendarFeeds`; `GET /api/me/feed` `{ allowed, on, made, readAt }`, `POST` 201 `{ url, made }` (403 with its two sentences), `DELETE` 204; `DELETE /api/users/:key/feed`; `calendarFeed { on, made, readAt }` on user records, never the hash; `GET /feed/<token>.ics` (404 "There is no calendar at this address.", 429 past 30 reads a minute with `Retry-After`, `ETag` and 304, `Cache-Control: private, no-cache`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`). Only the address's SHA-256 is stored; on a hosted server a token works only in its own environment. No new environment variables; times use `TZ`. A template may set `calendarFeeds`.
  - **For module authors:** a produced kind offers itself to the feed with `"feed": true` (needs `"dated"`), and `"dated"` takes an optional `"repeat"` field (`{ every, until }`). The manifest validation for `feed` and `repeat` landed early, inside the #176 merge.
  - Documented in `api-modules.md` ("Calendar feeds" and the manifest), `api-module-sdk.md` ("Keeping links true"), `architecture-modules.md`, `architecture-environments.md`, `userguide-accounts.md`, `userguide-environment-settings.md` and `userguide-calendar.md`. Checked by the new `tools/check-feed.mjs` (18 groups) and new cases in `tools/check-secrets.mjs` and `tools/check-modules.mjs`, each run again for this entry; not tried with a real calendar app.
- **Link previews in Chat, with Keep** (GitHub #158, `plan-chat-links.md`). When Research is on in the space, the first link in an ordinary message gets a box under it: the page's picture, its title as the link, up to three lines of description, the site and **Keep**, with Research's colour on its left edge and icon. With Research's **Fetch link previews** off it is one plain line (icon, site, Keep), and nothing is fetched. **Keep** saves the link into Research as the person who pressed it (it arrives when Research is next open), and everyone at once sees "Kept by <name>"; each link is kept once. **Keep** is hidden from anyone who can't add to Research, guests by default. A private message keeps its preview for its author only. Pictures come only through the server, and a preview's title and picture come only from the server's `chat-preview` notice or the history, never from a live chat message sent by another page.
  - API: messages carry `preview { url, module, title?, description?, image?, at }` and `kept { by, who, at }`; new `POST /api/spaces/:id/chat/:messageId/preview`, `GET .../image` and `POST .../keep` (answering `{ status: "pending" | "kept", message }`; the plan said `queued`, the bus says `pending`); `may` on `GET /api/spaces/:id/actions`; the call hears `chat-preview` and `chat-kept`. Documented in `api-modules.md`, `api-module-sdk.md`, `userguide-chat.md` and `userguide-research.md`.
  - Research 0.2.34: links and notes kept from Chat, or from an AI answer's **Keep**, are credited to whoever pressed Keep; links up to 500 characters are kept whole; `saveLink`'s `url` input is now `text`.
  - Checked by the new `tools/check-chat-links.mjs` (17 checks), `check-research`, `check-chat-page` (22 tests) and `check-nav` (63 tests), each run again for this entry; not verified in a real call.
- **Saved layouts** (GitHub #161, `plan-saved-layouts.md`). **Layout ▾** has a third section, **Layouts**, after **Arrange**: the space's shared layouts first, under **Shared**, then your own. **Save this layout** asks for a **Name** and, for owners, the admin and the space's moderators, "For everyone in this <space>"; a taken name offers **Replace it**. One press loads a layout and the panel stays open: modules it doesn't name close, except the Conference while you are on the call; a module in its own window comes back to the canvas; floating boxes scale to the window, snapped ones settle on the grid, and docked ones without a column wait (#156); modules not on in the space are left out with a note. It becomes the layout the space remembers for you. The **⋮** on a layout you may change offers **Replace with this layout**, **Rename** and **Delete** (confirmed). Notes: "Saved <name>.", "<name> now holds this layout.", "Deleted <name>.". Empty, it reads "No saved layouts yet." Not shown at 640 pixels and below, in an aside, or to a guest when there are no shared layouts. **A space's default layout:** owners and the admin get **Make default** and **Stop using as default** in a shared layout's **⋮**, and the default is marked "Default". A first visit opens it with its places, and a guest gets it on every visit; after that each person's own remembered layout wins. Space settings' **Opens with** shows "Opens with the layout <name>" with **Stop using it**, and changing a switch by hand stops using it.
  - Routes: `GET`, `POST /api/spaces/:id/layouts` and `PUT`, `DELETE /api/spaces/:id/layouts/:layoutId`, with `canShare` (owner rights or the space's moderator) and `canSetDefault` (owner rights), and the space's `defaultLayout` id. `PATCH /api/spaces/:id` takes `defaultLayout: id | null` from owners: an id sets `opensWith` to the layout's modules, `null` clears it and keeps `opensWith`, and `opensWith` sent on its own clears it; replacing the default's modules moves `opensWith`, and deleting it clears the default. `GET /api/modules/for-space` gains `defaultLayout`, the whole layout or null. Stored in `layouts.json`: 10 personal layouts per person per space, 10 shared per space, names up to 40 characters, at most 20 modules. For developers: `public/layouts.js` (`layoutOf`, `placeLayout`, `LAYOUT_LIMITS`); `createCanvas()` takes `onMenu(open)`; the canvas gains `captureLayout()`, `loadLayout()` and `isNarrow()`. No SDK change. Documented in `api-modules.md` ("Saved layouts"), `architecture-canvas.md`, `architecture-navigation.md`, `architecture-overview.md`, `userguide-call.md`, `userguide-spaces.md` and `userguide-modules.md`. Checked by the new `tools/check-layouts.mjs` (56 checks) and `tools/check-nav.mjs` (60 tests), run again for this entry, and cases in `check-canvas`; not verified in a real call.
- **Clear…** in Chat (GitHub #166, `plan-chat-clear.md`). Chat's menu reads **Save the chat · Clear… · Delete the chat** (the last for moderators, owners and the admin); **Delete your private messages** is gone. **Clear…** lists **Chat messages**, **AI**, a line per module (its name, icon and colour, or its command, such as "/t", if the module is gone) and **All of them**, each with its count, whatever the All / Private / Public filter shows. A member clears only their own ("Clear your 12 AI messages?"); a moderator, an owner or the admin picks **Clear yours (n)** or **Clear everyone's (n)** ("Private messages of others stay."). Clearing Chat messages or All also takes the pictures. When someone clears everyone's, the others see one line that isn't kept, such as "Mo cleared the AI messages.". It shows to signed-in people in a space, not an aside, when there is something to clear; never to guests. **Delete the chat**'s hint reads "Every message goes, for everyone, private ones too."
  - A notice sent from someone else's page can no longer hide messages that aren't theirs: only the server's notice can clear everyone's messages, or delete or clear the chat, on others' screens.
  - The route: `DELETE /api/spaces/:id/chat/messages?type=chat|ai|module|all&scope=mine|everyone[&module=<id>]`, answering `{ ok, deleted }`; `everyone` takes every public message of that type plus your own private ones, never anyone else's; the call hears `chat-clear-some` (`clearType` names the type). For host code: `openHostMenu` entries take `tint`, and `openConfirmMenu` takes `choices`. Documented in `api-modules.md` and `userguide-chat.md`. Checked by `tools/check-chat-model.mjs` (34 checks) and `tools/check-chat-page.mjs` (15 tests), run again for this entry; not verified in a real call.
- Project priority sync: after updating labels, the run now reports the board's order, with Now, Next and Later ranked top to bottom as dragged and Inbox unranked if it has anything. It goes in the step summary ('Order on the board') and as one notice per column. A board view with its own sort doesn't change it: the report follows the manual order. `tools/check-project-sync.mjs` now has 21 cases, run again for this entry; not run on GitHub here.
- Added a 'Project priority from the board' workflow (`project-priority-sync.yml`), run on demand, that sets each open issue's `priority:` label to match its column on the project board (Now, Next or Later sets it, Inbox or no column clears it, Done is left alone). It fails without changing any labels when the board's field is missing, isn't single-select, or has no Now, Next or Later option. It reports each change as a notice and in the run summary. Checked by the new `tools/check-project-sync.mjs` (21 cases, no network; in `npm run check`), run again for this entry. Not run on GitHub here. Described in `TODO.md` ("How issues are tracked").
- Added a GitHub Actions workflow that moves an issue's card on the project board when it gets a `priority:` label: `priority: now`, `priority: next` and `priority: later` move it to **Now**, **Next** and **Later**, and the issue keeps one priority label. **Done** is the board's own, set when the issue closes or its pull request merges. It needs the `PROJECT_TOKEN` secret and the `PROJECT_OWNER` and `PROJECT_NUMBER` variables. `.github/workflows/project-priority.yml`; the setup and the board are in `TODO.md` ("How issues are tracked"). Checked by the new `tools/check-project-status.mjs` (11 cases, no network; in `npm run check`), run again for this entry. Not run on GitHub here.
- **Calendar in the top bar** (`plan-calendar-destination.md`, approved by Thomas 2026-10-02; phase 6 of `plan-primary-nav.md`). An owner can turn on **Show Calendar** in Manage's new **Top bar** section, and **Calendar** then sits in the middle of everyone's top bar, under the Calendar module's display name and icon (in the narrow layout, in the menu after the spaces). It is one page for the Calendar and the To-do across the environment and every space you are in: a bar with **Month | Week | Day** and a filter named with the space word's plural ("Trips (3 of 5)"), listing the environment's own calendar by its name first, then your spaces; the calendar below; and on the right **Agenda | To-do** (the Agenda alone when the To-do is off or not yours to read). The view, the filter and the tab are remembered in this browser. In the narrow layout the page has three tabs, **Calendar | Agenda | To-do**, and opens on Day. You can add and change events and tasks in any space where you may, and a new one always asks **Where** it goes. Opened while you are in a space it opens over the space, and you are Away on the call until you go back. While Calendar is shown, `/modules/calendar`, `/modules/todo` and the **Coming up** and **Due soon** headings lead to it. Off by default; on in an environment made from the Travel template (version 4). Guests never see it. Documented in `userguide-calendar.md` ("Calendar in the top bar"), `userguide-todo.md`, `userguide-environment-settings.md` ("Top bar"), `userguide-spaces.md`, `userguide-modules.md`, `userguide-templates.md`, `architecture-navigation.md` ("Destinations"), `architecture-modules.md`, `api-modules.md` ("Destinations") and `api-module-sdk.md` ("A destination's part").
- **Map in the top bar** (`plan-map-destination.md`, approved by Thomas 2026-10-02). **Show Map**, below **Show Calendar**, puts **Maps** (the Maps module's display name and icon) after Calendar in the top bar: Maps' map and Places' list on one page, across your own places, the environment's and every space you are in. The bar holds the filter (**Mine**, the environment's own by its name, then your spaces) and a search field: typing filters the list and dims the pins that don't match, Enter asks the place search for new places (shown with **Save** at the top of the list and as dashed pins), Escape clears it, and coordinates or a map link start a new place there. Selecting a pin marks the place in the list, and clicking a place flies the map to it. **+** on the map ("Add a place: click the map") and **Add a place** on the list open Places' dialog, which asks **Where** every time. In the narrow layout there are two tabs, **Maps | Places**: picking a place in the list switches to the map, and the map's callout has **Show in list**; without WebGL the list takes the page. The filter, the map's last view and the tab are remembered in this browser. Shown only while Maps is on and a map file is set; Manage's switch is greyed with the reason otherwise. While Map is shown, `/modules/maps` and `/modules/places` lead to it; without it, `/modules/maps` now draws all your places from every space, yours and the environment's, as a map with no list. Documented in `userguide-maps.md` ("Map in the top bar"), `userguide-places.md`, `userguide-environment-settings.md` and the documents above.
  - For developers: one host page for both, `public/destination.html`, `destination.js` and `destination.css`; the rules in `server/destinations.js` (which destinations exist, the option that shows each, the one `main`, the panels' order, who sees what); the parts mounted with `mountModule({ destination })` sharing one state from `createDestination()` in `public/module-host.js`; the bar's entries from `destinationTools()` in `public/primary-nav.js`. New routes `GET /api/destinations`, `GET /api/destinations/:id`, `GET /calendar` and `GET /map`; `settings.showCalendar` and `showMap` (default `false`) in `GET` and `PATCH /api/settings`, which also gives `topBarReasons`; both are template settings, and Travel sets them (template version 4). A guest opening `/calendar` or `/map` goes back to their space's link.
  - **For module authors:** a module can offer a part of a destination with `surfaces.destination` (`[{ id: "calendar" | "map", part: "main" | "panel", entry, label?, order? }]`, the `environment` scope required; refusals in `api-modules.md`), and draws itself for it from `info.context.destination` and `host.destination` (`onState`, `set`). See the SDK changes under Changed.
  - Verified: by tools, `tools/check-destinations.mjs` (new, in `npm run check`; 35 groups), `check-module-host` (54), `check-nav` (46) and `check-drop` (15), each run again for this entry; and, by the builders, in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit and a stand-in place search. Not verified with real map tiles (headless Chromium had no WebGL, so the map's list-only state is what was seen), not in a real call, and not with a screen reader, Firefox, Safari or a real phone. Quality assurance was still running when this was written.
- The space bar shows who is in the space (Thomas, 2026-09-30: "Right now there is no way to tell when in a space who is in there"). In the middle of the bar are the portraits of the people here, up to four, then "+N", and the count ("3 here", or "Just you" when you are alone). As the bar narrows it drops portraits, then shows the count alone, before anything else in the bar folds. Click it, or press Enter or Space on it, for the list: everyone in the space (or the aside), you first with "(you)", each with their picture in this space (else their profile picture, else their first letter), and "on the call" beside those on the call. It follows people arriving, leaving, joining and hanging up as it happens. OBS viewers are not shown. The list opens under the button, or above it in the narrow layout's tab bar, stays inside the window and scrolls when it is long; Escape, the button, a click elsewhere or moving the keyboard away closes it. In the narrow layout it is a count in the tab bar. Thomas calls the live pieces in the two header bars **widgets**, and this is the first. For developers: `public/space-people.js` (`createWhoHere()`, and the pure `facesThatFit()`, `peopleIn()` and `hereWords()`), registered as the `who-here` tool in the space bar's middle zone with `fold: false` and a `fit(avail)` (see `architecture-navigation.md`). Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (the portraits that fit, who is listed and in what order, and the width the middle is given) and verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit (there is no LiveKit server here). Not checked in a real call with two people, with a real screen reader, or in Firefox or Safari.
- The conference's **Not in a call** says who is on the call and has a **Join the call** button. Under **Currently on the call** it lists each person on the call with their picture, live, or says "No one is on the call yet. Be the first to join." Under the button is the microphone note ("Your browser will ask for your microphone. You join with your camera off; turn it on whenever you're ready.") and, where the browser offers it, the install hint. The green phone on the toolbar does the same, and its tooltip reads "Join the call" (it read "Rejoin the call"). If the join is refused, the reason shows here: the plan's calls cap ("This <environment>'s plan allows N call(s) at once; one is running in <space>"), or "Could not reach the server to join the call. Try again in a moment." when the server does not answer. The conference's close button reads "Close" while you are out of the call and "Leave the call and close" while you are on it. Verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit; not checked in a real call.
- The space bar has one **Modules** button in place of a switch for each module (Thomas, 2026-09-30). It reads the environment's word for modules, and shows the unread count of everything in it. It opens a list of switches: the conference, the chat and each module on in the space, each with its icon, its name and its own unread count. A switch shows or hides its module on the canvas. The list stays open while you flip switches; the button, Escape or a click elsewhere closes it. From the keyboard, the list opens with the first switch focused, the Up and Down arrows (and Home and End) move, and Space or Enter flips a switch. On a narrow canvas, a switch on a module that is open but hidden behind another shows it. In the narrow layout (640 pixels wide or less, a narrow popped-out window included) the list is the tab bar at the bottom of the page, as before. For developers: `public/switch-list.js` (`switchListHtml()`, `switchRowHtml()`, `wireSwitchList()`) draws every list of module switches, each a real checkbox with `role="switch"`, so a screen reader says on or off; the chooser is the `module-chooser` tool in the space bar's left zone (`#modules-toggle`, `#modules-menu`). Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and `tools/check-nav.mjs`, and verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit. Not checked with a real screen reader, or in Firefox or Safari.
- The space bar never runs into itself (Thomas, 2026-09-30). When its tools do not fit, the right end folds them into a **…** button (**More**) just before **Leave space**, in this order: **Pop out**, **Full screen**, a module's own tools, the snap switch with its **Grid size** slider, **Dock all**, **Rejoin call**, then **Pull participants back**. They come back as the bar widens. **Leave space**, the **…** and the **Modules** button never fold. Who is here gives way first (fewer portraits, then the count alone), and the space's name is cut short only after everything that can fold has; hovering it shows the whole name. In the **…** menu a switch (the snap switch, **Full screen**, **Pop out**) shows whether it is on, a tool's count shows beside it, and the counts of folded tools show on the **…** itself. Picking an entry does exactly what the tool does in the bar. The page's shared menu (`public/host-menu.js`) gained checkbox entries (`checked`, drawn as `menuitemcheckbox` with `aria-checked`) and counts (`badge`). For developers: `foldSteps()`, `foldCount()`, `middleCentred()` and `middleWidth()` in `public/nav-bar.js`, and a tool's `fold: false`. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (the fold's order, how much folds, the menu) and verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit at several widths. Not checked with a real screen reader, or in Firefox or Safari.
- `POST /api/call/join`, which the page asks just before joining the call, for the plan's calls cap. A signed-in person sends `{ space }` (the Lobby when left out; the same membership rule as `POST /api/token`); a guest sends `{ guest }`, their guest link's token (in the body or as `?guest=`), and joins that link's space. It answers 200 `{ ok: true }`; 401 "sign in first"; 404 "no such space" or "that guest link is off or wrong"; 403 "you are not in that space", "that guest link is for another space", or the calls cap's sentence. Joining an aside's call is never refused here: the cap was asked when the aside was made. Documented in `architecture-overview.md` and `architecture-environments.md`. Checked by `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` against a stand-in LiveKit, and by curl.
- The word on the button for entering a space is set by the environment's template, and an owner can change it: **Enter** by default, since entering a space doesn't join the call (Thomas, 2026-09-30; addendum 4 of `plan-environment-templates.md`). It is the space list's main button and its pop-out button ("<word> in a pop-out window"), and the guest form's heading and button. Owners set it on Manage > **Template** > **Words**, in a last row, **Button for entering a space**: one field, blank for the template's word or Enter, "From the template" or "The template's: <word>" when the template gives one, saved with the words, and **Reset**. The host admin sets a template's word in the host template editor, under **Words**, and the compare panel lists **Verbs**. The Travel template sets none, so a trip reads Enter. For developers: a `verbs` set beside `words`, one key, `enter`, resolved as the owner's (`settings.verbs.enter`), then the template's, then the default. `GET /api/branding` carries `verbs`; `GET` and `PATCH /api/settings` also give the owner `ownVerbs` (`{}` for none) and `templateVerbs` (null with no template, `{}` for a template without verbs). `PATCH /api/settings` `{ verbs: { enter: "<text>" | null } }` is owner only; null goes back to the template's or the default; the text is tidied, 1 to 20 characters of letters, spaces, hyphens and apostrophes, and a refused verb refuses the whole patch with one of "Verbs must be given by name, each as text.", "There is no verb called <key>; the verbs are enter.", "The enter verb must be text, or null to use the default.", "The enter verb must be 1 to 20 characters." or "The enter verb can use only letters, spaces, hyphens and apostrophes.". A template's optional `verbs` field is live: no version bump, not in the switch's offer or the applied-once fingerprint, and in the whole-template fingerprint only when set, so no edited bundled template's notice opens for it; in a template file a verb that isn't text, null included, is refused with "verbs: The enter verb must be text.", and an export leaves unset verbs out (`format` and `formatVersion` unchanged). The host editor's `PATCH` takes `verbs`, and null removes it. Not in `host.locale()`. Pages: `verb()`, `verbs()` and `setVerbs()` in `public/words.js` (re-exported by `brand.js`), `data-verb="enter"`, `{enter}` in `fill()` and `data-fill`, kept in the browser as `app.verbs`. Space settings' launch-link hint reads the word ("Shown on the button next to <word>…"). No module version changes. Documented in `architecture-environments.md` ("Templates"), `architecture-overview.md` ("Words"), `userguide-environment-settings.md`, `userguide-templates.md`, `userguide-spaces.md`, `userguide-accounts.md` and `userguide-call.md`; the plan's addendum 4 says built. Verified live on a single install and with `BASE_DOMAIN`, in headless Chromium and by curl: the defaults, an owner's word everywhere, **Reset**, a host template's word reaching an environment at once, every refusal, the guest form, and the space list at 1280, 1024 and 390 pixels wide with long words. Checked by tools: `tools/check-templates.mjs` (34 groups), `tools/check-template-switch.mjs` (14), `tools/check-names.mjs --words` and `tools/check-slot-names.mjs`. Not checked: **Back to <space>** in a real call (no LiveKit server here), and a screen reader.
- A picture can be pasted into any picture box, as well as chosen as a file: the profile photo and call background, the Participant and Character sets and the per-space sets on a profile, the environment icon, the sign-in background and the Default and Guest sets on Manage, and a space's picture in its settings. Tab to the picture and press Ctrl+V (Cmd+V on a Mac), or choose the small **Paste** button on its corner (beside the small environment icon), shown on hover and always on a touch screen; the browser may ask to read the clipboard. Enter or Space on the picture still opens the file chooser, and a hint reads "Paste or click to choose". A pasted picture has the same types, the 20 MB limit and the messages of a chosen file; the new messages are "No picture to paste. Copy one first.", "Can't read the clipboard here. Press Ctrl+V now to paste." (focus goes to the picture), on a touch screen "Can't read the clipboard here. Tap the picture to choose one.", and "That picture couldn't be read. Try choosing the file." A box you can't change takes no paste, and a paste in a text field is left alone. Each box has its own name for a screen reader ("Profile picture", "Default Online picture"), in the environment's words. The profile's header now says "Click it to change it, or paste a picture". Chat already took pasted pictures and is unchanged. For page authors: `public/slot-paste.js`, imported by a page, does all of this for every slot, and `tools/check-slot-names.mjs` (now in `npm run check`) holds each slot to a name and each page with slots to the import. No module version changes. Documented in `userguide-images.md` ("Set a picture"), `userguide-accounts.md`, `userguide-environment-settings.md`, `userguide-spaces.md` and `architecture-overview.md` ("Picture slots"). Verified live in headless Chromium: every kind of slot by Ctrl+V and by the **Paste** button, a text paste, a text field's paste left alone, a 21 MB picture and a BMP refused as chosen files are, the keyboard, a 390 px touch layout, names following the environment's words, and slots that can't be changed. Checked by tools (`tools/check-slot-names.mjs`). Not checked: Firefox, Safari, a real phone, a real screen reader, and a real clipboard holding a file copied from the computer. The **Paste** button gets only a PNG in Chromium; see [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- `POST /api/host/themes/check`, for the host template editor's theme picker (plan-kind-names). A host admin sends a theme file's text (or its JSON), at most 16 KB, from the host console's own origin; the server reads it exactly as a theme import does and stores nothing. 200 `{ theme, dropped }`; 400 with the theme file's sentence; 401 without a host admin's session; 403 from another site; 404 on an environment's own address and on a single install. Documented in `architecture-environments.md` (the host API). Checked by `tools/check-themes.mjs` and `tools/check-origin.mjs`, and verified live with curl on a local server; the template editor's theme picker verified live in headless Chromium.
- An owner can choose what a space opens with the first time someone enters it (GitHub #3, `plan-entering.md` Part 1, steps 1 and 2). Space settings, Space tab, **Opens with**: ticks for the conference (unless it is switched off), the chat and the space's modules, saved as you click, in the order listed (switches since 2026-09-30; see Changed). After a first visit each person's own layout is remembered. With nothing ticked, the page names what opens instead ("Nothing ticked: Chat and To-do open."). A ticked module that can't open now stays listed with a note saying why, such as "(off in this space)". The space stores `opensWith`, up to 20 module ids; `PATCH /api/spaces/:id` takes a list or `null` (owner only; 400 "Opens with must be a list of modules." for anything else), and the space carries it in `GET /api/presence`, `/api/spaces`, `/api/status` and `GET /api/modules/for-space`. A repeated id is dropped and an empty list means not set; the plan kept repeats. Documented in `userguide-spaces.md`, `architecture-overview.md` and `api-modules.md`. Checked by `tools/check-template-switch.mjs` and verified live with curl; the settings verified live in headless Chromium at 1280 and 390 px.
- Joining the call is its own control, in the middle of the space bar: "2 in the call · Join", or **Join** alone with nobody in it. Its hover and focus hint is the microphone note, which has left the space list. It is not shown in the call, in an aside, without the conference permission, with the conference switched off, or in the narrow layout, where the **Conference** tab is the way in. Read as code only; the count and **Join** need a real call. Removed again before release (see Removed).
- A space chooses which pictures the conference shows: Participant images or Character images. The box is the same either way. A space still on the older Roleplaying choice shows Participant images. Both sets stay on the profile page, and Coffee Pub Studio still uses either or both on its own.
- A research link's picture is taken from the page's own structured data when the page gives one: `primaryImageOfPage`, or the picture on the article, product or place. The Open Graph picture is used when the page has none of those, and the old Twitter picture only after that. Checked by `tools/check-link-preview.mjs`.
- A link's menu has **Refresh link**. It reads the page again and updates the title, the description and the picture. Research 0.2.29.
- A new link asks for the address first, then the title, with the page's picture to the right of both. The picture's place is there even before the page is read. Research 0.2.28.
- A research link's card shows the page's picture and its description, so they are there without opening the link. The picture is read by this server. Research 0.2.27.
- Adding a link can read the page. With **Fetch link previews** on for Research, an empty title or description is filled in from the page, and its picture is shown while editing. Off, which is how it starts, the server fetches nothing. Research 0.2.26.
- Entering a space opens the remembered layout, then that space's own list, then the list the environment set for a new space, then the conference (or chat, when the conference is off). A Travel space opens the Planner and chat, and does not open the conference. Home base in that environment opens chat only, because the Planner is not on there. This is step 6 of Planner phases. A space's own list is not stored yet, so the canvas route answers null for it and sends the environment's list beside it; storing it belongs to entering a space (#3). Checked by `tools/check-template-switch.mjs` (`tools/check-travel.mjs` also passes, though it does not test what opens), and the canvas route's answer (`opensWith`, `spaceDefaultsOpensWith`) verified live with curl on 2026-09-30. Not watched in a browser: signing in there was blocked on this machine, and there is no LiveKit server here, so a join never reaches the canvas. A real call was not checked.
- The host admin can edit a bundled template, such as Travel, in place on the host console (GitHub #91, pull request #97; addendum 3 of the templates plan). The id stays and the version goes up, so environments made from it get the words, phases, home icon and module names and icons at once and are offered the rest. The row shows **Edited**; **Reset to shipped** brings the shipped template back and still raises the version; **Duplicate**, on any row, makes a host template at version 1. When a newer image changes a template the host edited, the edits are kept and a notice reads "<name> was updated in this image: review": **Review** compares each part, then **Keep mine** or **Take the new version**. A bundled template can be hidden, never deleted. Stored as `bundledEdits` in `host.json`; new routes `DELETE /api/host/templates/:id/edits`, `POST .../update` and `POST .../duplicate`. Documented in `userguide-templates.md` ("Edit a bundled template") and `architecture-environments.md`; the plan's addendum 3 says built. Verified: the full `npm run check` passed on the Mac, including `tools/check-templates.mjs` and `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`. The console (rows, editor hint, notice, compare, duplicate) was read as code only, not walked live.
- The Planner page shows the plan from the start: the space's name until it is renamed, and a phase line ("Planning · 12 days to go", "Trip · day 3 of 9", or the label alone). The plan form has one optional start and end per phase. An object with no date sits under "Not on a day yet", grouped by phase, and a new one starts in the current phase. A dated phase is named above its first day. Planning starts on the first phase and planning ends at the close of the phase before the main one; the booked start and end stay where they are. Planner 0.10.0. This is step 5 of Planner phases. The plan's 0.8.0 is stale; 0.10.0 is the version after the linked-objects work. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`. Verified live in a browser on a throwaway Travel environment: a new trip opened as the space's name with "Planning" and "Nothing on the plan yet. Add something below."; renaming it and setting phase dates showed "Booking · 5 days to go" and a Trip band; clearing the main dates left the name and "Booking"; adding an object with no date put it under "Not on a day yet", in Booking, which is where the phase list started. The home widget still read "No trips planned", so its phase line was not seen. A real call was not checked (there is no LiveKit server here). Planner phases steps 1 to 5 (#95, pull requests #105 to #109) are documented in `userguide-planner.md` ("The plan", "Phases", "Not on a day yet"), `userguide-templates.md` (phases and what a new space opens), `api-module-sdk.md`, `api-modules.md` and `architecture-environments.md`; step 6, what opens on entering, is its own entry above, and the plan now says every step is built. The home widget's phase line was seen live later, on 2026-09-30 ("Booking · 10 days to go", with the trip's dates written through the API rather than the Planner page). The full `npm run check` passed on the Mac.
- A plan stores its phases (`v: 2`): each phase may have a start and an end, the main phase's dates stay the plan's own, and an empty name shows the space's name. The days run from the earliest date to the latest. An object in another phase does not move those dates. An older plan is brought forward once; when the first phase is not the main one, that phase starts on the day the space was created. This is step 4 of Planner phases. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`. The pages are the next step. Not verified live in a browser.
- The host template editor lists phases (id, label, one main phase, add, remove, reorder) and what a new space opens, in order. The offer's new-space line names those, in that order. This is step 3 of Planner phases. Verified live in the host console: a new template saved a phase marked main and an ordered open list, Edit showed them back, and a repeated phase id was refused beside the list. The offer sentence was read as code only.
- A module's context answers the space it is open in (`id`, `name`, `createdAt`, or null outside a space) and the template's phases. `host.space()` and `host.phases()` read them. A template edit is on the next read. This is step 2 of Planner phases. Checked by `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`. Not verified live in a browser.
- A template can list phases (an id, a label, and at most one main phase) and what a new space opens with. Phases follow the template as it changes. What opens is applied once, with the new-space profile, and a switch offers it when that part changes. The Travel template is version 2: Planning, Booking, Buffer, Pre-trip, Trip, Post-trip, and a new space is set to open the Planner and chat (stored and offered only; nothing opens from it until step 6). This is step 1 of Planner phases. Checked by `tools/check-templates.mjs` and `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`. The host editor does not edit these yet.
- A dated object on a plan is on the Calendar too, and the two stay in step (GitHub #96, pull requests #102 to #104), on top of the finished linked-objects plan (#98 to #101): a link follows its object's title and day unless it is kept on its day, goes when the object is deleted, and a delete that would leave something pointing at nothing says "Used by N." first. Planner 0.9.0, Calendar 1.18.0. Documented in the Planner and Calendar guides, `api-module-sdk.md` ("Keeping links true": `host.objects.onChange`, `holds`, `dated`, `mirror`, `create`, the pairs), `api-modules.md` (`tz`, `refchange`, the backfill) and `architecture-modules.md` ("Keeping links true"); both plans say built, and #96 is off the to-do list. Verified: the full `npm run check` passed on the Mac on the stacked head. Not verified live in a browser. The backfill on a hosted install (once per environment) was read as code only; `tools/check-links.mjs` covers the single-install restart.
- Existing dated objects get their twin once, the first time the server starts after a kind declares that it sends them. A later start leaves that twin as it is. A timed value uses the server's time zone. This is step 3 of keeping plans and the calendar in sync. Checked by `tools/check-links.mjs`.
- A Planner object with a date, and no pointer, declares that it sends a dated twin. An event declares that it can receive one. Planner 0.9.0, Calendar 1.18.0. This is step 2 of keeping plans and the calendar in sync. The two manifests do not name each other. Read as code only; not verified live in a browser.
- A dated object that sends twins gets one in each kind that receives them, in the same space, and the two stay in step: title, day and time write both ways, clearing the day deletes the twin, and deleting the twin leaves the sender's day until that day changes. A personal object, one that holds a pointer, and a receiver that is off in the space get no twin. A module's own links never remove the pair. This is step 1 of keeping plans and the calendar in sync. Checked by `tools/check-links.mjs`. Neither module is named.
- Deleting a Calendar event or a Planner object that something points at says "Used by N." before the usual question. Calendar 1.17.18, Planner 0.8.1. This is step 4 of the linked-objects plan. Read as code only.
- The Planner keeps a link on the day you put it (`pinned`, "Keep on this day"). Dragging a link to another day pins it; dragging it back to the object's day clears that. A change to the linked object is picked up through `host.objects.onChange`. Deleting that object removes the link. Planner 0.8.0. This is step 3 of the linked-objects plan. `pinned` is checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`. The drag and the live refresh were read as code only.
- A write or delete of a linked object sends `refchange` on the module stream to the modules that point at it (`host.objects.onChange`). A write that leaves the summary the same sends nothing. The writer's time zone travels with the storage write (`tz`); a zone the server does not accept falls back to `TZ`, then UTC. This is step 1 of the linked-objects plan. Checked by `tools/check-links.mjs`.
- When a module declares `holds` on a kind, deleting the object it points at removes that holder (`onDelete: remove`) or marks it (`onDelete: mark`). A title change rewrites the followed title, and a day change moves a holder that was placed on the old day and is not pinned. The holder is written only when a followed value differs. A bad `holds` is refused. This is step 2 of the linked-objects plan. Checked by `tools/check-links.mjs`.
