- One input in Chat (#58, phase 1): `/ai` asks the AI privately (thread saved per person), a command picker lists `/ai` and each placed module's commands, and pasting an AI answer with `magpie` blocks (now `objects` blocks; see the formats entry under Changed) offers **Bring in N objects** (a preview with ticks; **Keep ticked**). Research `/r`, To-do `/t`, Planner `/p`, Calendar `/c` and Polls `/v` open that module's add form filled in. The Assistant module is retired; use `/ai` in Chat. Assistant 0.1.20 has no window (`surfaces.canvas.menu: false`, a new manifest field). For module authors: a top-level `commands` field in `module.json`, `host.chat.ask`, `host.ui.moreButton`, and the space's AI, thread, command, actions and objects-check routes (see api-modules, "Chat routes"); threads are kept in `DATA_DIR/ai-threads.json`. Checked by a tool only (`tools/check-one-input.mjs`, `tools/check-modules.mjs`, `tools/check-module-window.mjs` in PRs #77 to #84); not verified live in a browser or a real call. Documented in the new Chat guide.
- Research from any AI can be brought into Magpie as objects (phase 1 of #73, parts 1 and 2). The Assistant has **Copy instructions for another AI** (the text is generated on the server from the same source as the Assistant's own rule) and a **Bring in research** panel: paste a whole answer (`magpie` or `card` fences, or raw JSON), or choose a `.magpie-objects.json` file (now an `objects` block or a `.objects.json` file; see the formats entry under Changed). A preview shows a tick per object; **Keep ticked** saves them through the same Research and Planner actions the Assistant already uses. Imported text ends with the line `External source`. Shared format and checker: `server/object-format.js`, `GET /api/objects/format`, `GET /api/objects/format/schema`, `GET`/`POST /api/modules/:id/objects/check`, and `host.objects.format` / `checkAvailable` / `check`. Part 3 (a direct connection from AI apps) waits for #64. Documented in `userguide-assistant.md` (**Bring in research from another AI**), `userguide-planner.md`, `userguide-research.md`, `architecture-modules.md` ("The objects format"), `api-modules.md` (the routes, the format and every refusal) and `api-module-sdk.md` ("Bringing objects in"); the plan's status says phase 1 is built. Checked by tools on 50ef1bd: `check-object-format`, `check-ai`, `check-assistant` and `check-travel` pass. `npm run check` did not pass on the merge commit (hard-coded words, and the check route missing from the same-origin scan); a follow-up fixed both, and it passes now. The Assistant is 0.1.18.

### Fixed
- **Security: the product pages no longer list every environment or take a password** (`plan-product-site.md`, slice 0; GitHub #435). Anyone could read the name and address of every environment on a host from `GET /api/product/environments`; that route is removed and answers 404 on every host. On the landing, travel and games pages, **Sign in** is now a typed address (the base domain is shown after it) and a **Go** button, which opens that environment's own sign-in page once the address is found. There is no dropdown, no password field and no post to another site. A line says "Got a link from someone? Just open it."
  - **Removed.** The form `POST /login` and its `?error=1&login=` return, and `CROSS_ORIGIN_EXEMPT`: every write now goes through the same-origin rule, with no path exempt. The `env_hint` cookie is still set and nothing reads it.
  - **Sign-in no longer leads to another site.** On the sign-in page and its second step, the `next` address is kept only if it starts with a single `/` and stays on this site. `//evil`, `/\evil`, full addresses and tab or newline tricks go to `/`. This fixed an older open redirect.
  - **Lookup limits.** `GET /api/product/environment?slug=` answers 404 for an environment refused at startup, and 429 `Too many checks from this address; try again in a minute.` beyond 120 a minute, counted together with `GET /api/product/address`. The limit is checked before the address is read, so unknown and malformed addresses count too.
  - **For developers.** `public/next-address.js` (`sameSiteNext`), `public/landing.js`, `server/index.js`. `tools/check-brand-hosts.mjs` now catches the studio's domains without naming them in code. New cases in `check-origin`, `check-host-registry`, `check-product-pages` and `check-auth`. No new environment variables. For hosts: nothing to change. See [architecture-environments](documentation/architecture/architecture-environments.md).
  - **Checked:** by the builders' checks, as reported, not rerun by this entry's author: `check-auth` (26), `check-origin` (21), `check-host-registry` (59), `check-product-pages` (15) and `check-brand-hosts`; and an independent security read, live on throwaway servers. **Not verified:** going from the product host to an environment on real separate domains in Chromium, Firefox and Safari (a hand check after deploy, which should also `curl` the live `/api/product/environments` and expect 404), and the `next` address in the second sign-in step (two-step), live.
- **Manage > About no longer says the product is MIT-licensed.** It said "Released under the MIT license."; it now says "Proprietary software. All rights reserved.", as the README and `package.json` (`UNLICENSED`) do. Thomas confirmed on 2026-11-08 that the product is proprietary. `tools/check-nav.mjs` has a new assertion that guards the wording.
  - **Checked:** `check-nav` (92 tests), run by the builder, as reported; this entry's author did not rerun it. **Not verified:** the About page on a hosted install.
- **An owner's copy of an environment no longer carries sign-in secrets or other people's private data** (security; held branch `pm/export-no-secrets`). **Download a copy** is now built from an allow-list of fields and files (`server/environment-export.js`). It leaves out password hashes, personal links, second-step secrets, guest links, invites, the session key and the stream key; other people's private chat (`chat-private.json`, `ai-threads.moved.json`); and each person's own module data, held clears and uploads, which are private to the person even from admins. The reply says `Cache-Control: private, no-store`. The host's backup is still complete, every regular file byte for byte, but it no longer follows symbolic links: each skipped link is logged by path, and a link inside an environment (for example `modules` linked to another disk) is left out of the backup and the copy. A symbolic `DATA_DIR` or environment folder itself still works.
  - **What a restore does now.** A restore (of an owner's copy or a host backup) removes `secrets` from the zip, so a new session key and a new stream key are always made. Everyone is signed out and "remember this device" stops working; guests open their guest link again; visitors to a password-protected Vault share enter the password again; every OBS browser source and keyed link needs the new stream key from Settings. From a host backup, passwords, personal links, guest links, invites and calendar feeds survive; after restoring an owner's copy nobody has a password or link until the host admin gives out new ones. A stored session key shorter than 43 base64url characters (stream key under 24) is replaced on load. A person record with `hostAdmin: true` keeps no password, link or second-step secret. A hosted restore turns any record with role `admin` that is not the host admin's stand-in into `owner`.
  - **For developers.** `tools/check-environment-export.mjs` (8 groups, in `npm run check`) fails when a new person, space or aside field, or a file, is on neither list; `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` has new restore cases. Settings-field coverage in the guard is by hand. See [architecture-environments](documentation/architecture/architecture-environments.md), "The owner's copy and a restore".
  - **Checked:** live on scratch servers by independent security reads, and by the checks above. **Not verified:** a real call (there is no LiveKit server here). **Still open:** an export blocks the whole hosted server while it builds (planned as its own piece of work: streaming, a size cap, one at a time, a rate limit); a host admin's password alone opens every environment, even if that host admin uses a second step at the console (read as code only). The copy still carries logins and display names, module notifications, AI-log counts, module setting values, a space's link and the Vault's file records. All three are in [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- **A plan can no longer be saved without a name** (GitHub #26). On the host console's **Plans** tab, **Save plans** with an empty or spaces-only plan name now shows "Give the plan a name." beside the field and puts the cursor there, and the name is trimmed. Before, the plan was saved under its id as its name. The server refuses it too: `PUT /api/host/plans` answers 400 with the same sentence and saves nothing ([architecture-environments](documentation/architecture/architecture-environments.md)). Loading stays lenient: a stored plan with an empty name (only a hand-edited `host.json` can have one) loads under its id, and the next save asks for a name. Checked by a unit test and a live `PUT` case in `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` (58 groups), and live in headless Chrome (a spaces-only name showed the message and nothing saved, then "Pro" saved); `check-syntax` passes. Not run: the full `npm run check`. Not confirmed in the browser that nothing was stored; the live `PUT` check covers the server's refusal.
- **`check-names` allows two more words, on purpose.** `tools/check-names-allow.json` gains the strong-coffee theme's `--surface-card` and `--bg-card` colour tokens (a style, not an object; Thomas decided on 2026-11-06 to allow them, not rename them, [plan-names](documentation/plans/plan-names.md) decision 6) and the `modules` server-sent event name in `server/index.js` (an event name a script listens for, not text a person reads). No behaviour changes. `node tools/check-names.mjs` now reports only the two root-only `--migration` cases that fail when run as root (see CLAUDE.md).
- **Printing a PDF from the Vault in a module's pop-out or a destination page no longer hits a blocked frame** (GitHub #401, item 1). **Print** holds the PDF in a hidden frame whose source is a `blob:` address, and the content security policy of `public/module.html` and `public/destination.html` (`frame-src 'self'`) blocked it, so the print could show an error page. `blob:` is now allowed in `frame-src` on those two pages. `public/keyed.html` was left alone: keyed viewers (an access key, no session) are refused by the library routes, so they cannot reach library files and Print can never run there. `public/space.html` needed no change (its `child-src` already covers it). Only `frame-src` changed, and the server sends no policy that overrides these tags. `tools/check-syntax.mjs` now checks that `blob:` is in `frame-src` on exactly those two pages, that `keyed.html` does not name `blob:` in `frame-src`, `child-src` or `default-src`, and that no other directive takes it except `frame-src`, `img-src`, `media-src`, `worker-src` and `child-src`. Verified live in headless Chromium: before the change, one `securitypolicyviolation` for `frame-src` and an unreadable frame; after it, no violation and the frame loaded a `blob:` source without error. Not verified: the real print dialog and the printed PDF (headless Chromium has no PDF viewer), and the Vault upload-then-**Print** button path (read as code only).
- **Print in the Vault starts faster and says it is working.** Print could take 5 to 10 seconds on the NAS before anything happened. The server was not the slow part (the list took 5 to 7 ms for 203 files, a download 13 to 175 ms). The page did its steps one after another and showed nothing. `host.library.print` now asks for the viewer code, the file's bytes and the list together; the list still enforces the download-only rule and stops the download if the file may not be printed. **Print** shows "Preparing to print..." from the click until printing starts or fails (`progressNote` in `public/brand.js`, shown for 60 seconds at most), and a failure, a network error too, gives a plain sentence. The file window's **Print** does the same. No server or security change. **Checked:** measured locally, click to print frame loaded, before then after: 1 MB PDF 139 to 83 ms, 10 MB PDF 258 to 162 ms, 3 MB PNG 122 to 96 ms, and a 10 MB PDF with 300 ms added to each request 1354 to 671 ms; source assertions in `tools/check-module-host.mjs`, as reported, not rerun by this entry's author. **Not verified:** the gain on a real network, the NAS and the reverse proxy (a local run has no network delay, so the 5 to 10 seconds has not been reproduced or shown to be fixed); the real print dialog; the real PDF viewer being ready. The fixed 400 ms wait for a PDF is unchanged, and a second click while one print is preparing starts a second print ([known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md)).
- **A module turned on or off shows in an open space at once.** In **Manage > Modules**, turning a module on or off (or installing, removing or rolling one back) now shows in or removes it from the space bar for everyone who has that space open, with no refresh. Before, the bar read the list once on entering the space. The server now sends a bare `modules` event (empty payload, no module ids) down `GET /api/notifications/stream`; `public/brand.js` raises `app:modules-changed` (also after the stream reconnects) and `reread()` in `public/canvas.js` reads `GET /api/modules/for-space` again, which still filters by viewer and by whether the module is on in that space, redraws the Show list, tabs and menu, and closes an open module that is no longer on. Only that environment's streams hear it.
  - **Checked:** verified live with two browser contexts (admin and a member): the Show list updated on enable and on disable with no reload. By tools, as reported by the builder, not rerun by this entry's author: `tools/check-hooks.mjs` (single and hosted install: off then on gives exactly one `modules` event each with an empty payload; a second environment's stream hears nothing), `check-syntax`, `check-canvas`, `check-streams`, `check-links`, `check-host-registry`, `check-module-host`, `check-module-window`, `check-live`, `check-conformance`. Not exercised: the Set up panel and the Chat command list fetch when opened; read as code only. No real call (no LiveKit locally).
  - **Known gaps:** guests have no notifications stream and still need a refresh; a change to a module's permissions still needs a reload. Both are in `known-issues.md`.
- **A blocked pop-out no longer closes the module** (GitHub #288). When the browser refuses the window for **Open in its own window**, the module stays where it was and the status line reads "The browser did not open the window. Allow pop-ups for this site to open it in its own window.". Before, the module closed and nothing opened. The call's pop-out uses the same sentence (it said "to pop out the call"). **Checked:** live in headless Chrome with `window.open` stubbed to return nothing (the module stayed and the sentence showed) and with `window.open` allowed (the window opened and the module left the column); new source-text cases in `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and `tools/check-module-host.mjs`; independent review, read as code. Not tried in Firefox or Safari.
- **A file the Vault keeps as download only can't be printed by a module** (GitHub #401, item 2). `host.library.print` now refuses it with "This kind of file can't be printed." before it fetches the file, as the Vault's menu and viewer already did. This is a rule of the page and the SDK; the server still serves the bytes for download, by design ([api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md)). **Checked:** source-text case in `tools/check-module-host.mjs`; read as code. **Not verified:** the refusal in a browser, because there is no download-only PDF to try it on. Item 1 of #401 (a PDF print in a pop-out or destination page) is still open; see [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- **The past-due sweep also runs when the server starts** (GitHub #51). On a hosted server (`BASE_DOMAIN` set) the sweep that moves an environment past due for fourteen days to the free caps ran only on an hourly timer, so a server restarted more often than hourly never ran it. It now runs once at start and then every hour, and a failed sweep is logged instead of stopping the timer. A single-environment install is unchanged. **Checked:** by `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` (57 groups, including a test that reads the start-up source for the wiring and one that the sweep does not throw on a fresh registry), `check-syntax`, `check-chat-model` and `check-modules`. **Not verified:** a hosted server booting and running the sweep at start.
- **Chat history and module data files are written readable by the server's user only** (GitHub #69). New writes of the chat history and of a module's data file use mode 0600, as `app.json`, `host.json`, `ai.json` and `secrets.key` already did. A file already on disk becomes 0600 the next time it is saved; there is no migration. The check for masked fields in `tools/check-syntax.mjs` is broader, with 12 new self-tests. **Checked:** by `check-syntax`. **Not verified:** the file modes themselves (read as code only, not seen on a disk).
- **A module's live-update events no longer cross to another environment** (hosted server; GitHub #295). The object-link events (`refchange`) came from one source shared by every environment, so a write in one environment could reach another environment's open module stream when the module and scope matched. Each event now carries its environment and a stream hears only its own (`listen(env, fn)` in `server/object-sync.js`). Only pointers crossed, never object text. Checked: a new two-environment test in `tools/check-links.mjs`, which fails with the fix removed; the leak reproduced on `main` with two live hosted environments and stopped with the fix (independent QA). Not checked: the notification and presence streams, and a real LiveKit call.
- **Chat citations open without the Assistant, and on a guest link** (GitHub #395). A citation under an AI answer no longer reads "An object that is gone" when the Assistant module is not installed, and a visitor on a guest link now sees them too. Chat asks the new `POST /api/chat/citations`, which answers like `POST /api/objects/resolve` but does not check for an asking module; space access, the role's read, guest rules and hidden fields still apply ([api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md)). Covered by `tools/check-chat-links.mjs` and `tools/check-access-key.mjs` as the builder reported (not rerun here); not seen in a browser.
- **Phone bars are easier to tap** (GitHub #394). On a phone (640px wide or narrower, measured on the top page), the shared title-bar and toolbar buttons, the view switch and the toolbar search are drawn 44px high instead of 28px (title bar 52px, toolbar 56px, switch track 50px). Desktop sizes are unchanged, and pages that do not load `theme-head.js` (the design-system prototype pages, `keyed.html`) keep them. The focused toolbar search box no longer shows a second inner ring. Covered by the updated `check-canvas`, `check-conformance` and `check-module-chrome` as the builder reported (not rerun here); the sizes were read as CSS, not measured on a phone.
- **Card kind labels read on a light page when the environment's default is Light** (`--card-kind-l` and `--card-disc-l` now follow `data-mode-shown="light"` as well as `data-theme-mode="light"`). `tools/check-light-default.mjs` is new. Checked as reported by the builder; not rerun here.
- **Module guards, a second round** (Polls 1.15.26, Research 0.4.29, Calendar 1.24.32; from the commit message, not read in full by this entry's author). In Polls only the voter removes a vote, and only while the poll is open; once a vote exists the options can only be added to; look-alike keys are refused. Research's "Send to Research" of a picture is taken only by the sender's own page. The Calendar's published feeds write a repeat's skipped days as EXDATE. More special-use address ranges are blocked from link previews. A space's fallback icon is the template's, else a folder.
- **A taken port prints one sentence.** Starting the server on a port that is in use used to end in a TypeError. It now prints "Port 3000 is already in use. Another copy of the server may still be running: close its window or stop it, then start this one again." with the command to find it on Windows, Mac and Linux, and exits; a port the account may not use says to set `PORT` to a free one above 1024. **Checked:** by `tools/check-startup.mjs` (a second server on a taken port, exit code and wording), as reported; not rerun here.
- **Module pages in a frame can load the icons without a session.** `GET /api/icons/:style/:name` needs no sign-in, as the icon font does not, so a guest's pages in a sandboxed frame get their icons. Documented in [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md).
- **Markdown no longer turns underscores inside an address or a word into italics** (SDK, `host.util.markdown`). Code, links and addresses are set aside before bold and italic are looked for, so `.../Belem_Tower` stays a whole link and `snake_case_word` stays as written; `_italic_` counts only at word edges. **Checked:** by `tools/check-travel.mjs` (the Belem_Tower case), as reported; not rerun here.
- **The Calendar's today and chosen day no longer look alike.** See the Calendar page's toolbar entry above; the entry in `known-issues.md` is removed. Read as code and as reported by `check-calendar-todo`; not seen in a browser by this entry's author.
- **The title on the Maps and Places pages no longer shifts 8px after the page loads.** An empty actions group in a title bar kept its gap, so the title ended 12px from the right edge until the **+** arrived. A title bar with no actions now ends 4px from the edge (`.mf-titlebar-tools` is hidden when it holds nothing visible). This was the cause of the intermittent `check-conformance` failure, which was a product defect, not the check; its entry is gone from `known-issues.md`.
- **On a touch screen, search boxes and all text fields are 16px**, so iOS no longer zooms in when one is focused. It is decided by pointer type, not width (`--field-font` is 16px under `(pointer: coarse)`); desktop is unchanged. Not verified on a real iOS or Android device; `check-touch` emulates touch in Chromium.
- **The host console's Host tab wraps long values** instead of scrolling sideways at 360 and 390 wide. The pop-out page now has a viewport tag.
- **In a narrow Planner pane, "Day N of M" no longer breaks into fragments** (Planner 0.15.57).
- **Light-mode card kind labels are slightly darker**, so every kind reaches 4.5:1 (light `--card-kind-l` is 0.38, was 0.4).
- **The Planner hides the Unscheduled group heading when it is alone** (Planner 0.15.56). In a space with phases, the area headed **Unscheduled** no longer repeats the word on its only group.
- **The dark-mode card kind label is lighter**, so every kind reaches 4.5:1 in every theme (dark `--card-kind-l` is 0.88, was 0.78; every kind is at least 4.58:1 in dark).
- **Verify: the call-bars check repeats the pointer's real screen position** when it tests a mousemove that does not move. The check had replayed screen coordinates equal to client coordinates, so it failed; the product ignores a mousemove only when the screen position repeats, and its code is unchanged.
- **The Calendar and Map editors keep the page's layout behind them.** The window now keeps its width and shows the Calendar or Map behind the dimmed editor, instead of shrinking to the editor's size and changing its layout. A window narrower than the editor gets the narrow-layout sheet at that width. See `architecture-module-window.md`. Known problems with it are in `known-issues.md`.
- **Dragging in the Planner no longer shifts the list**, and the banded card's plain head now keeps 4px of room above and below when its title wraps.
- **What was checked for this group of entries.** The commits report `check-travel`, `check-module-host`, `check-narrow`, `check-prototype-parity` and `check-theme-contrast`; none was rerun by this entry's author, so treat them as the builders' reports. **Not verified:** a real call (there is no LiveKit server here), a real phone, and any browser other than Chromium (Firefox and Safari).
- **The Planner's day menu Add works again** (Planner 0.15.22). Its **Add** button opened nothing on some days. Read as code and checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs` as reported; not walked live by this entry's author.
- **Typing a command in an empty Assistant hung the page.** The empty line's own change called the check for an empty chat again, for ever. It now changes only when its words differ.
- **Signed-in people are not capped at five chat streams** and chat streams retry, then say "Live chat is paused. Reload the page to try again." when the server will not take them back. The call timer restarts when a page comes back from the cache.
- **An access key gets a plain 401** ("sign in first") from the routes that need a signed-in person (objects, the module context and list, markers, the action bus, notifications), instead of a 500.
- **Frames.** The app page (`/space.html`, `/`) refuses to be framed. Our own pages that frames hold (`/profile`, `/admin`, the sign-in pages, a module's page, `/calendar`, `/map`) allow this site only. A framed page that would have redirected into a page that refuses frames answers with a short sentence and its status instead. Documented in `architecture-overview.md`.
- **A click in the middle of a call toolbar button always does that button's action;** the chevron is a small corner target. A screen share's lift into the Workspace survives a resize from narrow to wide.
- **The budget amount `1.234,56` is read as 1234.56**, and changing only the currency in Set up keeps the stored amount.
- **Known issues closed:** **Opens with** no longer saves each switch as you click, so it cannot show an older list after a failed save; it saves with the one **Save** in Set up. There is no per-window snap to a grid, so snapped modules no longer come back small after a reload or overlap after a resize.
- A link to another module's object from a frame (the Calendar or Map layout, or a pop-out) asks the Workspace to open it, instead of loading the app inside the frame.
- A bad `Range` request for a file answers 416, and an upload refusal says why in plain words (too big, wrong kind of file). A failed upload removes its part-file safely: the stream is closed before the file is removed. The settings listing sends the folder name and a true shared flag.
- Phase days are read as UTC days everywhere (the sheet, the strip, the Calendar, the Planner); a partial save no longer leaves a false unsaved dot.
- Research cards open in place and a pen edits, where a click used to open the editor (Research 0.4.6). The Calendar's Day view keeps its phase bar in sight (Calendar 1.24.9).
- The map pick belongs to the page that asked for it.
- The Planner is named by its module name (**Itinerary** in the travel template), not the trip's name (Planner 0.15.9).
- The Set up panel's refusal line stands clear of the last hint.
- The Lobby and oversized-request sentences read in plain words.
- Research's window title reads **Research** (or the name the owner gave it), no longer "Research 2" with the number of objects after the name (GitHub #191). Research 0.4.3 (was 0.4.2). Only Research put a count in its title; the count in the header of its page without a titlebar went too. Checked by `tools/check-research.mjs`; read as code by the builder, and passed by quality-assurance.
- With Research off, **Keep** on a note or an event in Chat said "Nothing here can keep that yet." even with the Planner on, which takes both. Keep now finds its place by what modules take. Checked by `tools/check-chat-page.mjs`; verified live by the builder for the preview.
- Fixes from QA of #182 steps 6 and 7 (Calendar 1.23.0, Research 0.3.0, Polls 1.14.0). Items 1 to 3 were verified live by the builder; all five are checked by tool (`check-object-handoff`, `check-research`, `check-travel`, `check-modules`).
  - An event handed to the Calendar whose end is a time with no day of its own, and isn't after the start, now ends the next day (22:00 to 01:00), instead of being given no end.
  - Research's `savePhoto` makes a thumbnail only on the uploader's, an owner's or an admin's page, and otherwise skips it quietly, so the photo shows the whole picture rather than trying and failing; a thumbnail the picture already has is reused.
  - A closed poll offers another module's action only when it can fill every required input: a title, notes, a date (when the winning option has one) and the poll itself.
  - `host.util.detailLines` words a car's `departs` and `arrives` as "Pick-up time" and "Drop-off time", rather than repeating "Pick up" and "Drop off".
  - The bus refuses a `date` or `datetime` input that names a day that doesn't exist (2026-02-31) with 400 and the usual sentence, `<field> must be a date` (or `... a date and time`), where before it was rolled into the next month. `needs` now has both its meanings written down: what a pointed-at object must have, and, on any action, what the request must carry. It was already kept on any action, and the bus still never enforces it.
- A finished poll's **Add it to the calendar** now shows only when the winning option has a date, and puts the poll on the Calendar on that day. Before, it showed on any finished poll and the request was refused for want of a date. Polls 1.14.0, Calendar 1.23.0. Read as code; the Polls form was verified live by the builder, this button not separately.
- A line just before a list in Markdown (a note's "Links:" line, say) is now drawn above the list instead of after it, in Chat, Research and everywhere else `host.util.markdown` draws text. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`.
- Objects dated more than 60 days from the rest of the plan no longer vanish from the Planner. Such an object, saved before, showed on no day and nowhere else; it now shows under **Not on a day yet**, its row reading "Dated <day>, outside the plan", and keeps its date until you change it. Planner 0.12.0. `tools/check-travel.mjs` checks that the page's list includes it by matching the code's text; it was not run in a browser.
- A journey's **Arrival on the ticket** keeps how many days after the departure it lands while the journey is under **Not on a day yet** or on the line, and is dated again from the day it goes back on, instead of losing its day. Planner 0.12.0. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`; the editor's part was not run in a browser.
- Moving a journey to another day (dragging it, **Move to**, changing its day in the editor, or dropping it on a day) now moves its dated **Arrival on the ticket** by the same number of days, rather than leaving the arrival behind on the old date. Planner 0.12.0. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs` for the plan and a drop; the editor's day change is read as code only.
- Opening the editor of an object under **Not on a day yet** and saving no longer moves it to "Before the first day": **When** now shows "Not on a day yet" for it. Planner 0.12.0. Read as code only.
- Asking a module for something from Chat no longer fails with a server error when the input is refused: `POST /api/spaces/:id/action` and `POST /api/spaces/:id/command` answer the refusal's own status and sentence instead of 500, so a command with no text gets 400 "text is needed". A body over 64 KB on these routes and the `/api/bus/` routes gets 413 "that request is over 64 KB". Checked for `/action` and the bus by `tools/check-object-handoff.mjs`; the `/command` case is read as code only.
- Profile's **Other calendars** always says why it can't be used: **Calendar feeds** are off, or the Calendar needs to be approved, turned on, updated or installed in Manage > **Modules**; an owner is told to do it, anyone else that their owner needs to. `GET /api/me/external-calendars` answers `why`. **Calendar feed** now says that Google Calendar reads the address on its own schedule, often every 8 to 24 hours, and Apple Calendar is usually quicker.
- Link previews, Research and chat links now refuse IPv6 literals in addresses (such as `https://[::1]/`), and addresses that resolve to private or special IPv6 ranges, as they did private IPv4 ones.
- Opening a module's page from a space kept its **Back to <space>** link only until the redirect to Calendar or Map: the redirect now keeps the query.
- After **Pop it back in**, the main space bar no longer keeps the pop-out's fold, and Online no longer shows its full words in a narrow pop-out (#160).
- An AI answer's **Keep** shows "Kept" again (#158).
- Map pins sit on their places again, on every Maps surface (a space's canvas, a pop-out, Maps' own page and Map in the top bar). Each pin was drawn lower than its place, further down for each pin before it, because a style gave the pin a position of its own over the one the map library sets (`.pin` in `modules/maps/src/maps.css`). It was already wrong in the previous build. Maps 0.8.0. Read as code and seen by the builder in headless Chromium; the map with WebGL from real tiles was not checked.
- Leaving a space with Maps open no longer logs an error: Maps stops its refresh and its drawing once its markup is gone. Maps 0.8.0.
- A module reminder no longer stops the server. When a module's schedule fired (a Calendar or To-do reminder, say) while anyone it was for had a page open, the whole server stopped and the reminder was lost; a finished map region cut could do the same. It was there before the destinations. Now the schedule timer runs inside its own environment (`moduleHooks.runIn`), every stream listener runs in its own (`listenIn()` in `server/index.js`), a listener that fails is logged and can't stop the server (`tell()` and `safeTick()` in `server/module-hooks.js`), and a notification is stored before anyone is told. Checked by the new `tools/check-hooks.mjs` (4 groups, on a single install and with `BASE_DOMAIN`; it fails on the old code), run again for this entry. Not verified on a real server.
- Saving a place found by the place search now marks the search result as used for anyone who may read Places there, so it is kept when unpicked results are removed. `POST /api/modules/:id/geocode/use` needed the right to change Places at the level of the page, so a result saved into a space from Map in the top bar, by someone who only reads Places for the whole environment, was not marked; it now needs `read`, as the search does. Documented in `api-modules.md`.
- A private conversation no longer tells everyone who is in it. Before, `GET /api/presence` gave anyone signed in, and any guest, where everyone was and every aside's record with its members, private conversations included, and `aside-started` sent a private conversation's members to everyone on the call; the calls cap's refusal named the running space to anyone. Now presence is filtered by membership, a private conversation's `aside-started` carries no members, and the refusal names a space only to its members (see presence by membership under Changed). Checked by `tools/check-presence.mjs`; verified live against the API with a stand-in LiveKit; not tested in a real call.
- Clicking a module card's heading on the spaces page no longer drops the call. While you were in a space, including with the spaces page shown over it, the heading left the page and the call. Now it opens the module's page over the space: you are Away on the call until you go back, and **Back to <space>** returns you. A card's "open in full", a link to a module inside a card, and opening an entry that isn't in a space do the same. When you are in no space, the heading opens the page as before, and Ctrl-, Cmd- or middle-click still opens a new tab. For developers: `openModulePage()` in `public/dashboard.js` sends `app:open-page` with kind `module`, which the space page handles again, deciding with `pageOpens()` in `public/primary-nav.js`. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` and verified in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit; not verified in a real call.
- Going home while you are in a space never drops the call. **Spaces**, the logo and the narrow-layout real iPhone's home entry always keep you on the page, also when home is already showing and from the header of a page opened over the call. Before, clicking one of them again on home reloaded the page and dropped the call. Closing a page you opened from home now leaves you on home and still Away, and Away clears when you go back to the space; before, it cleared while you were still on home. For developers: `goHome()` and `HOME_LINKS` in `public/space.js`, and `closeOverlay()` keeps Away while `viewingHome`. Checked by new cases in `tools/check-nav.mjs` (39 tests) and verified in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit at 1280 and 390 pixels: **Spaces** from the space, **Spaces** again, the logo on home, and the logo inside a page opened over home all stayed on the page with the call connected; closing a page opened from home kept Away, and **Back** on the space card cleared it. Not verified in a real call.
- In a space, the space bar's **Modules** list shows again at desktop widths. With the new top bar (#147) the button looked active but no list appeared, so modules couldn't be shown or hidden; a style added for the top bar clipped the space bar's left zone, where the list hangs. The narrow layout's tab bar was not affected. Now only the top bar's own left zone is clipped (`.topbar > .nav-left`). A new case in `tools/check-nav.mjs` fails if any rule clips the space bar or its zones.
- The top bar no longer crowds between 641 and 800 pixels wide. The old bar's right end (the light or dark switch, Manage, the time, your picture) ran over the **Spaces** link there; the new bar drops your name, then the environment's name, and cuts the place name short instead (see the top bar under Changed). Checked in headless Chromium.
- After being pulled into an aside, the space bar shows **Rejoin call** again, and the aside's name reads "<space> · Aside" instead of just "Aside". Before, **Leave** was the only way out, and it took you out of the space. **Rejoin call** takes you back to the space's call, still on it if you were. The cause: the server tells everyone on the call that an aside started (`aside-started`), the people pulled included, and the page listed the aside at once without the space it came from (its `origin`); a pulled person's join found that entry before `GET /api/presence` answered and kept it. The entry is now marked as a stand-in (`pending`), so presence's full record replaces it (`adoptPendingSpace()` in `public/space.js`). Introduced by 54933f7 (the page no longer waits for LiveKit). Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, which runs the page's `DataReceived` handler against a stand-in call, then a join and presence's answer, and verified in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit. Pressing **Rejoin call** in a real call is not verified (see TODO.md).
- A person who joined the call after you no longer shows "muted" on their tile while their microphone is on (Thomas's real call, 2026-10-01). The mark now follows their real mute state for everyone, whenever they joined. The cause: the mark was refreshed only on mute and unmute events, and someone joining says they are on the call before their microphone is published, so their tile started marked muted, and a microphone published unmuted sends no unmute event. The mark is now read from the microphone's own state (`micMarkedMuted()` in `public/space.js`) and refreshed whenever a microphone is published, unpublished or subscribed, yours too. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, which runs the call's event handlers against a stand-in call (joining, publishing unmuted, muting, unmuting, unpublishing, publishing again, and your own microphone), and verified with a faked LiveKit before and after the fix. Not verified in a real call.
- Away now mutes what you hear as well as your microphone and camera, and nothing opens your microphone until you come back (Thomas's real call, 2026-10-01). Away used to switch off only your own microphone and camera, so you still heard everyone. Now, while you are away, everyone else's audio is muted on your end, including anyone who starts talking after you went away, and the microphone button, **M**, push to talk and joining the call all leave the microphone off; pressing the microphone button or **M** says "away: come back to turn your microphone on" in the status line. Opening your profile or the Manage page over the call, or going to the space list, marks you away as before, so it now mutes what you hear too until you come back; Thomas confirmed this is intended (2026-10-01). **Back** brings back what you heard (unless you had deafened yourself) and the microphone and camera that were on before. Joining the call while away leaves the microphone off, and **Back** turns it on unless you use push to talk. The away button's tooltip now reads "Away: pauses your mic and camera, mutes what you hear, and lets everyone know", and "Back: unpause your mic, camera and sound, and let everyone know" while away. For developers: `hearingOff()` and `applyHearing()` in `public/space.js`. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` (what you hear, the microphone button and the join while away) and `tools/check-module-host.mjs` (push to talk opens nothing while away), and verified with a faked LiveKit before and after the fix. Not verified in a real call.
- Someone who joins the call after you went away now sees you as away (found while fixing away, 2026-10-01). Before, going away told only the people on the call at that moment, so anyone who joined, reloaded or reconnected later saw your tile as normal. Now your tile shows "Away", or your away message, for everyone, whenever they arrive. Leaving the call while away clears the mark for everyone. After a dropped connection comes back, others keep your tile, and the server keeps counting you on the call. For developers: away is carried as two participant attributes, `away` (`on` or `off`) and `awayMessage` (`''` for a plain Away), set beside `call` (`awayAttributes()`, `syncAwayAttributes()` and `showAway()` in `public/space.js`); they are said again on connecting and on joining the call, and after a full reconnect `syncCallAttributes()` sends `call` again together with them (before, `call` was not said again, so others might drop your tile), and `stopCall()` clears them with `call` set to `off`. The old one-time `away` data message is still sent and read for one release, for pages still on the old script. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, which runs going away and coming back, the reconnect, and the call's event handlers (a late joiner already away, a change of the attributes, the old message), against a stand-in call. Not verified in a real call.
- The camera stays off while you are away (found while fixing away, 2026-10-01). The camera button, **V** and your **Toggle video key** no longer turn it on; they say "away: come back to turn your camera on" in the status line. Changing the camera or the video quality in Settings or your profile while away no longer turns the camera on; the change is applied when you come back, or, if your camera was off, the next time you turn it on (before, turning it on opened the old camera). Going **Back** and then away again quickly no longer leaves your microphone on. For developers: `toggleCam()`, `restartCamera()`, `keepCameraOffWhileAway()` and `keepMicOffWhileAway()` in `public/space.js`. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` (the button, a camera change while away applied on **Back** or on the next turn-on, and the microphone after a quick **Back** and away). Not verified in a real call.
- Videos in the pop-out window no longer freeze when the main window is minimised or covered, which happened most in the installed app (Thomas's real call, 2026-10-01). The cause: LiveKit's adaptive stream pauses a video it thinks nobody can see, and it judges that by the main page, so a hidden main window paused every video, and it makes an exception for the browser's own picture-in-picture but not for this pop-out window. While a video from someone else is in the pop-out, Collaborator now tells LiveKit it is shown in a window of its own, at its size there; popping back in drops that. For developers: `keepPoppedVideoLive()`, `keepPoppedVideosLive()` and `dropPoppedWatches()` in `public/space.js`, which rely on LiveKit's internal `observeElementInfo()` (see TODO.md). Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, and verified with a faked LiveKit before and after the fix. Not verified in a real call or in the installed app.
- The space bar's Modules list and the who's here list now open above a module floating or snapped on the canvas, instead of underneath it (also in the pop-out window); in the narrow layout, an open who's here list no longer shows through your profile page. The cause was the header's stacking order: the header is its own layer (20), under the modules (25), so the lists could not draw above a module however high their own z-index (60). The lists now share the account menu's level, a `--z-menu` token (26, above the module layer and under a page opened over the call) in `public/style.css`, and while either list is open the header rises to it. Guarded by `tools/check-nav.mjs`. Verified live in headless Chromium before and after the fix (floating, snapped and docked modules at 1280, 900 and 720 pixels wide, the pop-out window, and the profile page over the call at 1280 and 390); checked by a tool. Not checked in a real call.
- A join cancelled while the browser was still getting the microphone no longer publishes it. Hanging up, switching the Conference off or losing the connection while a join was still under way could leave your microphone published, and you shown as on the call, with the Conference gone. Each step of a join now checks whether it was stopped meanwhile, and backs out, releasing the microphone. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit; not checked with a real microphone in a real call.
- Push to talk does nothing when you are not on the call. Holding the key out of the call could open and publish your microphone while you were only in the space. Letting go still always stops talking. Checked by `tools/check-module-host.mjs`.
- With push to talk on and its key at Space (the default), Space presses a button, menu entry or other control you reached with the keyboard in a space, instead of talking. Since the start (b37b0a1) push to talk took Space everywhere outside a text field. Now Space or Enter presses a control reached by Tab or the arrow keys, and picks an entry in an open menu. On the page itself, on a link, or on a button you just clicked with the mouse (including after picking from a menu with the mouse, and after clicking a button you had first reached by keyboard) the key still talks, so clicking the microphone and then holding Space talks. Typing in a field is unchanged. With another push-to-talk key, such as T, Space always presses controls; with Enter as the key, Enter on a link talks rather than following it. Fixed in `public/space.js` (`pressesControl`, which tracks whether focus arrived by keyboard or by pointer), in the space and its pop-out windows; no module version changes. Documented in `userguide-call.md` ("Push to talk"); the entry is off [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md). Verified live in headless Chromium, with the microphone call spied on and "in a space" set by hand (no LiveKit server here); guarded by `tools/check-module-host.mjs` (35 groups). Not checked: a real call, the pop-out windows (read as code only; the check pins that they are wired), Firefox and Safari. Still open: the SDK menu's link entries don't open on Space ([TODO](documentation/TODO.md)).
- Push to talk no longer leaves the microphone open. Since the start (b37b0a1) talking could carry on after you let go of the key if focus had moved into a text field meanwhile, if a modifier such as Shift came down before you let go, or if you switched window or tab, or left the page, while holding it. Letting go of the key that started talking now always stops it, and so does switching window or tab, hiding the tab or leaving the page. Fixed in `public/space.js` (`releaseTalk`, and the key's code kept while it is held), in the space and its pop-out windows. Documented in `userguide-call.md` ("Push to talk"). Verified live in headless Chromium with the microphone call spied on; the window switch and the hidden tab were fired by hand, not done for real. Guarded by `tools/check-module-host.mjs`. Not checked: a real call, real window switching, the pop-out windows (read as code only), Firefox and Safari.
- A guest's header, and a guest's menu in the narrow layout, no longer end with a divider before nothing. A tool that other code hides, such as your picture for a guest, now counts as not shown, and the bar is drawn again when it is hidden or shown (`isShown()` and a watch on `hidden` in `public/nav-bar.js`). Verified live in headless Chromium on a guest's page (the pages were checked at 390, 640, 641 and 1280 pixels wide); checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs`.
- The header's menu in the narrow layout works from the keyboard. Opening it puts focus on its first entry; Tab moves through the entries and back to the menu button; Escape closes it and puts focus back on the button; Tabbing out of the menu or its button closes it. Widening the window past the narrow width closes the menu, and an account menu open when the window narrows to the narrow width closes too, with focus left on something you can see. Verified live in headless Chromium at 390, 640, 641 and 1280 pixels wide. Not checked with a real screen reader.
- Moving **Grid size** in the space bar no longer makes snapped modules overlap. Each snapped module moves to the cells nearest where it was put and keeps about its size on screen, not its number of cells. No two overlap while the canvas can hold them all at their smallest size: if rounding to whole cells leaves no room, they all shrink a little together, so equal modules stay equal; then they go in rows at their smallest size; they share cells only when even that does not fit. Moving the slider back gives the old sizes and places back exactly. While you drag, the modules already sit where letting go leaves them, and the layout is saved once, when you let go (each arrow key press counts as letting go). It stays fast, a few milliseconds a step, and a crowded canvas no longer freezes. The grid's arithmetic moved to `public/snap-grid.js` (`resettle`), and each module remembers the box it was put in (`placed`). Documented in `architecture-canvas.md` ("Free or snapped") and `userguide-call.md`. Verified live in headless Chromium: four modules from 130 to 50 to 320 and back to 130, by keyboard and by mouse, never overlapping and back in identical boxes; five and six modules sharing only where they can't fit; a drag followed by a size change; a module snapped on its own; the snap switch off and on; and an older saved layout without `placed`. Checked by tools: `tools/check-canvas.mjs` runs the real snap-all tilings of 2 to 8 modules on several canvas sizes at every grid size, checks that equal boxes stay equal, and holds the arithmetic to time budgets; QA also ran 3,000 random layouts. Not checked: a real call (no LiveKit server here) or a phone with touch. Two older problems remain, after a reload and after a window resize; see [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- In the narrow layout, the header's menu no longer runs 16 pixels past the right edge of the screen. Verified live in headless Chromium at 390 pixels wide.
- A page opened over a call keeps its way back. Going from your profile to Manage, or from Manage to **View profile**, kept the page over the call but lost its **Back to** button. **Sign out** on such a page now signs the whole window out, not only the page over the call. Verified live in headless Chromium, except over a real call (no LiveKit server here); read as code there.
- **Clear** in a module's own window works again (the title bar's **…**, **Clear**, then "Clear everything and start again?"). Since the Clear menu came in (2aaf316) it stopped with "scope is not defined" and cleared nothing. It now clears that module's data in the space the window is open in, or in the environment when the window is not in a space. A role without the right still gets "your role can't do that in this module". Fixed in `public/module.js`; no module version changes. Guarded by `tools/check-module-host.mjs`, and verified live: an admin in a space, an admin with no space, a guest refused, and a module kept for the environment only.
- An install whose environment was never renamed now takes the product's name when `PRODUCT_NAME` is set but empty or only spaces. It kept the old placeholder name, "Coffee Pub Tavern". `PRODUCT_NAME` unset, empty or blank now means the default, Collaborator, everywhere, by one rule (`productName()` in `server/product-name.js`, where the default is written once). Documented in `architecture-environments.md`. Checked by `tools/check-templates.mjs`, which starts servers with `PRODUCT_NAME` set to `""` and `"   "`, and verified live: a single install with each value answered "Collaborator" for the product name and the environment's name in `/api/branding` and started with "Collaborator v0.4.0"; a hosted server with `" "` answered "Collaborator" in `/api/product`.
- A space's **Turn AI off in this space** is kept after a restart. Since the switch was added (b37b0a1, PR #78) it was dropped every time the data file was loaded, so AI came back on after a restart and the next save erased the setting. Checked by `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`, which restarts a server and confirms the setting is kept and AI still refused, and verified live.
- The Chat, Assistant, Themes and Templates guides name the files as the server writes and reads them: now `.objects.json`, `.theme.json` and `.template.json` (they said `.magpie-`), and the Assistant guide calls the pasted blocks `objects` blocks. Checked against `server/object-format.js`, `server/theme-file.js` and `server/template-file.js`.
- The help for Research's **Fetch link previews** setting no longer calls a link a card. It now reads "The link shows the picture and that description.", which also makes the names check pass again after PR #137. Research 0.2.30. Checked by tools (the names check and the other checks) and seen served by a running server; not viewed in a browser.
- Portrait size is a conference setting, under Call features. The conference tile and Coffee Pub Studio's participant box both use it. Stream does not keep its own. Stream 0.1.7.
- A conference portrait uses the Online picture in the same order as the other views: this space's own, then that person's, then the default set. The profile photo is used only when none of those exist.
- A conference portrait with a background is a centered picture over that background, the same size when the camera is off and when that person is away. It is not stretched to fill the tile.
- A planner card's note is drawn with the same markdown as chat and research, and a link in it opens in a new window. Planner 0.10.6.
- A note kept from chat takes its type from the imported icon, or from its kind when the icon is a general one: a flight is a plane, a hotel stays a hotel, a meal is a meal. Research 0.2.25.
- In card view a research note, link or answer shows its whole text, so a long one is taller than a short one. The list still shows one line. Research 0.2.24.
- A research note, link or answer is drawn with the same markdown as chat, so bold, lists and links show as such. The note editor has the same Bold, Italic, Code and list buttons. Research 0.2.23.
- Chat in grid mode fills its window: the messages use the space, and the input sits across the bottom. Docked, it was already fine.
- A research note has a type, chosen from icons: a general note, a flight, a hotel, a map, and others. The title sits beside that icon. Research 0.2.22.
- An import in chat is a message titled **Imported**, with the same header as any other message: portrait, time, private or public, and the menu.
- Turning the grid on keeps a layout the user already placed, each window on the cells nearest where it is, clear of the others. Windows that were never placed are tiled across the canvas instead of stacked. Dragging or resizing one remembers that place for the next time the grid is on.
- Only the person who posted a chat message can change it between private and public. Everyone else still sees which it is.
- A module's buttons stay inside the module. They are icons, and as the module runs out of room they fold into **...** from the left, the same way the conference bar does. Bring in research uses icon buttons and folds the same way. Calendar 1.18.2, To-do 1.11.17, Polls 1.12.19, Planner 0.10.5. Research's bar already had icons; the host now draws the icon alone.
- Opening Bring in research on an empty chat no longer squashes the formatting row, and closing it brings that row back. The import panel shrinks and scrolls instead of pushing the row out of the column.
- An AI question and its answer keep the time they were written. Opening the space was painting every saved turn as now, because the stored time was ignored.
- A command for a module that is not open asks what to do: add it and open the module, add it and leave the module closed, or cancel.
- A chat message shows its date and its time.
- Chat scrolls again. A long thread was growing the column and the bottom was cut off, including the object cards. A sender's portrait is their photo.
- Chat's **Save the chat**, **Delete the chat** and **Clear your AI thread** are in the chat header's **…** menu, under that button. **Delete the chat** is for an owner or a moderator. It removes every shared message for everyone, and every private AI thread in that space, so the chat stays empty after a refresh. An owner or a moderator can delete any one message. Anyone else can delete only a message they sent, including a question or answer in their own AI thread. That delete stays gone after a refresh. **Clear your AI thread** still leaves answers already shared in the chat.
- The title bar **…** menu opens under that button. A docked module has no box of its own, so the menu was measured from the left edge of the canvas and opened over the chat. It is now placed from the button, the same way a menu inside a module is.
- Clear sits in the host's own **…** menu, under that button, on every installed module on a space (and in a module's own window). The conference and the chat do not have it. **Clear**, then **Clear everything and start again?**, deletes everything that module holds in that space. A dated object's twin goes with it. The Planner no longer draws its own clear menu. Planner 0.10.4. Checked by `tools/check-links.mjs` and `tools/check-travel.mjs`. Not watched in a browser.
- An edit window in a module stays open when you click outside it or drag a selection past its edge. Save, Cancel or Close is what dismisses it. The Planner, Calendar, To-do, Polls, Places and Research editors all did this. Calendar 1.18.1, To-do 1.11.16, Polls 1.12.18, Places 0.8.19 and Research 0.2.21.
- The old product name, Magpie, no longer appears in anything a person reads (#110). Those sentences use `PRODUCT_NAME`, which defaults to Collaborator. Theme, template and research files that still use the old keys or file names are refused. The formats were first renamed after the current product; they are now named by their kind instead, and files and answers named after either product are refused (see "Theme, template and research files ... are named by what they are" under Changed). `tools/check-names.mjs` fails if a product name is written back into the code (see the product name guard under Changed). Planner 0.10.2 and To-do 1.11.15 are comment-only. Checked by `tools/check-themes.mjs`, `tools/check-templates.mjs`, `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`, `tools/check-object-format.mjs`, `tools/check-modules.mjs` and `tools/check-names.mjs`. Not watched in a browser. The guides, the architecture and API documents and the design system no longer say the old name, apart from the container names and data paths an old install still has; several plans still do. The logo images still read it (see known-issues).
- A Planner trip no longer grows past 60 days, which is as long as the plan shows. A day that does not fit is left off, and the page says so. Planner 0.10.1. Fixes #76. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`. The note on the page was read as code only.
- The call page and the space list no longer sit still for 10 seconds when LiveKit does not answer (GitHub #71, pull request #93). The server gives up on the call service after 2 seconds, and `GET /api/presence` and `GET /api/status` then answer with nobody in a call. Joining a space, a reload back into one and the join screen no longer wait on presence; it fills in when it answers and tries again on the next poll. Documented in `architecture-overview.md` and `architecture-environments.md`. Verified: checked by a tool on a server pointed at an address that does not answer (`/api/presence` in 2017 ms, `/api/status` in 2046 ms, both 200; the case is in `tools/check-host-registry.mjs`), and the full `npm run check` passed on the Mac. The page's give-up was read as code only. Not verified on the real server or in a real call.
- A server with environments that was started without `BASE_DOMAIN` wrote a second, empty install into its data folder, and every later start with `BASE_DOMAIN` then refused (GitHub #90, PR #92). Now a start without `BASE_DOMAIN` on a data folder with `host.json` or `environments/` stops with "This data folder belongs to a server with environments: set BASE_DOMAIN." and writes nothing. Documented in `architecture-environments.md` and `userguide-getting-started.md`. Checked by tools: `npm run check` passed on the PR branch, including the new case in `tools/check-host-registry.mjs`. Not verified on the real server.

### Changed
- **The repository and the container image are renamed `coffee-pub-chameleon`.** The repository is `Drowbe/coffee-pub-chameleon` and the image is `ghcr.io/drowbe/coffee-pub-chameleon`. The documentation links and the server's address in its user-agent use the new name.
  - **For hosts.** Change the `image:` line in your compose file to `ghcr.io/drowbe/coffee-pub-chameleon:sha-<7>`. The old image name is not updated any more; an install that keeps it keeps running but gets no new builds. GitHub redirects the old repository address; it does not redirect the old image name. The data folders and container names are unchanged.
  - **Checked:** code checks by the builder, as reported: `check-compose`, `check-geocode`, `check-product-name`, `check-product-pages`, `check-names`, `check-module-versions` and `check-syntax`. **Not verified:** a real image build and push under the new name (needs GitHub Actions after the rename), wiki publishing to the new address, and the new package's visibility.
- **A shorter compose file, and a settings reference** (`docker-compose.yml` went from 140 lines to 70). The file now holds only what a server must set. The app service is `chameleon` (container `chameleon-app`) and the media service keeps the name `livekit` (container `chameleon-livekit`). Data is at `/share/appdata/coffee-pub-chameleon` and the Vault's files at `/share/appdata/coffee-pub-chameleon-vault` (`VAULT_DIR=/app/vault`, with its own volume line). The image is pinned: `ghcr.io/drowbe/coffee-pub-collaborator:sha-CHANGE_ME`, never `:latest` on a NAS; the image keeps the repository's name for now. `BASE_DOMAIN` and `PRODUCT_DOMAIN` are commented examples, and every other optional setting moved out of the file into the new [Settings reference](documentation/userguides/userguide-settings-reference.md), each with its default checked against `server/`. [Getting Started](documentation/userguides/userguide-getting-started.md) follows the new names, says how to find the tag, and says again that the app starts without LiveKit and what the admin does on one environment and on several. In the architecture overview, "Hosting" uses the new service name.
  - **For hosts.** An existing install keeps working as it is. To use the new file, copy your values across, pin the image tag, and note that the container names change if you recreate (an install that still names `collaborator-app` or `collaborator-livekit` keeps them, and its paths, working). Copy the Vault's volume line too, or the Vault's files get an anonymous volume.
  - **Checked:** `tools/check-compose.mjs` (48 checks) and the builder's own checks, as reported; the defaults in the settings reference were read in `server/index.js` by this entry's author (`HOST_MFA_REQUIRED` is `false` in the code; the old compose comment's `true` was an example). `npm run check:docs` passes. **Not verified:** the new file started live under Container Station, and a real LiveKit call.
- **The product is renamed Coffee Pub Chameleon** (`plan-rename-chameleon.md`, step 1: the name only). The name setting `PRODUCT_NAME` now defaults to "Coffee Pub Chameleon", written in full in titles, the logo area, the README, the wiki and first mentions, and Chameleon after that. The old name was Collaborator. The repository, the image, the app icon and the logo are not renamed yet; those are later steps.
  - **For hosts.** Change `PRODUCT_NAME` in your compose file to "Coffee Pub Chameleon" (`docker-compose.yml` now says so); a file that still sets "Collaborator" overrides the new default and keeps the old name. Names already stored in environments do not change. A branded version, such as one named "Travel Pub", shows that name everywhere.
  - **What changed for people.** Manage > About prints the product's name once; the fixed line "A Coffee Pub product" is gone. The app derives the short form by dropping a leading "Coffee Pub ", so a name without that prefix is its own short form. The documentation and the README use the new name (the repository, image and wiki addresses keep `coffee-pub-collaborator`, and the companion app Studio is not touched).
  - **For developers.** `DEFAULT_PRODUCT_NAME` in `server/product-name.js` is "Coffee Pub Chameleon"; the new `shortProductName()` gives "Chameleon", and `productShortName` is in `/api/branding`, `/api/host/settings` and `/api/product`. `tools/check-product-name.mjs` is new. Documented in [architecture-environments](documentation/architecture/architecture-environments.md) and the [getting started guide](documentation/userguides/userguide-getting-started.md).
  - **Checked:** by tools, run by the builder and by the project manager: `tools/check-product-name.mjs` (17 assertions), `check-names` (only the 2 root-only cases fail, as expected in a container running as root), `check-startup`, `check-compose`, `check-host-registry`, `check-product-pages` and `check-docs`. **Not verified:** a real call (there is no LiveKit server here), the installed app's icon and short name in the manifest (the front end is not changed yet), and the repository and image rename (a later step). The documentation wording was read in context and not checked by any tool beyond `check-docs`.
- A few sentences in the module host, Clear and Undo, and their check name the space, the module and the rename through the environment's word helpers rather than typing the words (read from the commit's list of files and subject, not from a walk of the sentences).
- **The weekly warnings in [architecture-teams-and-budgets](documentation/architecture/architecture-teams-and-budgets.md) are at about 70% and 80%** of the plan, under the 85% ceiling, where they were 50% and 60% (recorded from the project manager's instruction; Thomas to confirm).
- **Clear and Undo, hardened** (To-do 1.15.25, Polls 1.15.28, Calendar 1.24.35, Budget 0.1.22, Places 0.12.20, Research 0.4.31, Planner 0.16.4). Several things the first entry got wrong or left open. **Undo works only on the page that cleared:** the token lives in that page, so closing or reloading it ends the chance to undo although the server holds the data until the time is over; the dialog now says "You can undo it for N seconds, until you leave this page." and N is the module's real time, not always 30. **A refused Clear is read in the dialog** and the dialog stays; a failed Undo keeps its note and can be pressed again. **The budget is fairer:** five clears a minute for a module in an environment, but one person may take 3 and members and guests together 3, so two are always left for a moderator, an owner or the admin; a refused clear gives its count back. The 409 sentence counts to the time the note shows, and in the few seconds of grace after it says when you can clear again. **A clear ends early when its module is turned off,** including by an update or rollback that turns it off for approval, and the modules that need it. **Every module that keeps data now handles the `changes` event** (one pass, drawn once; a big event that comes without values is read again), the Undo note sits at the bottom edge so it does not cover a title bar's menu, and **Clear** appears when a module that was empty gets data. A big Clear costs the same with many streams open as with one. For developers: `host.on('changes')` entries may have no `value` when the batch is over 256 KB; the SDK reads them for a module with no listener, and a `change` replay carries `batch: { count, last }`. See [userguide-modules](documentation/userguides/userguide-modules.md), [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md), "Runtime routes" and "The `changes` event", and [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md), "Live changes". **Checked:** by `tools/check-clear-undo.mjs` and `tools/check-undo-page.mjs` (headless Chromium, if installed), as reported by the builders; not rerun by this entry's author.
- **Documentation:** [userguide-vault](documentation/userguides/userguide-vault.md) is new. [architecture-ai](documentation/architecture/architecture-ai.md) is rewritten for slice 1; [architecture-modules](documentation/architecture/architecture-modules.md) gains "Clear and Undo" and "Library modules: the Vault"; [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md) gains "What AI may see, and AI use" and "Library routes"; [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md) gains the Files, Time fields and Picking an object sections; the Chat, Assistant, Modules, Spaces and Planner guides and [design-theme](documentation/designsystem/design-theme.md) are brought up to date. The statuses of the AI and Vault plans say what is built.
- **Documentation, the Vault's links and sharing, Clear, and module guards.** [userguide-vault](documentation/userguides/userguide-vault.md) gains where a file is used, missing files, sharing and the notes for the person running the server; [userguide-planner](documentation/userguides/userguide-planner.md) gains "Attach files to a stay or a journey"; [userguide-modules](documentation/userguides/userguide-modules.md) and [userguide-getting-started](documentation/userguides/userguide-getting-started.md) say the Undo is the page's, the clear budget, the second volume, and what a backup does not carry; [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md) gains "Share links", "The `changes` event", the file kind and the `kind` and `op` of `POST /api/objects/links`; [api-module-sdk](documentation/api/api-module-sdk.md) gains "Linking files from your objects" and the `changes` and `library` events; [architecture-modules](documentation/architecture/architecture-modules.md) and [design-theme](documentation/designsystem/design-theme.md) follow. The data guards section now lists three fields the code has read since the guards were built and the documents did not: `only`, `settled` and `linkImage`, with the Polls and Budget rules as examples. The known issue "The Vault checks for new files every 15 seconds" is removed (the server now pushes file changes).
- **AI follows one rule, and asks the service more carefully** (Assistant 0.1.25, Research 0.4.28). One rule now serves Chat's `/ai` and every module's ask: signed in and not a guest, the space not turned off, the role's **Use AI in modules**, then the service itself (set up, its key readable, the month's allowance). **Use AI in modules** now starts on for moderators and members (it was off for everyone), still never for guests. `GET /api/spaces/:id/ai` and `GET /api/modules/:id/ai` answer `{ available, why, reason }` with `reason` one of `guest`, `space-off`, `role`, `not-set-up` or `allowance`, and Chat says why before you type. Chat's `/ai` takes only objects from the current space (400 "only objects from this space can be sent to the AI"), and a module asked from the environment's or a person's own page refuses an object from a space with AI turned off or where the role may not use AI. Pasting research from another AI is no longer counted as AI use: it works in a space with AI turned off. The old `/ai/thread` routes are gone (Chat has kept the thread as private messages since the move).
  - **What is sent.** Instructions and data travel in separate messages: the system turn holds the instructions, the user turns hold only the objects (between numbered markers) and the question, escaped so nothing in them can close a marker or open a code fence. The prompt's example object is made of placeholders, and an object whose title is still a placeholder is dropped, so the example never comes back as an answer. An answer that ran out of room is flagged, keeps its complete objects and says so.
  - **Money and keys.** A failed ask is given back to the person's six a minute only when the service did no work (nothing sent, a refused address, a connection that failed before an answer, a busy or down service, a refused key); a bad ask is refused before it counts. The environment's monthly token allowance and, on a host, the plan's monthly AI calls answer 429. A saved key stays with its address: changing the service or the address drops it, and a managed Other service's key from the server's environment goes only to its own address. A key with a line break is refused when typed.
  - **On a host with environments**, the address an owner types for an Other service must be `https` and not private, loopback, link-local or shared, nor any IPv6 form that reaches one; it is checked when saved and again at every connection, and a refusal says only "That address cannot be used." `AI_ALLOW_HTTP=1` and `AI_ALLOW_PRIVATE_ADDRESSES=1` relax it for a developer running a host on one machine, and the server prints a warning at start for each that is set (both are commented examples in `docker-compose.yml`). Turning AI off and on again leaves the modules as they were; a module whose AI hook is `"optional"` stays on.
  - **Not built.** The plan's slice 1 as written (a prompt registry, a context builder, a policy layer, provider adapters, a fake model, `AI_LOG_PROMPTS`, `AI_PROMPT_VERSIONS`, What AI may see) is not in the code. This entry is the must-fixes from the audit that landed. See [architecture-ai](documentation/architecture/architecture-ai.md) and [api-modules](documentation/api/api-modules.md), "Chat routes".
  - **Checked:** by tools, as reported (`tools/check-ai.mjs`, `check-ai-prompts.mjs`, `check-ai-page.mjs`, `check-chat-model`, `check-chat-page`, `check-one-input`, `check-object-format`), against a stand-in service on this machine; not rerun by this entry's author. No real AI service was called, and the address guard was not tried against a real host.
- **Window frames on a space's Workspace share the page's event stream.** A Map or Calendar window used to open an event stream of its own, and with the page's other streams that used all six connections a browser allows to one site, so the Map window never finished loading. The page now holds one stream per view (the space's and the page's own ride one connection, `?space=<id>&page=1`), a frame asks the page over `postMessage` for what it needs, and every event has an id the same on every stream, so moving from one stream to another hears each thing once. A frame whose page does not answer within about ten seconds opens a stream of its own. A popped-out call's frames hear the page that opened them. `ModuleLinks.set` gives each links event its own id. Documented in [architecture-modules](documentation/architecture/architecture-modules.md), "Live changes". **Checked:** by `tools/check-streams.mjs` (the hub and relay against a stand-in clock; the server's streams; in headless Chromium at most 3 open streams, both windows loaded, a change from a second session reaching the Map window), as reported; not rerun here. Not tried over HTTP/2 or on a real phone.
- **Cards have a stacked head and a solid circle icon.** On a banded card the kind (11px capitals, in its colour) sits above a 16px bold title that wraps, and the icon is a solid circle (36px in the head, 28px in a row) in the kind's colour with the icon in the page colour, not a tinted tile. Under 300px wide the icon sits above the text on every module. See `design-theme.md`, "The banded card".
- **The Planner lines up and reads in time order** (Planner 0.15.55). The time column reads from, to, (length), on three lines, or on one line above the card in a narrow Planner; the **+** between days sits midway; phase bars are full width, 32px high, with a flag or chequered flag, the name and the date; a Planner narrower than 720px uses the narrow layout (**Add** shows only its **+**, taps are 44px); a docked 256px column gives the day heading a layout of its own; and a card from another module, such as the Calendar, says "from Calendar" above its title. See `userguide-planner.md`.
- **Unscheduled is a default phase** (Planner 0.15.55). An object with no phase, one whose phase is no longer listed and one dated outside the plan (unless it has a listed phase of its own) are under an **Unscheduled** group, listed first, and a new object starts there. It no longer follows the calendar into today's phase. It is not in the status strip, the date strip or the phase settings. The object form's **Phase** lists **Unscheduled** first. Documented in `userguide-planner.md`, `architecture-modules.md` and `plan-planner-phases.md`.
- **Muted text is easier to read in Strong Coffee's light mode.** Muted text is `#63564b` (5.07:1 on a card) and the accent fill `#a6682a` (4.53:1 with white text on it). Some muted text on tinted surfaces is still under 4.5:1; see `known-issues.md`.
- **Small buttons have a 44px tap area on a phone**, counting the border, so they are easier to hit without changing how they look.
- **One segmented switch, and a quicker design check** (Planner 0.15.48). The Planner's **How you get there** now uses the shared segmented switch. The switch (`.mf-switch`) is a radio group: arrow keys move and choose, **Home** and **End** jump, and **Tab** leaves it; the old joined switch is gone. A count badge is a circle for one digit and a small corner for two or more, and chips use the small corner radius.
