The design system check now loads the production page and its snapshot in four scenarios (1280 and 390, light and dark) in about 4 minutes instead of 27; `npm run check` runs 1280:light, `npm run check:design` all four, and `.github/workflows/design-check.yml` runs them on GitHub. **Checked:** read from the commits only; the check and the workflow were not rerun here, and the workflow has not yet run on GitHub.
- **Collaborator works without LiveKit** (GitHub #376, `plan-realtime-optional-livekit.md`, steps 1 to 7; Stream 0.1.15). Entering a space, who is here, chat, away and asides no longer go through LiveKit. LiveKit now carries only the call's audio and video, and a server without it starts, runs and is useful: spaces, chat, presence and modules all work. A person connects to LiveKit only when they press **Join the call**.
  - **What a person sees.** Entering and leaving a space need no call service. On a server with no LiveKit keys the space has no Conference, **Start call**, **Join the call** or asides, and **Manage** > **Modules** says "Voice and video are not set up on this server" on the Conference card, with its switch off. A kick now also removes the person from the call if they are on it, and ends their streams at once. A picture in the chat is shown live and kept in the server's memory for a short time (15 minutes by default), not stored on disk; a page that comes later within that time can still see it, and it can be deleted by whoever sent it or by a moderator.
  - **For the person running the server.** `LIVEKIT_API_KEY`, `LIVEKIT_API_SECRET`, `LIVEKIT_HOST` and `LIVEKIT_API_URL` are optional; without them the log says "No LiveKit: voice and video are off." and `docker-compose.yml` says LiveKit is the part you add for video. `CHAT_STREAMS_PER_PERSON` and `CHAT_STREAMS_PER_GUEST_LINK` are now `LIVE_STREAMS_PER_PERSON` and `LIVE_STREAMS_PER_GUEST_LINK` (same defaults, 50 and 25); the old names are not read, so set the new ones if you had set the old. New: `CHAT_PICTURE_MAX_MB` (default 3), the most one chat picture may weigh.
  - **API** (`api-modules.md`, "The live stream"). New: `GET /api/live` (server-sent: presence, the chat notices including `chat-picture`, the asides' nudges, and `removed` with the reason `access` or `kicked`; `ready` carries `stream`; a guest sends `?pass=`), `PATCH /api/spaces/:id/presence` (away and its message, on the call; 409 with no open stream) and `POST /api/spaces/:id/chat/pictures` (415 for a wrong type, 413 over the limit). Changed: `POST /api/call/join` now answers `{ ok, token, livekitUrl, call }` and is the only way a person gets a token; `POST /api/token` is an OBS viewer's only (400 for a person); `POST /api/guest-join` answers `{ spaceId, spaceName, permissions, guest: { id, name, pass } }` with no token; with no call service the call's routes answer 503 "voice and video are not set up on this server" (asides with the Conference off answer 403 "voice and video are off"). Some sentences changed: recall 400 "you need to be in a space yourself to recall anyone", return 400 "you are not in an aside", kick 404 "they are not in a space right now" and 403 "you are not in their space", mute 404 "they are not in a call". Removed with no alias: `GET /api/spaces/:id/chat/stream`, and the `call` and away participant attributes at LiveKit. `host.media.watch` reports `unavailable` and `reason` when there is no call service (`api-module-sdk.md`); Stream 0.1.15 draws pictures from presence without one.
  - **After the second QA.** A stream whose page falls too far behind is dropped instead of piling up in the server (`LIVE_STREAM_BUFFER_MB`, 8); the page reconnects. Pictures have a limit a minute for a person or guest link (`CHAT_PICTURE_MB_PER_MINUTE`, 20), and so does saying away or on the call (`PRESENCE_CHANGES_PER_MINUTE`, 60); a person over either gets 429. A guest's chat name, in text and pictures, now comes from the guest pass, never from what the page sends. Home's chat refuses pictures. A failed join is a notice in Chat's status line, a failed mute says so, and a guest whose link was turned off is told so. Read as code and checked by the tools above; not tried against a live call.
  - **After the third QA** (`2ba5f56`, `0199288`, `5fb2abf`). A chat picture no longer travels on the live stream: `chat-picture` is `{ id, by, who, file, mime, size, url, at }`, `POST /api/spaces/:id/chat/pictures` answers `{ id, url }`, and each reader fetches the bytes from the new `GET /api/spaces/:id/chat/pictures/:pictureId` with their own access (401, 403, or 404 `that picture is no longer available` once it has expired, been pushed out or deleted), so a slow reader is never sent megabytes. The bytes are held in memory only: `CHAT_PICTURE_KEEP_MINUTES` (15), and past `CHAT_PICTURE_SPACE_MB` (30), `CHAT_PICTURE_ENVIRONMENT_MB` (100) or `CHAT_PICTURE_SERVER_MB` (200) the oldest goes first. `PRESENCE_CHANGES_PER_MINUTE` now counts per person per space, and a refused change uses none of it. A failed call join is said where the person is looking: Chat's status line, or a toast at narrow width or with the column collapsed. Read as code and names checked against it; not run.
  - **Not built:** stored chat pictures (step 8), and the call as a module (`plan-call-as-module`).
  - **Checked:** by tools, with no LiveKit server, as reported by the builders and the commits `32cf2c8` to `6b980c5`: the new `tools/check-live.mjs` (replaces `check-chat-stream` and `check-guest-chat`), `tools/check-startup.mjs` (the server starting with no keys), `check-presence`, `check-canvas`, `check-nav` and `check-host-registry`. This entry's author read the code and ran `npm run check:docs` and `check-names`, and did not rerun the others. **Not verified:** any call on a real LiveKit (join, hang up, tiles, the calls cap, kick and mute on a live call, pulls while on the call, reactions, the OBS video), and the pages in the narrow layout; listed in `TODO.md`.
- **Every module window is one shared frame** (`plan-unified-layout.md`, slices 1 to 9; `createModuleFrame`, `95bd664` to `01862e9`). A canvas window (docked or floating), a module's own page and its popped-out window, a destination's page, the composite on the Workspace and the Chat and call bands are drawn the same way: a title bar (36px), one toolbar row (40px), the content and a status bar (24px). The title bar and status bar are a darker surface, an object's row is a gentle tint of the theme, and a count is always the shared badge with the real number (no `9+`). There are no pills: the status bar is words with dots between.
  - **What a person sees.** The title bar has the module's own actions, a plain **+**, a menu and **Close**. The menu holds the standard actions that apply (**Dock as a panel**, **Open as a window**, **Open in its own window**, **Settings**, **Share**, **Print**) and, last and red, **Clear**. The toolbar row has the view switch, the title and the buttons at the left, and at the right the search and one **Filters** button with a count; its menu has the filter groups, with counts, and **Clear filters** first. A narrow row folds in a fixed order (Filters, icons for the switch and buttons, the search to a magnifier, then **More**) and nothing is cut.
  - **Modules moved onto it** (Budget 0.1.17, Calendar 1.24.29, Places 0.12.16, Polls 1.15.22, Research 0.4.23, To-do 1.15.20): Budget's sort, category and payer, Calendar's spaces, markers and other calendars, Places' category, Research's kind and tags (tags are switches), and the spaces of Polls and To-do are all **Filters** groups. The Planner, Maps, Stream and Assistant needed no change. Maps' contract was stale from the earlier slices and is corrected.
  - **For module authors** (`api-module-sdk.md`): `host.toolbar.set` takes `tabs`, `title`, `text`, `button`, `search` and `chips` (every `chips` item becomes a Filters group); a button may be placed in the title bar or its menu (`placement`); `host.header.set` actions go to the bar or, with `overflow`, the menu; `host.add.set` and `host.status.set` are unchanged in shape. The calls now answer `true` wherever a module is drawn; only a dashboard widget answers `false`, so the "draw it yourself" advice is gone. A module may not style the frame's `mf-` classes or draw a band (`tools/check-module-layout.mjs`).
  - **Removed:** the toolbar's `progress`, `slider` and `separator` items and the status item's `chip`, the old helpers (`createToolbar`, `createAddSlot`, `createStatusSlot`, `drawHeaderItems`) and the old modulechrome styles (`.mod-header`, `.mod-toolbar`, `.mod-status`, `.sdk-titlebar`, `.sdk-toolbar`, `.sdk-status-line`) and the `--module-header-h`/`--status-bar-h` tokens, now `--mf-*`.
  - **The design system pages** are four: **Design System** (what is in production, rebuilt from the families of `system.css`), **Proposed** (none staged), **Prototype** (the frame, drawn by the real `createModuleFrame`) and **Legacy**. `tools/check-design-system-catalog.mjs` fails a catalogue example that uses a class no stylesheet defines.
  - **Not built:** the content builders (`host.ui.list`, `card`, `table`, `form`, slices 10 to 13); modules still write their own card, row, table and form markup.
  - **Checked:** the commits record that `check-conformance` now measures the frame (sizes, the end group 12px from the edge, no cut toolbar) and that the other chrome checks were updated; for this entry the author re-ran `check-module-chrome` (20 checks), `check-modules` (40 groups), `check-design-system-catalog` and `check-module-layout`, which pass. **Not re-run or not verified:** the browser checks (`check-conformance`, `check-canvas` and the rest), the look at 1280 and 390 pixels, and a real call (there is no LiveKit server here).
- **A module's AI use is required or optional** (GitHub #373; Research 0.4.23). In `module.json`, `hooks.ai` is `true` (the module cannot work without the AI service) or `"optional"` (it uses the service when it is on); any other value is refused. A required module cannot be turned on while the AI service is off, and turning the service off asks first. An optional one is not held back, does not stop the service being turned off, and shows **Can use the AI service** on its card on the Modules page. Research is optional: it works with the AI service off, and its **Suggest tags** and **Research this** appear only when the service is on. Checked by `check-modules` (40 groups, re-run for this entry); not seen on the Modules page in a browser.
- **Every corner uses one of five radius tokens** (`design-theme.md`, "Corner radii"). `--radius-min` (3px), `--radius-small` (5px), `--radius-medium` (7px), `--radius-large` (11px) and `--radius-round` (999px) are defined once in `public/style.css` and again in `public/sdk/host.css`, and every `border-radius` in the product's stylesheets and scripts uses them. Corners are slightly different from before in places that had their own number. `tools/check-radius.mjs`, in `npm run check`, fails on any other value; it allows two things, the map pin's teardrop (`50% 50% 50% 4px`, in Maps and the Planner) and MapLibre's own files. All bundled modules had a patch bump. The page's stale "8 px" and "6 px" radius lines in the design documents were replaced with the tokens.
- **The call's looks.** A call tile whose camera is off shows its picture as a circle (it was a rounded square), the call toolbar's buttons are circles, and reactions use a heart. The space's link button is gone from the call toolbar; it stays on the card before you join. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`; not walked live in a call (there is no LiveKit server here).
- **The call column.** **Start call** sits at the right end of the chat's **Discussion | Assistant** row and reads **Join call** while a call is running (in the narrow layout the tab reads **Start** or **Join**). The call's titlebar has a **Pop out** icon, and so has the call's own titlebar when it floats or has a window. The call toolbar is now the microphone, the speaker, the camera, share, **Tiles**, away, reactions and leave, in round, quiet buttons of one size, with a solid red leave button; the position, pop-out and settings buttons are gone from it, and the small arrows beside the microphone, speaker, camera and tiles are now chevrons. **Call position** is only in the **⋮** menus (the call's and Chat's). The guest link switch is under **Settings** in the call's **⋮** menu, for people who can manage the space. The chat gets a top border when the call is above it. Checked by tools: `check-nav`, `check-canvas`. Not verified: a real call, a real phone. Documented in `userguide-call.md`, `userguide-getting-started.md` and `architecture-canvas.md`.
- **Home.** **Set up** is gone from the space cards; open it from the top bar inside a space, or with `/?setup=<id>`. **Add <space>** is a dashed ghost card, always last in the grid, instead of a button above it. The whole space card enters the space, and the separate pop-out button on the card is gone (GitHub #225). A person who makes a space is now one of its members, so they can be picked in its people lists (GitHub #239). Documented in `userguide-spaces.md`. Checked by tools: `check-nav`, `check-space-create`.
- **The status strip's countdown is one chip.** "35 days to Trip", "1 day to Trip" or "Trip starts today"; it is hidden once the main phase is under way, and the old "8 days left" is gone. The story sentence lost its "· Trip in 35 days" tail, so the countdown is said once. Phase icons stay in the bands when the names have gone, and show in the phase sheet's rows and on Set up's **Phases** tab. Checked by tools: `check-phases`, `check-nav`. Documented in `userguide-spaces.md`.
- **The Itinerary has no plan form with phases** (Planner 0.15.16). The pencil is gone in a space with phases: the dates are the bar's and the currency is the budget's, set in Set up. A space without phases keeps **Edit dates**, with the first and last day and the currency (it no longer asks for the plan's name, Where or Notes; stored ones are kept). Costs are worded in the budget's currency, then the plan's, then the locale's.
- **Selected, on, active and current states are fills, never borders.** One token, `--selected-bg` (28% of the accent), carries them, and the nav tab underline and the state borders are gone. `tools/check-nav.mjs` now fails any state rule that adds a border, outline colour or ring, except focus rings, hover borders, drop targets, the phase band for today and the Maps selected pin. `--on-danger` is a new token for the glyph on a solid red or green button. Documented in `design-theme.md`.
- **Module versions now:** Budget 0.1.0, Calendar 1.24.11, Places 0.10.10, Polls 1.15.5, Research 0.4.7, To-do 1.15.7, Planner 0.15.16 (read from `tools/module-versions.json`).
- **Operators: `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`** (default 1) says how many reverse proxies stand in front of the server. The sign-in and guest limits count by the address the nearest proxy saw, so a made-up `X-Forwarded-For` no longer defeats them. A server reached with no proxy sets 0; two in a row (say Cloudflare, then Nginx Proxy Manager) set 2. A value that is not a whole number logs a line and uses 1. The new `CHAT_STREAMS_PER_PERSON` (50), `CHAT_STREAMS_PER_GUEST_LINK` (25) and `GUEST_CHAT_POSTS_PER_MINUTE` (20) are in `docker-compose.yml`, commented. Documented in `userguide-getting-started.md`. Checked by tools: `check-auth`, `check-guest-chat`.
- **One text style for the top bar and the strip:** 13 px, weight 600, from the layout tabs. The wordmark stays larger. Strip bands are filled with the phase colours; the coloured borders are gone.
- **Modules are on in every space when they are on.** The per-space availability switches ("Available in every ...") are removed; the **Modules** tab lists what is on. Stored lists are still read.
- **Module versions:** Calendar 1.24.9, To-do 1.15.6, Planner 0.15.10, Polls 1.15.4, Research 0.4.6, Maps 0.8.11, Places 0.10.9, Assistant 0.1.23.
- **The space bar in the narrow layout** (GitHub #160, `plan-phone-space-bar.md`). In the narrow layout (a window or a pop-out 640 pixels or narrower), it reads: the module tabs that fit (at least 56 pixels each, the shown one always kept), **Join**, Online's count, **Rejoin** in an aside, **…** (**More**, only when something is in it) and **Leave**, always inside the window. **…** holds the tabs that didn't fit, a line, then **Pull participants back** and the modules' tools; in a narrow pop-out also **Full screen** ("Exit full screen" while on) and **Pop it back in**. Its badge adds up what it holds, and if the Conference's tab is in it while you are on the call it shows the green dot, red while your microphone is live. Join no longer squeezes the tabs: one tab fewer shows. A narrow pop-out shows as many tabs as the main window would, though it always needs **…** for Full screen and Pop out; its bar sits on the header's second row, sliding away with it when idle, and Online shows its count. Online's list opens upward in the narrow layout.
  - **For module authors:** `host.nav.set` is unchanged; in the narrow layout a module's tools are always entries in **…**. For page code: registry fields `phone: 'bar' | 'popout'`, `short` and `activeLabel`; `nav` exports `phoneTabs`, `phoneFold` and `TAB_MIN`. Documented in `architecture-canvas.md` ("Narrow layouts"), `architecture-navigation.md`, `api-module-sdk.md` and `userguide-call.md`. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (63 tests), run again for this entry; not verified on a real phone.
- A window holds only as many docked modules as it has columns for (GitHub #156, `plan-docked-limit.md`). Every docked column is at least 240 pixels wide (it was 160): 2 columns from 641 pixels, 3 from 760, 4 from 1000, 5 from 1280, 6 from 1600, 7 from 1920. The Conference and the Chat always keep their columns; installed modules give way from the right. A module with no column floats and waits: its remembered mode stays docked, and it docks again in its old place, at its width, when the window is wide enough; dragging or resizing it makes it floating for good. Its **Dock** is dimmed ("Docks when the window is wide enough"); another floating module's says "The window is too narrow for another column" while the columns are full; the Conference's and the Chat's always works, and the rightmost module waits instead. **Layout ▾** says why at the end of **Arrange** ("The window is too narrow for another column, so Calendar is floating. It docks when the window is wide enough.", or after **Dock all** "N docked. M stay floating until the window is wide enough."), clearing after 6 seconds or when the panel closes; nothing is said on entering, resizing or popping out. 640 pixels and below is the narrow layout for both the space bar and the canvas, where nothing waits. Floating, modules in their own window and narrow layouts are unchanged.
  - For developers: `public/snap-grid.js` exports `DOCK_MIN`, `FLEX_MIN`, `NARROW`, `dockLimit()` and `fitDock()`; `createCanvas()` takes `onNote`; `dockAll()` returns `{ docked, waiting }`. Documented in `architecture-canvas.md`, `architecture-navigation.md`, `userguide-call.md` and `userguide-modules.md`. Checked by new cases in `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, run again for this entry; not verified in a real call.
- New environments start with Thomas's curated reactions and icons (GitHub #169): 105 reactions, whose first six, the **1** to **6** keys, are Heart, Black Heart, 100%, Hi, Thumbs up and Thumbs down, and 113 icons (111 solid, plus the brands `bluesky` and `xbox`). An existing environment still on exactly the old six reactions or the old 21 icons moves to the new list once; reactions and icons are judged separately, and a list an owner changed is kept. A template may now carry up to 120 reactions and 120 icons (it was 30 and 60); a template's `iconSet` still takes only solid icons. For developers: the lists are in `server/default-lists.js`; the settings flags `reactionsCuratedSeeded` and `iconsCuratedSeeded` record the one-time move; `MAX_REACTIONS` and `MAX_ICON_SET` in `server/templates.js`. Documented in `architecture-environments.md`, `userguide-environment-settings.md`, `userguide-call.md` and `userguide-templates.md`. Checked by the new `tools/check-defaults.mjs` (9 cases), run again for this entry; not verified on a real server.
- **Chat keeps one kind of message, private or public** (GitHub #157, `plan-chat-model.md`). A command you type, `/ai` included, shows as your own private message with its module's icon and colour; an AI answer is a private message from **AI**, in gold. Click a message's **private** or **public** badge to **Make public** or **Make private**: the message itself moves, with no copy, and appears for or disappears from everyone else at once. A public AI answer reads **AI, for <name>** with the question above it. A filter above the messages, **All | Private | Public**, hides the rest on your screen. **Delete your private messages** in Chat's menu removes all of yours in the space. **Shared**, **Share to the space**, "AI answer shared by …" and **Clear your AI thread** are gone. Each module has a colour, shown on its messages, its titlebar icon and its line in **Layout ▾**: AI gold, Research blue, To-do green, Calendar red, the Planner teal, Polls purple, Places orange, Maps pink. The Assistant's **Use** permission no longer decides who may use `/ai`; **Turn AI off in this space** still does. Calendar 1.19.3, Maps 0.8.1, Places 0.9.1, Polls 1.12.21, Research 0.2.33, To-do 1.12.3, Planner 0.11.2.
  - Storage: public messages stay in `chat.json`, in its old shape, and private ones go in a new `chat-private.json` beside it, so going back to an earlier release keeps private messages private. A flip moves a message between the two, destination first; one found in both counts as private. Messages carry new optional fields `visibility`, `kind`, `command`, `module`, `summaries` and `replyTo`, and a read-only `question` on a public AI answer. A space keeps 500 public messages, and each person 200 private ones, 30 days. On the first start the old `ai-threads.json` is moved into `chat-private.json` once and renamed `ai-threads.moved.json`; a previously shared answer stays private, since its copy is already in the chat. Deleting the chat, deleting your private messages and deleting a space reach both files.
  - Routes: new `PATCH /api/spaces/:id/chat/:messageId` `{ visibility }` (the author only; 400, 403, 404, 429) and `DELETE /api/spaces/:id/chat/private`; the call hears `chat-visibility`. `POST /api/spaces/:id/command` and `/ai` return the stored private `message` (and `question`); `share` is ignored. `/ai/thread` keeps its old shape, read from the chat. `GET /api/modules/for-space` gives each module's `color`.
  - **For module authors:** a manifest may set `color` to one of gold, blue, green, teal, purple, red, orange or pink; any other value refuses the install. Module pages get the eight `--tint-*` tokens from `/sdk/host.css` and with the theme. New: `data-mode-shown` on the page, so the light tints show under the environment's light default.
  - Documented in `api-modules.md` ("Chat routes", the manifest), `api-module-sdk.md` ("Theme"), `architecture-overview.md`, `architecture-modules.md`, `design-theme.md` ("Tints"), `userguide-chat.md`; `plan-chat-model.md` is built and `plan-one-input.md` notes its steps 7 and 8 are superseded. Checked by the new `tools/check-chat-model.mjs` (28 checks, the move from AI threads included) and `tools/check-chat-page.mjs` (10 tests), run again for this entry; quality assurance's own runs are theirs. Not verified in a real call.
- **Join the call** is in the space bar (GitHub #165): **Layout ▾ | Join the call | Online**. One press shows the Conference if it is hidden and joins; it is gone while you are on the call, and in an aside it joins the aside's call. In the narrow layout it is a **Join** tab with a green phone. The label is fixed, with no verb to change it. This is a deliberate exception to `plan-entering.md` change 3, which had taken the call control out of the space bar; entering a space still never joins the call. Documented in `userguide-call.md` and `architecture-navigation.md`.
- The space bar's **Modules ▾** button is now **Layout ▾**, with a new icon, and it holds the canvas's layout too (`plan-layout-menu.md`, steps 1 and 2; Thomas, 2026-10-02). Its panel has two sections. **Show** is the module switches, as before. **Arrange** holds **Dock all** ("Dock every floating <module> beside the call"), **Clean up** ("Tidy the floating <modules>"), **Snap to a grid**, a switch, and **Grid size**, a slider shown only while snap is on. **Clean up** moves the floating modules fully onto the canvas so none overlap, keeping each one's size where it fits; with snap on each keeps its size in cells wherever a place exists, and only one with no place is made smaller. Pressing it again changes nothing. It leaves docked modules and modules in their own window alone, and the new places are remembered. Dock all, snap and Grid size are gone from the space bar's right side and its **…**, which now hold Full screen, Pop out, Pull participants back or Rejoin call in an aside, a module's own tools and Leave space. Narrow layouts are unchanged: no Layout button, the modules are tabs. No new shortcut and no SDK change.
  - The button's word is a verb an owner or a template can change: the verbs are now `enter` and `layout` (default "Layout"), and Manage > Template > **Words** has the row "Button for the {canvas} layout". The template's and owner's `verbs` take `layout`; the refusals name both verbs.
  - For developers: the button keeps its id `#modules-toggle` and the controls theirs (`#dock-all`, `#snap-all`, `#snap-size`, new `#clean-up`); the panel's sections are `#modules-menu-show` and `#modules-menu-arrange`, and `update()` in `public/canvas.js` rewrites only Show; `canvas.cleanUp()` uses the new pure `tidyCells()` in `public/snap-grid.js` for snapped modules (`resettle()` only when they can't all fit even at their smallest) and the new `tidyBoxes()` for free ones.
- The top bar's links share one style: Calendar, Map, **Spaces ▾** and "› <space>" are all `core-link` at 13px (Spaces was 14px and the space's segment 15px). `tools/check-nav.mjs` holds it.
  - Both documented in `architecture-navigation.md`, `architecture-canvas.md`, `architecture-environments.md` and `architecture-overview.md` (the verbs), `userguide-call.md`, `userguide-modules.md`, `userguide-environment-settings.md`, `userguide-calendar.md` and `userguide-todo.md`. Checked by tools, each run again for this entry: `tools/check-nav.mjs` (56 tests) and `tools/check-canvas.mjs` (including `tidyCells` and `tidyBoxes`: no overlaps, every box inside, a box that fits keeps its size). Quality assurance passed the batch; not verified in a real call.
- The header is two bars of two zones each, with nothing centred (`plan-two-zone-nav.md`, steps 1 to 5; Thomas, 2026-10-02: "we have overcomplicated the nav layout").
  - **The top bar** reads, left to right: the logo and the environment's name, **Calendar** and **Maps** when shown, **Spaces ▾**, and only in a space "› <space>" (in an aside "› <space> › Aside: <names>"). No other page adds anything there: Manage, a profile or a module's page no longer show "› Manage" or their name. From 641 to 820 pixels Calendar and Maps are icons only. The right side, the bell and your picture, is unchanged.
  - **Every other page has a second bar**, in the space bar's colour: its name and then its controls. Manage and Profile have their tabs there; someone's profile and a space's settings have **Manage** first (owners and the admin only), then the name and the tabs; AI configuration and a module's configuration have **Manage** and the name; the module settings page and a module's page have the name. Calendar and Map keep their bar with no name, and their entry in the top bar is marked. In the narrow layout the top bar shows the page's name as a plain label, and the second bar holds only the controls.
  - **The space bar** is two zones in one colour: **Modules**, then **Online** (who is here, unchanged) on the left, and the right side unchanged. As it narrows, Online gives way first, then the right side folds into **…**. The narrow layout's tab bar is unchanged.
  - **Themes** have three top bar colour areas: the branding area, the left (the header background) and the right side. Manage > Theme has four new rows, **Branding area background** and **text**, and **Right side background** and **text**, each on **Auto** until set, which looks as before. They are new optional keys `navBrandBg`, `navBrandText`, `navRightBg` and `navRightText` (`--nav-brand-bg`, `--nav-brand-text`, `--nav-right-bg`, `--nav-right-text`), `null` or missing for Auto; theme files export and import them, and old themes and files load unchanged. `--nav-primary-edge-bg` is now opaque, with the same look.
  - For developers: the registry has two zones, `left` and `right`, and `register()` refuses `middle`; `phoneZones()` returns `{ left, bar, right }`; `fitWidth()` replaces `middleWidth()` and `middleCentred()`; `#subnav-middle` is gone; `renderPageBar()` and `pageAnchor()` are new and `crumbLink()` is removed. Documented in `architecture-navigation.md`, `design-theme.md`, `architecture-overview.md` ("Themes"), `api-module-sdk.md`, `userguide-spaces.md`, `userguide-call.md`, `userguide-themes.md` and `userguide-environment-settings.md`.
  - **For module authors:** `zone` in `host.nav.set` is ignored from this release: every module tool goes in the space bar's right zone, where it can fold. A tool that names `left` or `middle` is placed there, not refused. No bundled module is affected.
  - Checked by tools, each run again for this entry: `tools/check-nav.mjs` (54 tests), `tools/check-theme.mjs` (14 checks) and `tools/check-themes.mjs` (18 groups). Not checked here in a browser by me, not in a real call, and not with a screen reader, Firefox, Safari or a real phone.
- Research's **Fetch link previews** now starts on (Thomas, 2026-10-02). In an environment that never changed the setting, adding a link fills in an empty title or description from that page and shows its picture; the request goes from this server to the address typed. An owner can turn it off in Research's settings (**Module Configuration**), and an environment that turned it off, or turns it off now, stays off. The protections are unchanged: a private, loopback or link-local address is refused, after a redirect too. Research 0.2.32 (the setting's `default` in `module.json` is now `true`). Documented in `userguide-research.md`. Checked by tools: `tools/check-link-preview.mjs` (9 checks, run again for this entry) has a new case that holds the default on, a saved off staying off, and staying off after a restart. Not verified live in a browser or against a real page here.
- Every switch shows an icon beside its word: the chat's **Private | Shared**, the Calendar's **Month | Week | Day | Agenda**, the Planner's **Days | Decisions | Bookings | Money**, **Open | Closed | All** in Polls and **Open | Done | All** in the To-do, **Mine | This space** in Places and Research, and on the Calendar page **Month | Week | Day** and **Agenda | To-do**. Too narrow for the words, a switch shows its icons only; hovering shows the word, and a screen reader reads it. The To-do's **Open | Done | All** on the Calendar page now looks like every other switch. The chat's hint shortens to "Chat, or / for commands" or "Chat" in a narrow chat, which also stops it wrapping after the larger **Send** button. Polls 1.12.20, To-do 1.12.2, Planner 0.11.1; Calendar stays 1.19.2.
  - **For module authors:** `host.ui.viewSwitch` options take `icon` (and `regular`); a new `element` option draws the switch in the module's own page. The look is in `/sdk/host.css` (`.tb-tabs` and its parts), which a module must not size; a new check, `tools/check-switches.mjs` (8 cases, passing when run again for this entry), holds that and that every switch is drawn the shared way with its icons. No new tokens. Documented in `api-module-sdk.md` and `design-theme.md` ("The switch"). Read as code for the narrow widths; not looked at in a browser here.
- The Calendar's views are **Month | Week | Day | Agenda** (Calendar 1.19.2). **List** is now **Agenda**, and **Month + list** is gone: Month and Week always list their events under the grid, and Day doesn't. **Open on** offers Month, Week, Day and Agenda, and a saved Month + list opens on Month; nothing stored changes. Calendar in the top bar keeps **Month | Week | Day** with nothing listed under the grid, since its **Agenda** panel is the list, so in the narrow layout the month's list is the **Agenda** tab. Documented in `userguide-calendar.md`.
- While Calendar is in the top bar, the To-do card's heading on home opens Calendar on its **To-do** panel (the **To-do** tab in the narrow layout), over a space or in a new tab too, and that counts as your choice: the Calendar card's heading then opens on whichever panel you chose last. For developers: `GET /api/modules/widgets` gives a destination's panel module the `href` `<path>#panel=<module id>` (`/calendar#panel=todo`), and the destination page reads `#panel=` (`hashPanel()` in `public/primary-nav.js`), then takes it out of the address. Documented in `api-modules.md`, `architecture-navigation.md` and `userguide-todo.md`. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (49 tests) and `tools/check-destinations.mjs` (35 groups), run again for this entry; read as code for the page.
- The add buttons of the Calendar, the To-do, Polls and the Planner, Places' **+** and the chat's **Send** have icons as large as the call's controls, a 20px icon in a 38px button, keeping that share at the smaller sizes and in the narrow layout. A module's bar gets the same square icon buttons when it floats or is on its own page as when it is docked. **For module authors:** the base stylesheet (`/sdk/host.css`) has `.action-btn`, with `.primary` for the accent look; its size comes from `--action-btn-h` and `--action-icon-ratio`, which a module must not set. A new check, `tools/check-buttons.mjs` (in `npm run check`), keeps the button defined once in `public/style.css` and `public/sdk/host.css` and fails a bundled module that sizes it. Research 0.2.31 drops its own now-unused button styles. Documented in `design-theme.md` ("The action button") and `api-module-sdk.md` ("The action bar"). `check-buttons` passes, run again for this entry; the sizes were not looked at here in a browser.
- Home's **Coming up** and **Due soon** cards open Calendar in the top bar while it is shown. An event or a task opens there with its editor open (a task on the **To-do** panel, or its tab in the narrow layout), and a day in the small month opens Calendar at that day; from inside a space it opens over the space and you are Away on the call. Without Calendar shown they work as before, and **Need your vote** is unchanged. Ctrl-, Cmd- or Shift-click, a middle click, or Ctrl or Cmd with Enter opens a new tab and leaves home and the call alone. The Planner's **Trips** card is gone from home; a summary of each space's plan on its space card is planned (`plan-dashboard.md`, phase 5). Calendar 1.19.1, To-do 1.12.1, Planner 0.11.0.
  - **For module authors:** `host.objects.open(ref, { newTab })` and `host.page.open(hash, { newTab })`. On home, a widget's own objects and `page.open` go to the destination its module is part of while that is shown (`tileRefTarget()` in `public/primary-nav.js`). Documented in `api-module-sdk.md`, `architecture-navigation.md`, `architecture-modules.md` and the Calendar, To-do, Planner and Spaces guides. Checked by new cases in `tools/check-nav.mjs` (48 tests) and `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` (50 groups), both run again for this entry; read as code for the rest, not verified in a real call.
- Calendar 1.19.0, To-do 1.12.0, Maps 0.8.0 and Places 0.9.0, for Calendar and Map in the top bar.
  - **Calendar** has a **Day** view, on its own page and in a space as well (and in **Open on**, which also sets Calendar in the top bar's first view on a wider screen). On the environment's calendar a space's events can be changed where you may add events there, and others open read-only, saying "Only people who can add events in <space> can change this."; a new event asks **Where** (the environment's own calendar, named by the environment's name, or a space where you may add) every time, and **Save** says "Pick a <space> first." until you choose. A space's event reminds that space's people. Anyone may drop on a day now; what is offered is only what they may do. It offers the destination's calendar (`main`) and its **Agenda** (`panel`).
  - **To-do** works the same way on its environment page (**Where**, editing a space's tasks where you may, reminders in the task's space) and offers the **To-do** panel: open tasks grouped **Overdue**, **Today**, **This week**, **Later** and **No date**, with **Add task** and **Open | Done | All** at its top.
  - **Maps** gains the `environment` scope and an environment page, offers Map's main part (the filter, dimming, the search's pins, the shared selection, **+** at the map's top left), and reads only objects with a position, up to 1,000, so a busy space's pins are all drawn rather than the newest 50.
  - **Places** offers Map's panel: Mine, the environment's own and each space's places in one list, each marked with where it is kept, the category chips counting what the filter and search leave; **Where** in the dialog for a new place, its prompt naming only what is offered ("Pick where it goes first." until one is picked), and for a read-only place showing its place, greyed; the place menu's copies name their target ("Save to mine", "Share to <space>"); one search for the list and the map.
  - Modules are told the environment's name (`info.context.environment.name`), and the Calendar, the To-do and Places name the environment's own list by it.
- **For module authors:** what the SDK gained for destinations, all documented in `api-module-sdk.md`.
  - On a module's environment page, `{ space: <id> }` on `host.storage.get`, `set`, `delete` and `list`, `host.schedule`, `host.cancelSchedule`, `host.notify` (`to: 'space'` is then that space) and `host.objects.setLinks` reads and writes one of the viewer's spaces; the server checks the module's permission there. `host.spaces()` (and `GET /api/modules/:id/spaces-data`) gives each space `write`. A page in a space still reaches only its own.
  - `host.objects.search(text, { scope: 'spaces', has: 'place' })`: every space the viewer is a member of at once, each pointer with its space, and only summaries with a place, up to 1,000 (`GET /api/objects/search?scope=spaces&has=place`). Environment pages only.
  - `host.actions.list({ ref })` lists only the actions the viewer could carry out where that object is, and the drop menu passes the dropped pointer. From an environment page, a request about one of the provider's own objects in a space is checked by the viewer's right to change the provider in that space, and only someone with that right may take it (`GET /api/bus/actions?ref=`, `space` on a request, `pending`, `claim` and `complete` checked there).
  - `info.context.environment.name`, and `info.context.destination` with `host.destination` on a destination's part.
  - `host.presence`'s people carry `asidePrivate` (from step 3 of `plan-primary-nav.md`).
- Where people are is shown only for the spaces you belong to (`plan-primary-nav.md` step 3, decision 6). Everyone belongs to the Lobby, and owners and the admin belong to every space and every aside. Someone in a space you don't belong to shows as "in another space", never with its name and never as in the call; someone who stepped into an aside from one of your spaces shows as "in an aside" or "in a private conversation", never with whom; you always see where you are yourself. A guest sees only their own space and its people. On home, a space card's "N here" and the switcher's "N here" no longer count people who stepped into an aside; their dot is off, and hovering over them reads "<name> is in an aside", "<name> is in a private conversation" or "<name> is in another space". **Who's around** uses the same words. The call's placeholder tiles read "In an aside", naming the others only for an ordinary aside (to its people, owners and the admin, and to those who saw it start without reloading since), or "In a private conversation", never naming anyone, also after a reload. When the stream is somewhere you can't see, the people you can place are marked "off stream", and those in another space or an aside you aren't in are not.
  - `GET /api/presence` is filtered by viewer (`server/presence-view.js`, `presenceViewer()` in `server/index.js`). Each user gains `aside`, `asidePrivate` and `elsewhere`. In an aside of the viewer's space that the viewer isn't in: `space` is the parent's id, `aside: true`, `inCall: false`, and `asidePrivate` says whether it is private, never with its members. Somewhere the viewer doesn't belong: `space: null`, `elsewhere: true`, `online: true`, `inCall: false`. `asides` lists only the asides the viewer belongs to. `activeSpace` is `null` when the stream is somewhere the viewer can't see, an aside they aren't in included. The access key (Coffee Pub Studio, OBS) still reads everything; `GET /api/status` is unchanged. See `architecture-overview.md`, "Spaces and calls".
  - For module authors: `host.presence` follows the same rules. Each person gains `aside` and `elsewhere`, `asides` lists only the viewer's, and `activeSpace` may be `null`. A keyed page, which carries the access key, still reads everything. No module changed.
  - The calls cap's refusal names the running call's space only to its members; anyone else reads "one is running in an aside" or "one is running in another space".
  - `aside-started` is `{ type, spaceId, members, private: false }` for an ordinary aside and `{ type, spaceId, private: true }`, with no members, for a private conversation. The space page reads a message without `private` as private.
  - Thomas has still to confirm one choice made while building: members of the parent space may see that an aside is private (`asidePrivate`), never who is in it, which keeps the placeholder that showed before. See TODO.
  - Checked by `tools/check-presence.mjs` (15 checks: the rules on their own, then a real server on a throwaway `DATA_DIR` against a stand-in LiveKit) and by new cases in `tools/check-nav.mjs` (`isHere`, `steppedOut`, `hereCount`, `asidePlaceholder`, `whereWords`, `offStream`). Verified live against the API and in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit. Not tested in a real call.
- The top bar's **Modules** menu is gone (Thomas, 2026-10-02, `plan-primary-nav.md` step 2b, decisions 30 and 31). It shipped with the new top bar (#147), but most module pages aren't places to go on their own. A module's page is reached from its card's heading on the spaces page, or at `/modules/<id>`; in a space, the space bar's **Modules** list shows and hides modules as before. Module notices still count on the bell, on the cards on the spaces page and on the space bar's **Modules** button. The narrow-layout real iPhone no longer lists module pages. The top bar no longer asks `GET /api/modules/nav`, which stays for a module's own page (`public/module.js` names the page from it); `refreshModuleNav()` is gone from `brand.js`. Calendar (with To-do) and Map (with Places and Maps) are to become two direct destinations in the top bar, under a plan of their own. A module page without a card (Places, Stream) is now reached only by its address. No module changed. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (seven slots, no Modules slot in the bar or the narrow-layout real iPhone).
- The top bar is new (`plan-primary-nav.md`, steps 1 and 2; Thomas's spec of 2026-10-01, approved 2026-10-02). It reads, left to right: the logo and the environment's name, both going home; **Modules**, a menu of every module page with its unread count (not shown when there are none); **Spaces**, where the word goes home and the arrow beside it (**Switch space**) opens a menu of your spaces, with "You are here" on yours, "N here" where people are, and **New space** for owners and the admin, which opens Manage's Spaces tab with its **Add space** button focused; then, while you are in a space, where you are ("› Disneyland", or "› Disneyland › Aside: Michelle" in an aside, where clicking Disneyland, titled "Rejoin call in Disneyland", does what **Rejoin call** does; picking Disneyland in the switcher from the aside does the same, bringing everyone back). Where you are always stays in the bar, cut short with "…": between 641 and 1000 pixels wide your picture shows without your name, and between 641 and 820 the environment's name is left out too. On the right are the bell and your picture with an arrow. Gone from the bar: the clock, the couch or suitcase icon beside the name (the logo box shows the uploaded logo, else the home icon), the light or dark switch, Manage, and the module page icons. The space bar no longer repeats the space's name.
  - **The bell** counts unread module notices for everyone, and for owners and the admin the module updates waiting; that count moved off your picture and Manage. Its list shows the notices newest first, with "N module updates available" first for owners and the admin (it opens Manage > Modules, over the space too), or "Nothing new."; opening it marks them read, and a notice arriving while it is open appears at the top, already read. Reading the bell on a page opened over a space updates the space page's counts too. A notice doesn't open its object yet, and there are no mentions (see known-issues).
  - **The menu under your picture**: View profile, **Dark mode**, **Manage** (owners and the admin), **Host console** (a host admin signed in to an environment on a hosted server), **Install as an app** (while the browser offers it, never for a guest) and **Sign out**. In a space, Manage and a module's page open over the call, and the host console in a new tab. A link to a section of Manage (such as `#modules`) now lands on it when opened over a space: the page's handoff query goes before the `#section`.
  - **Guests** see the logo, the environment's name and the space's name, nothing else (in the narrow layout, no menu button), and always the environment's default light or dark look, whatever the browser remembered.
  - **In the narrow layout** the bar is the logo, where you are, the bell and the menu button; the menu holds the environment's name, home and your spaces, New space, the module pages, then your picture and the entries above.
  - For developers: the pure parts are in the new `public/primary-nav.js` (`profileEntries()`, `hostConsoleUrl()`, `switcherEntries()`, `switchPick()`, `bellState()`, `anchorSegments()`, `crumbSegments()`); `brand.js` draws the bar and exports `setPresentSpace()`, which the space page calls; two new cancelable document events, `app:open-page` `{ href, kind }` and `app:enter-space` `{ id, action }`, let the space page open a page over the call or enter a space; a primary tool may say `phone: 'bar'` to stay in the bar in the narrow layout (`phoneZones()`); `useDefaultMode()` and `toggleThemeMode()` in `theme-mode.js`. See `architecture-navigation.md`. No module changed. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (the slot order, the narrow layout fold, the profile menu for each role on a single and a hosted install, the switcher, the bell, the breadcrumb, an old-contract module tool) and verified live in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit at 1280, 1024 and 390 pixels, as a member, a moderator, an owner, the admin and a guest. Not verified: a real call, **Host console** on a hosted install (only its address, as a pure function), **Install as an app**, the breadcrumb in a real aside, and a screen reader.
- The home page has a word of its own, `home` (`plan-primary-nav.md`, step 1, decision 13), the thirteenth word key. Unless the owner or the template sets it, it is the space word's plural with a capital, so it reads **Spaces** as before, and **Trips** where a space is a trip, with nothing set. The top bar's **Spaces** uses it. Owners set it on Manage > **Template** > **Words**, in a **Home page** row with no article field; the host template editor has the same row. A refusal names it "The word for the home page…". It is in `/api/branding`'s, `/api/settings`'s and `host.locale()`'s `words`, and in the SDK's default words; `fill()` has no placeholder for it. Checked by `tools/check-templates.mjs` (its default following the template's and the owner's space word, a template's and an owner's own word winning, the refusals, `/api/branding` with a template) and `tools/check-names.mjs --words`.
- `GET /api/modules/nav` lists every enabled module with an environment page that the viewer may read at environment level, widget or not; `nav` is always `true`, and a manifest's `surfaces.page.nav` is no longer read. Maps and Research aren't listed, since they have no environment page. Checked by `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` (Places, Stream, To-do and Travel listed; Maps not; nothing signed out or for a guest).
- **For module authors:** `host.nav.set` ignores `bar` and `system`. A module's tools always go in the space bar; one written for the old contract (`bar: 'primary'`, `system: true`) is placed there like any other and is no longer refused. `surfaces.page.nav` in `module.json` is ignored: every environment page is listed in the top bar's **Modules** menu. Nothing else in the SDK changed; `host.locale().words` gains `home`. See `api-module-sdk.md`, "Registering into the nav bars".
- Guests get no install option at all (Thomas, 2026-10-01). On a guest link, and the windows popped out from it, there is no **Install as an app**, no install hint under **Join the call** in **Not in a call**, and no install line in the call's settings (the gear). The browser's own offer to install is dropped too. Before, a guest saw the hint telling them to use **Install as an app** in a profile menu they did not have. Members, moderators, owners and admins see the same as before, except that the space page now shows the hint, and registers its service worker, only once it knows who you are. For developers: `barInstall()` and `installBarred` in `public/brand.js` (run at load on a guest link's page; `loadMe()` also runs it if `/api/me` ever answers with the role `guest`, a guard for a future role, since no account can have that role today) keep no `beforeinstallprompt` event and remove the page's manifest link; `isGuestViewer()` and `offerInstall()` in `public/space.js` paint `#install-hint` and `#install-note` and register `/sw.js` after `/api/me` answers, never for a guest. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and reviewed by quality-assurance. Not tested in a real browser's install flow.
- Who is here, in the middle of the space bar, has a new look and says who in words. The portraits are small squares with rounded corners and a thin neutral border, side by side rather than overlapping, and "+N" is a square of the same size. There is no coloured ring on your own portrait any more: you are first in the row and "(you)" in the list; the only colour left is the camera mark on those on the call. Beside the portraits it says "Just you", "You and Alex", "You, Alex and Sam" or "You and 4 others" (first names, a long one cut short). When the bar is short of space it first says "5 in this space" (the environment's word for a space, or "aside" in one), then drops portraits, then shows an icon and "5 here", which is also what the narrow layout's tab bar shows. Its tooltip still says how many are here and how many are on the call, and the list still reads "In this space (N)", with each person's name, "(you)" and the camera with "on the call". For developers: `hereLabel()` in `public/space-people.js` and the steps in its `fit()`. Read as code: `tools/check-nav.mjs` still holds `facesThatFit()`, `peopleIn()` and `hereWords()`, but not `hereLabel()` or the new steps, and no check of the new look in a browser was reported. Not checked in a real call.
- **Install as an app** moved from an icon in the header into the menu under your picture, between **View profile** and **Sign out**, and in the narrow layout into the header's menu after **View profile**. It shows only while the browser offers to install. The hint under **Join the call** and in the call's settings now reads "For a window without browser bars, use Install as an app in your profile menu once your browser offers it." Someone without an account menu, a guest, no longer sees Install. For developers: the `install-link` tool is gone; the entry is in `accountMenuItems()` and, in the narrow layout, the `account-install` tool in `public/brand.js`. Read as code only.
- The Conference switch always reads the Conference's name (or the name the template or the environment gives it), never "Rejoin call" (Thomas, 2026-09-30: "it is BAD UX to rename a switch"). Like every other switch, it only shows and hides the module. Switched on, the Conference shows **Not in a call**, and nothing asks for your microphone until you choose **Join the call**. Switched off (or closed with its x) while you are on the call, it hangs up first, so nobody is left on the call with the Conference hidden. In the narrow layout the Conference tab's dot now means you are on the call, not only that the Conference is open. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit; not checked in a real call.
- Entering a space never joins the call, for members and guests alike (Thomas, 2026-09-30; this overrides decision 7 of `plan-entering.md`, which had guests enter with the call open). If the Conference is among what a space opens with, it opens in **Not in a call**. Joining is always a click on **Join the call** or the green phone, and the browser asks for the microphone only then. The guest form no longer mentions the microphone. For developers: the page connects out of the call (the token's `call` attribute is `off`, and the page says `off` again on connecting) and sets `on` only on a join. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and `tools/check-host-registry.mjs`, and verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit; not checked in a real call.
- Being pulled keeps you on the call (Thomas, 2026-09-30: "Pull keeps you on the call"). Being pulled into an aside or a private conversation, or back (**Pull participants back**, **Rejoin call**, or following the others back), keeps you on the call if you were on it, through the same join check and micronarrow-layout rules as a click; if you were not, you arrive in **Not in a call**. Going somewhere yourself (entering another space, accepting an invitation, a module opened from the dashboard) never joins. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and read as code. Not verified with a real pull: an owner's pull into a real aside, and **Rejoin call** within the server's 15-second hold, need a LiveKit server.
- **Open with** on the space list, and **Opens with** and the **Modules** list in space settings, use switches in place of ticks, the same switches as the space bar's **Modules** list (`public/switch-list.js`). The words follow: "Nothing switched on: Chat and To-do open.", "Nothing switched on: nothing opens.", "None of the ones switched on can open now, so nothing opens.", and the space's modules list says "Switch one on to make it available to this space's members, in the Modules button above the canvas." Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and verified live in headless Chromium.
- The plan's calls cap counts only real calls (Thomas, 2026-09-30). Entering a space is never refused. A space counts as a running call only while someone there is on the call (their `call` attribute is `on`), not merely connected. The cap is asked just before joining (`POST /api/call/join`, see Added), and over the limit the join is refused with "This <environment>'s plan allows N call(s) at once; one is running in <space>", shown in **Not in a call**. Joining a call already running is never refused, and moving from one call to another never counts your own. A start that passes holds its space's place for 15 seconds, in memory for each environment, so two people starting calls in two spaces at the same moment cannot both pass. `POST /api/asides` asks the cap before pulling anyone, and answers 403 with the same sentence; an aside whose members leave nobody on their old call takes that call's place. `POST /api/token` and `POST /api/guest-join` no longer read `call` from the request, are never refused by the cap, and always start the person out of the call (`call: 'off'`); an OBS viewer's token carries no `call` attribute, as before. The environment's **Calls at once** and the host console's count use the same rule (the console's `usage.callsNow` on `GET /api/host/environments`, only for an environment whose plan caps calls; it was always 0 there). The cap is enforced by the page asking first: a modified page could skip the check, as before (see `TODO.md`). Documented in `architecture-environments.md` and `architecture-overview.md`. Checked by `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` against a stand-in LiveKit (entering over the cap, a second call refused, the running one joined, a guest, an OBS viewer, the console's count, two starts at once), and by curl. Not checked against a real LiveKit server.
- An aside's card on the space list reads **Join** with a phone icon ("for now", Thomas, 2026-09-30), and its pop-out button "Join in a pop-out window"; it read **Enter**, as plan-entering Part 1 built it. An aside is a call, so it never takes the word for entering a space. **Back to <aside>** is unchanged. Verified live in headless Chromium with presence faked (no LiveKit server here); **Back to** read as code only.
- The guest form reads as entering, not joining a call: the heading "Enter as a guest", then "Enter <space>" once the link is read, and the button **Enter** with a door icon (it was "Join" with a speech bubble). Both follow the environment's word for entering a space. The prose that said a person "joins" a space now says they "come in" or "come into" it, as fixed text: the guest link's hint in the call settings and in space settings ("Anyone with this link can come into this space with just a name…"), the Roles hint on Manage ("…everyone who comes in from a space's guest link"), and the product page ("People come in with a link… guests can come into a space with just a name…"). Verified live in headless Chromium.
- A space card's main button comes first, and the smaller buttons (the launch link, pop-out, choose what opens, space settings, module settings) follow it as one group, moving together to their own line on a narrow card. The main button's label reads in full unless it is wider than the whole card, when it ends with "…"; its tooltip always holds the full label. The **Open with** box opens above the buttons, so it never covers the main button. Verified live in headless Chromium at 1280, 1024 and 390 pixels wide with long words, with measurements.
- Your picture and name are the last thing in the header, at the right after the time, with a divider before them, and clicking them opens a menu with **View profile** and **Sign out**. The separate Sign out button is gone. The header's right end now reads: the light or dark switch, Manage and Install, then the time (with Call settings before it in a call), then you. A long name is cut short with "…"; a screen reader still hears the whole name. A guest has no picture and no menu, and the host console still hides this part of the header. **View profile** opens your profile over the space in a space, so the call keeps running, and brings a popped-out app back first; on your profile page it just closes the menu. **Sign out** forgets the light or dark choice made in this browser, as the button did. The ai-config and module-config pages now show your picture and name too. In the narrow layout the header's menu holds Spaces, light or dark, Manage, Install and the time, each once; your picture first stayed in the bar there, and now folds into that menu (see the next entry). For developers: `#whoami-link` is now a button (ids `whoami-link`, `whoami-img` and `whoami` kept), and `#logout-link` is gone; see `architecture-navigation.md`. Verified live in headless Chromium at 1280, 700 and 390 pixels wide on the spaces page, /profile, /admin, module pages, module settings, space settings, ai-config, module-config, a guest's page, a hosted environment and the host console; Sign out checked (the session cookie gone, `/api/me` answering 401); a name with markup shows as text. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs`, `tools/check-syntax.mjs` and the other checks. Read as code only: the pop-out window and the space bar in a real call, since there is no LiveKit server here.
- In the narrow layout your picture is no longer in the header; it folds into the header's menu (**Menu**, three lines) with everything else, as Thomas decided on 2026-09-30, reversing the keep-in-bar of the entry above. Open the menu: after a divider come your picture and name, only a label, then **View profile** and **Sign out**, which do what the account menu does. A guest has none of these. On a wider screen nothing changes. The narrow width is the one where the header folds, 640 pixels and below. For developers: `keepOnPhone` is gone; the fold is the pure `phoneZones(byZone)` in `public/nav-bar.js`, and View profile and Sign out are the page tools `account-profile` and `account-sign-out`, shown only in the narrow layout while your picture shows, sharing `viewProfile()` and `signOut()` with the account menu in `public/brand.js`; see `architecture-navigation.md`. Documented in `userguide-accounts.md` ("Your profile and signing out"). Verified live in headless Chromium at 390, 640, 641 and 1280 pixels wide on the spaces page, /profile, /admin, /modules/todo and a guest's page. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (17 tests). Not checked: a real screen reader (your picture read as a label through `aria-disabled`), the pop-out window, and a real call, since there is no LiveKit server here.
- **Leave space** in the space bar uses the same icon as **Sign out** (`right-from-bracket`). Checked in the page's markup; not seen drawn in a real call.
- The page's own menus work from the keyboard: the account menu, the canvas title bars' **…** and a module's **…** that the host draws. The first entry takes focus when a menu opens; the Up and Down arrows, Home and End move; Enter or Space picks; Escape closes it and puts focus back on its button; Tab closes it. Screen readers hear each entry as part of a menu, and the button says whether its menu is open. The shared menu moved from `public/module-host.js` (`toggleOverflow`, gone) to `public/host-menu.js` (`openHostMenu`, `closeHostMenu`, which module-host.js re-exports), and a page can take over **View profile** with the cancelable `app:open-profile` event; see `architecture-module-window.md`. Keyboard use verified live in headless Chromium. In a space with push to talk on, Space does not pick (see known-issues.md); Enter does.
- Theme, template and research files, and the block an AI writes objects in, are named by what they are, not by the product (plan-kind-names, the follow-up to #110), so the product's name can change without changing a format. A theme exports as `<name>.theme.json`, holding `{ "format": "theme", "formatVersion": 1, "name", "author"?, "light", "dark" }`. A template exports as `<name>.template.json`, holding `{ "format": "template", "formatVersion": 1, ...its fields }`, with its theme inside and unmarked. Research from another AI comes as one ```` ```objects ```` block or as `<something>.objects.json`, holding `{ "format": "objects", "formatVersion": 1, "objects": [...] }`; **Copy instructions for another AI** asks for those. `GET /api/objects/format` answers `fence: "objects"` and `fileSuffix: ".objects.json"`; its schema has no `$id`, and a file there requires `format`, `formatVersion` and `objects`. One rule reads the marker for all three (`server/file-format.js`). A ```` ```card ```` block is no longer the format's own: its objects are found only by the loose reading, which still reads an answer that lost its fences. The host template editor's theme picker no longer reads the file itself: the server does (`POST /api/host/themes/check`, under Added), and the picker shows " Left out: ..." after the theme's name when part of the file was dropped. The pages no longer write a product name of their own: they start with none and fill it in from the server's branding (the product page's title shows the brand, Coffee Pub, until then). Documented in `api-modules.md` ("The objects format"), `api-module-sdk.md`, `architecture-modules.md`, `architecture-overview.md`, `architecture-environments.md`, `design-theme.md` and the Themes, Templates, Chat and Assistant guides; the plan says built. Verified: checked by tools (`tools/check-themes.mjs`, `tools/check-templates.mjs`, `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`, `tools/check-object-format.mjs`, `tools/check-ai.mjs`, `tools/check-origin.mjs`, `tools/check-module-versions.mjs`, and the rest of `npm run check`, apart from the two `--migration` cases of `tools/check-names.mjs` that fail only as root in a cloud container), through two rounds of QA; verified live on a local server with curl and in headless Chromium. Not checked: the chat's **Bring in** button in a browser, which needs a call, and a real `/ai` reply, since there is no AI key here.
- **Files and answers in the old formats are refused, not read.** Anything named after a past product name, the one before Collaborator or Collaborator itself, gets a sentence saying what to do, and nothing is imported. A theme file: "That theme file is in an older format. Export the theme again and import the new file." A template file: "That template file is in an older format. Export the template again and import the new file." An objects file: "that file is in an older format: copy the instructions again and ask the AI for a new file". A pasted answer with a block fenced with a past label: 400 "that answer is in an older format: copy the instructions again and ask the AI for a new answer". An old-named block counts in any letter case, with words after the label, indented, inside a list or a quote, with Windows line ends, unclosed, and even beside an ```` ```objects ```` block, so half an answer is never brought in without a word; a label that only starts with a past name (```` ```collaborators ````) does not count. A real object with a key such as `relatedObjects` is still read as an object. "That isn't a <product> theme file." and "This theme was made by a newer version of <product>." are as before, as are the template ones. To move an old theme or template, export it again from this version; for research, copy the instructions again. Pasted into the Chat box, an old answer offers no **Bring in** button, because the objects check route refuses it (the route was checked; the button was not seen in a browser). Checked by `tools/check-object-format.mjs` (every case above, with the old files and answers kept in `tools/fixtures/format-old/`), `tools/check-themes.mjs` and `tools/check-templates.mjs`, and verified live with curl.
- `/ai` asks the AI for the part worth keeping in ```` ```objects ```` blocks and calls each one an object; it asked for ```` ```card ```` blocks. Its answers are read from blocks labelled `objects`, `summary`, `json` or nothing; `card` and the product-named label are no longer read. Assistant 0.1.22 (a comment only). Checked by `tools/check-ai.mjs` and `tools/check-object-format.mjs` (the rule's text); a real reply was not seen, since there is no AI key here.
- The version line's commit comes from `APP_REVISION`, which the image sets to the commit it was built from. `TAVERN_REVISION` is no longer read. Only the image ever set it, so nothing needs changing unless your own deployment sets `TAVERN_REVISION`: set `APP_REVISION` instead, or the version shows `dev`. Documented in `architecture-environments.md` and `userguide-getting-started.md`. Read as code in the Dockerfile and `server/index.js`; not checked in a built image (no Docker here).
- The server names itself when it fetches from elsewhere: `<product>/<version>`, from `PRODUCT_NAME` and the package version (for example `Collaborator/0.4.0`), for link previews, and the same followed by `(+https://github.com/Drowbe/coffee-pub-collaborator)` for place search, so the service can identify and reach whoever runs it, as the OpenStreetMap services ask. It sent `Collaborator` and `CoffeePubApp`. Only printable ASCII is kept from the name, spaces become `-`, and the default stands in when nothing is left. Documented in `userguide-places.md`. Checked by `tools/check-link-preview.mjs` and `tools/check-geocode.mjs`.
- `tools/check-names.mjs` fails on any product name written into the code: the default, read from its one definition in `server/product-name.js`, and the past names magpie and tavern, in any letter case, in any line (comments too) and in file and folder names under `server/`, `public/`, `modules/`, `tools/` and `templates/`, and in the `Dockerfile`. The brand, Coffee Pub, and the repository and image names are allowed; anything else only by a `product-name` entry in `tools/check-names-allow.json` with its reason (the read-once migrations, the checks that prove them, the default's definition, the two past block labels, the old fixtures). The checks whose sentences carry the name run with `PRODUCT_NAME=Testname`, so a hard-coded default fails them. If the default ever changes, the old one must be added to `PAST_PRODUCT_NAMES` by hand. Documented in `architecture-environments.md` ("The product name guard"). Checked by running `tools/check-names.mjs`, which also tests its own matching on made-up text.
- The space bar's **Grid size** goes one step smaller: 50 to 320px (it was 60), in steps of 10, 130 by default. A module still takes enough cells for its smallest size, and a saved size of 60 or more keeps working. Documented in `architecture-canvas.md`. Verified live in headless Chromium (the slider's smallest value is 50, the keyboard steps down to 50 and stops, and two modules snapped side by side with the grid already at 50 do not overlap) and checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and `tools/check-nav.mjs`; not checked in a real call. Moving the slider can still make modules tiled side by side overlap; that was there before (see known-issues).
- Opening a space is entering it, not joining the call (GitHub #3). The space list's button is **Enter**, with a door, and **Back to <space>** while you are connected to that space; the pop-out button is "Enter in a pop-out window", and the sliders button's list is headed **Open with**. The card says "3 here · 2 in the call", "3 here" or "Nobody here" (it said "n/m Online"), and a camera mark shows who is in the call. The status line reads "in <space>" whether or not you are in the call. The space settings' launch-link hint says "next to Enter". Verified live in headless Chromium at 1280 and 390 px (presence faked; there is no LiveKit server here). Entering and **Back to** were read as code only. The guest form still says **Join**.
- A space you have not been in opens the chat and every module on in it, without the conference. It opened the conference. The order is: a fresh request from the dashboard, your remembered layout (never for a guest), the space's **Opens with**, the environment's list for a new space, then that default. A guest with nothing set gets the conference and the chat, and the guest form mentions the microphone only then (since 2026-09-30 the conference opens out of the call and the guest form has no microphone note; see Changed). A list with nothing that can open opens nothing. In the narrow layout the first tab is the first thing opened, not always the call. Worked out by `whatOpens` in `public/opens-with.js`, checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`; entering itself, a first and a second visit, a guest's visit and the narrow layout's first tab were read as code only, and a real call was not checked. Documented in `userguide-call.md`, `userguide-spaces.md`, `userguide-accounts.md` and `architecture-canvas.md`; `plan-canvas.md`'s old default is marked replaced.
- The conference switch holds for owners and the admin too. With the conference switched off, nobody is offered it in a space, and entering does not open it. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` (`conferenceAllowed`); read as code only in the page.
- A command typed in chat always appears as a message, starting **private**. Its portrait is that command's icon and its name is the module's (**Polls**, **Calendar**, **AI**). **Make public** posts it later.
- A chat message is a header and a container. The header is a step darker than the message. On the left: a portrait, the sender's name, and the time. On the right: **private** or **public**, then **…** with **Copy**, **Reply**, **Send to...** and **Delete**. **Delete** on a chat message leaves "Message deleted". **Send to...** is a placeholder. Choosing private or public offers **Make public** or **Make private**. Making an AI answer public posts it to the space.
- Chat's formatting row is a short strip again. Formatting sits on the left; bring-in, commands and help sit on the right. Emoji, help and the command list open above their button and stay on screen. Bringing research in opens a row above that strip: paste a whole answer, then **Copy instructions**, **Choose a file**, **Close** and **Import**. Import puts the objects in the chat, and **Keep ticked** is where you decide what stays.
- The shared bottom row has one height, `--bar-h`. A second row in chat takes its space from the messages, so the other modules' bars stay `--bar-h`. **Private** / **Shared** sits on the same toolbar a module uses under its title, and only when `/ai` is available.
- The product is **Collaborator**. The GitHub repository is `coffee-pub-collaborator`, and new images are `ghcr.io/drowbe/coffee-pub-collaborator`. An existing NAS can keep its old data folder; point the compose volume at the folder you still have, or rename that folder to `/share/appdata/coffee-pub-collaborator` first.
- Chat's formatting menu icons: **Bring in research** is `square-share-nodes`, **Commands** is `square-poll-horizontal`, and **Formatting help** is `square-pen`.
- Chat's **Private** / **Shared** switch for `/ai` replies sits in the Chat header, next to **Clear your AI thread**, not in the formatting menu. It shows only when you may use `/ai`.
- Planner 0.7.44: today's day no longer has an accent bar on the left of the header. **· today** in the heading and the dot on the day chip still mark it.
- The prompt for Magpie's own AI and **Copy instructions for another AI** now asks for one fenced JSON array of every object, not a separate fence per object. An answer that still uses one fence per object is still read. Checked by a tool; not verified live.
- Research 0.2.20 and Planner 0.7.43: the in-page title is gone where the titlebar already names the module. Research's count sits next to the name in the titlebar. The Planner titlebar is the trip's Name; days and travellers sit after a | on the dates line.
- Chat /ai follow-ups from the docs pass: **Ask the assistant** asks in Chat (it no longer waits on a window that never opens); **Clear your AI thread** is in the chat header; Bring in research uses the same Assistant **Use** rule as `/ai`; a **Private** / **Shared** switch sits in the Chat header (Shared posts the reply to the space); new installs leave the Assistant out even if a template lists it. The Assistant's old window is gone (0.1.21). Checked by a tool (`npm run check`); not verified live in a browser or a real call. Documented in `userguide-chat.md`, `userguide-assistant.md` and `plan-one-input.md` (steps 7 and 8 built).
- The **...** on a card, a day, a place, a poll, and a module bar is one shared button (`host.ui.moreButton` / `.sdk-more`): the icon is centered. Research 0.2.19, Planner 0.7.42, Places 0.8.18, Polls 1.12.17.
- Polls 1.12.16 (#58 phase 2): the add box is **New poll**. Typing is `/v` in Chat, which opens the same form.
- A module's action bar puts the primary button on the far right and the rest to its left. As the window shrinks, leftover buttons come off the left into **...**.
- Research 0.2.18: **Add a note**, **Add a link** and **Add a photo** each show an icon and their words. A blank link form; a URL in Chat `/r` still opens one filled in. **Research this** in an object's menu asks in Chat (a private `/ai` about that object), not the Assistant window.
- To-do 1.11.14, Calendar 1.17.17 and Research 0.2.17 (#58 phase 2): each add box is a button (**New todo**, **Add event**, **Add a note**). Typing is `/t`, `/c` or `/r` in Chat, which opens the same form.
- Planner 0.7.41 (#58 phase 2): the add box is **Add plan**, with icon-only **Flight**, **Hotel**, **Restaurant** and **Note**. Typing is `/p` in Chat, which opens the same form.
- Chat's box now reads **Chat or type / for commands...**
- Chat (#58): **Bring in research** is in the formatting menu (file-import). **Copy instructions for another AI**, paste or a file, then Preview: a tick per object and **Keep ticked**. Pasting an answer still offers **Bring in N objects**, which now opens that preview. The command picker sits in the formatting menu, a **private** badge marks AI replies, and an answer's objects show as a preview with Keep on each. The Assistant stays a module (its **Use** permission still gates `/ai`) but has no window: `surfaces.canvas.menu: false` (0.1.20).
- Research 0.2.16, To-do 1.11.13, Planner 0.7.40, Calendar 1.17.16 and Assistant 0.1.19 for Chat commands.
- Planner (Travel) 0.7.39: when an object is added to the trip or its day is changed, the trip's first and last day grow to cover it — a date before the first day, a date or stay check-out after the last day, or the day a journey arrives. The range never shrinks. Import via the Assistant and Keep ticked use the same path.
- Assistant 0.1.17 and Planner (Travel) 0.7.38 for the research import: an object's text can be up to 6,000 characters, links may be `http` or `https` and are shown and kept, a waiting keep counts as sent, the action bus keeps a `text` field up to 8,000 characters, and the Planner stores notes up to 8,000. At most 50 objects per import; the Assistant's own replies stay at 20. Guests, and a space with AI turned off, cannot import; anyone else who may use the Assistant can, with or without an AI service.
- **Magpie is no longer marked MIT.** `package.json` now says `"license": "UNLICENSED"` (commercial, all rights reserved) and the README says so; the license terms, a server lock and purchased keys are coming (#72).
- **Breaking: `TAVERN_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, `TAVERN_ADMIN_KEY` and `ADMIN_KEY` are no longer read, on single and hosted installs.** **Before updating, move the value to `ADMIN_PASSWORD`.** An install that set only one of them loses that password: a new install gets an admin with a random password, logged once; an install that already has accounts makes and changes nobody, and logs "This install has no server admin. Set ADMIN_LOGIN and ADMIN_PASSWORD, then restart, to have one." Each start that still sets one logs "<NAME> is no longer read: use ADMIN_PASSWORD instead."; the old "stops working in a later release" and "ignored on a server with environments" lines are gone.
