They join the names in `REMOVED_CONFIG_NAMES` (`server/index.js`). Documented in `architecture-environments.md`, `userguide-getting-started.md` and `plan-names.md`. `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` now covers each name alone on a new single install (the line is logged, the old value signs nobody in, a random password is made) and all three on a hosted server (one line each, the old value sets no password); its passing is QA's to report. Not verified live here.

### Removed
- The full space settings page and the module settings page (their old addresses redirect to Set up), the bottom button bar of module windows, the space bar, the separate view code, and the "hide the video" control.
- The call control in the middle of the space bar ("2 in the call · Join", from `plan-entering.md` decisions 2 and 16, added earlier in this release) is gone (Thomas, 2026-09-30). Joining lives in the Conference: switch it on from **Modules** and choose **Join the call**. Its microphone note and install hint moved there. The middle of the bar now shows who is here. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, which fails if the control comes back.

## [0.4.0] - 2026-09-25

### Fixed
- **Security: a page on another site could make changes as whoever was signed in.** A signed-in person's cookie still went along with a request from another origin on the same site (every environment is a subdomain of the host's domain), and a plain form needs no permission to send one. Now any POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE that carries a cookie and comes from another origin is refused with 403 "This request came from another site, so it was refused." Not affected: Studio and anything else signing with a `Bearer` token, requests with no cookie, and `POST /login`, the landing page's sign-in. Follow-ups are GitHub #70. Documented in `architecture-overview.md`. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- Theme import follow-ups (GitHub #67): an environment holds at most 100 themes, and an import past that is refused with "This environment has 100 themes, the most it can hold. Delete one to import another."; control and text-direction characters are taken out of a theme's name and author, and a name is cut at 40 whole characters (never half an emoji); a file in an encoding the server can't read gets "That isn't a Magpie theme file."; a UTF-16 file imports; and Manage's Theme tab fits the narrow layout. `userguide-themes.md` covers it. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- **Security: the stream access key could be used to sign in as anyone, an owner included.** `GET /api/status`, which the access key in every OBS link can read, sent every person's personal sign-in link, and a personal link signs its holder straight in. Separately, anyone signed in, a guest or a key holder could read every space's guest link (`GET /api/spaces`, `GET /api/presence`, the aside answers). Now a personal link is sent only to owners and to that person, and never in `/api/status`; a guest link only to owners and to members of that space who may manage its guest link (anyone else gets `null`). The environment's AI key and the host's managed AI keys are now encrypted at rest (a plain one is encrypted when first loaded), and `app.json`, `host.json`, `ai.json` and `secrets.key` are readable only by the server's own user, set on every start and after a restore or a migration. Moving a single server onto a hosted one keeps its AI key and two-step sign-in working, re-encrypted to the host's key. An AI key that can't be read on this server (data restored onto another host) no longer stops the other AI settings from saving; the AI settings say to enter the key again. **After upgrading, regenerate the stream access key (Manage > Environment > Access key) and give Coffee Pub Studio, or each OBS source, the new one.** File modes for the chat and module files, and some checks, are follow-ups (GitHub #69, in `known-issues.md`). Verified live on throwaway servers in every role, with the pages in a headless browser; the original exploit no longer works.
- Password boxes are masked (GitHub #61): Manage's new user, a profile's new password, and on the host console a host admin's and a first owner's. Each has a **Show** / **Hide** button, and is masked again after saving or cancelling. A check now fails on a password or secret field that isn't masked. Verified live.
- The Calendar no longer shows an error after the whole app is popped out, or when several modules close at once (GitHub #21). The chat in a window of its own now puts the cursor in its message box and sends on Enter; both used to show an error (GitHub #22). Polls and the To-do no longer show an error after they are closed, and the drop menu no longer fails when a drop offers two or more choices. Verified live.
- A link to a Calendar event opens the event again when the link is what first loads the Calendar (Calendar 1.17.11, GitHub #19), including the Calendar's space page and a Calendar just opened on a space's canvas. Before, it opened the Calendar but not the event.
- The page for a personal link that was turned off or replaced (`GET /j/:token`) shows the environment's name and icon (GitHub #18). Before, the page's own security policy blocked its script, so it always said "Coffee Pub". `tools/check-syntax.mjs` now fails on any page whose inline script its own security policy would block.
- Six things found and fixed while the pages were renamed (step 5b of the Names plan): inviting someone to a private conversation from **Who's around** always failed; Manage > Users never showed that someone was "in <space>"; a module's configuration never showed its line about each space's moderators choosing their own settings; the **Spaces** link during a call reloaded the page and dropped the call; the **Clear chat** overlay lost its styling to a broken style-sheet comment; and an old link to an item (`/modules/<id>?space=...#ref=...`) opens its item again. Verified live without a call; anything in a call is read as code only.
- On the Modules tab, a change the server refuses now changes nothing (GitHub #17). Before, a request that turned a module off together with the modules that need it, and also asked for something refused (an unknown run mode, say), still left them off while showing the error. Verified live, through the API and on the Modules tab.
- A Planner journey can be up to 7 days long (Travel 0.7.31, GitHub #14 and #15). Before, anything over 24 hours was cut to 24 hours, so a 24 h 30 min flight saved as 24 h and its arrival showed the same time as its departure. A day's time column now puts a later-day arrival on its own line ("→ 10:00 AM", then "the next day" or "+2 days"), and the cards and the editor say it in full. **Trip ends** sits on the day the last journey arrives, and after the plan's last day it shows the date over the time. A length over 7 days is refused with "A length can be at most 7 days (168 hours)." (a journey's length, a return leg's, and how long it takes from the stop before). The Bookings, Money and Decisions views follow the environment's clock ("9:40 AM" on the 12-hour clock). Verified live.
- A refused save now changes nothing (GitHub #16). Before, the parts of a save that were fine were kept even though the page showed an error: Manage's settings (a clock change sent with a currency the server doesn't know), editing a person (a refused role still saved the new login or name), a space (a refused link or profile still saved the name or description) and, on the host console, an environment (a refused status still saved the name or plan). Now the whole save is refused, and the error names the field to fix. A module update that is refused part-way can still apply its first part; that is GitHub #17, in `known-issues.md`. Verified live.
- Manage's **Currency** list shows every currency by its code and name, the common ones first, and the server refuses a code it doesn't know with "XYZ is not a currency this server knows. Choose one from the list, such as USD." (GitHub #4, the server's setting). Before, a request made directly could store any three letters. A code already stored is kept, and still shown in the list. The currencies are the ISO 4217 codes the server's own Node knows (`Intl.supportedValuesOf('currency')`, `CURRENCIES` in `server/store.js`). The Planner's trip currency is still typed; a shared picker is next. Verified live.
- The Planner (Travel 0.7.27) is clearer about journeys and stays (GitHub #5, #7, #8, #10, #11). A length is entered as hours and minutes and shown as "8 h 15 min", instead of a number of minutes. A journey's fields say what they are ("Departure day", "Departure time", "Flight time", "Departs from", "Arrives at", "Departure airport", "Departure gate", "Coach" for a train), and once it has a time and a length the form shows when it arrives, "the next day" when it runs past midnight (time zones are not taken into account); a flight's and a train's card say "Departs" and "Arrives". A stay has **Check in** and **Check-in time**, and **Check out** is now any date after check-in, even after the trip's last day, with its time; the stay's **Room** field is called Room again. The Bookings view shows a stay as a date range with its nights. The trip form asks for **Name**, **Where**, **First day** and **Last day**. Verified live in the Planner in a headless browser.
- Anthropic refused every request made with an organisation-level key ("This API key is not scoped to a workspace, so this request must include the anthropic-workspace-id header"; found the moment the AI diagnostics landed). The host console's Anthropic row and an environment's AI configuration page (custom source) gain a **Workspace id** field, sent as `workspace` and carried as that header by the server half; a key made inside a workspace needs nothing there. The seed from the server's environment is `AI_ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE`.
- The host console never got Font Awesome: `admin.<base>`'s own hostname resolver handed every request straight to `hostRouter`, whose catch-all 404'd `/fa/css/*` and `/fa/webfonts/*` before they ever reached the two static mounts that serve them (registered on `app`, since the bare base domain needs them too). Visible as the shared map files table's delete buttons drawing as empty red ovals -- any icon on the console was silently missing its font. Fixed the same way the bare base domain already exempts its own handful of paths: `/fa/` on `admin.<base>` now falls through past `hostRouter` to those mounts, Font Awesome Pro first if the admin has dropped one in, same as everywhere else.
- `server/region-cut.js`'s two "the world file address is not set up right" messages named a "Module Configuration" screen that does not exist anywhere in the product; reworded to "the world map address is not set" (`RegionCutJobs.estimate`/`start`, `validSource` false), leaving it to whichever page raised the error to say where to fix it -- on the console, the address field sits right above the region cutter itself.
- The same file's other two CLI-failure messages, in `reason()`, said "world file"; reworded to "world map" so every region-cut error shares the one noun -- "could not be reached" and, for a 404 or `NoSuchKey`, `answered "not found"; check the address` (that last clause replacing the same stale "Module Configuration" reference the `validSource` messages had).

### Added
- The host console's template editor shows module names instead of ids. `GET /api/host/settings` gains `modules: [{ id, name, icon }]` (the conference and the chat, then the bundled modules by id), and the console's template view of an environment always carries `source`, `version`, `appliedVersion` and `offerOpen`; for an environment that isn't open, `offerOpen` comes from the stored record, so it can over-report until the environment is opened. Documented in `architecture-tenants.md`. Checked by the tools only (`check-templates`, `check-template-switch`); not verified live.
- The host admin can make templates, and templates travel as files (plan-environment-templates, addendum 2, GitHub #68). The host console has a **Templates** tab: every template with where it comes from (**Bundled**, **Yours**, **Imported**), its version and how many environments use it, with **New template**, **Edit**, **Hidden**, **Delete**, **Import…** and **Export**. A template can now also give reactions, a theme and a set of icons, each applied once when an environment is made. Each template has a version: editing one makes a new version, its words, home icon and module names and icons reach every environment using it at once, and the rest is offered on Manage's **Template** tab ("Version N, updated: review what's new"), listing only what the template changed since the owner last applied or passed it over, with the reactions, the theme and the icons as unticked choices. A hidden template is no longer offered for new environments, and one in use can't be deleted ("Environments use this template: <names>. Hide it instead."). Templates export as `<name>.magpie-template.json` (at most 64 KB, its theme inside) and import keeping the file's version; a taken id asks for another. On a single server, Manage > Template has **Template files**: **Export**, **Import…** and **Delete** for an imported template not in use; on a hosted server an owner can only export the template their environment uses. A bundled template's version is raised by hand when what it applies once changes, recorded with `node tools/check-templates.mjs --update` and enforced by `check-templates` (`tools/template-versions.json`). New routes: `GET`, `POST`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /api/host/templates[/:id]`, `GET /api/host/templates/:id/export`, `POST /api/host/templates/import`, and for owners `POST /api/templates/import`, `GET /api/templates/:id/export` and `DELETE /api/templates/:id`; the offer and apply gain `reactions`, `theme` and `iconSet`. Not yet built: **Reactions** and **Icons** still sit on the Theme tab rather than moving to the Template tab. Documented in `architecture-tenants.md` (Templates, and the host API) and `userguide-templates.md`. Checked by the tools and a headless smoke test only (new cases in `check-templates`, `check-template-switch` and `check-themes`); not yet verified live in a browser, and full QA is to come.
- Owners and the host can switch an environment's template, or remove it (GitHub #59). On Manage's new **Template** tab, **Switch to** and **Switch**; on the host console, the **Template** choice on an environment's card. Words, icons and module names change at once, and the owner's own still win. Then the template offers what else it can add: its modules (ticked; one the plan leaves out is shown, not ticked, with why), and, unticked, its Lobby name and its new-space profile. Only what is ticked is applied (**Apply**; **Not now** keeps it for later behind **Review**). Nothing is turned off, and no data is touched. For developers: `PATCH /api/settings { template }`, `GET /api/environment/template`, `POST /api/environment/template/apply`, and on the console `PATCH /api/host/environments/:slug { template }`, `GET /api/host/environments/:slug/template` and `POST .../template/apply`; a switched template carries `switchedAt` and is never applied on its own at a restart; `templateHistory` in `app.json` keeps the last 20 switches. Documented in `architecture-tenants.md`, "Templates", and `userguide-templates.md`. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- Manage has a **Template** tab (plan-environment-templates, addendum 2, GitHub #68, step 2). It holds the template and its offer, **Words**, **Module names and icons** and **Home icon**, which has its own **Save**. Old `#words` and `#home-icon` links open it at that section. Each Modules card now reads "Shown as <name>" with a **Change** link to its row. Rows of tabs scroll sideways within themselves in the narrow layout rather than pushing the page wider. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- Owners can share a theme as a file (GitHub #67). On Manage > Theme, **Export** saves the theme in the chooser, Strong Coffee included, as `<name>.magpie-theme.json`, and **Import…** adds a theme from such a file. An import is always a new theme (a taken name becomes "Name (2)"), is never applied until you press **Apply**, keeps its author's name, and carries only colours; anything else in the file is left out and listed. The API is `GET /api/themes/:id/export` and `POST /api/themes/import`; the owner's guide is the new `userguide-themes.md`, "Creating themes".
- A light or dark switch next to the gear at the top of every environment page (GitHub #62). It changes at once, without a refresh. A signed-in person's choice is saved on their account and follows them to other devices; a guest's stays in the browser; and once someone has used it, the owner's default no longer changes it for them. Manage > Theme's switch is now **Dark by default**, the environment's default. An owner's change of theme or default now reaches every open page without a refresh (a guest's within about a minute), which fixes pages needing a reload to show a new theme. For module authors: `host.on('theme', fn)` now fires on a change of mode as well as of theme. For developers: `PATCH /api/me` takes `{ themeMode: 'light' | 'dark' | null }`, users carry `themeMode`, `branding()` carries `themeMode` and `themeVersion`, `/theme.css` holds both modes under `data-theme-mode`, and the notifications stream sends `theme` and `mode`. Verified live in every role, in a headless browser.
- **Templates: an environment can be set up for one use when it is made** (step 3 of `documentation/plans/plan-environment-templates.md`, which is now done). The first is **Travel**: spaces are called trips, the Lobby is "Home base" ("Everyone on every trip."), the Planner shows as "Itinerary", the Itinerary, Places, Maps, Research and the Calendar are on in every trip with the chat and the call, the home icon is a rolling suitcase, and a new trip starts with the Participants profile. A template is picked only when the environment is made: a **Template** choice (and a **Plan** choice) on the host console's New environment form, the same on the product page's sign-up when the host has templates, or `TEMPLATE=<id>` on a single server's new data folder (ignored, with a log line, on existing data or on a server with environments; an unknown id stops the start). Its settings, modules, Lobby and new-space defaults are applied once; its words, home icon and module names and icons keep following it, and anything an owner changes wins, with **Reset** going back to the template's. A module it can't turn on (not in the plan, needing one left off, or Research until the AI service is on) is listed, with how to fix it, on the console card and in Manage's new **Template** panel. Backups carry the template's record. For developers: `templates/<id>.json` (checked at start and by `tools/check-templates.mjs`), `GET /api/host/templates`, `template` on `POST /api/host/environments` and `POST /api/product/signup`, and `template`, `ownHomeIcon`, `templateWords`, `templateHomeIcon` and `spaceDefaults` on `/api/settings`; see `architecture-tenants.md`, "Templates", and the new guide `userguide-templates.md`. Planner 0.7.35 rewords a sentence that read "A trip belongs to a trip". The chat and the call as switchable modules stay in `plan-optional-conference.md`. Verified live on hosted and single installs.
- An owner can show a module under another name and icon (step 2 of `documentation/plans/plan-environment-templates.md`). Every card on Manage > Modules, the built-in Conference and Chat included, has **Shown as**: an icon (the module's own first, then the environment's solid icons and every module's own) and a name (blank for the module's own), with **Save** ("Saved. Shown as <name>") and **Reset** ("Back to its own name and icon"). The card's title is the display name, with the module's own name, version and author beside it. That name and icon are shown everywhere the module is named: the header, the dashboard (beside a widget's own title, which stays), the Roles grid, a space's module menu and each module's header, Join with, pop-outs, Module Configuration, notifications, activity and messages, and the module's own headings. A refused name or icon is explained and nothing is saved. Uninstalling with the data deleted clears them; a plain uninstall keeps them. The text in a module's `module.json` (its description, widget title, permission and setting labels, help and options, event and action labels, kind names) now follows the environment's words too, through `{space}`-style placeholders, so the known issue about it is gone. For developers: `PATCH /api/modules/:id` takes `{ displayName, displayIcon }`; `GET /api/modules` adds `displayName`, `displayIcon`, `ownDisplayName` and `ownDisplayIcon`; `GET /api/modules/for-space` gives the built-ins, and notifications carry `moduleName` and `icon`; a module prints its own name from `host.info.module.name`, now the display name; `tools/check-names.mjs --words` reads manifests. Assistant 0.1.14, Calendar 1.17.10, Maps 0.7.16, Places 0.8.15, Polls 1.12.13, Research 0.2.13, Stream 0.1.6, To-do 1.11.10, Planner 0.7.34. Verified live.
- An environment can use its own words (step 1 of `documentation/plans/plan-environment-templates.md`). On Manage > Environment, **Words** lets an owner rename the ten changeable words (environment, space, aside, canvas, module, object, owner, moderator, member and guest): a singular, a plural and, when "a" or "an" is wrong, the word with its article. Blank means the default, **Save** saves them all, **Reset** puts one back at once, and a refused word is explained in the server's own sentence. The host's words (host and admin) stay as they are. Everything people read in the environment follows: the pages, the server's messages, the Roles grid, the built-in modules' texts, new spaces' names ("New trip", "Trip 3") and the bundled modules' own words; the text in a module's `module.json` does not yet (see `known-issues.md`). For developers: `PATCH /api/settings` takes `{ words }`, `branding()` carries `words` for all twelve keys and `GET/PATCH /api/settings` give the owner `ownWords`; modules get `host.locale().words`, `host.util.word(key, { many, cap, a })` and `host.util.fillWords(root)`; pages use `public/words.js` with `data-word`, `data-word-form` and `data-fill`; and `tools/check-names.mjs --words` fails on a changeable word typed into text people read. Assistant 0.1.13, Calendar 1.17.9, Maps 0.7.15, Places 0.8.14, Polls 1.12.12, Research 0.2.12, Stream 0.1.5, To-do 1.11.9, Planner 0.7.33. Verified live: with no words set, 44 page visits in every role and 28 API refusals read identically to the previous build; with words set ("trip", "hour" with "an hour", "game master", "player", "visitor"), everything checked read naturally; two hosted environments never shared words, the browser's cache included. Read as code only: the call page in a real call.
- A currency picker, shared (finishes GitHub #4). The Planner's trip **Currency** (Travel 0.7.30) is now a list with **Default (<the environment's currency>)** first, instead of three letters typed by hand; a trip saved earlier with a lower-case or odd code still shows it. Manage's **Currency** list is now exactly the list the server accepts, with names in the viewer's language. Under it: `GET /api/currencies` (signed in; 401 "sign in first") answers `{ currencies }`, the sorted codes the server takes, plus the stored code if it is not among them; modules get the same list as `host.locale().currencies`; and the SDK gains `host.ui.currencySelect(select, { value, empty, onChange })`, returning `{ set, value, destroy }`, which draws a "Common" group and then "All currencies" by name and keeps an odd stored value as its own option. Pages that are not modules get the same drawing from `window.hostCurrency` in `/sdk/host.js`. The contract is in `api-module-sdk.md`, "Shared tools". Verified live.
- Round trips in the Planner (Travel 0.7.29, GitHub #9). Any journey except a rental car has a **Round trip** switch; the return gets its own day, time, length, ends, number and seat, and an optional title. The booking reference, cost and payer are entered once and cover both legs, so Money counts the cost once and Bookings shows one row with both days and the route both ways ("LHR ⇄ JFK"). Each leg sits on its own day and names the other's ("Round trip · Return" and its day), a return dated before its outbound is flagged, and editing either leg opens both. Turning the switch off asks, and removes the return on Save; **Delete** asks whether to delete both legs or only this one, and deleting only the outbound moves the booking details to the return. A second return saved by mistake can be deleted from the form. Verified live.
- An empty day in the Planner (Travel 0.7.28) invites you to add to it (GitHub #6): it reads "Nothing planned yet. Click to add." ("Tap to add." on a touch screen), and clicking, tapping or pressing Enter opens the same menu as the day's "..." button, with the day already filled in when the form opens. Only people who can plan the trip see the invitation; others see "Nothing planned yet." A day with things on it is unchanged: its blank space does nothing, and you add through its "...", the + between days or the bottom bar. Verified live in a browser with a mouse, the keyboard and touch emulation.
- The Planner (Travel 0.7.27) has three more ways of getting there: **taxi**, **ride share** and **shuttle**, each with its own tile, card colour and fields (a taxi or ride share has a pick-up time, a travel time, and where it picks up and drops off). **Getting to this stop** also offers ride share. Verified live in a headless browser.
- The groundwork for renaming the code to the architecture's names (step 1 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). A server now refuses data a newer version of Magpie has updated, rather than misread it, and the host console can restore a backup. On a single-environment install, or when `host.json` is affected, the start stops with one log line naming the file. On a hosted server only that environment is refused: it answers 503 with a plain sentence, drops out of the product page's list, and its console card shows "won't open", why, and what to do, while the others keep running. Every card gains **Restore backup**: choose a zip, then confirm **Replace its data?**. A restore of a newer backup answers 400 and changes nothing; a restore whose data still won't open answers `{ ok: true, refused }`; a good one brings the environment back without a restart. Under it: `server/migrate-names.js`, the migration's frame with no parts yet (the record in `app.json`'s `version` and `migrations` and in `host.json`'s `migrations`, the copies in `pre-names/`, a staged commit); `refused` on each environment in `GET /api/host/tenants`; `server/studio-alias.js`, wired to bearer-token requests on `GET /api/me` and `GET /api/status` and adding nothing yet; and `tools/check-names.mjs` (in `npm run check`), which reports the old names left in the code by level, reports "room" and "table" in what people read (`--words`), and runs the frame twice over `tools/fixtures/names-v1/` (`--migration`), with its allow-list in `tools/check-names-allow.json`. No module changed. Details are in `architecture-tenants.md`, "The Names migration" and "Refused environments". Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`, single-environment and hosted, with the console driven in a headless browser; checked by `npm run check`. With no part built yet, the migration's writes and moves have been run only with stand-in parts in the check, never on real data. Nothing here touches the call.
- The agent team: `CLAUDE.md` (the project's instructions for Claude), seven agents in `.claude/agents/` (project-manager, product-planner, experience-design, server-development, bug-fixes, quality-assurance, content-manager) and the `/manage` and `/plan-feature` skills in `.claude/skills/`. `.claude/worktrees/`, which agent runs leave behind, is ignored.
- Two-step sign-in, the server half (`documentation/plans/plan-mfa.md`): TOTP (RFC 6238, six digits, thirty seconds, one step of drift, a code accepted once via `mfa.lastStep`), ten recovery codes hashed like passwords, and the secret encrypted at rest (AES-256-GCM, `DATA_DIR/secrets.key` on a single server, `host.json`'s `secretsKey` -- one key for the whole host -- with environments; neither ever rides along in an owner's export or the console's backup). New in `server/auth.js`: `totpSecret`/`totpCode`/`totpVerify`/`otpauthUrl`, `encryptSecret`/`decryptSecret`, `recoveryCodes`, and the pending token (`issuePending`/`readPending`, ten minutes, the `mfa_pending` cookie) alongside the thirty-day "remember this browser" one (`mfa_trust`, reusing `issueSession`/`readSession` directly -- same shape, same lifetime); `userStamp` folds in `mfa.version`, so a reset, a fresh enrolment, or a spent recovery code signs every session and trusted browser out at once. The user record gains `mfa: { secret, enrolledAt, recovery, version, lastStep, pending } | null`; `publicUser` exposes only `mfaEnrolled`. The pending step sits between the first factor and a session at all three doors (`POST /api/login`, the form `POST /login`, `GET /j/:token` -- a personal link is a first factor, not a bypass) and the host console's own (`POST /api/host/login`): `POST /api/login/verify` and `POST /api/host/login/verify` take a TOTP or a recovery code and finish signing in; `GET /login/verify` and `GET /login/enrol` serve the pages session's `login-step.html`. Enrolment (`POST /api/me/mfa/start`/`enable`/`disable`, and the host router's mirrored `/api/host/me/mfa/*`) works for the signed-in person or -- with no session yet -- whoever holds a valid `enrol` pending token; the QR comes from the `qrcode` package as an SVG string, never fetched from anywhere. An admin resets a member's factor (`DELETE /api/users/:key/mfa`, never their own); a host admin resets an owner's by login, never a key, since the console never learns an environment's own user keys (`DELETE /api/host/tenants/:slug/owners/:login/mfa`, a peer session's amendment to the contract). Policy is `settings.mfa` (`off | optional | owners | everyone`, default `optional`, through `PATCH /api/settings` and carried by `branding()`) for an environment, `HOST_MFA` (`optional | required`) for the console; a hosted environment's own host-admin cross sign-in is never subject to either, since its own factor is the console's. Regaining access: `MFA_RESET` (`login`, `slug:login`, or `host:login`, comma-separated, applied and logged every start rather than tracked as done -- the operator is meant to remove it again) and `MFA_OFF=1` (skips the step everywhere, logged as a warning on start, exposed as `mfaOff` on `GET /api/me`, `GET /api/host/me` and `branding()`). Found and fixed live, before committing: enabling or disabling a factor bumps or clears `mfa.version`, which changes the very session that just made the call's own stamp -- without reissuing it, the caller enrolling or turning it off would be silently signed out of the action they just took; all four routes (`/api/me/mfa/enable`/`disable`, the host router's mirrors) now reissue the session cookie as part of the same answer. `tools/check-auth.mjs` (new, in `npm run check`) covers the TOTP math (drift, replay), the encryption round trip and a tampered ciphertext, recovery codes, the pending token (scoping, expiry) and the stamp fold-in; `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` gained a group for the host admins' own mirrored state machine. Verified live end to end against self-hosted and `BASE_DOMAIN` test servers: enrolling, a real TOTP code closing the loop (computed with the server's own `totpCode`), the replay block, a spent recovery code refused a second time, the `optional` and `everyone` policies (including the no-session-yet `enrol` pending flow for a brand new account), the trusted-browser cookie skipping the step and an admin's reset killing it, the admin and host-admin reset routes (including the 404 for an unknown login), `MFA_OFF` overriding an `everyone` policy on an already-enrolled account, and `MFA_RESET` in all three forms.
- Modules register into the nav bars, and the bars are drawn from one registry (`public/nav-bar.js`, the "Modules register into the bars" step of `documentation/plans/plan-nav.md`). A tool is one registration, `{ id, bar, zone, icon, label, title, order, group, groupOrder, href or onClick, visible, toggleable, active, badge }`, and the host draws it in its own look; the primary nav's middle and right zones (Spaces, each module's page, your profile, Manage, Install, the clock, Sign out) and the secondary nav's right zone (Dock all, the stage-level snap and its slider, Full screen, Pop out, Pull participants back, Rejoin call, Leave) are now registrations of that shape, with every element id the code and the checks look up kept, and the narrow-layout real iPhone (the primary's middle and right zones fold into the menu, the secondary is the bottom tab bar) unchanged. Groups draw with a divider between them and sort by `groupOrder`, tools by `order`, in the bands 1-10 core, 11-50 secondary, 51-100 utility, 101-998 a module's own, 999 last, so the system's tools stay ahead of a module's without coordinating numbers; `visible` is a boolean or a function, and a toggle's `active` and a tool's `badge` change in place (`setActive`, `setBadge`), never by registering again. The SDK gains `host.nav.set([...])`, `host.nav.setActive(id, on)` and `host.nav.setBadge(id, n)` and the `nav` event: a module's tools go into the secondary bar under its own namespace, drawn while its pane is open in the space and removed when the pane closes or the module is unmounted; the primary bar takes a module's tool only when it is marked `system: true`, the module's manifest has `surfaces.page.nav: true`, and it goes into the right zone, anything else refused with an error that says why. `/api/modules/:id/context` reports `module.nav` for that check. `tools/check-nav.mjs` (in `npm run check`) holds the ordering, band, visibility and namespace rules; the contract is "Registering into the nav bars" in `api-module-sdk.md` and the structure in `architecture-navigation.md`.
- The OBS pieces are a bundled module, **Stream** (0.1.0): the per-player view pages (`/view/<key>?s=...`, unchanged in URL, parameters and pictures, so Coffee Pub Studio and every existing browser source need nothing), the links page (every player's Player and Character links, and the access key with show, copy and regenerate, behind the module's `links` permission, admins always), and the box settings (borders, name plate, picture background and size, dim and tint for offline, aside and private) as the module's own server settings. It installs and turns itself on once per environment on the first start that carries it, taking the values the Server tab had, so no stream goes dark on the update. Built on generic host conduits, none named after it: a **keyed page** (`surfaces.keyed: { path, entry }`, a module page about one person opened with the server's access key and no session, served by `public/keyed.html` at `/<path>/<key>?s=`, one enabled module per path), `install: { auto, settingsFrom }` for a bundled module that arrives installed, the `color` setting type, and four SDK additions for a page that follows people: `host.presence` (who is where, the rooms, the active room, the reaction glyphs; `onChange` polls), `host.images.get(key, slot, { room })` (a person's picture as a blob URL), `host.media.watch(key, { video, audio, room }, handlers)` (a read-only viewer connection the host keeps, handing the module the media elements, the person's state and their reactions; page mode only) and `host.access.key()` / `regenerate()`. `GET /api/status` gains `pages`, the keyed paths currently served, asked for by Studio so it can say "install the Stream module" instead of showing a blank source. The host's own words for the stream key are now "access key" (the API names `streamKey`, `?s=` and `x-stream-key` are unchanged). Gone from the core with it: the Server tab's "Participant video defaults" panel (the module's settings form has every field, colour pickers included), the profile's "OBS link" row (the module's page lists every player's links), `public/view.html` and `view.js`, and `GET /view/:key` (the keyed route answers `/view` now). The plan and the contract are `documentation/plans/plan-stream-module.md`. Verified live: the module installs itself on a server that had the old settings and takes their values; `/view/<key>?s=` serves the module's box with those settings applied, refuses a missing key (403) and an unknown player (404); the links page and the settings form draw. The live camera path (`host.media.watch`) is a port of the old page's LiveKit code and was exercised only as far as the token and the connection attempt, since the development machine runs no LiveKit server.
- The server half of that contract: `modules.keyedFor`/`keyedPaths`/`keyedClaimant` (the last for the 404 sentence, checking installed modules and, in `index.js`, the bundled ones too); enabling a module fails naming the other when its keyed path is already claimed, and a keyed module can never be set to run sandboxed (media only draws into the page). `GET /<path>/<key>` is registered last, right before the error handler, so `GET /view/:key` keeps answering "view" itself for now -- the module's claim on it is real, just unreachable until phase 3 removes that route. `moduleViewer` gains a third viewer, a keyed one (the access key, no session): read-only, and only for a module with a keyed surface (`moduleAccess`), with `{}` permissions and a `{ key: 'viewer', name: 'Viewer', role: 'viewer' }` identity at `/api/modules/:id/context`. `GET /api/pages/:path` answers a keyed page's own module and entry. Verified live against the real Stream module (auto-install, the settings carried over exactly, the color setting's validation) and a throwaway one (the exclusivity and sandbox guards, a reserved path refused at install, the 403/404/200 route outcomes, a keyed viewer reading a keyed-surface module and refused a plain one or a write).
- Tenants, phase 1, the server half: one host serving several environments, each its own `store`, `modules` and the rest, from its own data directory (`DATA_DIR/tenants/<slug>/`), resolved from the request's hostname at the door. `DATA_DIR/host.json` is the registry (which environments exist, their plans, the host admins); `server/environment.js` builds one environment's full set of services; the names every route handler already reads (`store`, `modules`, `moduleData`...) are now Proxies resolving through an `AsyncLocalStorage` context the resolver middleware sets, so no handler body changed. With no `BASE_DOMAIN` there is exactly one environment and the app behaves exactly as before -- verified live, not just in the unit tests (see `documentation/plans/plan-tenants.md`'s progress note). With `BASE_DOMAIN` set: `admin.<base>` is a separate host console API (`/api/host/*`: login, list environments with usage, create one with its first owner, edit a plan, suspend, delete (moved to `tenants-deleted/`, never removed), backup and restore as a zip, host admins), the bare base domain a placeholder page, `<slug>.<base>` that environment, anything else a plain 404; `PREVIOUS_BASE_DOMAINS` 301-redirects an old base domain's hostnames to the new one; a pre-tenant install refuses to start until `MIGRATE_TENANT_SLUG` names what its data becomes. LiveKit room names are prefixed with the environment's own slug (the smallest safe thing ahead of phase 4's own call-service work). Not in phase 1: the owner role, enforcing plan caps, the console page itself (a parallel session's own work against this API). The `PREVIOUS_BASE_DOMAINS` redirect now carries the request's own port through to the new Location (found in the console's own integration testing): invisible behind a real proxy, where the port is implicit, but a redirect used to silently drop to the scheme's default port in local development. The console moved from `host.<base>` to `admin.<base>` before the first real use (the author's call: "admin" reads better to a customer) -- `host.<base>` is not kept as an alias, since nothing has ever depended on the old address; `host` stays a reserved slug alongside `admin` regardless.
- The bare base domain now serves the product's own landing page (`public/landing.html`, a parallel session's own work) instead of a placeholder, plus the handful of other paths it needs (`landing.css`, `landing.js`, `style.css`, `theme.css`, `img/site/icon`, `fa/*`) -- no environment is ever resolved there, so `theme.css` and the site icon both fall back to "nothing set" rather than needing one. `GET /api/product` (public, no session, answering at the bare base, `admin.<base>` and anywhere else it happens to be reached) is `{ name, contact, baseDomain, version }`; `name` (`PRODUCT_NAME`, default "Coffee Pub Tavern") and `contact` (`CONTACT_EMAIL`, default none) are configuration, never code, since the product's own name is not settled yet. `GET /api/host/settings` mirrors both for the console.
- What dropping one module's item on another does is now one shared decision, `tavern.refs.dropMenu`, instead of each module's own (an audit found four: Calendar and Planner built a menu of choices, copied between them; To-do and Polls only linked; Research always started a note; Maps looked one action up by name; Places and Assistant took no drops at all). A target says what is under the pointer (the drop context: the card, its own item there, the day, the time, the map position) and what it would offer of its own; the SDK adds what the dropped item's own module can do with it there -- set this task's due date to this day, link this task to this event, put this place at this spot on the map -- and shows one menu; one offer runs at once with nothing asked. A drop is about the item and what is here: a third module making something new of the item is not offered (the first version did, and a research note dropped on a plan day asked about a calendar event, a task and the assistant instead of just landing on the day). An action can say what the item it takes must have (`needs: ["place"]` on `actions.provides`), and the menu wears each offer's module icon, in the same look as `tavern.menu.show`. A drag can carry a card instead of a pointer, for a module with nothing stored: an answer the assistant wrote drags onto a plan, the calendar, a task or research as itself. Calendar's `createEvent` links the item it was made from; To-do's leftover browser-drag handlers are gone. Planner's typed-card placement (`acceptSuggestion`) is `fromSuggestion`, shared with its drop. All eight modules: Assistant 0.1.9, Calendar 1.17.3, Maps 0.7.5, Places 0.8.6, Polls 1.12.5, Research 0.2.4, To-do 1.11.3, Planner 0.7.17. The fill rules are `tools/check-drop.mjs`, part of `npm run check`; the design is `documentation/plans/plan-drop.md`, the contract "Dragging" in `api-module-sdk.md`. The chat as a drop target is left (host-side).
- The module window framework: a titlebar, an optional toolbar under it, the content, and an optional action bar -- the same four zones and the same overflow rule everywhere a module can be shown (docked, floating, its own window, its own page), documented in `documentation/architecture/architecture-module-window.md`. `tavern.toolbar.set([...])` is a new optional row under the titlebar for text, a tab switch, a progress bar, a slider or a button, reusing the same reuse-across-dock/float plumbing the frame and action bar already had, so a module's toolbar survives a dock<->float switch too; it is deliberately not a second row of titlebar icons, so a tab switch is text, not more icon buttons. `tavern.ui.viewSwitch({ id, options, value, onChange })` is the toolbar's first reusable tool -- a labelled view or filter switch that owns its own diffing and event wiring, so a module calls `.set(value, options?)` on every render instead of hand-rolling it. `header.set`, `bar.set` and `toolbar.set` now collapse anything past five items (or one marked `overflow: true`, always tucked away) into a host-drawn "..." instead of silently dropping it -- the host's own analogue of `tavern.menu.show`, for its own chrome rather than a module's frame.
- `tavern.menu.show({ id, items, at, anchor })`, a shared, reusable action menu drawn inside a module's own frame, replacing near-duplicate "..." menus each with its own positioning math and Escape/outside-click handling. Gained `iconColor` (an item's own icon in a fixed color, for a set of kinds already told apart by color elsewhere, e.g. Planner's marker types) and `href` (a real link, not a click handler faking navigation, so hovering, copying and opening in a new tab still work). Polls (1.12.4), Places (0.8.5), Research (0.2.3) and Planner's (0.7.15) gap-add menu are now on it; a module's own item menu with real form fields (a day picker, a type picker) stays native, since the shared menu only draws a flat list of actions.
- Polls (1.12.4), Calendar (1.17.2), To-do (1.11.2) and Planner (0.7.15)'s Open/Closed/All, Month/Week/Month+list/List, Open/Done/All and Days/Decisions/Bookings/Money switches moved off titlebar icons and onto the toolbar's `tavern.ui.viewSwitch`, each now text tabs instead of icons repeating the module's own name. Planner's two other titlebar actions (Hide/show empty days, Edit trip) are unaffected -- they are actions, not a view switch.
- A `tabs` toolbar item's options can now carry an icon, a label, or both (`iconOnly` keeps the icon and drops the visible label), the same shape `bar.set`/`header.set` items already had -- for a switch where the icon itself is meaningful, not decorative. Places (0.8.5) and Research (0.2.3)'s own Mine/This room/Everyone switch, previously a bespoke pill row built into each module's own page, moved onto `tavern.ui.viewSwitch` with it. `.mod-toolbar` scrolls horizontally instead of clipping when a row does not fit a narrow pane.

Verified live against a running server: the toolbar's tabs (text-only and icon+label), text, progress and slider render and dispatch events correctly (via a temporarily-instrumented Polls build, reverted after), Places' menu opens, arms and deletes a real place and its "Open in my maps app" is a real link, Planner's gap-add menu adds a marker with each type's own icon color intact, and Places' Mine/This room/Everyone switch (all three options, an icon and a label each) switches correctly down to a 280px-wide pane.

### Changed
- **Breaking: the old names are gone (version 0.4.0, step 10 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`; the Names plan is done).** Coffee Pub Studio must be v0.2.3 or later. The Studio alias is removed: a bearer-token `GET /api/me` or `GET /api/status` answers exactly as the pages get it, with no `tableName`, `serverName`, `rooms` or `activeRoom`, and roles `owner`, `member` and `admin`. **For whoever runs the server: these settings are no longer read**, and each start that still sets one logs "<OLD> is no longer read: use <NEW> instead.": `ADMIN_USER`, `TAVERN_ADMIN_USER` and `HOST_ADMIN_LOGIN` (use `ADMIN_LOGIN`), `HOST_ADMIN_PASSWORD` (use `ADMIN_PASSWORD`) and `MIGRATE_TENANT_SLUG` (use `MIGRATE_ENVIRONMENT_SLUG`). An install still using them: the admin signs in as `admin`; a hosted server with only `HOST_ADMIN_*` keeps its existing host admins but creates or resets none; the old slug moves nothing. `TAVERN_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, `TAVERN_ADMIN_KEY` and `ADMIN_KEY` are still read, with their "stops working in a later release" line. A new migration part, `names-copies-removed`, deletes each environment's `pre-names/` and the host's `pre-names-host/`; when other parts ran in the same start, it waits one start, so the copies survive the start that changed the data (a departure from the plan, the project manager's decision). The call-name fallback from step 3 is still read and goes later. Every level of `tools/check-names.mjs` fails now, and the allow-list holds no alias. Checked by a tool: `tools/check-names.mjs` (8 of 8 levels enforced, and `--migration`), `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` and `tools/check-auth.mjs` pass. Not verified live here, and not with Coffee Pub Studio itself.
- The documentation uses the product's names throughout (step 9 of the Names plan, GitHub #24): space, canvas, module, object and environment where it said room, stage, pane or panel, item or card, and tenant or server. Five wiki pages are renamed, so a saved link to an old page name no longer finds it: **Rooms** is now `userguide-spaces`, **The Call** `userguide-call` (was `userguide-table`), **Manage** `userguide-environment-settings` (was `userguide-server-settings`), the room layout architecture `architecture-canvas` (was `architecture-room-layout`) and the tenants architecture `architecture-environments` (was `architecture-tenants`); three plans are renamed the same way (`plan-environments`, `plan-linked-objects`, `plan-smart-summaries`), with git history kept. The Home page now also links the module window, navigation and environments architecture. Along the way, the documents were corrected where they described code as it was before the rename: the space bar's tools are registered with the nav registry (no `#snap-tools`), Leave is `#leave-space`, the canvas takes width with `takeWidthFromOthers()`, the dashboard opens an object with `openInSpace`, a widget runs in the `environment` scope with `host.spaces()` and `{ scope: 'spaces' }`, the drag's messages are `objects.ptrStart` and friends with `objectdrag` events, kinds are listed at `GET /api/objects/kinds`, a summary also carries `kindName` and `open`, and a module is switched on in the space bar, not from a Modules button in the toolbar. Documentation only; no code changed. Checked by `npm run check:docs` and `npm run docs:build` (no built page links to an old page name); the corrections were read against the code, not verified live.
- An aside is now the call and nothing else (step 8 of the Names plan, GitHub #24). In an aside the page no longer offers the chat or chat pictures, only the call; modules were already off there. The aside cards on the join screen look as they did. Under the hood an aside is no longer a space: `app.json` gains `asides: [{ id, members, origin, private, createdAt }]`, and spaces lose `ephemeral`, `origin` and `private`. A new migration part, `names-asides`, drops the old aside rows (asides are temporary; anyone in one during the upgrade lands back in a space) and keeps the original in `pre-names/names-asides/`. API: `GET /api/presence` gains `asides` (each with `mine`) and its `spaces` no longer include asides; `GET /api/status` gains `asides`; `GET /api/spaces` no longer lists asides; `POST /api/asides` and `POST /api/asides/invite` answer the aside's record; `POST /api/token` accepts an aside's id; an aside's id on any other space route (chat, settings, modules) answers 404 "no such space". Coffee Pub Studio, signed in with its bearer token, still finds asides in `/api/status` as old-shape rows in `rooms`, named with the environment's word for an aside, until step 10. No bundled module changes. Checked by tools (`npm run check`, including `tools/check-names.mjs`) and a short smoke on a local server; a real call in an aside is not verified, since there is no LiveKit server locally.
- **Reactions** and **Icons** are on Manage's **Template** tab now, after **Home icon** (plan-environment-templates, addendum 2, step 3b, GitHub #68), since a template can give both. The icon list, headed "Font Awesome" on the Theme tab, is headed **Icons**; nothing about either changed but where it sits. The Theme tab ends with a line pointing to them, and an old link to `#reactions` or `#icons` opens the Template tab at that section. On the host console's template editor, the rows under **Words** and **Module names and icons** line up: the name column is one width, so a long name or icon no longer pushes a row's fields out of line. Checked by the tool (`npm run check` passes) and a quick headless look at both pages; not verified live.
- The Lobby is for being together (GitHub #63): it has the chat, the call and only the modules made for it, which declare `surfaces.canvas.lobby: true` in their manifest (Calendar 1.17.15 does). Any other module is taken out of the Lobby when the server starts, and stays on in its other spaces with its data kept. Turning one on there is refused with a sentence naming the module and the Lobby; **Available in every space** reads "(not in <Lobby>)" beside it; the Lobby's settings page lists only the Lobby's modules; and a template's modules go in every space but the Lobby. Documented in `api-modules.md`, "The Lobby", `architecture-modules.md` and `userguide-modules.md`. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- The home icon is stored only when an owner picks one, so a template's home icon shows after a switch. A default home icon ("couch") stored by an older version is cleared once, on the first start. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- A thing a module holds is an **object**, and what is shown of it its **summary** (step 7 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). What people read says object where it meant item ("Link to other modules' objects", "...summarise and answer questions about the objects a person selects", "that object is no longer there"); the Assistant's results are "answers"; and a Planner entry linked elsewhere shows under the Planner's shown name ("Itinerary · Ferry"). **For module authors, this is a breaking change: `host.refs` is `host.objects`, with the same members, and `host.util.refKey` is `host.util.objectKey` (the old names throw, naming the new ones); `resolve`, `search` and the links answer summaries instead of cards; a drag carries `{ ref, summary }` with the type `application/x-host-object`, the drop context has `summary`, and `draggable`'s resolve returns `{ summary }`; `host.ai.ask({ objects })` answers `summaries` with `{{summary:N}}` markers (`items` is refused); and the manifest's `refs.produces[].card` is `summary` (the old field is refused at install).** A kind's name may use `{name}` for the module's shown name, and a new manifest field, `storage.renamed: [{ from, to }]`, has the server keep a module's stored keys matching the version running (renamed on start, install and update, moved back on a rollback, never over a key already taken). The routes are `/api/objects/resolve`, `/kinds`, `/search`, `/links` and `/api/modules/:id/objects/:kind/:objectId`, answering `summaries` or `summary`; `/api/refs/*` answers 404; the AI route takes `objects` and answers `summaries`. Each environment records the migration part `names-objects`, which changes no data. **For owners: the modules that ship with Magpie now update themselves on start to the version the server ships, whenever the update needs no new approval**; one that asks for more waits for you while the old version keeps running, and uploaded modules are never touched. Assistant 0.1.16, Calendar 1.17.14, Maps 0.7.18, Places 0.8.17, Polls 1.12.15, Research 0.2.15, To-do 1.11.12, Planner 0.7.37. Verified live on data upgraded from the previous build: links, backlinks, search, drag and drop, the dashboard, the Assistant with a stand-in AI service, Research's tags, and the Planner's plans before it is opened. Read as code only: anything in a real call.
- The to-do list lives in GitHub issues (https://github.com/Drowbe/coffee-pub-tavern/issues): every open item from `documentation/TODO.md` became an issue (#24 to #58), and TODO.md is now a short index of them. The "a list is not an editor" rule moved to the architecture overview's new "Design rules" section.
- The canvas and its modules are called that everywhere (step 6 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). In a space, the two tools read "Dock every floating module beside the call" and "Snap every floating module to a grid" (in the environment's word for module); the Roles grid's group for the conference and the chat is **Modules** (it was Panes); installed modules' cards say "Space canvas" (it was Space panel); a space's settings say "Modules that open on a space's canvas."; and the product page no longer says "pane". **For module authors, this is a breaking change: the manifest's `surfaces.panel` and `panel.html` are now `surfaces.canvas` (`{ entry, width, height, mode }`) and `canvas.html`.** A module still using `surfaces.panel` is refused at install ("module.json uses the old surfaces.panel; use surfaces.canvas (Magpie renamed a module's panel to its place on the canvas).") or, if already installed, marked as needing an update; the bundled modules update themselves. `GET /api/modules/for-space` answers `canvas` instead of `panel`; the SDK is unchanged. For developers: `#stage` is `#canvas`, with the matching classes (`.canvas`, `.module-canvas`, `.module-floating...`, `.builtin-floating`, `.module-lifted`, `.subnav-modules`, `--canvas-cols` and the rest) and data attributes; `createCanvas` in `public/canvas.js` with `registerBuiltin` and `openBuiltin`; `tools/check-canvas.mjs` (was `check-room-layout.mjs`); and `tools/check-names.mjs` fails on stage, pane and panel for these levels. Assistant 0.1.15, Calendar 1.17.12, Maps 0.7.17, Places 0.8.16, Polls 1.12.14, Research 0.2.14, To-do 1.11.11, Planner 0.7.36. Verified live without a call: an upgrade from the previous build keeps everything, saved canvas layouts included; docking, floating, snapping, resizing and pop-outs; the narrow layout's tab bar. Read as code only: anything in a real call.
- Modules speak space and environment too (step 5c of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). **For module authors, this breaks every module written for an earlier Magpie.** The SDK and `module.json` take only the new names, and an old one is refused with a sentence naming the new word: `info.context` is `{ scope: 'environment' | 'space' | 'keyed', spaceId }`; storage scopes are `environment`, `space`, `spaces` and `person`, and `change` events carry `spaceId`; `host.rooms()` is `host.spaces()`; a pointer is `{ module, kind, id, scope: 'environment' | 'space' | 'person', space }`; `images.get` and `media.watch` take `{ space }`, `follow(spaceId)`; `host.presence` answers `{ people: [{ key, name, online, space, inCall, isOwner }], spaces, asides, activeSpace, ownerOnline, reactions }`; `notify` and `schedule` take `to` as `space`, `environment` or a person's key. A manifest's `scope`, a setting's `scope` and `install.settingsFrom` use `environment`, `space` and `person`, and permission defaults are keyed `member`, `moderator` and `guest`; anything else is refused at upload with 400. A permission can name the key it had before with `replaces`, and each role's choice carries over. `host.refs` and the resolved "card" keep their names until step 7. An installed module in the old names stays installed but can't run: the Modules tab marks it **Needs an update**, with the reason, and offers no Enable; its old versions are marked "(needs an update)"; turning it on, or rolling back to such a version, answers 409, and a module that requires it doesn't run either. A module runs only while everything it requires is running, at any depth, and comes back by itself when that does; a module that can't run shows no Module Configuration link and isn't counted under Configurable, and the card's header wraps at narrow width. **For owners: the nine modules that ship with Magpie update themselves on the first start, keeping on or off, their spaces and their data** (Assistant 0.1.12, Calendar 1.17.8, Maps 0.7.14, Places 0.8.13, Polls 1.12.11, Research 0.2.11, Stream 0.1.4, To-do 1.11.8, Planner 0.7.32); a new permission that is off for every role needs no approval on that path, and anything else they ask for waits for you; they update the ones others need first, and one that can't be turned back on stays off, the log saying why. Links between modules saved before keep working: each environment's first start rewrites the old pointers in modules' data and in stored events (recorded; the originals in `pre-names/names-pointers/`). Stream has a new permission, **See the Stream page**, off for members, moderators and guests (a role that had **See the stream links and the access key** keeps it), and the access key stays with owners and the admin. Places' setting is now **Save places in this environment**, and Places' and Research's saved views go back to their default once. Presence and `/api/status` say `isOwner` and `ownerOnline`, and the aside pull topic `byOwner`. On a hosted server, each environment's startup lines start with `[<slug>] `. Verified live on single and hosted data upgraded from the previous build: all nine modules, the links between them, the dashboard, drag and drop, the Stream page for each role, and a module in the old names refused. Read as code only: anything in a call.
- The pages say space and environment too (step 5b of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). Manage's first tab is **Environment** (it was Server), opening with **Name and icon** and its **Name** field, and the rooms tab is **Spaces**; the profile's tab is **Spaces**; the crumb on a space's settings, a profile being edited, a module's configuration and the AI configuration says **Manage** (it said Server Settings); a new space is called "Space N", the Spaces tab counts "N spaces", and a built-in module's card says "On a space's canvas". Saved links keep working: `/admin#server`, `#settings` and `#rooms`, the profile's `#rooms`, and `/room.html`, `/roomconfig.html` and `/module-settings?room=` (now `?space=`, answered with a 301 keeping the rest of the query). Each browser keeps its layout: the saved canvas (`app.panels`, `app.panels.<id>`, `tavern.panels.<id>`) moves to `app.canvas` once on load, and the current space (`host.room`) to `app.space`, each only where the new key is empty; chat keys keep their names. For developers: `room.html`/`room.js` are `space.html`/`space.js`, `roomconfig.*` is `space-settings.*` and `room-modules.js` is `canvas.js`; ids and classes use space names; `mountModule` in `public/module-host.js` takes `environment` or `space` and a `spaceId`, while modules still see the old SDK names until step 5c; `tools/check-module-host.mjs` is new, and `tools/check-names.mjs` fails on "room" in `public/` too. Verified live on installs upgraded from the previous build, fresh and hosted, as an owner, a member, a guest and a keyed viewer: every page, the old hashes and addresses, old browser keys including malformed ones, a narrow width and the keyboard. Read as code only: anything in a call.
- The server and its stored data say space and environment (step 5a of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). **On the first start after this update, every environment's stored data is renamed: `rooms` becomes `spaces` in `app.json` and `chat.json`, `serverName` becomes `environmentName`, the image folders `rooms/` become `spaces/`, and each module's `server.json` and `room-<id>.json` become `environment.json` and `space-<id>.json`, with its uploads, links, events, schedules and notifications to match. It is recorded in `app.json`, and the originals are kept in `pre-names/names-spaces/`. Links people saved keep working: `/rooms/<id>`, `/img/room/<id>`, picture addresses with `room=` and `roomOnly=1`, and a module popped out with `moduleRoom=` redirect for good to their new addresses. Reload any call page that was open during the update.** The routes are `/api/spaces...`, `/api/me/spaces/:spaceId...`, `/api/users/:key/spaces/:spaceId...`, `/api/modules/for-space?space=` and `/api/modules/:id/spaces-data`; the old `/api/rooms...` paths answer 404. Fields follow: presence and `/api/status` give `spaces`, `activeSpace` and each person's `space`; `environmentName` replaces `serverName`; `POST /api/token` takes `space` and answers `call` and `spaceId`; an aside answers `{ aside }` and a return `{ space }`, and the aside topics carry `spaceId` and `spaceName`. Module routes take `scope=environment`, `space`, `spaces` or `person` with `space=` (no scope means environment), and an old scope is refused with a sentence saying which are allowed. The server's sentences say "space", and the Roles grid's group is "In the Space". Coffee Pub Studio, signing in with its token, still gets `serverName`, `rooms`, `activeRoom` and `users[].online.room`. Modules see no change yet: the SDK and manifests keep `server`, `room` and `roomId` until step 5c, and the host translates; no bundled module changed. `tools/check-names.mjs` fails on "room" in `server/` and `tools/`. Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`: single and hosted data made by the previous build, and a backup made before the update restored after it, each read back identically, with 20 access checks unchanged, the redirects, Studio's token answers against the pages', the pages as an owner, a member and a guest, and all nine modules' pages. Read as code only: anything in a real call.
- The compose file holds only the server's admin, never an owner (a follow-up to step 4 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`; Thomas: "The compose file should be for the admin of the entire server... they own environments."). `ADMIN_LOGIN` and `ADMIN_PASSWORD` make the server's admin on every install and reset its password on each start when it differs. On a hosted server that is the host admin (`HOST_ADMIN_LOGIN` and `HOST_ADMIN_PASSWORD` still work, with a line in the log; if the two passwords differ, `ADMIN_PASSWORD` wins, with a warning). On a single install it is the environment's **admin**: every right, not an owner, shown as "Admin: runs this server", and its role, login, password, personal link and two-step sign-in can't be changed or reset from Manage, nor can it be deleted. Owners are made in Manage, and an environment may have none ("keep at least one owner" is gone). The lockout bypass covers the admin. **`OWNER_PASSWORD`, added in step 4, is gone: it is ignored, with a line in the log. If you set it, put the same login and password in `ADMIN_LOGIN` and `ADMIN_PASSWORD` before you update, and that account becomes the admin again on the next start; without them, the owners run the server and there is no admin.** With no `ADMIN_PASSWORD`, a brand-new install makes an admin with a random password, logged once, and an install with accounts makes nobody and says so in the log. `ADMIN_USER` and `TAVERN_ADMIN_USER` are now read as `ADMIN_LOGIN`, and `TAVERN_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `TAVERN_ADMIN_KEY` as `ADMIN_PASSWORD`, each with a line. Moving a single install onto a hosted server makes its admin the new environment's owner. `docker-compose.yml` has one `ADMIN_LOGIN` / `ADMIN_PASSWORD` pair. `PORT=0` now works and logs the real port. Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`: a fresh single install, one upgraded by step 4, and a hosted install with each combination of the variables, with the pages in a browser. Checked by `npm run check`.
- The person who runs an environment is an **owner** and everyone else a **member**, on every install (step 4 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). The words were Admin and User on a single server and Owner only on a hosted one; now the Roles grid's columns are Owner (locked, "Owners can always do this"), Moderator, Member and Guest everywhere, and a profile says "Owner: runs the environment", "Member" or, for the host admin's own account inside an environment, "Host admin: runs this server". Each environment's first start renames the stored roles (`admin` to `owner`, `user` to `member`, in the accounts, `settings.roles` and invites), records that in `app.json`, and keeps the original in `pre-names/names-roles/`. **On a hosted server the host admin's account in each environment becomes `admin` with every right, even where an owner had changed its role before.** That account's role can't be changed, and it doesn't count toward "keep at least one owner". A single install's configured account is an owner: set `OWNER_PASSWORD`; `ADMIN_PASSWORD` still works, logging on each start that the name has changed, and `OWNER_PASSWORD` wins if both are set (`ADMIN_USER`, `ADMIN_KEY` and `ADMIN_MFA_LOCKOUT_BYPASS` keep their names). The roles route is `PATCH /api/roles/member` (`/api/roles/user` answers 404), and owner-only requests answer 403 "owners only". Coffee Pub Studio, signing in with its token, still sees the old values (`admin` for an owner or the host admin, `user` for a member). Modules ask `host.can()` instead of reading the role, and an owner has every module permission; a permission key must match `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,23}$`. Polls 1.12.10 adds **Close and delete polls other people started** and Research 0.2.10 **Remove photos other people added**, both off by default, so each update waits for an owner's approval once. Maps 0.7.13 with no map files tells everyone "Maps is not set up yet. An owner adds map files in Manage. Your places are in the Places module."; Stream 0.1.3 shows the access key panel to owners and the host admin; Assistant 0.1.11 says owner. A restore now refuses a backup whose `app.json` can't be read, before replacing anything: 400 "This backup's data can't be read, so nothing was restored." `tools/check-names.mjs` now fails on the old role values in code and in words. Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`: single and hosted data made by the previous build, with every right compared before and after the upgrade item by item, and the pages and modules in a headless browser. Checked by `npm run check`. Read as code only: anything in a real call.
- The call is the call, not "the table" (step 3 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). Every page now says "the call" or "a space" where it said "the table": the call page, Manage ("N of M in a call"), the profile, the invitation ("You're invited to join <environment name>."), a space's settings and the product page. Each environment's first start removes the two settings the call was named from (`tableName` and `settings.room`), records that in `app.json` (now `version: 2`), and keeps the original in `pre-names/names-table/`. The Lobby's description changes to "Where everyone meets." only if it was still the old default. Calls are now named from the space: `lobby`, `<id>` and `aside-<id>`, each prefixed `<slug>.` on a hosted server, and nothing about the name is stored. For one release the old names are still recognised, so people already in a call during the update are found, placed, muted and kicked where they are. **After updating, reload any call page that was open**: until it is reloaded it does not follow a pull aside, a recall or a return, or show who is online. The routes are `GET /api/presence` (was `GET /api/table`) and `POST /api/asides`, `/api/asides/invite`, `/api/asides/invite/:id/decline`, `/api/asides/recall` and `/api/asides/return` (was `/api/table/...`, which now answers 404); the data-channel topics are `aside-pull`, `aside-started`, `aside-recall` and `aside-return`. `room` and `tableName` are gone from the branding every page reads (`/api/branding`, `/api/config`, `/api/me`, `/api/settings`, `/api/presence`), and `table` from `/api/status`; Coffee Pub Studio, signing in with its token, still gets `tableName`, set to the environment's name. A browser's saved call preferences move from `host.table` to `app.call` on load. Stream 0.1.2 says "in the call". `tools/check-names.mjs` now fails on "table" in code and in words. Fixed along the way: another environment's calls no longer count against an environment's calls cap. An environment whose `app.json` is there but is not valid JSON, or is not an object, is now refused instead of opening empty and being written over on its first save: a single install stops with the file named, and a hosted environment answers 503 ("This environment's data could not be read. The host admin has been told.") and shows "won't open" on the console, so restoring a broken backup no longer leaves an empty environment. Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`: single-environment and hosted data made by the previous build, a stand-in LiveKit answering the server's room-service calls (for the call names, the calls cap, placement, mute and kick), and the pages in a headless browser without a call. Read as code only: a real call, meaning the media, tokens joining the new names at LiveKit, moving between calls, and the data topics in a browser.
- A hosted server's environments are called environments on disk and in the host API (step 2 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). On its first start, a server with environments renames `DATA_DIR/tenants/` to `environments/`, `tenants-deleted/` to `environments-deleted/` and `host.json`'s `tenants` to `environments`, records that in `host.json`'s `migrations`, and keeps the original `host.json` in `pre-names-host/names-environment/`. A `host.json` listing different environments under both keys stops the start with a sentence saying which file to fix; nothing is changed. Moving a single-environment install onto a hosted server now takes `MIGRATE_ENVIRONMENT_SLUG`; `MIGRATE_TENANT_SLUG` still works, with a log line on each start saying to change it. The host API is `/api/host/environments...` (list, create, edit, delete, backup, restore, an owner's second-factor reset); the old `/api/host/tenants...` paths answer 404, and `POST /api/host/billing` answers `{ environment }` instead of `{ tenant }`. The migration now keeps a note of each folder it moves, so a start stopped between two moves still records every move when it runs again, and its error messages say where a folder or the original files are. On the console: a suspended environment's button reads **Resume** (it said **Restore**, beside **Restore backup**); focus returns to **Restore backup** after a restore, and to the message line after a delete; a card that won't open no longer offers the owner's second-factor reset, which could not work; and **Save plans**, the new environment's slug and the product page's sign-up slug are checked by the browser before sending, with a blank plan name now refused. `tools/check-names.mjs` now fails on `tenant` in code and in words. Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`: a hosted data folder made by the previous build, with active, suspended, deleted and refused environments, upgraded and opened, and a backup made before the upgrade restored after it; a single-environment install behaving as before; the console driven by keyboard in a headless browser. Checked by `npm run check`, with interrupted starts simulated by killing the process (`SIGKILL`), not by a power loss. No module changed, and nothing here touches the call.
- Finished work is committed once it has passed QA and has its documentation, so Thomas can pull the image and test it: `CLAUDE.md`'s rule no longer waits for him to commit, the project manager makes the commit, and the other agents still don't commit. Pushing and publishing the wiki still wait for him to ask.
- The naming rule: `CLAUDE.md` has a Names section (host, environment, space, aside, canvas, module, object; admin, owner, moderator, member, guest), used in code and in words, and the rule that "the code keeps `room`" is gone. The agent files in `.claude/agents/` follow it. The code still says room and tenant until the Names plan's later steps rename it.
- The project-manager agent now plans in two halves, because an agent it calls can't talk to Thomas. product-planner researches the options and the open questions. The project manager puts them to Thomas, a few at a time. product-planner then writes the plan from his decisions. Nothing is built until Thomas approves a short summary of the plan.
- Self-service sign-up on the product page is off unless the server opts in with `SIGNUP: "on"` (the author, on finding the form live on their host: "Not ready for this"). It was on by default with `SIGNUP=off` as the way out; a freshly upgraded host now offers no sign-up form until the operator turns it on. With it off, every call to action on the product page (the nav's Get started, the hero's Get your own environment, every plan's button) reads **Coming soon** and does nothing, and the Get started band is titled Coming soon with a mail link to ask only when the host has given a contact address. The compose sample and the getting-started guide say so; the server half is its own entry.
- The server half of the above: `signupEnabled` flips from `SIGNUP !== 'off'` to `SIGNUP === 'on'`, so `POST /api/product/signup` and `GET /api/product`'s own `signup` field both default closed with no `SIGNUP` variable set at all, same as a host that has never heard of it. Verified live: no `SIGNUP` set answers `signup: false` and the sign-up route its plain "sign-up is off"; `SIGNUP=on` still opens it, unchanged.
- Two-step sign-in's switches, the pages half, after the author's redesign ("durable settings with booleans so the admin need not guess"): three layered switches instead of a four-way policy and two set-once-then-remove variables. The server offers it or not (`ENABLE_MFA`): when not, the profile's Two-step row, Manage's switch and the console's Host-tab panel are not shown at all (`mfaOffered` on `GET /api/me`, `GET /api/host/me` and `branding()`). Each environment requires it or not: Manage > Server's select became one checkbox, **Require two-step sign-in for everyone** (`settings.mfaRequired`), and the sign-in page's note keys off `branding().mfaRequired`. Each person chooses on their profile when it is offered and not required. Getting back in is `ADMIN_MFA_LOCKOUT_BYPASS`, a plain boolean: while it is on, an admin's profile and a host admin's Host tab say "on, bypassed", hide Turn off and offer **Reset my second factor** confirmed with the password (`POST /api/me/mfa/reset` and `/api/host/me/mfa/reset`; `mountDisable` in `public/mfa-enrol.js` gained a `password: true` variant), and Manage and the console carry a banner saying to turn it off again (`mfaBypass`). `MFA_OFF`, `MFA_RESET`, `HOST_MFA=required` and their banners are gone from the pages and the guides (`HOST_MFA_REQUIRED` is the console's requirement now); the compose sample carries the two booleans set to their defaults, uncommented, so they are seen. The plan's "Regaining access" is rewritten as "The server's two switches".
