- Two-step sign-in's switches, the server half, matching the pages entry above. `ENABLE_MFA` (default `true`) replaces the whole feature's on/off: when `false`, no door ever issues a pending step regardless of an environment's own policy, and every enrolment and reset route (`/api/me/mfa/*`, `/api/host/me/mfa/*`) answers 403 `{ error: 'two-step sign-in is not offered on this server' }` -- nothing already enrolled is touched or deleted, it just cannot be reached while the switch is off. `settings.mfa`'s four-way string becomes `settings.mfaRequired`, a plain boolean through `PATCH /api/settings`; a saved `'everyone'` migrates to `true` on load (anything else to `false`, since `'owners'`-only had no boolean equivalent and `'off'` is now the server's own `ENABLE_MFA`), the old key dropped from what `updateSettings` builds so the very next save leaves it out of the file for good (`server/store.js`'s new `migrateMfaSettings`). `ADMIN_MFA_LOCKOUT_BYPASS` (default `false`) replaces the old `MFA_RESET`/`MFA_OFF` pair with one durable switch: while it is on, every admin (an environment's own, and a host admin) skips the pending step and the enrol requirement entirely -- members are still asked -- and a new `POST /api/me/mfa/reset { password }` (mirrored `/api/host/me/mfa/reset`) clears just the caller's own factor by password rather than a code, for an admin who has one enrolled but cannot produce it; refused with 403 for anyone it does not apply to, and logged as a warning on every start while it is on, the same as an open registration or a missing secret would be. `HOST_MFA_REQUIRED` (`true`/`false`) replaces `HOST_MFA=required` for the console; the old spelling is still honoured. `GET /api/me`, `GET /api/host/me` and `branding()` all carry `mfaOffered` and (on the two `/me` routes) `mfaBypass`, `mfaOffered`'s and `mfaBypass`'s session-specific truth for that exact caller. Not released to any real install yet, so `MFA_RESET`/`MFA_OFF` needed no compatibility shim -- gone outright, along with the `mfaOff` field and the startup log lines that went with them. Verified live: `ENABLE_MFA=false` overriding an environment already set to require it, every enrolment route's 403 while it is off, `ADMIN_MFA_LOCKOUT_BYPASS` letting an admin straight past a `mfaRequired: true` policy while a member is still asked, `POST /api/me/mfa/reset` refusing a non-admin and a wrong password before clearing a real enrolled factor on the right one, the settings migration (a hand-built `app.json` with the old `mfa: 'everyone'` loading as `mfaRequired: true` with the old key gone from the file after the next save), and both `HOST_MFA_REQUIRED=true` and the legacy `HOST_MFA=required` requiring enrolment at the console.
- The product's old name is gone from the pages, the tools and the documents (the author: not anywhere in the code). The product's name is configuration, `PRODUCT_NAME`, defaulting to "Coffee Pub Magpie" -- what the logo says, and one line to change. Pages carry no product name of their own: a title is the page's own name ("Sign in", "Manage") until the server's branding arrives and the environment's name is put in front of it; the umbrella "Coffee Pub" stands in the markup until then. Prose that used the old name for the platform says "the host" or "the server"; the documents say the product's name. Two window globals lost the old name (`hostText`, `appApplyCallPrefs`), and the package is `coffee-pub-magpie`. Kept, on purpose: the repository's URL and the image's name (they follow the GitHub rename), and the one line that reads a browser's old `tavern.*` keys to move them. The compose template's container names, data path and admin variables are new-install defaults; an existing install keeps its old ones, all still honoured (see the getting-started guide). The server's side (the default server name, the cookie, the data file, the variables, with compatibility) is its own entry.

### Added
- The product's brand, from the author: a **logo** (the brandmark and the words, `logo-light.png` for a dark bar and `logo-dark.png` for a light one) and a **brandmark** (the cup on its own, in colour and in white), under `public/assets/images/brand/`. The product page and the host console wear the logo in their bar; the bundled default app icon (`public/icon.png`, the favicon and the installed app's icon wherever an environment has not set its own) is the brandmark. Inside an environment the customer's own name and icon stay, as before.
- A product page at the bare base domain (`public/landing.html`), where "This is a host" stood: what it is (a call that stays open, tools beside it, tools that know each other), the eight modules, how an environment works, three plan shapes without prices yet, and a way to ask for an environment. The product's name, the contact address, the base domain and the version come from the host (`GET /api/product`, public; `PRODUCT_NAME` and `CONTACT_EMAIL` on the container), so the page never hard-codes a name while the name is still being chosen: with no contact set it says to ask whoever runs the host. Its own stylesheet (`landing.css`) on the theme's tokens; the primary nav's colour on its bar; one column in the narrow layout. Its **Sign in** is a list of the host's environments to choose from (a public list, fine on a host run for a handful of groups), then the login and password right there: the form posts to that environment's own `/login`, a plain top-level form post, so the session is first-party and the person lands inside signed in; a refused password sends them to that environment's sign-in page with the login filled in and the usual message. An environment leaves an `env_hint` cookie on the parent domain when someone signs in there (slugs, most recent first, never a name), so the list preselects where this browser was last and the nav's Sign in goes straight there. A first version had a free-text box for the environment's name, which read as a username field and was typed into as one; replaced the same day.
- The host console (`admin.<base domain>`, `public/host.html`): the deployment's own door, with its own sign-in for a host admin (a separate kind of account, a member of no environment). It lists the environments with their plan's caps against their use (members, spaces, storage, AI calls this month, concurrent calls, the modules allowed), creates one (a slug, a name, the first owner), edits a plan, suspends and restores, backs one up as a zip, deletes one (its data is kept aside, never removed), and manages the host admins (never the last). It shows nothing of an environment beyond the counts. An environment's **Edit** (it said "Plan" at first, and the name was in there where nobody would look) changes its name and its plan; the slug is its address and stays. Built against the `/api/host/` contract in `documentation/plans/plan-tenants.md` ("Phase 1 in detail"), which the server half implements; checked against a stand-in for that API in the browser, and again once the server answered.

### Changed
- **The host is not the brand.** The object a module talks to was called `tavern` since the first module, which bound every module's code to the product's name. It is `host` now, the word the architecture always used: `host.refs`, `host.storage`, `host.toolbar.set`, `/sdk/host.js` and `/sdk/host.css`, `createHost(env)`, `document.currentScript.host || window.host`, `data-host-sdk` on the page's script tag, `[host]` in the trace, `window.hostModules` for tests. What the SDK draws inside a module is `sdk-` (`sdk-menu`...), telling it from the host's own `host-menu`. Keys and events the pages keep are `app.*` and `app:*` (`app.panels.<space>`, `app:unread`...), and a page moves anyone's old `tavern.*` keys over once, so no layout, chat history or remembered choice is lost. A clean break, no alias: there are no third-party modules yet and the product is at 0.3, so an alias would have been compatibility for nobody, carried for years; every bundled module was changed in the same sweep. The product's name stays where a name belongs (the server-name default, titles, the About tab, the repository, the data file, the session cookie), so a rebrand now touches only those. Decided in `plans/plan-modules.md` ("The host is not the brand"); the SDK and contract documents follow. Verified by every check and live: the room page, a module's own page and the drop check, all on `host`.
- Rooms are **spaces** in everything a person reads: the navigation, the pages, the buttons, the tooltips and the modules' own views ("This space" beside "Mine"). The code, the routes (`/rooms/:id`), the API fields and the stored data keep `room`, so nothing anyone has stored or linked changes; the documentation's prose follows in its own pass (see TODO.md). Done as one sweep over the pages' text and attributes and the prose in their strings, with the code left alone, then checked for anything code-like caught in it.
- The header's rows have their own colours, as theme tokens: the primary nav's middle zone is the header colour, its left and right zones a shade darker (`--nav-primary-edge-bg`), and the secondary nav (the space's bar) its own (`--nav-secondary-bg`), between the header and the page. All three derive from `--header-bg` until a theme sets them (`design-theme.md`); the theme editor does not offer them yet.
- The crumb is gone at the table: the secondary nav names the space, so the primary nav's "> Lobby" said it twice. The aside's Rejoin call button, which lived in the crumb, is in the secondary nav's right zone with the other space actions. On every other page the crumb still says where you are (Server Settings, a profile, a module's page), since those have no second row.
- The header is two rows in three zones each, and every control now has a zone by what it is about (the system or the space) and what it does (navigation, action, information), not by where it fit. The primary nav (every page): left, the logo and where you are; middle, centred on the row whatever the other zones hold, the core navigation (Rooms, each module's own page, shown with their names now rather than as bare icons); right, the system's actions and information (your profile, Manage, Install, the time on the server's clock, Sign out). The secondary nav (the room bar, at the table): left, the room's name and the module selector; middle, the space's own information and navigation (empty so far); right, the space's actions (the stage-level snap, Full screen, Pop out, Pull participants back -- moved here from the primary row, since it is the room's action -- and Leave). In the narrow layout the primary row keeps the logo, the crumb and a menu, with the middle zone's links moved into the menu, and the secondary row is the tab bar as before. Documented in `architecture-navigation.md`; what each zone grows to hold, and modules registering tools into the bars (after the author's Blacksmith menubar), are in `documentation/plans/plan-nav.md`. Verified live: both rows measure with the middle zone at the row's centre and the right zone flush right, at the table and on the room list; the narrow-layout real iPhone takes the core links.

### Added
- The stage-level snap: a switch in the room bar (beside Full screen) that puts every pane that can float onto the grid -- docked ones are floated first, their docked mode remembered, and any pane opened later comes up floating and snapped; off, the ones that were docked dock again -- and, while it is on, a slider for the grid's size (the grid shows while it moves, and every snapped pane refits). The grid is about half the size it was (a 130px pitch, adjustable from 60 to 320), and a pane never takes fewer cells than its smallest size needs, so a fine grid still gives a usable pane. Both are remembered with the room's layout. Verified live with the chat pane: the stage switch snapping it and setting its own switch, the slider previewing the grid and refitting the pane, releasing hiding the grid, the stage switch off, and the pane's own switch still working alone.
- Language, time and money: a new section on Manage > Settings' Server tab. **Clock** (12-hour, the default, or 24-hour) is how every time is shown across the server and every module; **Currency** is the one amounts are shown in unless a trip names its own; **Language** is the interface language (English only, until translations arrive). The SDK carries them to modules (`tavern.locale()`, `tavern.util.time`, `tavern.util.hour12`, `tavern.util.money`), and Planner (0.7.24; every time on a card, a row, a day's summary, the trip markers and the Trips widget, and money through the server's currency when the trip has none), Calendar (1.17.4, and its widget), Polls (1.12.7, and its widget) and To-do (1.11.5) follow them; before, Planner always showed 24-hour times and the others took the browser's choice. Showing a trip's own currency with a conversion beside it is left until a source of exchange rates is chosen (see TODO.md).
- Planner (0.7.22): a marker with no time marks the whole day -- "Travel day", "Free day" (two new default marker types, beside the timed ones) -- drawn as a tag in the day's header in its marker's colour rather than as a row among the day's items; clicking the tag opens its menu (edit, move, change type, remove). The day's "..." gained a "Mark the day" group that adds one at once, ahead of the timed blocks, which now read "Add free time at a time". A card folds what its face has no place for under a "More" line: the note, who is on it, the booking reference, the terminal, the platform, the room, the cost and who paid, only what the item has and the face did not show; a card someone opened stays open through redraws. The editor's placement field is **When** (it was Where for a day), and a length is any whole number of minutes (it was steps of five; minutes matter in travel).
- Research (0.2.6): cards pack like masonry -- each takes the rows its own height needs, so a short card beside a tall one leaves no hole under it, and the columns follow the pane's width -- and a **Cards / List** switch beside Mine / This room, the list being one line per item; remembered per person. `tavern.ui.viewSwitch` learned to share the toolbar between several switches (in the order they were made, a separator between), so a module's second switch does not draw over its first. Not run live here (Research needs the server's AI service on): checked as code, the switch composition in a node check of the SDK.
- A floating pane can snap to a grid. Floating was only ever free (anywhere, any size); a floating pane's titlebar now has a **Snap to a grid** toggle that makes it sit in the cells of a grid laid over the stage (cells of about 260 x 200, as many as the stage fits, a 16px gutter): dragging moves it a cell at a time, resizing grows it a cell at a time, the grid shows while it moves, and what is remembered is its cells, so a window resize re-tiles it rather than just keeping it on screen. Off, it floats freely as before; turning it on settles the pane into the nearest cells at once. Docked and window are untouched -- snap is a way of floating, not a replacement for docking -- and a pane keeps its DOM through every snap, as through a free drag. The chat and every module pane have it; documented in `architecture-room-layout.md` ("Free or snapped"). Verified live on the chat pane with the call unavailable: toggling on, a snapped drag and resize (with the grid shown), free again, snapped again, and a viewport change re-tiling it.
- Planner (0.7.21): the plan line. Anything can now sit *between* days as well as on one -- an idea near the first day, a few more near the tenth, a poll between two days, a research card near day 3 -- and the organizing rule is date, then time, then where a person placed it. Every item is in one of two places, and the model enforces it: on a day (`date`, `time`, `order`) or on the line at a joint (`after`, the day the joint follows, `''` for the head before the first day; then `order`). Nothing stored changed shape: the old Ideas column is simply the head of the line, and a between-days marker was already placed that way. So Ideas is gone as a column and the items with no day yet are drawn on the line before Day 1, the same way a marker between days always was; a card on the line is the card it would be in a day, with no time. Every joint takes anything: its + offers the same kinds the day's "..." does (plus the between-days marker types, added at once), the editor's Day field became **Where** (the days and the joints in line order, "Before the first day", "Between Oct 1 and Oct 2", "After the last day"; a marker sees only the joints), **Move to** in an item's menu lists the same, and **Back to ideas** became **Back to the line** (off its day, to the joint before it). Dropping: while anything is dragged over the plan -- one of its own items by its body, or another module's item or card -- every joint opens into a drop zone, as it did only for a marker before; a plan's own item dropped on a joint moves there, among the items already at it; another module's item dropped on one is offered "Put it here, between Oct 1 and Oct 2" through the shared drop menu. Earlier and Later on the line swap with a neighbour at the joint and hop to the next joint at the ends. A hidden day's joint still draws its items after the hidden-days badge, so nothing disappears with the day. The design and the author's mental model (what do I know, what are my options, what are my decisions) are in `documentation/plans/plan-travel.md`; `tools/check-travel.mjs` covers the placement rule. Verified live: Move to a joint and back, Back to the line, the joint's + adding a note at that joint through the editor, a To-do task dropped on a joint, a card dropped on the head of the line, a plan item dragged by its body onto a day and back onto the line before a marker.
- The SDK delivers a module's own dragged item to its own `tavern.refs.dropTarget` when it is dropped on the module itself (`over`, `leave`, `drop`, with its own pointer as `ref`), so moving an item within a module is the same code path as taking one from another; the host only ever handed a drag to the *other* modules, which had left Planner's own between-days marker drag (built on `dropTarget`) unreachable by the pointer. Documented in `api-module-sdk.md` ("Dragging"), with the one thing it implies: `leave` comes just before `drop`, so a drop zone that opens while a drag is over you must not close on `leave` until a tick later, or the drop is hit-tested against the wrong layout (found live, when a drop on a day landed on the joint below it).
- Planner (0.7.19): a "..." on every day (and on Ideas) listing everything that can be added there -- each kind the editor's tiles offer (a flight, a train, a ferry, a bus, a rental car; a stay; a restaurant, a café, a bar, a sight, a museum, a tour, a show; a note) and the time blocks -- each opening the editor on that day with the kind chosen. Typing to add was the only way in before; it stays the fast path, this is the plain one.

### Changed
- Themes come in a light and a dark version, picked with a switch beside the chooser on Manage > Theme; the built-in themes are renamed: Staying Blonde is Calming Teal, willhavebeen is Burnt Orange, and Default is Strong Coffee (the Bug fixes session, 161e53f).
- Every "..." in Tavern is the one icon, Font Awesome's `ellipsis-vertical`: the host's overflow buttons at the end of a titlebar, toolbar or action bar, the call's More, a card's own menu in Places, Research and Planner, and a poll's, which drew a text glyph. They were horizontal everywhere but the poll. `tools/check-module-window.mjs`, in `npm run check`, keeps it that way, the first mechanical rule for the module window (see `architecture-module-window.md`, "Rules"). A long menu (`tavern.menu.show`, and so the day's) scrolls instead of running off a short pane.
- To-do (1.11.4): dropping something on a task links it there, and on the list's empty space starts a task from it -- one own offer each, so the drop just does it; a menu appears only when the dropped item's own module adds something (its due date, on a day). Before, landing on a task offered both, making the common case a second click.

### Added
- Two-step sign-in (the pages; the server half is its own entries; the plan is `documentation/plans/plan-mfa.md`): a six-digit code from an authenticator app after the password. On the profile, **Two-step sign-in** turns it on (scan the QR or type the key, confirm with a code, keep the ten recovery codes shown once) and off (a code); an admin editing someone can **Reset their second factor**. Signing in gets a code page (`/login/verify`, with a recovery code as the alternative and "Remember this browser for 30 days"), or the set-up page first (`/login/enrol`) for an account whose role requires one; a personal link gets the same step. Manage > Server has the rule (off, optional, required for admins, required for everyone) and the Users tab marks accounts with a factor. The host console asks its own second step at sign-in, has the host admin's own set-up on the Host tab, and resets an owner's factor from the environment's card by login. Getting back in: `MFA_RESET` and `MFA_OFF` on the server (the compose sample and the getting-started guide say how), with a banner on Manage and the console while `MFA_OFF` is set. Asked as "how can we support mfa?", "plan it", "build it; we need a way for an admin to regain access, e.g. a flag in the server yml".
- Environments, phases 2 to 5 (the pages; the server half is its own entries): on a hosted server the environment's admin is its **owner** in every word (the Roles grid, a profile, the New user form), what only the host does (uploading a module zip, running a module in the page) is not offered to an owner, and Manage > Server starts with an **Environment** panel: the plan's name, each cap with what is used as a bar, the past-due banner with the date the environment goes to the free plan, **Upgrade** (the product page's plans), **Download a copy** (the environment as a zip) and **Ask for deletion** (a request the host carries out, withdrawable). The Modules tab's Available list marks a module the plan leaves out "Not in your plan". The host console gains a **Plans** panel (the catalog: `free` and any named plan, each with its caps), a plan picker on an environment's card that fills the caps from the catalog, past-due and deletion-requested facts on a card, and the billing webhook's address and rules under "The host". The product page lists the plans from the catalog (the free one to start, a bought one on the provider's own page) and its Get started is a sign-up form: an address checked as it is typed, a name, the owner's account, straight into the new environment. A new guide, [Your environment](documentation/userguides/userguide-environments.md), is the owner's view; the getting-started guide covers plans, sign-up and the webhook for the operator. The contract for both halves is plan-tenants.md, "Phases 2 to 5 in detail". Asked overnight as "tenants phases 2 to 5: owner role, enforcing caps, call limits, self-serve and billing".
- The host console is five tabs, the same bar Manage has: **Host** (the facts, the host admins), **Plans**, **Environments** (the default), **AI** and **Maps**, so a host with many environments reads one thing at a time. And it follows the design system properly now: environments as spaced cards with an edge in their status's colour and their facts as tiles with a bar where the plan has a cap, the Edit form as a hint, two fields grids and a row of buttons (it used to put its hint and buttons into the grid), the plan catalog as cards on the fields grid, and number fields styled like every other field (a gap in the shared stylesheet that left them white). Asked as "we need to allow the server to scale, let's add tabs; make it look nice too; there are no spaces between sections; this is laid out poorly; they should be using the unified design system".
- The space bar has a **Dock all** button beside the stage-level snap: every floating pane that can be a column goes back beside the call in one click, and the stage-level snap goes off with it (it would float a pane again the moment it opened). A pane in its own window, or one that can only float, is left alone. Asked as "we are missing a way to snap all of them back into panel view at the space level".

### Changed
- The AI keys are the host's. The host offers a **managed** AI service per company: give the server `AI_OPENAI_KEY` or `AI_ANTHROPIC_KEY` (or both), or set a key on the host console's Managed AI panel (one row per company: the key write-only, the model listed from the company with a default chosen for it, an address for an OpenAI-compatible service of your own), and that company is offered to every environment. Each environment's AI configuration page starts with a **Source** choice: one Managed entry per offered company ("Managed: OpenAI, gpt-...") or Custom (its own service and key, the fields as before). Changing the source or the company switches AI off until it is enabled again; the allowance and this month's use stay the environment's. An install that only ever used `AI_KEY` becomes managed by itself, its provider and model filling that company's slot. Asked as "the setting for the API key for AI should be at the [host] level; then they choose managed or custom", then "we have never set anything but the key; let the host offer both, `AI_ANTHROPIC_KEY` and `AI_OPENAI_KEY`, and if set you do the rest". The environment's form is `public/ai-form.js`.
- The product page's hero carries the logo, large, above the headline; the bar's small one stays as the way home. Asked as "a bigger logo in the middle".
- The map is the host's ("same with maps", "only the admin can slice"): Maps (0.7.10) declares its map-tiles folder shared with the host, so with environments there is one folder of map files every environment shows, all together, and only a host admin adds files or cuts regions, on the host console's new **Maps** panel (files with size and zoom, delete, the world map address, and Add a region, the same region cut the module's page had, now `public/region-cut.js` shared by both). An environment's Maps configuration lists those files under "Provided by the host" and chooses nothing; the map-at-a-web-address option is gone for this phase (the host's map or nothing). On a single server nothing moves: the folder, the ticks and the region cut stay on the module's page. A `files` setting may declare `"shared": "host"` in any module's manifest.
- Research (0.2.9): the tags are chosen from a **Tags** button in the toolbar (a menu of every tag in use with its count, the chosen ones ticked, and Clear tags at the top) instead of every tag in use laid out as a wall of chips under the kinds; only the chosen tags show there, each with an x to drop it. And no more pills: the kind and tag chips, a card's tags and the backlinks are small rounded rectangles (5px corners, 22px tall), the tag's colour a small square. Reported as "the tags need a tag chooser and then only show what is filtered; no more hotdog designs, rounded-corner rectangles, small". The chooser is `host.ui.toolbarButton`, new in the SDK: one button that shares the toolbar row with the view switches.

### Changed
- Hang up leaves the call but keeps the conference pane: it says "Not in a call", the toolbar's red phone turns green to dial back in, and the other toolbar buttons wait. The pane's own close (the x on its titlebar) is what closes it now, taking the call with it as before. Asked as "when not in a call I think we still show the window but with Not in a call; that means this becomes a green button to dial back in".
- Closing a pane's own window (the conference, the chat) brings the pane back into the page, docked or floating as it was before it left, with whatever it holds untouched: the call keeps going, a chat keeps its draft. Before, closing the window closed the pane, which for the conference meant leaving the call. And the conference's titlebar in its own window has no close button any more, since it read as "close this window" while it meant "leave the call"; Hang up on the toolbar leaves. Asked as "if the user closes the popped out window it should restore itself in the main browser; I do not think we show the leave-the-call x in the popout window titlebar, they can hang up to leave".

### Fixed
- An organisation-level Anthropic key answered every call with a 400, "This API key is not scoped to a workspace, so this request must include the anthropic-workspace-id header" -- found from the diagnostics just below this entry. A new optional `workspace` field (letters, digits, underscore and dash, up to 100 characters; an id, not a secret, so it is shown plainly) on the host's managed Anthropic service (`PUT`/`GET /api/host/ai`, seeded from a new `AI_ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE` env var the same way the key is) and on an environment's own custom slot (`PUT`/`GET /api/ai`, carried through `effective()` for the managed offer too): `complete()` and `listModelsFor()` send it as the `anthropic-workspace-id` header whenever the provider is Anthropic and a workspace is set; every other provider ignores it. `tools/check-ai.mjs` gained coverage for the header being sent only for Anthropic, the field's validation, and the host's own managed-slot handling.
- Switching the AI source, company or provider always turned AI off, even between two already-working services -- reported live: with the Assistant and Research on, changing Source from Managed Anthropic to Managed OpenAI and saving answered "Assistant and Research needs the AI service; turn them off too?" and saved nothing, since `set()`/`previewEnabled()` treated any such change as turning AI off before the dependents guard ever got to ask. Now a change of source, company or provider keeps `enabled` as it was whenever the result is ready to answer with (a managed company the host offers, or a custom provider with its key); it turns `enabled` off only when the result is not ready (provider none, an unoffered managed company, a keyless custom provider) -- "setting a service up does not turn AI on" still holds, since an environment that has never enabled stays off either way. `effective()`, `managedOffer()` and the new `hasActiveService()` now take an optional candidate config, so `set()` and `previewEnabled()` can ask "would this be ready" about the patch about to be saved rather than only about what is saved already; `previewEnabled()` is rebuilt on top of the same `applyAiFields()` `set()` itself uses, rather than a hand-duplicated approximation of the rule (the old version, notably, never checked for a key at all). `tools/check-ai.mjs`'s existing coverage of switching services is updated to the new, correct behaviour rather than the old bug.
- A managed AI answer that failed said only "the AI service could not answer", with nothing to tell a 400 (a bad field) from a 404 (a model the account does not have) from a 529 (overloaded) -- and nothing logged either, so it could not be diagnosed after the fact. `server/ai.js`'s `complete()` now reads the provider's own error out of the response (Anthropic's `{ error: { type, message } }`, OpenAI's and a compatible one's `{ error: { message } }`), logs one line server-side with the provider, the model, the status and that message (never the key or the prompt), and folds the status and a sentence of the message into the answer itself: "the AI service could not answer (404: model: claude-haiku-4-5-20251001)". A refused key and a busy service keep their own plain wording, unchanged, rather than growing the provider's raw text too. The network-failure path (a timeout, a DNS failure, a refused connection) is logged and answered the same way, from the fetch error's own name and message. `tools/check-ai.mjs` gained a group driving all of this against its own stand-in service, including that the key and the prompt never reach a log line. The Anthropic request shape itself (`model`, `max_tokens`, `system`, `messages`, the `x-api-key` and `anthropic-version` headers) was checked by re-reading against the Messages API as documented, not against a live call -- no Anthropic key was available in this sandbox to place one.
- The map's search said "not configured for this space" with nothing anywhere saying where to configure it (the author: "there is no way to configure it"). Search is the place-search module's (Places) setting, chosen on that module's configuration page, and now both places say so: the map's message names the module that offers the search ("Search is not set up. It is the Places module's: choose a place search in its configuration (Manage, Modules)"), and Maps' own configuration page carries a note, **Search on the map**, saying the same. The note is a new setting type, `note`, in the module contract (`api-modules.md`): a label and a help with no control and no value, for a module to say where something is set up; the server half validates it. Maps 0.7.12.
- "Add a region" names the cut after what was typed ("thailand" becomes `thailand.pmtiles`), not after the name the place search answers with, which is often in the place's own script (Thailand came back as "ประเทศไทย", which slugged to nothing, so every cut was offered as `region.pmtiles` and the second one collided). And a new **Replace a file already there with that name** tick in the confirm block (both the console's Maps panel and a module's settings page) sends `replace: true` with the cut, for a fresh cut of the same region over the old file; the server half honours it.
- The world map address's help (the host console's Maps panel, the Maps module's "World file" setting and the Maps guide) now says what the address looks like: `https://build.protomaps.com/YYYYMMDD.pmtiles` with the build's date, an example given, and the console field's placeholder shows one. It used to say only that a daily build exists. The console's address form itself was sloppy (the author's word): the hint sat inside the label and came out in uppercase capitals over two lines, the Save button hung off the row, and the files table squeezed each file's name into the narrow first column meant for a thumbnail. It is now a label, the field with its button beside it, and the hint under them in sentence case; the file name column is as wide as it needs to be and the delete button sits at the right. Maps 0.7.11.
- "Add a region" with no world map address set said an admin needs to fix it "in Module Configuration", a place that does not exist. The shared cutter (`public/region-cut.js`) now turns the server's missing-address answer into "the world map address is not set: fill it in above and save it first", which is where the field sits on both the host console's Maps tab and a module's settings page (`wireRegionCut` takes a `sourceHint` for any page that puts it elsewhere).
- With a managed AI service chosen, Manage's AI service card said Not set up and kept Approve and enable disabled, so AI (and the Assistant, which needs it) could not be turned on; a space's AI section stayed hidden for the same reason. Both read the environment's own custom service, which is empty while the host's is in use. `GET /api/ai` now sends `ai.active`, the service in use whichever the source, and both pages read that.
- The conference popped out into a window of its own kept its titlebar and toolbar on screen for good: the idle-hide (the chrome slides away after 2.5 seconds without movement, back on the next) only knew the whole-table popout (`body.popout`) and only marked the page's own stage, not the window's. It now marks the stage the conference is wrapped in inside its window too (`roomModules.nativeWindow`), and the same slide-away rules apply to `body.conference-window`. Two more faults kept it from working on the first try ("the hiding is not working"): the timer looked up the settings popover, the reaction tray and the floating toolbar in the page, but they live inside the conference section and had moved into the window with it, so the lookup threw before anything was marked idle; and "keep the chrome while the chat is open" was meant for the whole-table popout, where the chat is in that window, not for the conference alone in a window with the chat left on the page. The timer looks in the conference's own document now, and the chat rule applies only while the call is on the page. And movement wakes only the window it happens in, so the pointer on the page no longer brings the popped-out window's chrome back ("a little odd that mousing over either shows it"). Reported as "when popped out, the video call is supposed to hide the titlebars and action bars when the mouse is not over".
- With the stage-level snap on, opening a pane that had never been opened in that space before (Calendar, say) threw on the missing saved entry (`saved[id].cell`) before the pane was registered or its module mounted: an empty pane with a header that could not be moved or closed, the switch still off, and another pane on every click; reported as "I turn on and it doesn't switch, keeps spawning windows, can't move it, close it, and it doesn't render". The cell is optional now (the snap settles from the pane's own box), in the three places that read it.
- Stream (0.1.1): the Participant box shows the Online or Offline picture (the space's own, the person's, then the server's Default Images) and falls back to the person's profile photo only when none of those is set, the rule the table's tiles already followed; before, a player with no Participant picture showed nothing. The real photo only, never the initials plate (`host.images.get(key, 'profile', { fallback: 'none' })`), and never on the Character box, which shows Character pictures alone. Asked as "make sure we are sending participant and character images, not profile images; profile only if the others are not set".
- Maps installed on a fresh environment showed a disabled Approve button; reported as "the approve button for maps was not enabled for the owner". It was not the role: Maps needs Places, and only the card said so, in small text under a button that could not be pressed. The "Available with this server" list now says "Needs Places installed and turned on first" in Maps' own row, before Install (`/api/modules`' bundled entries carry `requires`), and the Maps guide says to install Places first. Maps (0.7.9) and Places (0.8.12) settings help text still named the old product ("Tavern reads a PMTiles map file..."); it says "the server" now.
- Manage > Modules, "Available with this server": each row was a flex line with the button loose at the end, so a long description pushed Install about and short ones left it in a different place on every row; reported as "this is formatted poorly". A row is a three-column grid now (icon, name and description, Install), the buttons in one column at the right edge, a rule between rows. And the bundled modules' own comments still called the host "Tavern"; they say "the host" now, so the old product name is only ever read as old data (the old cookie, environment variables, data file and stored keys).
- Calendar (1.17.5) could not start after the clock setting landed ("Cannot access 'setClock' before initialization"): the shared lib is inlined after the page's own code inside one async function, so nothing of it may be called at start-up, only later. The lib now reads the clock itself each time it formats a time; no start-up call.
- Planner (0.7.20): a note card had no "..." at all -- `tpl-card-note` was the one card template without the menu button -- so an idea typed into the add row (a note) had no controls once it was there; reported as "once it is there, there are no controls". Every card has the one "..." now. And the item menu opened under its button even when that put it below the bottom of the pane (the last card of the plan, exactly where an idea used to sit), so it was there but unreachable; it opens above the button when there is no room below.
- Planner (0.7.18): the boarding pass lost its stub -- the perforated edge with seat, gate, class and the barcode, which is the whole point of the card -- in any pane under 720px, folding into a plain strip under the route with no barcode. It keeps the stub beside the route down to a tiny pane (`.app.tiny`, under 480px, set beside `.narrow`), and where it does fold it keeps the barcode across the strip. Verified live at 605px (the stub stays) and 420px (it folds, barcode kept).
- Assistant (0.1.8) repeated its own name and icon: the titlebar already draws them, but `.ask-head` drew them again in-page, unconditionally, since Assistant had never wired up `tavern.header.set` (unlike every other bundled module). New conversation is now the titlebar icon wherever there is one; the in-page button stays only as the fallback for the module's own page when it is not popped out (no titlebar there).
- Planner (0.7.16): a time block or a marker between days left untitled in its own editor (its type's label is only the field's placeholder, not a stored value) was saved with a genuinely empty title, which read as generic wherever a title is shown outside Planner's own rendering (Assistant's context picker showed a wall of "Untitled" rows for exactly these items, one per day's automatic marker). An untitled block or marker now stores its type's own label as its real title, so it has a name anywhere it's shown, not just in Planner's own view, which already fell back to the type's label on its own.
- `/api/modules/nav` serves two different callers -- the topbar's icon list (`brand.js`) and a module's own page asking "does this module exist, what is its entry" (`module.js`) -- and filtering the endpoint itself on the new `surfaces.page.nav` opt-out (added for Places, below) broke the second: any module with `nav: false` could no longer load its own standalone page at all. The opt-out now only affects the topbar's list; the endpoint itself is unfiltered.

### Added
- Map region cutting: the Module Configuration UI (step 4 of plan-map-region-download.md). "Add a region" -- find a place, a real dry-run size estimate before anything downloads, a progress bar over the job's own server-sent events, the new file ticked in the files list above on success. Generic, gated on a module declaring `regionSource`, not Maps-specific. Verified live end-to-end against the real Protomaps daily build: a real search ("San Marino"), a real estimate, a real cut, landing on disk and ticked at the matching size; also checked a no-match search and Cancel.

### Changed
- Polls (1.12.1): Close and Delete moved off the card into a "..." menu in its corner, labeled End poll/Reopen poll and Delete poll -- "Close" read as ambiguous next to a card, easy to mistake for dismissing it rather than ending the poll. The existing "Really delete?" double-confirm is unchanged, just inside the menu. Verified live: open/close the menu, End<->Reopen, and the full delete confirm flow.
- Planner's itinerary timeline (0.7.13): a run of hidden empty days no longer piles a separate "+" joint onto the single gap badge already standing in for it (each hidden day used to get its own, all landing on the same spot on the line as the badge); a plan-wide marker's date now shows inside its pill on a narrow pane instead of stranded in its own row above with empty space beside it; the gap-add pill's outer edge is fully rounded (an 8px radius read as barely rounded against the pill's own height). Verified live at narrow and wide widths, and with cards of different heights.
- A module's page can leave itself out of the main nav's icon row (`surfaces.page.nav: false`, default true) for one better reached another way -- Places (0.8.3) now does, since it is reached from a room's own pane. Generic, not Places-specific: any module can set it.
- Calendar (1.17.1) and Planner (0.7.13)'s view-switcher and action icons had drifted to a bigger, in-page button style instead of the small titlebar icons every other module (To-do, Polls) uses once hosted in a pane or its own window -- neither had ever wired up `tavern.header`. Both now do, the same way To-do and Polls already did, with the in-page row hidden once the host draws the icons (still shown on a plain page, with no titlebar to draw them into). Verified live: Calendar's four views and Planner's four views plus its two actions all show and work as small hosted icons, with the bigger in-page row gone underneath.

### Fixed
- A floating module pane kept showing on top of the room list after leaving a room's own view (still connected, just not looking at the stage) -- it lives in its own layer beside the stage, which hiding the stage never touched. `room-modules.js` gained `showFloating()`, called from `showRoomList()`/`returnToStage()`; nothing is torn down, so the pane is exactly as it was when the stage comes back. Verified with a throwaway harness driving the pane manager directly: the layer hides and shows on cue, the pane stays open and floating throughout, same DOM node reused both times.
- A module pane was torn down and rebuilt from scratch on every dock<->float switch (the same code path as closing it), silently dropping whatever the module was holding onto -- a conversation, a draft, an open editor. Switching now moves the pane's existing frame, action bar and header buttons into the new chrome instead, the same way the chat (a native pane) already did -- a core-SDK fix (`public/room-modules.js`), so every bundled module gets it, not just Assistant. Popping into its own window is a real new document, so that still starts the module over. Verified with a throwaway harness exercising the pane manager directly: one mount, zero remounts, across repeated dock<->float switches.

### Added
- Map region cutting: a basic world layer under detailed regional cuts. "Add a region" gained **cover the whole world** (a fixed global box, skipping search, at a low default zoom) and a **Minimum zoom** field, so a detailed regional cut can skip the low zooms a wide base layer already has (`estimate` reflects the trimmed range too). "Map files" gained a **Zoom** column, read straight from each `.pmtiles` file's own header (new `server/pmtiles-header.js`) -- works for any file there, cut through this flow or dropped in by hand. Verified live end-to-end: a whole-world cut (zoom 0-5, ~15 MB) alongside a Lisbon cut trimmed to zoom 11-14 (42 kB versus 563 kB untrimmed at the same box, about 92% smaller), both landing ticked with their real zoom ranges shown.
- A delete button on every row of any module's `files` setting table (an admin-placed file, "Map files" being the first): `DELETE /api/modules/:id/files/:name`, admin only, gone for good, with a confirm first; also un-ticks it from whatever setting named it, so nothing keeps pointing at a file that is no longer there. Not Maps-specific -- any module's `files` setting gets it. Verified live: deleted an unticked file and a ticked one, both removed from disk and from the setting's stored value, the table redrawing without either.

### Changed
- Maps (0.7.4): a place's label prefers its English name (`name:en`, an OpenStreetMap tag most named places carry) over the local script, never blank for the rare place that has no English name recorded. Verified against real tile data for a real region (not a guess at the field name): the Protomaps basemap docs confirm `name:en`, and every one of 13 real localities decoded from a live tile (Nikko, Utsunomiya and others, in Tochigi/Fukushima) carried both `name` and `name:en`.

### Fixed
- Map region cutting: after a successful cut, Check size stayed disabled for the rest of the page's life -- invisible cutting one region per page load, but every second cut in the same session (exactly the basic-then-detailed pattern above) could silently not be checked or cut at all.
- A region cut (map region download) failed on a real deployment with `EXDEV: cross-device link not permitted`: the background job wrote its temp file to the system's own `/tmp`, a different filesystem from `DATA_DIR` in a container with a separate mounted volume, so the final move into place could never succeed there (a local dev setup happens to keep both on one filesystem, which is why this passed local live testing before). The temp file now lives inside `DATA_DIR` itself (its own `tmp/region-cut/` folder, cleaned up the same way as before), so the move is always on one filesystem. Verified with a real cut against the real Protomaps daily build. `tools/check-region-cut.mjs` gained an assertion that the temp path stays under `DATA_DIR`.
- A setting's own `help` text (module.json) was silently cut off at 200 characters, mid-sentence, with nothing to say so happened -- a choice option's own help already had ten times the room (600, with line breaks kept). Maps' `worldSource` setting was the one that surfaced it (its help ends with a URL, past the 200-character mark). Now 600, keeping line breaks, the same treatment a choice option's help already had. New `tools/check-modules.mjs`.
- Map region cutting's default Maximum zoom was 10, which never shows individual streets -- a poor default for a planning app, and the field carried no explanation of what it actually controlled. Now defaults to 14 (streets and buildings visible) and explains itself: what the number trades off (detail against file size, not how far the cut reaches), roughly what 10, 14 and 15 each look like, and that Check size shows the real cost for whatever is chosen before anything downloads.

### Added
- Cutting a map region, steps 1-3 of plan-map-region-download.md. The Dockerfile now vendors the `pmtiles` CLI (a static Go binary from its own GitHub release, one per architecture, checked against a pinned sha256; verified by a real push and a real multi-arch build, not locally). A module opts in to region cutting with `regionSource` in its manifest (a file folder and a url setting naming the source); admin-only routes give a dry-run size estimate before anything downloads, start a background cut (one at a time per module, a temp file renamed into place only on success, nothing half-written left on failure), and follow it over server-sent events. Maps is the first to use it (`worldSource`, a new url setting for the world file to cut from). A new `GET .../region-cut/find?q=` route turns a typed place name into a rough rectangle, asking whichever enabled module has a place search configured (the existing `geocoder` conduit, found generically, never by naming Places) and reading the `extent` a Photon-compatible service returns for an administrative or area result. Verified against a stand-in CLI (`tools/check-region-cut.mjs`, no network) and, separately, against the real Protomaps daily build and the real Photon service on a running server: the estimate and the finished file matched the CLI's own numbers exactly, a duplicate name and a second concurrent cut were both refused, and "Mexico" and other named places came back with a real box. Step 4 (the Module Configuration UI) is still to build.

### Added
- Send all to plan (Assistant 0.1.7): under a reply with more than one card, **Send all to plan** keeps every card not already kept, in order, each the way its own keep button would (a card with a recognised `kind` through the suggestion-shaped action, any other through the note-shaped one), after one confirm naming what is about to go out grouped by kind ("2 hotels, 1 sight, 1 note"). Verified against a running server with a stand-in AI (three cards: a hotel, a sight, a plain note): the button showed the right count, the confirm named the right groups, and all three requests queued with the right shape and action per card (two `acceptSuggestion` to Planner, one `saveNote` to Research); Research's own page claimed and saved its note live. Planner's two, and a wider multi-tab run, were confirmed by the same queued-request shape rather than a second live claim (the test browser exhausted its connections to the origin with five tabs open against long-lived SSE streams).

### Added
- An admin's own Font Awesome Pro package, if they have a licence, dropped at `DATA_DIR/fontawesome-pro/` (the "Web" download's `css/`, `webfonts/` and `svgs/`, the same shape as the bundled Free set): served and looked up ahead of the bundled Free icons, falling back to Free for any style or icon it does not have. Never built into the shared image, never a token in this repository; every other self-hoster stays Free-only. Verified against a running server with a stand-in Pro folder: a Pro-only icon served, a Free-only icon still falling back, and both the CSS and webfont routes preferring the Pro file when present.

### Added
- Multiple typed cards per reply (Assistant 0.1.5, Planner 0.7.9). A card may carry an optional `kind` (a flight, a hotel, a sight, and ten more everyday words; ordinary domain language, checked against a fixed list, never a module's own names), and the model is told to write one card per thing when a request plainly asks for several (an itinerary, a few hotel options), with the per-reply ceiling raised from 3 to 20. Planner provides a new generic action, `acceptSuggestion` (`title`, optional `kind`, `content`, `place`, `date`), found by Assistant by name and input shape, never by naming Planner: given a `kind` it recognises, it places a properly typed journey, stay or stop on the day the card names; keeping a card with a recognised `kind` now prefers this over saving a plain note when Planner is installed. Verified against a running server with a stand-in AI and both modules: a card marked `kind:"hotel"` kept as a Planner stay of type hotel on the right day, its content in the item's notes; `tools/check-ai.mjs` (the kind field, the raised ceiling) and `tools/check-travel.mjs` (`acceptSuggestion` for a journey, a stay, a recognised stop, an unrecognised kind, and none). The "Send all to plan" bulk action and a kind-coloured card badge are still to build (interface side).
- Assistant renders the AI's own Markdown (Assistant 0.1.5): the reply text and a card's content use the shared `tavern.util.markdown` instead of showing literal asterisks and hashes; a card's content slot is a `div` now, not a `p`, since rendered Markdown can hold headings and lists. Verified in a browser against a running server and a stand-in service: headings, a numbered list and bold/italic in both the reply text and a card's content render correctly.

### Added
- A module that declares the `ai` hook now depends on the AI service the way one module can depend on another (`requires`): it cannot be turned on until the AI service is enabled (the module view's `missing` carries the reserved id `ai` for that, alongside any `requires`), and turning the AI service off while such a module is enabled asks first (`PUT /api/ai` refuses with a plain message unless `force: true`, which turns those modules off with it), with `GET /api/ai` carrying `dependents` for the same client-side confirm the Modules tab already does for `requires`. `ai` can no longer be used as a module's own id, so the sentinel can never collide with a real one. Verified against a running server: Assistant blocked from enabling before AI is set up, enabled once it is, then both a direct disable and a provider switch away from a working service refused without `force` and applied (cascading Assistant off) with it; and with `tools/check-ai.mjs` (`previewEnabled`, the pure check behind the refusal).

### Added
- Assistant, a new module (0.1.1): a standalone pane (dock, float, popout) for an open-ended conversation with the AI the admin set up. It keeps no data of its own. Context is added by hand (a picker over everything reachable: the room's items and the viewer's own, from any module) or arrives with the generic `askAssistant` action (optional `ref`, optional `question`; a `local` action, so it opens and runs only in the requester's own already-open Assistant, never someone else's), which any module can request to open a conversation about one of its items. A card the model writes is kept by asking whichever module offers a note-shaped save action (a `title` and a `body`, found by name and input shape, never by naming a module); Research's `saveNote` is the first to offer one, and now also takes an optional `tags` string. A kept card's question and sources fold into the note's body as plain, readable lines, since the action bus carries no list of tags or several pointers of its own; a single source still travels on as the note's `ref`, a live link. Verified in a browser against a running server and a stand-in AI service: Research's "Research this" queues `askAssistant`, opening Assistant for the first time claims and answers it with the item already added as context, the reply and its card render (tags, the basis note, a resolved source pill), the picker lists and adds items, and keeping a card creates a linked, tagged note once Research's own page is open to claim the save. New userguide-assistant.md. Not verified: the light theme and the narrow layout in a real session (checked only by resizing/emulating), a real hosted or local model, two people using Assistant in the same room at once.

### Changed
- Research 0.2.0: the Ask conversation (0.1.10-0.1.12) moved out to the new Assistant module. A card's menu now has **Research this** (was "Ask about this"), which requests `askAssistant` from whichever module offers it, found generically; Research's own Ask button, its context picker and the conversation thread are gone. The `answer` kind still reads items saved before this change, but nothing new creates one; a card kept from Assistant arrives here as an ordinary, tagged note, linked to what it was about. `saveNote` gained an optional `tags` field (a comma- or space-separated string).

### Changed
- Ask answers like an assistant (Research 0.1.12). `tavern.ai.ask({ task: 'ask' })` works with no items: the model may use its own knowledge, uses any items as context and says which parts came from them, and is told to be honest about uncertainty and how recent its knowledge is (places, prices and opening times may be out of date). Cards carry a validated `basis` (general, items or both; general when no items were given). Summarise and tags still use only the material. In Research, Ask opens with no context; items are added from a picker as chips, and the card says where its content came from. Verified against a stand-in service and in a browser (an answer with no context, its basis note, the picker adding a chip). Not verified: a real model's answers.

### Added
- An explicit **enable** step for the AI: `enabled` in `GET /api/ai` and `PUT /api/ai`. Nothing reaches an AI service until the admin enables it; `ready()` and a module's `available` need it. A setting saved before this step, with a service chosen, counts as enabled; choosing a different service switches it off again until enabled. Enabling needs a service and model saved. Verified with the stand-in service.

### Added
- The conference can be switched off (optional conference, stage 1). A server setting `conferenceEnabled` (on by default, accepted by `PATCH /api/settings`, listed on the Conference card of `GET /api/modules` as `enabled` with its wording): off, the "See and join the conference" permission reads false for everyone, so joins carry no media (no publishing, not in the call) and the room page shows no conference, tiles or camera and microphone buttons, while chat, presence, asides data and the modules keep working over LiveKit. The Roles tab keeps the stored ticks. LiveKit is still required at startup; making it optional is a later stage. Verified against a running server (the token with and without media, the setting, the card, the Roles tab unchanged). Not verified: the room page with the conference off in a real call, the OBS pages and the guest link.

### Changed
- The AI service is chosen by company: **OpenAI** and **Anthropic** need only a key (Tavern knows their addresses, and any address sent for them is ignored); **Other OpenAI-compatible** takes an address and may have no key. A setting saved earlier as an OpenAI-compatible address is read as Other. New admin route `POST /api/ai/models` lists a service's own models for the admin to pick from (OpenAI: chat models only, newest first; Anthropic; another service's `/v1/models`), using the key just typed or the saved one, with plain errors (no key, refused, unreachable). Saving needs a key and a model for OpenAI and Anthropic, and an address and a model for Other. Requests to OpenAI use `max_completion_tokens`. Verified against a stand-in service (the lists and their filters, the typed key winning, errors, the migration, both new providers' requests). Not verified: the real OpenAI or Anthropic lists.

### Added
- Research 0.1.8: backlink pills on a card (room view): one pill per kind of item that points at it, "Task: book the hotel" or "2 plans", with the linking module's icon; a click opens the first. Asked once per item and refreshed when links change. Not verified in a browser.

### Added
- Research 0.1.6: dropping an item from another module on the pane starts a note about it (titled from the item, linked to it in the room view; a note in Mine is not linked), with the pane framed and a hint shown while something is over it; a photo whose picture will not load shows a placeholder. Not verified in a browser: the drag and drop, and the suggest-tags button.

### Added
- Research 0.1.4: the conflict bar and the answer source pills come from the interface's templates; a **Suggest tags** action (a button with `data-action="suggest-tags"` in the dialog, shown for a saved note, link or answer when the AI is available) asks the AI for tags and adds them to the tags field for the person to keep or change. Not verified in a browser: the button is not in the templates yet.

### Added
- Research 0.1.2: notes, links and photos a group keeps, each a card (tags, search by word, kind and tag, drag onto a plan, a place and a date, Mine and This room), with an Ask panel over the AI hook where cards the model writes are drawn inline and can be kept as answers. Photos are resized in the page (about 2000 px and a 400 px thumbnail), uploaded through the new file store, and their position is left out unless the person keeps it; removing a photo removes its file. Other modules can save a note or a link through the `saveNote` and `saveLink` actions. The file store gained `tavern.uploads.inspect` (what the start of a picture says: when it was taken, the camera, whether it has a position), and `uploads.put` and `uploads.thumb` now answer with the file itself. Verified in a browser against a running server: the panel, a note through the dialog with tags, a photo through the whole upload (inspect, resize, upload, thumbnail, card with its picture, removal deleting the file), the Ask panel with a stand-in AI (the card drawn between the sentences, its source named, keeping it stored an answer with the question and sources). The model is checked by tools/check-research.mjs. Not verified: a real phone or camera, the position question with a photo that has one, drag onto a plan, the light theme, the narrow layout, and Mine.

### Added
- The AI hook. A module declares `ai` and asks the server (`tavern.ai.available`, `tavern.ai.ask`); the admin sets up one AI service for the whole server (an OpenAI-compatible address, which also reaches a local model server, or Anthropic; a model; a key kept on the server and never sent back, or `TAVERN_AI_KEY`; an optional monthly token limit; usage counted by task). A new role permission, **Use AI in modules**, is off for every role, guests never have it, and a room can turn AI off for itself (`aiOff` on the room). The server reads the chosen items as the person asking, sends them inside a frame that says they are data and not instructions, gives the model no tools, and returns its words with any cards the model wrote checked field by field (icon from a list, one-line title, plain content, one-word tags, in-range place, real date, https links, sources only from the given items). Tasks: summarise, ask, suggest tags. A card's `text` field (a note's own words, up to 8 KB) is read only for the AI. Nothing is stored server-side; the activity list gets who, module, task and tokens, never text. Verified with a stand-in service (the request's frame and key header, both providers, cards checked, tags, the monthly limit) and against a running server (not set up, then set up, the key never returned, an item read as the person, a missing item left out, the activity line). Not verified: a real hosted or local model, the role and room switches for a non-admin, and streaming (the answer comes whole for now).

### Added
- A file store for modules: a manifest can declare `uploads` and keep pictures per scope (server, room, person) under the same permissions as its data. The server identifies each file from its own bytes (JPEG, PNG or WebP; anything else is refused), strips what rides along (comments, text chunks, thumbnails, maker notes, editing history, XMP) and the position unless the person keeps it, reads when it was taken and the camera, keeps a JPEG's orientation, and serves it as a picture only (`nosniff`, a sandboxing policy, private cache). Size and count limits, a per-person rate limit, a thumbnail per file (made by the page), and removal by the person who added it or an administrator. `tavern.uploads.put/thumb/list/remove/url` in the SDK. The server does not decode or resize pictures (Tavern's dependencies are pure JavaScript so the images build for every architecture); the page sizes them before sending. Verified with pictures built by hand (position and hidden data gone, orientation kept, non-pictures and truncated files refused) and against a running server (upload, read back, thumbnail, wrong type, size, count, sign-in, removal). Not verified: an upload from a real phone or camera.

### Added
- Place search answered by the server, with a saved collection of results (Places 0.8.0, Maps 0.7.2). A module's manifest can declare `geocoder`; `tavern.geocode.search` asks the server, which looks in the places it has saved first and asks the chosen service (Photon, or the admin's own address) only when fewer than five saved places match, and keeps what comes back (a **Save places to the server** setting, off by default, shown once a search is chosen; a new `showWhen: { key, not }` form). A record is the OpenStreetMap type and number, name, address, position, category, first and last seen, how often, and whether it was picked (`used`, set when a result is saved as a place); never the query, the searcher or the room. Each result says where it came from. New admin routes give the counts and remove unpicked, old unpicked or all saved places. The page no longer contacts the service itself, so the service's CORS setting no longer matters. Verified against a running server with a stub service: one outside call for two searches, the second answered from saved results, the used mark, the counts and every purge, saving off keeping nothing, sign-in required. Not verified: the Places page with results from a real service, and the settings and admin section drawn (the interface side is still to do).

### Added
- Planner 0.7.8: markers between the days (a new item kind, `lane`): a marker of one of the marker types, with an optional label and a note and no time, that sits on the plan's line after a given day (or before the first day) rather than in a day. Added from the small + on the line at each joint (and from a hidden-days badge, which now adds one at its place rather than a time block), moved with the menu (Earlier, Later, Move to a joint) or by dragging the pill to another joint and among the markers there, edited with a type, label and note only, and removed from the menu. They never count as something planned. Verified in a browser: the joints and the markers between days in place, adding from a joint's menu, the menu's Move to and Later saving `after`, and the editor with the Between days tiles and no day. The order arithmetic for dropping among markers is tested. Not verified: the pointer drag itself, the light theme and the narrow layout.

### Changed
- Planner 0.7.4: a time block's menu has **Change type** (a choice of the marker types; the pill's icon and colour follow) and says **Remove** instead of Delete. The automatic plan-wide markers are drawn, not items, so they have no menu. Verified in a browser: the type list, a change from rest to buffer saved and recoloured, and the ordinary items' menu unchanged.

### Added
- Planner 0.7.1: markers as one two-part pill in a colour of their own, from a **Markers** list in Module Configuration (a new `list` setting type: rows with a label, an icon and a colour, some fixed; the four automatic markers can be changed but not removed, and others can be added, reordered and removed). Time blocks, a new kind of item for what happens inside a day and has no place (free time, rest, buffer, meet-up, leave-by to start with), with a **Time** group in the editor. A badge on the line stands in for each run of hidden empty days, its + adding a time block on the first hidden day or showing the days again, replacing the "N empty days hidden" note. Verified against a running server (a fixed row cannot be removed, a bad colour is refused, a new row's id is made from its label) and in a browser (the marker colours from the list, a time block, the gaps and their menu adding a block, the editor's Time tiles). Not verified: the settings page's list control, the light theme, the narrow layout.

### Changed
- Planner 0.6.7: the plan-wide markers (Planning starts and ends, Trip starts and ends) now sit on the main timeline between the day blocks instead of inside a day: the plan's start and the trip's start above their days, the trip's end and the plan's end below theirs. The days the trip itself covers (first to last booked item) are marked `.in-trip`, so their badges can be coloured differently from the planning days around them. Checked in a browser: the order of edges, markers and day blocks and the marked days.

### Added
- Planner 0.6.6: automatic markers (Planning starts and ends on the first and last day; Trip starts and ends at the first and last booked item, which is a journey, a stay or anything with a booking reference, else the first and last timed item), buttons to add 1 to 30 days before the first or after the last day (up to 60 days in all, said in the page's note), a toggle that hides empty days (remembered per person, off by default, never hiding everything), and dragging a card by its body to move it within the plan: an untimed item reorders, a timed item keeps its time on another day and, dropped between two items on the same day, takes the end of the one before it. Verified in a browser: the markers in place, the empty-day toggle and its note and storage, adding days after (the trip's dates saved), the 60-day limit note, and moving items with synthetic drag events (a timed item to 15:00 after a 14:00 one, to another day keeping its time). Not verified: the pointer drag itself on a real page (it shares the move logic), the light theme and the narrow layout.

### Added
- Maps 0.7.0: a **Map source** choice, as Place search has: **A map file on this server** (the default, so existing servers keep working) or **A map file at a web address**, an https link to a PMTiles file hosted elsewhere and read in pieces over the web. Its help says what it costs: every viewer's browser contacts that host, which can see their address and roughly the area they look at, and the host must allow CORS and range requests. A `url` setting can now be limited to https and to a path ending in a given extension, and always refuses a user name or password in the address. An unreachable address shows the map's "could not load" state with Try again. Verified: the three refusals against a running server and the unreachable state in a browser. Not verified: reading a real map from a real host (no https host to try here).

### Fixed
- Planner 0.6.4: a linked item that has been deleted (a poll, an event, a place) no longer shows as if it were still there. Answers about a pointer now say what to draw (`state`: `gone` or `hidden`), and Planner draws a muted "No longer available" (or "Not available to you", without revealing anything else) with a **Remove from plan** button, never opens an editor for it, hides Open and Edit, and shows a linked item's current title instead of one it stored. It looks again every 20 seconds and when the page comes back into view. Verified in a browser: a poll linked to a day, deleted, then drawn as gone, its Edit hidden and Remove taking it off the plan. Not yet: the server announcing a deletion at once, cleaning up when no page is open, and date sync (the linked-items plan, steps 2 to 5).

### Changed
- Planner 0.6.2: dragging one of your own private places (Mine in Places) onto a day now copies it to the room first, through the module that saves places, and the plan points at the copy, since a pointer to a private item would show as gone to everyone but you. Without such a module, or if the copy fails, it says so instead. Places 0.7.1 (the UI's) can now be dragged out to other modules. Not verified by dragging on a real page.

### Changed
- Places 0.7.0: the search setting is a **Place search** choice: None (the default), Photon, the public service (searches go to photon.komoot.io; its help says what is sent, its terms and the OpenStreetMap credit, and the results say "Search by Photon · © OpenStreetMap contributors"), or My own address (the old Search address, shown only for that choice). A server that already had a search address and never chose keeps it working as My own address. With None, a search answers that it is not configured. New generic manifest options: `showWhen` on a setting, `help` on a choice option, and `defaultIfSet` on a choice. Verified against a running server (the migration, an explicit None, Photon) and in a browser (the settings page and a Photon search with the request stubbed, showing the credit).

### Changed
- Maps 0.6.1: the map file setting is now a table of the files in the map folder, each with its size and a tick (a new `files` setting type: a list of names, up to 20, each checked to exist). Maps draws every ticked file together, layer by layer, so regions that sit side by side make one map; a single file chosen before still works. Verified: a bad name refused, both files saved and untick then re-tick saved through the settings page, and the map drawing with two files ticked; the style check covers several sources. Overlapping files of different builds are not reconciled.

### Changed
- Reloading the page keeps you in your room instead of sending you back to the rooms screen: the room is remembered for that browser tab and rejoined when the page starts again. It is forgotten when you leave, are removed, or the room cannot be joined; an aside or private word is not remembered (it is gone once it ends), and a guest link already stays in its room. Verified that a reload with a remembered room asks to join it; the live call itself could not be tested here.

### Added
- Places 0.6.0: the **Everyone** view, the places for the whole server (the module's server scope). Anyone who holds Places' edit right may add, change and delete there, the same right as in a room; nobody is singled out, and guests, who are tied to one room, do not see it. Places also has a page of its own outside any room, with Mine and Everyone. Maps 0.5.4 draws everyone's places too. Verified: another ordinary user reads and edits the server's places, guests are refused, and in a browser the three views in a room and the two on the page, adding a place to Everyone. Not verified: the light theme, the narrow layout.

### Added
- Places 0.5.0: a **Mine** and **This room** switch. Mine is the person's own places, kept in their private store (the same in every room, invisible to everyone else including administrators); This room is the room's, as before. A place's menu copies it between them (**Share to this room**, **Save to mine**). Personal places are not linked to other modules' items. Maps 0.5.3 draws the viewer's own places too, with `data-scope="person"` on their pins and callout. The Everyone view is not built yet (who may change it is undecided). Verified in a browser: the switch and its note, the two lists, a copy from Mine to the room, and Maps drawing a personal place with its category and scope marks.

### Added
- A `person` scope for module data: a module that declares it keeps data for the signed-in person alone, from any page of theirs (`scope: 'person'` in the SDK), with the same store, versions and live changes as a room's. Nobody else can read it, not even an administrator; guests have none. Pointers to personal items resolve only for their owner and are never linked. Places 0.4.0 declares it (the my-places view is next). Verified against a running server: an admin's personal data invisible to another user, refused for guests and for a module without the scope, card search and resolve returning only the owner's, and links refused. Not verified: live changes to a personal store over the stream in a browser.

### Changed
- Maps 0.5.0 has no list of places any more: Places is the list (Maps requires it), so the panel, its rows and groups, the narrow-layout sheet, the list button and the list-only fallback are gone. The map fills the pane; a selected pin shows a callout over it (name, where, where it comes from, open in my maps app, copy coordinates, and Open in the owning module), and a device without WebGL is told so. Verified in a browser: the full-width map with a categorised pin and its callout.

### Changed
- Travel is now called **Planner**, since it will hold more than trips. Only the name changes (the module's id, its stored data and its pointers stay as they were): the Modules tab, the pane and page titles, its messages and the user guide (now userguide-planner). Planner 0.6.1.

### Changed
- Search moved from Maps to Places. Places 0.3.0 owns the Search address setting and finds places by name from its bottom bar (results over the foot of the pane, **Save** keeps one), and offers two view actions: `searchPlaces` (answers with a few results) and `newPlace` (opens its own dialog for a new place at a spot). Maps 0.4.0 has no Search address or place dialog of its own: its search box shows what Places finds as dashed candidate pins, and a click on a pin, a result, or the map (in add mode) asks Places to open its dialog with the spot filled in. An action's result can now carry a small `data` (up to 8 KB of plain data). Maps also no longer treats a missing glyph range as the map failing to load. Verified in two browser tabs: a name in Places' bar searching and saving a result, Maps' search returning Places' results as candidate pins, and a click on one opening Places' dialog with the name, address and position filled in. Not verified: the light theme, the narrow layout, the new found-list styling (the design's CSS was not exercised by Maps' candidate pins yet).

### Added
- Modules can declare `requires` (other modules by id). A module cannot be enabled until what it requires is installed and on, the Modules tab says what is missing and disables the button, and turning off (or uninstalling) a module that others need asks first and turns them off with it. Maps 0.3.1 requires Places. Verified against a running server: enabling Maps first is refused, enabling Places then Maps works, disabling Places without `force` is refused and with it turns Maps off.

### Added
